Absolute Regression
===================

Chapter 851: The World Is Full of Swindlers
-------------------------------------------
When Geom Mugeuk went to find Mabul, he was sitting in the great hall, tending to a fishing rod.

At that sight, Geom Mugeuk was startled.

“How did you know?”

“Know what?”

Mabul looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression asking what he was talking about.

“I mean that fishing rod.”

“What about it? I just have nothing to do. I was planning to fish the time away.”

“Is that the only reason?”

“If not? Must there be another reason?”

Geom Mugeuk sat down next to Mabul with a still-suspicious gaze.

“Why are you looking at me like that?”

“It’s nothing.”

Mabul turned his eyes back to the fishing rod and asked nonchalantly.

“I heard you’ve been busy meeting with the Majons lately?”

“You know that too.”

Could it be a coincidence that he was preparing to fish? As if!

If Geukak Soma had a fishing rod, it might be a coincidence. But Mabul?

“You are truly amazing. How in the world did you find out that a fishing competition is going to be held?”

Geom Mugeuk was certain that he had figured out about the competition beforehand.

As expected, Mabul was not surprised in the slightest by the mention of a fishing competition.

“Did you not tell me so yourself? That I should return to my old self. Was there anything happening in the main sect that the old me wouldn’t know? I even know the location where the fishing competition will be held.”

“Something even I, the Sogyojo, did not know! Truly magnificent!”

For the first time in a while, Geom Mugeuk heaped praise upon him.

“Our little giant, Lord Mabul, has finally returned to his former self! The very incarnation of ambitious power! Our Lord Mabul has returned!”

An unknowable mischievousness flashed across Mabul’s face, which wore a pleased expression.

“By the way, are you skilled at fishing?”

“I did some in my youth.”

The way he handled the fishing rod was far from ordinary. This was bad. Dokwang needed to take first place. He hadn’t even told Geukak Soma that he was backing Dokwang.

“Are you aiming for first place?”

“Naturally.”

“Did not the Buddha teach us to discard greed?”

“Our Hyeolbul is different. He says to seize and take everything you can.”

Was it only his hands that were quick? Mabul was quick-witted too.

“Why? Is there someone you are secretly rooting for?”

“Of course not. Am I not the very incarnation of competitive spirit?”

He couldn’t be caught backing Dokwang. He had to somehow make him win.

Mabul began tidying the fishing line again. Geom Mugeuk sat beside him, gazing at the sky, then suddenly seemed to remember something and handed him the liquor he had brought.

“Right, save this liquor and drink it later. Lord Chwima gave me some of the liquor he had been cherishing.”

“He wouldn’t give such precious liquor for nothing. What do you want?”

“Even if I don’t say it, you know what I’m troubled by, don’t you?”

Mabul placed the fishing rod he was holding down on the floor of the great hall.

“The only problem remaining for you is the Gyojo.”

Geom Mugeuk asked with calm eyes.

“What should I do?”

“You’ve been bustling about; haven’t you already found the answer?”

If he combined all the solutions the Majons had given him, one answer would emerge.

And Mabul’s thoughts were absolutely necessary to create that answer.

Because he possessed a unique insight that only Mabul had.

“Still, among the Majons, you are the most brilliant and coldly rational one, Lord Mabul. I must certainly listen to your words.”

Even though he knew it was flattery, the golden radiance emanating from Mabul grew thicker.

“There is only one thing I will tell you.”

Mabul did not look at this situation emotionally.

He offered a single-word solution.

“Justification.”

“!”

Without needing further explanation, he knew exactly what was meant.

“You mean I must give Father a justification to abandon the war.”

Mabul nodded.

“He is your father, but to us, he is the Gyojo. It may seem like no one is watching, but everyone in the sect is observing the Gyojo. What decisions the Gyojo makes, and why he makes them. Each and every one of those decisions becomes authority and leads to loyalty.”

Mabul emphasized once more.

“You must give him a justification with which he can abandon it. A justification that everyone watching can accept. That is what is most important.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. It was the right advice. It was valuable counsel coming from Mabul, who had worked to gain power by navigating between people.

“Thank you, Lord Mabul. You have told me something important that I was overlooking.”

“So? Was it worth the liquor?”

“How could it compare to a single bottle of this liquor? I’ll go steal all the liquor from the Daecheollim right now.”

But Mabul did not even accept that single bottle.

“Take this and give it to someone else.”

“Why? This liquor is incredibly delicious.”

“I’ve already tasted it.”

“Pardon? You’ve tasted it?”

“Yesterday, Lord Chwima came by and we had a drink together.”

Geom Mugeuk was surprised. He had never imagined that Chwima would come find Mabul for a drink.

In fact, the two had grown close while fighting the Dark Palace Lord. Chwima had been deeply moved, especially by Mabul’s willingness to sacrifice himself in the battle within the painting.

“Truly, Lord Mabul is a man blessed with many ties. Lose one person, and another appears.”

“Whom did I lose?”

“Ah, you haven’t met him yet? If you meet Lord Dokwang showing his true colors, you’ll know who the greatest con artist in the martial world is. You must go and see for yourself. And take back all the poisonous herbs you’ve given him until now, too.”

Mabul could tell that Dokwang had shown Geom Mugeuk a new side of himself.

“It’s all a matter of fooling each other and playing along.”

Leaving behind Mabul, who acted like an enlightened monk, Geom Mugeuk gathered the liquor and stood up.

“Then I’ll take this back with me. I won’t need to stop by home.”

“Who are you taking it to now?”

“I’m going to Lord Geomjon.”

“You heard my advice, but you need different advice?”

Geom Mugeuk spoke as he headed outside.

“I plan to ask Lord Geomjon something different.”

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk arrived at Ilhwa Geomjon’s residence, she too showed a new side of herself.

She wore beautiful clothes and splendid makeup.

“It’s been a while since I put on makeup. How do I look?”

“You are too dazzling to look at.”

For some time, she had not paid the slightest attention to her appearance. She wore nothing but martial robes, let alone makeup.

Yet today, she was exceptionally elegant and splendid.

And there was a reason for this attire today.

“Since there’s an event tomorrow as well, I thought I’d dress up after a long time and practiced in advance.”

“What event is there tomorrow?”

“You still don’t know?”

Just then, a subordinate brought her a fishing rod.

“Huh? How did you know, Lord Geomjon?”

“Know what?”

“I mean the fishing competition.”

“The Gyojo sent someone to inform us earlier. He said we’d all go fishing together with the Majons tomorrow. He said there would be prizes for whoever catches the biggest fish.”

Geom Mugeuk blinked. Now he understood. That Mabul had played a prank on him. He had pretended to figure it out when he’d already received word from the Cheonmajeon.

The little giant title is revoked! Give back my praise! And Father, if you set a date, you should inform your son first! I was the one who brought up the fishing story!

How typical of his father.

“What is it?”

“The world is full of swindlers.”

“There’s one in front of you right now.”

Understanding it as a warning to beware of women wearing makeup, Geom Mugeuk laughed along with her.

“By the way, this is liquor that Lord Chwima cherished. He gave me a whole box, and I’ve been giving each of the Majons a bottle as a gift.”

“I know this liquor.”

“How do you know it?”

“He never used to give this even if I begged for a single bottle. And he gave you a whole box.”

And not only that, I took it to Lord Mabul and we drank it together.

Unable to bring himself to tattle like that, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said.

“In the end, I suppose he knew it would come to you like this.”

“Don’t side with that drunkard.”

Ilhwa Geomjon and Chwima were friends, so she could speak so frankly.

“Sogyojo.”

“Yes, Lord Geomjon.”

“I know what you want from the Majons. You want them to support you and dissuade the Gyojo. Am I wrong?”

“You are correct.”

“If I say this, our sparring friendship may sound like empty words, but I cannot help you. I shall follow the Gyojo’s will.”

Ilhwa Geomjon clearly stated her position.

“I understand your heart, Lord Geomjon. I will not resent you for it. Our sparring friendship is not just for show; we will remain good friends in the future as well.”

Ilhwa Geomjon smiled pleasantly. Just as she was feeling grateful for his words, at least.

“In that sense, shall we spar?”

Ilhwa Geomjon’s pupils trembled slightly. She had said clearly that she couldn’t help him, yet the Sogyojo was trying to help her.

“Sparring is always welcome.”

Her skill had been continuously improving.

But there was no one to verify that skill, to confirm how she should proceed. She couldn’t spar with the other Majons, nor could she approach the Gyojo. Now even the enemies with whom she could fight life-and-death battles had disappeared.

Geom Mugeuk was fulfilling that most important role in her life. Even at this very moment when she had uttered the words that she could not help him.

If it were not for the matter with the Gyojo, she would have definitely stepped forward for the Sogyojo.

The two came outside.

Both Geom Mugeuk and Ilhwa Geomjon had reached a different realm than when they had last sparred.

“My heart always flutters when I spar with you, Lord Geomjon.”

“Not as much as mine.”

Thus their sparring began.

Just as their realms had changed, this sparring was different too.

It wasn’t fierce like other days. The sparring was calm and elegant. It was the least intense sparring they had exchanged until now.

Their swords never drew the same trajectory twice. Like a painter endlessly creating new pictures, the swords continuously drew new lines.

The one leading today’s sparring was Geom Mugeuk. Just as his father had guided him, he guided her.

She felt it. The Sogyojo’s sword was constantly asking her.

What is a sword?

How could she, called the Geomjon, not have an answer to that?

There were many answers. That answer had changed throughout her life.

At times the sword had been power, and at times fear. At times it had been a wall, and at times freedom. At times death, and at times life. At times honor, and at times guilt.

The meaning of the sword in her life had changed endlessly.

And now, to the question asked by Geom Mugeuk’s sword, she could not answer.

Being unable to answer meant there was still a road left to travel.

Thus the sparring ended.

It was not a long sparring, but it was one that left the most with Ilhwa Geomjon.

Ilhwa Geomjon recalled the sparring they had just finished. The exchanges that she would have vividly remembered in the past were not coming to mind clearly. How had she survived with such a spirit? Had the Sogyojo gone easy on her?

“Sogyojo, I thought the more I swung my sword, the clearer the world became.”

“Is it not so?”

“But the more I swing, the blurrier the world becomes. Is it I who am aging? Or is it the sword that ages?”

Ilhwa Geomjon looked down at the sword she gripped. Had she not ascended to the next level but regressed? Why did everything feel so vague and hazy?

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze was deep and calm, as if guessing what was in her heart. The confusion of that moment when everything seemed to disintegrate was a path he too had walked.

“I like it when thick fog blankets the mountain. It is mysterious and beautiful. It has a feeling all its own that cannot be expressed in words. In that moment, I think of the fog as part of the mountain too.”

Ilhwa Geomjon interpreted those words thus.

Do not try so hard to find a perfect realm.

What if it is blurry? What if fog settles?

If a part is unseen because of the blur, you will see the whole better.

In youth, those parts are so clear that instead you cannot enjoy the deep haziness of the world like fog.

The moment she arrived at that thought, strength left from the hand that gripped the sword in impatience.

The moment she held the sword gently, Ilhwa Geomjon could vaguely feel it.

That a new sword path had been granted to her sword once more.

Ilhwa Geomjon clasped her hands in a respectful martial salute. Setting aside their relationship as Majon and Sogyojo, it was the respect one martial artist offered to another who walked ahead.

“Thank you truly for showing me that there is an endless new road before me.”

Geom Mugeuk also returned the greeting respectfully.

“I am happy that my path is not lonely.”

The two smiled at each other.

“Now I’ll wear makeup when I want to and not wear it when I don’t.”

Not caring about one’s appearance was, after all, still a matter of caring about it.

Before parting, Geom Mugeuk had something to ask her.

“There is something I would like to ask.”

“What is it?”

“It is about my mother.”

For a moment, Ilhwa Geomjon’s expression stiffened visibly.

“Why do you ask so suddenly?”

“During this closed-door training, I asked Father about my mother.”

“What did he answer?”

Geom Mugeuk conveyed it to her exactly as it was.

“He said she was the one he uniquely loved.”

Ilhwa Geomjon nodded slowly.

“Yes, she is the one the Gyojo uniquely loved.”

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. That she was flustered at the mention of his mother.

After a moment of silence, Ilhwa Geomjon sighed and spoke.

“To be honest, I did not like the Mahu.”

It was a difficult thing to say to Geom Mugeuk, but she did not want to lie.

“In fact, I was closer to hating her. That is…”

Ilhwa Geomjon spoke no further. Various complex emotions could be felt from her.

“I have brought up unnecessary words. I apologize, Lord Geomjon.”

It was clear that Ilhwa Geomjon had not hated her merely out of jealousy for liking his father. Her loyalty toward his father had always been pure and clear. There must certainly be another story behind it.

“Then I will see you at the fishing grounds tomorrow. Please come in an even more beautiful appearance tomorrow and give Elder Doma a surprise!”

Just as Geom Mugeuk bowed and turned to leave, a low voice from Ilhwa Geomjon came from behind.

“She was incomparably more beautiful than I.”

Geom Mugeuk turned back toward Ilhwa Geomjon.

The light in Ilhwa Geomjon’s eyes deepened as she recalled that day long ago.

“I still vividly remember that splendid floral palace gown that the Mahu loved to wear.”

Chapter 852: Fishing Is Just an Excuse for Everyone to Gather
-------------------------------------------------------------
“What are you doing here?”

Even at his older brother’s voice from behind, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze remained fixed on the nightscape of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“I heard you came up here every night.”

The place where Geom Mugeuk sat was the palm of a gigantic fiend statue, one that held aloft the brazier from which the demon fire rose.

“Every night? Hardly. This is only the third time.”

Now that he thought about it, it was already the third time. Whenever the demon fire flared up, Geom Muyang had stood here and gazed earnestly in the direction of Maga Village, where his father and younger brother had been fighting.

He did not know what feelings drove him, but even now, after the battle was over, he kept finding himself drawn here.

“So? You came to see me?”

“Don’t just stand there. Sit. I can see why you’d come up here often. The night view is incredible.”

Geom Muyang slowly walked over and sat beside Geom Mugeuk. The two of them perched on the tips of different fingers of the fiend statue, looking down over the full view of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Geom Mugeuk handed Geom Muyang the wine bottle he had been holding.

“Here. A gift.”

The wine given by the Drunken Demon was being handed out, one bottle to each person.

“It’s precious wine, so drink it on a special day.”

No sooner had he said it than Geom Muyang poured the wine and drank.

“You’re not ignoring what I said. This moment with me is special, right?”

Only one corner of Geom Muyang’s mouth lifted; he gave no answer.

The brothers sat like that in silence for a long while. The one who broke the long silence was Geom Mugeuk.

“What if I stop Father’s unification of the murim? Whose side will you take?”

“Of course…”

He had expected him to say Father’s.

“I’d have to take your side.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his brother in surprise.

“You’d take my side?”

“If I get on the wrong side of the next cult leader, won’t I suffer for the rest of my life? I have far more days to live with you.”

He was not the same brother as before. He even knew how to joke like this.

“I wonder if you could really defy Father in front of him.”

Geom Muyang looked at Geom Mugeuk and said something unexpected.

“I could. If anyone couldn’t, it would be you.”

As though he meant it sincerely, Geom Muyang’s expression was serious.

“You think I can’t defy Father?”

Geom Muyang nodded.

“You’ve become too close to Father.”

Geom Mugeuk could not refute his brother’s words.

He let out a light sigh and held out his hand, and the wine bottle in Geom Muyang’s hand flew through the air into his own through Void Grasping.

As if his insides were burning, Geom Mugeuk drank deeply.

“Don’t you have anything to tell me, Brother?”

“No.”

“Tell me one secret method for your younger brother’s sake. Is there some weakness of Father’s only you know?”

“There are quite a few weaknesses of yours I could tell Father.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk laughed. Even if his brother had a way, there was no chance he would tell him something that went against their father.

“This is enough. The fact that you’re listening to me like this is already enough to turn the world upside down.”

When Geom Muyang held out his hand, the wine bottle flew back to him. This time, Geom Muyang drank.

“If it were me, I wouldn’t stop Father. If Father unifies the murim, we’ll gain the world.”

“True. Maybe I should pretend I can’t win and just not stop him. Should I inherit the world later?”

Perhaps because he believed Geom Mugeuk would never do that, Geom Muyang merely stared silently at the night view.

Not all of the nightscape seen from here was beautiful.

Far in the distance, the place where the lights of the original Maga Village should have been was dark as pitch.

Then Geom Muyang suddenly asked.

“I heard you asked about Mother?”

“Who told you? I asked so many people.”

“Father told me.”

“So Father is the only one who looks after our eldest son, after all.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell that his brother’s expression had stiffened slightly.

“Why does everyone get like that whenever Mother comes up?”

“Who else did you ask?”

“Master, and the Sword Venerable too. The Sword Venerable remembered Mother’s flower-patterned palace dress.”

Of course, Ilhwa Sword Venerable likely remembered far more than that, but she had said no more about his mother. After parting from her, he had come to meet his brother.

“If you were going to ask about Mother, why didn’t you come to me?”

Geom Mugeuk looked toward the distant Heavenly Demon Hall and said,

“Because I thought the mood would get serious if I talked to you. Look, judging by your face right now, it’s an emergency where we need to light the demon fire again.”

Even at Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Geom Muyang did not laugh.

“When we were little, you told me I must never talk about Mother. To me, that came down like a prohibition. I grew up truly thinking I wasn’t supposed to.”

Perhaps Geom Muyang remembered that incident; he said nothing and only looked out at the view.

“That doesn’t mean I intend to resent you.”

After all, not only he but his brother as well had been very young then.

“What good would it do to talk about someone already gone? It would only have hurt.”

Mother, your second son speaks so coldly, doesn’t he?

Even these cold words came far too late. As you saw, Mother, my life before regression was too difficult, and my life after regression has been consumed entirely with saving everyone. I am sorry, Mother.

“I have never spoken of Mother since that day either.”

It was the first time his brother had brought up their mother.

“I don’t remember exactly how old I was, but I once asked Father about Mother. That was when I saw it. Father’s face stiffened all at once and changed. Even as a child, I could tell. That was pain.”

Perhaps his brother, too, had thought from that moment that he must not bring up their mother. And he had told his younger brother that. Perhaps back then, he had still had the heart to care for his younger brother.

Geom Mugeuk tried to recall again in that moment, but no memories of his mother came to him.

“To be honest, I don’t remember Mother. She died when I was three, right? At three, you’d think I’d remember at least faintly, so how can I not remember a single thing? Did she never even hold me?”

At that moment, a sentence slipped abruptly from Geom Muyang.

“She liked you more.”

A single scene rose in Geom Muyang’s mind. Their mother holding his young brother. He himself had been beside her. Their mother had smiled brightly and said to him, Yang-ah, from now on, you must protect your brother well.

“You remember everything, don’t you, Brother?”

Geom Muyang said nothing.

“For me, since I can’t think of anything, it’s only a vague longing. But it’s not like that for you, is it?”

Geom Mugeuk thought his brother must miss their mother more than he did. If there was sorrow or a wound there, it would be far deeper.

“Are you all right, Brother?”

Given his brother’s personality, the part he had never spoken of to anyone was likely the matter of their mother. Surely he must have wanted, at least once, to pour out the story to someone.

A small sigh escaped Geom Muyang’s lips.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned once more toward the distant Heavenly Demon Hall.

“If you ever want to talk about Mother, come find me. I want to hear it.”

Then Geom Muyang said something he had not expected.

“When I became cult leader, I intended to investigate Mother’s death.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Geom Muyang in surprise. His brother was serious.

“I couldn’t believe she died of illness.”

His brother could not believe that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had failed to save their mother from sickness.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. His brother missed their mother so much that he needed to place responsibility on someone just to endure it.

For him to say this must also mean that he wanted Mugeuk, at least, to uncover the truth.

“Yes, there may have been another story behind Mother’s death. But I won’t concern myself with it.”

Geom Muyang looked at him with an angry face, but Geom Mugeuk had a firm reason for answering that way.

“Father surely did everything he could to save Mother. If he still couldn’t save her, then it must have been something that couldn’t be helped. Even if there was a conspiracy behind Mother’s death as you think, Father would already have completed his revenge. She was the only person Father ever loved.”

Another thin sigh escaped his brother’s mouth. His brother was desperately holding back the surge of emotion rising in his chest. Geom Mugeuk could feel that longing for their mother filled his brother’s heart.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the night sky. Searching for his mother’s star, wherever it might be.

Mother, I’m all right, so please look after Brother first.

* * *

It was well past midnight when Geom Mugeuk came to find the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable.

She was inwardly surprised by the late visit.

“Welcome, Young Cult Leader.”

“Soul-Seizing, I apologize for coming so late.”

“Not at all. I was awake.”

“Let’s get some air together.”

Geom Mugeuk walked inside Seohwanjin with the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable. The ghost arts practitioners standing guard bowed respectfully to the two of them.

“Are your injuries all right?”

“Yes, they have already healed completely.”

Geom Mugeuk raised the wine bottle in his hand and said,

“Then you should be able to drink this. It is wine I received from the Drunken Demon. I brought it because I wanted to drink it with you, Soul-Seizing.”

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

As they walked, Geom Mugeuk looked around and asked,

“Does it seem different from when I came before?”

The old Seohwanjin had been filled everywhere with traps and ghostly energy, and ghost fires capable of plunging people into heart demons had bloomed throughout.

But the surroundings he saw while strolling with her were an ordinary, beautiful garden.

“We pulled up the existing ghost flowers and redecorated it.”

It was not merely for the sake of making it look nice.

“Ghost arts practitioners handle souls and meet ghosts every day, so I thought at least the place where they live ought to be ordinary and beautiful.”

This was why young blood was needed. The old Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable would never have thought this way.

“Are you preparing for tomorrow’s fishing competition?”

“I have never fished in my life. I’m simply hoping I’ll be lucky enough to catch at least one.”

“Come out early tomorrow. I will teach you at least the basics.”

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk forced himself to hold back the urge to boast that his was a skill that had even defeated his father.

At the end of the beautiful path lined with Soul Lamps, there stood a large tree.

“It is a tree called the Soul-Spirit Tree. It is said that when one is here, the ghostly energy of ghost arts practitioners stabilizes, and their minds become clear.”

Geom Mugeuk looked up at the Soul-Spirit Tree. Simply seeing it gave him a mysterious feeling.

“I had no idea there was such a tree here.”

“They say ghost arts practitioners do not lie beneath this Soul-Spirit Tree.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk looked at the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable with an expression that said this was his chance and asked,

“Soul-Seizing.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

“Whose side are you on?”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable smiled as well. They had spoken about this issue once before. Now it was time to put an end to it beneath the Soul-Spirit Tree.

“I am on your side, Young Cult Leader.”

She meant it sincerely.

That was why, in the fight against the Dark Palace Lord, she had been glad, thinking they had won as the young cult leader’s first Demon Venerable.

“Then now the ones firmly on my side are Soma and you, Soul-Seizing.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was even happier because the Extreme Evil Soma was on the young cult leader’s side.

Among the Eight Demon Venerables, she liked the Extreme Evil Soma the most. She liked the unique yet lonely feeling he gave off.

“At tomorrow’s fishing competition, I will announce it in front of Father as well. From now on, Father has only six Demon Venerables!”

It was a situation that would be awkward just to imagine.

“Yes, you may do so.”

“Before, when Father persuaded you, I sent you across to the other side. But now I will not send you away.”

“Now, even if you push me from behind, I will not go.”

The two looked at each other and laughed.

Only then did the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable truly feel that she had become Geom Mugeuk’s person. Someone needed her, and that someone was Geom Mugeuk. How could she not be pleased?

“To commemorate our moment, let us have a drink.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk drank first, then handed it to the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable. The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable also drank the wine heartily.

“Over the past few days, I have met with the Demon Venerables and spoken with them about Father. I asked for advice on stopping Father’s unification of the murim.”

She wondered if he could possibly be about to ask her.

“Soul-Seizing, what must I do to stop Father?”

For a moment, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was flustered.

“I still lack experience, so I likely cannot say anything that would be of help.”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“Even so, please tell me. If you, my first Demon Venerable, do not, then who will? I absolutely need your help, Soul-Seizing.”

It was a burdensome thing to hear, but it was far more gratifying than not being asked at all because she was young.

The Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable seemed to ponder for a moment as she looked up at the Soul Tree.

“The only thing I know is the Soul-Subduing Art.”

She spoke of what she had felt while learning the Soul-Subduing Art.

“When one first learns the Soul-Subduing Art, one begins by learning how to suppress a soul. In that process, the soul may scatter, or conversely, the practitioner may be overcome by the soul and go mad.”

Geom Mugeuk listened to her with a calm gaze.

“Once one becomes a master of the Soul-Subduing Art, one comes to understand what the soul desires. You gently soothe the soul and guide it down the path you want. Since every soul has its own story and nature, one’s skill in the Soul-Subduing Art is determined by how well one can soothe them.”

He had known that she was the type to curse well and provoke well, but she could also speak this calmly and eloquently.

“When one reaches the final realm of the Soul-Subduing Art, words are no longer needed, nor is soothing. If you stand before the soul with a strong will, the soul will naturally be drawn to that strong heart. Just as swordsmanship reaches Sword-Body Unity (劍身合一), the will of the one performing the Soul-Subduing Art and the soul that must be controlled become one.”

The Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“Do not try to persuade the Sect Leader. Show him your will, Young Sect Leader.”

Her words struck deep into Geom Mugeuk’s heart. Yes, this was not a battle of persuasion. It was a battle between the world his father wished to show and the world he himself wished to live in—a battle between world and world.

Seeing Geom Mugeuk lost in thought, the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable grew needlessly embarrassed.

“I spoke out of turn when I know nothing.”

“Do not say that. Your words were of great help.”

“Thank you for saying so.”

As the night had grown late, Geom Mugeuk bade her farewell.

“Soul-Subduer, I’ll be expecting a big catch tomorrow!”

“To be honest, knowing that the Sect Leader will be joining us makes me nervous.”

Geom Mugeuk said with a smile,

“Do not worry. Fishing is just an excuse for everyone to gather. As for Father, he simply wants to spend a pleasant time with the Demon Venerables.”

* * *

At the same time, the lights were lit in Geom Ujin’s residence.

A suffocating tension hung over the place.

Geom Ujin was awake.

As though tending to the Heavenly Demon Sword before going out to the battlefield, Geom Ujin handled the thing in his hands with utmost care. His gaze upon it was colder and sharper than anything, like a martial artist dreaming of revenge.

Hwi, watching while concealed, knew that the Sect Leader had not been this tense even when he went out to face the Heavenly Killing Star.

Sssk, ssssk.

Long past the hour when he should have been asleep, Geom Ujin was carefully tending to a fishing rod.

Chapter 853: The Heavenly Demon Cup Fishing Tournament Begins
-------------------------------------------------------------
The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable arrived at the appointed place.

Because she was to meet Geom Mugeuk, she had arrived earlier than the agreed time.

On the riverside, the guard warriors of the Heavenly Demon Hall had come out and were setting up tents for the Cult Leader and the Demon Venerables, while also searching the surroundings for any hidden ambushers.

Since it was an event attended by the Cult Leader and the Demon Venerables, they were taking every precaution. Of course, it was also an event that needed no security whatsoever more than any other.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable spotted a Demon Venerable standing by the river, fishing.

Since it was still early, she had naturally thought none of the Demon Venerables would be there yet, but an unexpected person had already arrived.

“Poison King, you’ve come?”

When the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable greeted him respectfully first, the Poison King also lowered his head and accepted her greeting.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable found it unexpected inwardly. Anyone else, perhaps, but the Poison King had come first to practice fishing? He, who seemed the farthest removed from such a sight?

‘Did the Poison King have this side to him?’

As she was thinking that, Geom Mugeuk also arrived.

After exchanging a light greeting with the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, he approached the Poison King first.

“Have you practiced much in the meantime?”

Hearing what he said to the Poison King, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable understood.

‘Ah, it’s because of the Young Cult Leader.’

Then it made sense. Just as he had made her come out here this early, if not the Young Cult Leader, who in the world could make the Poison King come out early and practice alone? Something must have happened between the two of them that she did not know about.

“How is it? Is my posture all right?”

The Poison King showed Geom Mugeuk the motion of casting the fishing line and reeling it back in.

“Who taught you?”

“The Chief Steward.”

“As expected! Your posture is excellent. But later, when you choose a spot, not here.”

Geom Mugeuk looked around.

“That place over there looks good. Father and Elder Blade Demon will certainly try to take that spot too, so even if you have to poison them, make sure you claim it.”

At that frightening joke, the Poison King nodded with a solemn expression.

Geom Mugeuk spoke quietly so that only the Poison King could hear.

“There is only one antidote for building a good relationship with Father.”

“Winning.”

“No. Winning isn’t an antidote. It may actually become an even stronger poison.”

When the Poison King looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression that asked what it was, then—

“It is the fact itself that you learned fishing in order to grow closer to Father. You must show him that you have learned the eye to recognize a spot like that.”

He had detoxified countless poisons throughout his life, but never before had he worked so hard to find an antidote for human relationships… Ah, no, this was not the first time.

When he had barked like a dog with Geom Mugeuk, and when he had left the Thousand Poisons Forest to go out into the Central Plains, all of it had been efforts to obtain an antidote for relationships. Because of those efforts, this one did not feel unfamiliar.

“Then keep practicing.”

Just as Geom Mugeuk was about to walk over to the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, the Poison King suddenly spoke.

“Did you find your antidote?”

Geom Mugeuk looked back at the Poison King. The antidote the Poison King had told him of was this:

Have you ever asked the Cult Leader about the unified murim he dreams of?

A question that might be the most important of all, even before everything else.

“I intend to ask that question today.”

The Poison King’s gaze deepened as he turned back toward the river.

“No one knows what anyone will catch today.”

Geom Mugeuk watched the river, where the morning sunlight shattered, together with the Poison King.

“I’m more nervous than when I fought the Heavenly Slaughter Star. Poison King, please be on my side.”

The Poison King cast his fishing line into the river again and said,

“Good attempt.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and went over to the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

“As for you, Soul-Capturing, just go wherever you feel drawn and cast your line. Enjoy the pleasure of your first time fishing.”

“Yes, I understand.”

Though he said that, Geom Mugeuk properly taught her how to fish. From how to bait the hook, to how and where to cast, and at what moment to jerk the rod.

While he was teaching her, the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon arrived. He stood at a distance and watched Geom Mugeuk teaching the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable how to fish.

Then a scene from long ago rose in his mind, as though superimposed over the two of them.

His own image from his youth, teaching the Cult Leader how to fish. Back then, both the Cult Leader and he had been that young.

Seeing that the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon had arrived, Geom Mugeuk greeted him warmly.

“Welcome, Elder!”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon spoke with a confident expression.

“Fishing, by its nature, is not something one masters after a day or two of practice.”

He had not brought only one fishing rod.

Shhhhhk.

As if weapons were being unfurled, more than ten fishing rods spread out wide in the air.

Looking up at the fishing rods floating in midair, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“I’m sure every one of these rods has a story behind it.”

As he said that, he subtly brought up words that would boost the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon’s spirits. The incident that must have been the most meaningful in the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon’s fishing life.

“Which rod was it? The one you used when you first taught my father how to fish?”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon pointed to the first of the rods floating there.

“This very rod.”

It was a fishing rod he would not sell even for a thousand gold.

“Please say a word for our Poison King and Lady Soul-Capturing, these two novice anglers.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon stroked his chin, wondering what to say, then spoke.

“Even in the murim, aren’t there enemies who are itching to draw their blades? It is the same. You must not be itching to catch something. Fish can sense the tremor of a fishing line like ghosts.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon approached Geom Mugeuk and gestured with his hand as though framing his face.

“And the bait should be this sort of bait.”

“What kind of bait is bait like me?”

But the Poison King and the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable nodded as though they understood without needing it explained.

The Young Cult Leader knew better than anyone what the other party liked. That was why, even knowing his bait was bait, one could not help but bite. In other words, it meant that it was important to use bait the fish liked.

“The fish in this river today may be fellows with no thoughts at all.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, someone behind him spoke.

“As if anyone came today with fewer thoughts than I did.”

The next Demon Venerable to arrive was the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. She wore even more beautiful clothes and makeup than yesterday. At that beautiful sight, the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon opened his eyes wide.

“Today’s first place will be the Sword Venerable.”

“Why is that?”

“Because the fish will swarm in to see your beauty, Sword Venerable.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled brightly.

“As expected! If only for our Young Cult Leader, it was worth getting up early and putting on makeup.”

She, too, had things that came to mind when it came to fishing. Looking at the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon, she said,

“Seeing you holding a fishing rod after so long brings back old memories.”

She had no interest in fishing at all, but there had been a time when the Cult Leader and the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon had gone around fishing for a while.

“Was it that fishing is waiting?”

“That’s right.”

“For someone so quick-tempered, I really don’t understand how you can like fishing so much.”

At her question, the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon answered,

“The feeling of catching a fish itself is good, but sometimes I get that feeling. The world is something that does not go as I will, but at some moment, it feels as though not a fish, but the world itself is being drawn toward me. I fish because I cannot forget that moment.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable shook her head as though she could not understand at all. She had yet to feel that sensation of pulling in either fish or the world.

At that moment, the Fist Demon arrived. The fishing rod in his huge hand looked as though it might snap at any moment.

“Master, do you like fishing?”

“When I was young, I followed the Cult Leader and tried it a couple of times.”

Imagining the Fist Demon fishing together with his father, it, too, felt like a scene from a painting.

“I have neither talent nor interest in fishing.”

Standing still and waiting until a fish bit the bait did not suit his temperament.

Nor did the sensation of catching a fish move him much. Perhaps it was because he had learned martial arts that mainly used the hands.

“Ah, if there are many beginners, it actually becomes harder.”

At that moment, someone arrived and received Geom Mugeuk’s worry.

“Set your concerns aside. As long as I am here, there will be no room for beginner’s luck to intervene.”

The one who arrived confidently, with a single fishing rod slung over his shoulder, was the Demon Buddha. Considering the Demon Buddha’s talent for gathering medicinal herbs, fishing could not be taken lightly either.

After exchanging greetings with everyone, the Demon Buddha came to Geom Mugeuk and asked quietly,

“You said the Poison King was a fraud? What exactly has changed about him?”

The Poison King was crouching by the riverside, searching for poisonous insects. Once the Demon Venerables had gathered, he had somehow begun showing his usual self again.

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“I’m telling you, that appearance right now is acting. He’s a great actor!”

The Drunken Demon arrived next. He, too, was on the confident side.

“Have you forgotten that Chwimong Pavilion floats upon a lake? Do you think all I did there was drink?”

The last Demon Venerable to arrive was the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon. To him, who had not the slightest interest in fishing, Geom Mugeuk gave lavish praise.

“The sight of you holding a fishing rod suits you so well, Smiling Demon.”

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon muttered a word in jealousy.

“What wouldn’t look impressive on him? Even if that man merely stretched out a finger, you would shout that he looked impressive, wouldn’t you?”

“People who look impressive just by stretching out a finger are hardly common in this world, are they? Such people should be told they look impressive.”

The two eyes behind the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon’s mask smiled brightly.

Since it was a competition hosted by the Cult Leader, no one arrived later than the appointed time. Because it was a competition after a great battle had ended, the atmosphere was softer and better than ever.

But when the eight Demon Venerables gathered in one place, their irrepressible presence made the riverside feel as though it would burst.

At that moment, one of the guard warriors informed everyone.

“The Cult Leader is coming.”

The Demon Venerables solemnly welcomed Geom Ujin. The relaxed atmosphere became taut in an instant.

Geom Ujin slowly walked into that place.

The one who accompanied and attended him, holding his fishing rod, was Geom Muyang.

“Father! Your second son is your son too! You didn’t tell me to come with you!”

The Demon Venerables could realize anew just how extraordinary what Geom Mugeuk had accomplished was.

Originally, this was an impossible sight. At an event like this, if the Cult Leader brought anyone along, it would be the Young Cult Leader. How could he possibly bring the Eldest Young Master, who had been pushed out in the power struggle?

The Young Cult Leader had arrived first and was chatting with the Demon Venerables, while the Eldest Young Master, who had lost out in the power struggle, was entering proudly while attending the Cult Leader.

“We greet the Cult Leader!”

The Demon Venerables greeted him in thunderous voices.

When his father arrived, Geom Mugeuk stepped forward.

“Today, I am also a participant in the competition, but at the same time, I have decided to serve as the host.”

How could there be any objection? He was the only person who could ease an atmosphere that might otherwise stiffen. Even if he said he would not do it, they would have to force him forward.

“Now then, before the fishing competition begins in earnest, the Cult Leader, who has hosted today’s event, will say a few words. Father, please speak.”

Geom Ujin met the eyes of the Demon Venerables one by one. From the ever-reliable Fist Demon to the Poison King, who was always cowed before him.

“You all worked hard fighting those bastards. Think of today as a day to rest at ease, and enjoy yourselves.”

After saying that brief word and stepping back, Geom Ujin suddenly said to Geom Mugeuk,

“You say something too.”

Everyone could tell. This was the moment when the Cult Leader officially acknowledged that the Young Cult Leader’s contribution had been great in bringing this battle safely to an end.

With a face feigning deep emotion, Geom Mugeuk spoke to everyone.

“Standing here in this moving moment, I suddenly recall the first time I saw all of you. First, our Elder Blood-Heaven Blade Demon. When we first met, your temper was so prickly and…”

As the speech threatened to grow long, the Demon Venerables began pretending not to listen. The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked at each other and conversed, while the Demon Buddha held up his fishing rod and swung it here and there. The Poison King searched the ground for bugs again, and the Drunken Demon discreetly drank the liquor at his waist.

“It was a joke, a joke! You’re all too much! How is it that our cult hasn’t changed at all!”

The gazes of the Demon Venerables, who had played along with the joke, focused on Geom Mugeuk again.

“Thank you. Thanks to all you Demon Venerables, we were able to eliminate all our enemies.”

After first expressing his gratitude, Geom Mugeuk added what he truly wanted to say.

“But I do not think there is any need to place great meaning on that. It was an obvious result, wasn’t it? We are, after all, the people in this murim who are best at killing enemies, aren’t we?”

As all the Demon Venerables smiled, Geom Mugeuk said to everyone what he had long wanted to say.

“An absolute evil that no one else can kill is one we must kill.”

Geom Ujin remembered. Those words his son had spoken to him long ago were the demonic path his son pursued. And his son was still upholding that path.

“I’ve heard that when external enemies disappear, internal crises are bound to come. I’m a man with many worries, so I intend to keep living without letting my guard down. For me, a new battle has already begun.”

How could the Demon Venerables not know what kind of battle that was?

It was the battle between the Demonic Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader, a battle on which the fate of the martial world depended.

That battle was also their battle. After all, it was a fight in which they would have to choose one of the two.

Before the atmosphere could grow heavy, Geom Mugeuk changed the subject.

“Now then, allow me to explain today’s fishing tournament.”

As Geom Mugeuk walked over to the table where the prizes had been placed, he announced the rules of the tournament.

“It goes without saying, but no martial arts may be used in today’s tournament. Revealing your aura is also forbidden. You must compete solely with pure fishing skill. The time limit is from now until the sun sets over that river.”

He had deliberately set the time limit long. So that they could all eat together and catch up on the stories they had missed.

“Now, I will announce the most important part of today’s event. The prize awarded to the winner of this tournament is!”

Geom Mugeuk checked the contents of the prize box.

“Oh! Our father has truly opened his heart today.”

Geom Mugeuk lifted the box and showed everyone what was inside.

“The person who catches the biggest fish today will receive a Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng. This is the first time in the history of the martial world that a Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng has been offered as a prize in a fishing tournament! Now, applause for our Cult Leader!”

The Demon Venerables laughed and applauded. Until now, whenever all the Demon Venerables had gathered, it had always been for something important. This was the first time the atmosphere had ever been this pleasant.

“There is another prize for the winner. Even if it isn’t the biggest fish, the person who catches the most fish will also receive a Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng!”

At that, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable asked a question.

“What if someone catches both the biggest fish and the most fish?”

“Naturally, they will receive both roots of Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng. In addition, anyone who catches even a single fish will receive one root of Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng as a memento.”

As he said that, Geom Mugeuk glanced sidelong at his father.

“Seeing how lavish the prizes are, I suspect our father intends to sweep them all back into his own hands.”

At those words, one corner of Geom Ujin’s mouth rose, and the smiles of the watching Demon Venerables deepened even further.

Geom Mugeuk raised his fishing rod high and shouted.

“Now, let the First Heavenly Demon Cup Fishing Tournament begin!”

Chapter 854: The Fate of This Murim Lies with You
-------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk restricted the area before the contest began in earnest.

“Today’s fishing must not go past that jutting rock over there, or that tree on the other side.”

He limited them to places within sight of everyone, and close enough that they could be heard if they spoke with internal energy behind their voices.

Since this was where the river bent, they would not be lined up in a row, but positioned where they could see one another’s faces.

After all, today’s gathering had been arranged not so much for fishing as for conversations they usually never got to have.

“Now, we’ll decide on our spots. Once you’ve picked your first spot, if you don’t like it, you may move later or stay where you are.”

In truth, securing a good spot was more important than the fishing itself. Especially since the area was restricted, choosing well at the start mattered more than anything.

“Father, you should choose your spot first.”

Geom Mugeuk offered him the chance, but Geom Ujin declined.

“I’ll choose later.”

Knowing his father’s personality, he would not want to hear that he had won because he had taken the best spot.

As if he had been waiting for this, Geom Mugeuk made a suggestion.

“Then how about this? To be fair, let those without fishing experience choose their spots first.”

His father nodded and accepted the suggestion. Today’s contest was both a fishing contest and a contest of pride.

Once his father had made the decision, priority in choosing spots was given to the beginners.

“First, those who were boasting earlier will step aside.”

The trio who had boasted were the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, the Demon Buddha, and the Drunken Demon. They were eliminated from priority seating.

The three of them knew very well how important it was to secure a good fishing spot, so though they did not show it, they were inwardly disappointed.

“Now, among those remaining, who arrived here first today?”

As everyone looked around, the Poison King quietly raised his hand.

“Then the Poison King will choose his spot first.”

He had said he had to defend that spot even if it meant scattering poison, and Geom Mugeuk had created an opportunity for him to choose it first.

“Then I will not stand on ceremony.”

After bowing to Geom Ujin, the Poison King was about to head for the spot Geom Mugeuk had told him to take.

Just then, someone’s voice flowed out.

“How strange.”

The Poison King flinched and stopped in his tracks.

The one who had spoken was, unexpectedly, the Demon Buddha.

“The Poison King has absolutely no interest in fishing, and yet you arrived here first today?”

Among the Demon Venerables, the one closest to the Poison King was the Demon Buddha, and yet he had stepped forward to put on the brakes.

“Why did you come out first?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Demon Buddha.

Was he trying to steal the Poison King’s spot in order to win the fishing tournament?

The Demon Buddha of the past would have done that and then some. He would have raised a conspiracy theory, claiming the Young Cult Leader and the Poison King had colluded to secure a good spot in advance. Then, under the pretext of having exposed the truth, he would have taken the spot for himself.

But there was no way the present Demon Buddha would do that. Because Geom Mugeuk trusted him, he could make such a joke.

“At last, the small giant’s revenge begins for being used as an herb gatherer.”

With those words alone, everyone could tell that the Demon Buddha had gathered poisonous herbs for the Poison King.

With everyone’s eyes focused on him, the Poison King calmly replied.

“Is this not the first tournament the Cult Leader has ever held? That is why I came early to practice. The Young Cult Leader taught me how to choose a spot.”

“I see.”

The Demon Buddha readily believed him.

Just from that reaction, it was clear. The Demon Buddha had led the Poison King into saying those words himself, so that his father could hear the reason.

It was just as Geom Mugeuk had expected. Upon hearing that the Poison King had arrived first, the Demon Buddha had guessed that he was making an effort to improve his relationship with the Cult Leader.

Since the Young Cult Leader had gone around meeting all the Demon Venerables, Geom Mugeuk must have presented him with a way to mend that relationship.

So he had stepped forward to help the Poison King.

The most important thing was Geom Ujin’s reaction.

He looked at the Poison King with calm eyes, then spoke in a gentle tone.

“Fishing will become an enjoyable pastime for you.”

The Poison King respectfully cupped his fist and bowed, then went to stand in the spot he had been heading toward. And so the Poison King took the best spot.

Geom Mugeuk whispered into the Demon Buddha’s ear.

“I told you, you’re being fooled! Our Demon Buddha is being fooled to the very end.”

The Demon Buddha smiled when Geom Mugeuk understood his intention. Yes, because the Young Cult Leader understood without misunderstanding, he had been able to step forward like that just now.

Once the Poison King had taken his spot, Geom Mugeuk called out.

“Who came next?”

Then the Soul-Subjugating Demon Venerable stepped forward.

It was surprising enough that the Poison King had been first, but the second to arrive was the Soul-Subjugating Demon Venerable? Everyone looked at her in disbelief.

The Poison King and the Soul-Subjugating Demon Venerable.

They were the two people martial artists feared most in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. And yet those two had arrived first at a fishing tournament. Even though they were fishing beginners.

The Soul-Subjugating Demon Venerable chose a spot as she pleased, and relief bloomed on the faces of the boastful trio. From an expert’s eye, she had taken a place where it would be difficult to catch even a single fish.

Geom Mugeuk called to her.

“Soul-Subjugating Demon Venerable!”

The moment she saw the mischief flash across Geom Mugeuk’s face, the Soul-Subjugating Demon Venerable tensed inwardly.

So it has come.

Now, in front of the Cult Leader, he would surely say, Our cult has not Eight Demon Venerables, but Six Demon Venerables.

She had already resolved herself to become the Young Cult Leader’s Demon Venerable, but even so, when she thought of having to acknowledge those words in front of the Cult Leader, her heart began to pound.

But the words Geom Mugeuk said were not those.

“Use your Soul-Subjugating Art to send all the fish to me! Absolutely do not send them to Father!”

Of course, since they were not allowed to use martial arts, it was a joke on Geom Mugeuk’s part.

Inwardly relieved, the Soul-Subjugating Demon Venerable smiled and said to him,

“For some reason, it sounds as though you are telling me to send them to the Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“I may be a naturally born filial son whose name will go down in the history of the Divine Cult, but not today! Today is not a fishing tournament. It is a fishing war!”

Indeed, Geom Ujin also did not hide the competitive fire in his eyes and expression.

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father with eyes that said he would never lose. The sight of the two of them stirred everyone’s fighting spirit.

“Now, the next to arrive was Elder Blade Demon, yes? Since you are a master among masters, I’m sure you’ll yield.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon smacked his lips regretfully. He had marked out several spots, and if all of them were taken, he would have no chance of winning.

The next person to choose was the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. Since she had no way of knowing which spot was good, she moved toward a place shaded by trees. She had dressed herself beautifully today and did not want to stand in the sun all day.

As she was about to settle there, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon quietly jerked his chin, telling her to go farther. A little farther back. Good grief, at least you should take a good spot!

He did not send a sound transmission because he thought that would truly count as cheating.

There was no way Geom Mugeuk would miss it.

“Our elder is beside himself with worry that the Sword Venerable will return with an empty basket.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled as she looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Please catch plenty for my share as well.”

Just this alone showed how much she had changed.

The Fist Demon simply strode forward and took an empty spot. Judging by appearance alone, he looked as though he would snap the fishing rod out of impatience and leap into the river at any moment to catch fish with his bare hands.

The Extreme Evil Lesser Demon took the farthest spot. Just by watching how they chose their places, one could sense the personalities of the Demon Venerables.

“Brother? Aren’t you participating in the tournament?”

“Today, I’ll watch Father fish and learn.”

Geom Mugeuk thought it was a wise choice. On a day like this, rather than competing, staying beside their father and attending to him would be better for their relationship in many ways.

Now the ones left were the self-proclaimed fishing masters: Geom Ujin, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, the Demon Buddha, the Drunken Demon, and Geom Mugeuk.

“Then, as the youngest, I will choose my spot first.”

When Geom Mugeuk tried to casually take a spot ahead of them, the Demon Buddha rolled his prayer beads and asked,

“Does our Young Cult Leader know what fishing and life have in common?”

“What is it?”

“There are days when you return from fishing with an empty basket and still feel good, and days when you return with a basket packed full of fish and still feel hollow. Is life not the same? There is no need to fill the basket so completely.”

“No, why are you saying such things during a fishing tournament of all times!”

Pretending not to hear, the Demon Buddha put on the air of an enlightened high monk.

“For you, would it not be more important how many people you spoke with than how many fish you caught?”

“So why are you doing this to me? Fish are more important to me! Do you think the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng is a joke?”

Everyone watching smiled. The mood was wonderful from the very start. As expected, with Geom Mugeuk leading the atmosphere, there was no chance for it to become dull or stiff.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Demon Buddha and smiled.

Since he had made everyone laugh like this, he deserved a reward. Geom Mugeuk said to his father,

“Since the Blood Buddha has come to the fishing grounds and become a benevolent Buddha, we should yield a spot to him.”

Geom Ujin nodded readily.

“Ha ha. When one lets go of greed, unexpected blessings come like this.”

“That’s not true! You haven’t let go of your greed!”

Leaving Geom Mugeuk’s shout behind, the Demon Buddha chose his spot first. Seeing him take a properly good spot, it was clear he had not been bluffing about his fishing skills.

“Now then, next is the young me…”

Just as Geom Mugeuk tried to step forward again, Geom Ujin said to the Drunken Demon,

“You choose next.”

“Thank you for yielding to me.”

The Drunken Demon was greatly pleased that the Cult Leader had looked after him. On top of that, a decent spot was still left.

‘This isn’t bad at all!’

Overall, he was choosing late, but he had secured a better spot than the powerful competitors: the Cult Leader, the Young Cult Leader, and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Geom Mugeuk had naturally thought his father would next tell the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to choose.

“Next is my turn.”

Seeing his father step forward as though he had yielded enough, Geom Mugeuk asked out of curiosity,

“I understand not yielding to me, but why aren’t you yielding to Elder Blade Demon?”

Then words he had never expected flowed from Geom Ujin’s mouth.

“I must protect the pride of the teacher who taught me fishing.”

At those words, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s face brightened radiantly. Just having him mention it in front of everyone like this was enough.

“No, Elder. Father stole your spot, so what are you so happy about that you’re smiling so guilelessly? Father is most wary of you. This fishing ground is crawling with conspiracies!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon smiled and said,

“From what I see, the one he’s most wary of is you.”

“Pardon?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon strode over and took the last passable spot, leaving Geom Mugeuk as the only one remaining.

“Ah! I was so busy watching the fight over spots that I forgot to look after my own!”

Everyone smiled at Geom Mugeuk’s joking complaint.

It truly would have been unimaginable in the old Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. There would not have been a tournament like this in the first place, but even if there had been, everyone would have been suppressed by the Cult Leader’s atmosphere, while the Demon Venerables engaged in contests of spirit among themselves. It would have been a brutal battlefield.

The place Geom Mugeuk took was between his father and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Because he had squeezed in between them, the three of them ended up standing unusually close together, but Geom Ujin and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not mind at all.

Rather, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was glad and pleased that Geom Mugeuk had come beside him.

Once everyone had taken their places, all eyes turned to Geom Ujin. After baiting his hook, Geom Ujin cast his fishing line powerfully toward the river.

Swaaaaish.

The fishing line flew over the glittering silver surface of the river.

Plop.

With that as the start, everyone cast their lines. Fishing lines sliced through the air all at once.

The Poison King, thanks to all his practice, made a plop that was unlike a beginner’s. The Fist Demon, who looked as though he would cast so far and so powerfully that the rod might snap, instead cast gently to a nearby spot. Conversely, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who seemed likely to cast with the most elegance, cast the farthest with the most excitement. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon moved with the greatest skill, the Demon Buddha cast his rod sideways in a peculiar manner, the Drunken Demon put on as much flair as he could with one hand, the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon moved with utmost simplicity, and the Soul-Subjugating Demon Venerable made a plop just as she had been taught.

Lastly, Geom Mugeuk cast his line. It was an ordinary cast, but the experience of having fished countless times was evident in his touch.

Thus the fishing began in earnest, and everyone gazed at the river, each lost in thought.

Geom Mugeuk had realized something in his life before regression, when he had searched for the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp.

Back then, his heart had been impatient. He had wanted to find it even a day sooner.

But in the end, he came to understand. Just as a life spent in haste ends chasing nothing but chances, an impatient fisherman only muddies the water.

One had to know how to wait. Calmly gazing at the river, one had to know how to wait a thousand breaths, ten thousand breaths, all for that single breath in which one struck. And if, just once, one did not miss that moment, one could change one’s life.

And do not despair because you missed an opportunity. No fisherman quits fishing just because he let a fish get away. It was because he had searched to the end with that sort of tenacity that he had succeeded.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon glanced sideways at Geom Mugeuk. The look in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes as he gazed at the river now was deeper than that of any Demon Venerable present.

‘You rascal, what are you thinking about now?’

At that moment, Geom Ujin also turned his head slightly toward his son.

Because of that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Geom Ujin’s eyes met.

‘Your son brought us all the way here. Do you intend to go to the end with a son like this?’

Had he read that thought?

Geom Ujin’s eyes seemed to say this.

‘As we are now, we can surely do it. Do you truly want to let this chance slip away?’

After gazing at each other for a moment, Geom Ujin and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned to look at the river at the same time.

A short while later, Geom Mugeuk secured his fishing rod and went to his father. His brother was standing beside their father, staring at the river.

Geom Muyang asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Should I move aside?”

“No. Unless I’m going to speak ill of you, what could I possibly have to say that I can’t say in front of you?”

Geom Muyang looked dumbfounded, but the words that there was nothing he could not say in front of him felt strangely gratifying.

“Come to think of it, this is the first time Father and the two of us have stood side by side on a riverbank like this, isn’t it?”

Geom Muyang nodded.

“It would be nice to bring that painter from back then and ask him to paint this scene too. Or else let’s ask the doll maker in Maga Village to make dolls of the three of us fishing.”

At that, Geom Ujin abruptly said,

“Seeing you act so relaxed, you must be confident.”

“Would a prize catch wake up and start moving this early? It’ll wake up late in the afternoon and start stirring then. It’ll be relaxed, like me.”

He even added a bit of smugness.

“Should I call this the composure of a victor?”

“Today won’t be easy.”

“Then, apart from this contest, shall we make a separate wager, you and I?”

At the word wager, Geom Ujin’s eyes gleamed.

“What wager?”

Geom Mugeuk seemed to have already thought it through.

“A wager on guessing who will take first place. Of course, you may predict yourself as first place.”

A fierce interest bloomed in his father’s eyes.

“We watch from now on, and after one shijin, we decide who will take first.”

“And if one wins the wager?”

Geom Mugeuk staked something far more drastic than ten-thousand-year snow ginseng.

“How about granting the winner’s wish?”

“Any wish?”

“Yes. Any wish, without fail.”

Geom Ujin snorted and said,

“You’ll lose.”

“That remains to be seen.”

After staring at his son for a moment, Geom Ujin nodded readily, for reasons of his own.

“Good.”

They should have set limits, saying that this much could not be granted, but neither of them did so. A truly great wager had been placed.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon, who had been listening beside them, shook his head as if they were beyond help and gazed at the flowing river as he spoke quietly. No, he spoke to the fish within it.

“The fate of this murim no longer rests on the Heavenly Killing Star, but on you.”

Chapter 855: Have You Decided Who to Choose?
--------------------------------------------
“I made a wager with Father over who would win. The wager is that the winner gets a wish granted.”

When Geom Mugeuk spoke with internal energy behind his voice, everyone heard him. The Demon Supremes all looked over with expressions of interest.

After publicly announcing the wager to the Demon Supremes, Geom Mugeuk said to his father,

“Now, then, in one shichen, we will choose who we think will win.”

Geom Mugeuk added one condition.

“And when we decide whom to choose, let’s not tell each other. We’ll write the names on paper and submit them, then open them once the final winner is decided.”

“Are you worried I’ll change my mind?”

“Would I dare change my word? And there is no way you would change yours, Father.”

“Then why?”

“Let us suppose, for example, that I choose my master.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze naturally turned toward the Fist Demon.

“It may happen. Master might win.”

Staring at the river, the Fist Demon answered bluntly.

“That will not happen.”

There was nothing in the world as dull as this. How long had he even been here, and he was already bored.

Even if he did catch a fish, what was so fun about that? The tug felt good? They only said that because they did not know the taste of striking something with a fist.

If this had not been a competition hosted by the Cult Leader, he would have gone back already.

“If I choose him, how could Master possibly do his best while thinking of Father? Conversely, what if he knows Father chose him?”

The Fist Demon would use every trick he had to win. Apart from his boredom, that much was certain.

Geom Ujin nodded. After all, there were more Demon Supremes who would be mindful of his own gaze.

“Very well. Let us do as you say.”

Acknowledging that his son was at a disadvantage, Geom Ujin readily accepted the proposal.

As Geom Mugeuk returned to his seat, his eyes met Geom Muyang’s.

“Hyung, keep a close eye on who might win too. If you get a feeling about someone, don’t tell Father—quietly tell me.”

Geom Muyang could not understand his younger brother’s intent. That clever fellow would not have made such a wager without thinking.

‘Just what are you planning?’

If their father had not been there, he would have grabbed him by the collar at once, shaken him, and demanded an answer.

As though troubled, Geom Mugeuk clutched his head and walked toward his own place.

“Who should I choose?”

Geom Mugeuk passed his own seat and went over to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Look at this. Even at this fishing spot, after Father, you are the first elder I come to. Do you see?”

“You don’t have to come today. Please, don’t make a racket. Go over there.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was sincere about fishing. Today, he intended to show everyone the skill befitting the Cult Leader’s fishing teacher.

“I’ll stay quietly beside you.”

Geom Mugeuk crouched beside the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Looking at the float drifting on the river, the two continued their conversation in low voices.

“What? Now that you’ve caused trouble, are you worried?”

“I did cause trouble, didn’t I?”

“A major disaster. Why would you wager a wish? The Cult Leader…”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not finish his sentence, but Geom Mugeuk could guess what he had been about to say. Everyone knew what his father’s wish was.

“So? Who are you going to choose as the winner?”

“If it were you, Elder, whom would you pick?”

“Me, of course.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled as if he had expected that answer.

“Then I’ll decide on you, Elder.”

Looking down at Geom Mugeuk with an expression that seemed to say, That easily? the Blood Heaven Blade Demon took a step back.

“Don’t choose me. Of course I’ll win, but still, don’t choose me. It’s burdensome. If the Cult Leader’s dream is broken because of me, he is still human; it will weigh on him for a long time. I don’t want to remain that sort of person to him.”

Geom Mugeuk knew well how much the Blood Heaven Blade Demon treasured his relationship with his father.

After silently gazing at the river, Geom Mugeuk suddenly asked,

“Whom do you think Father will choose?”

This time too, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon answered without hesitation.

“The Cult Leader will naturally choose himself.”

Given his father’s personality, that was highly likely. Especially since this fishing competition was a rematch staked on his pride.

“Then you must find someone who can beat the Cult Leader. Excluding me.”

Geom Mugeuk looked around at the Demon Supremes fishing, one by one. Perhaps because it was still early, they were all leisurely watching the river. Geom Mugeuk’s gaze stopped on the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, who stood beneath the shade of a tree.

“Elder, don’t just stare at the water here. Go talk with the Sword Sovereign and take a walk together.”

“In this important match?”

“That is exactly why she would be even more touched. Ah, even in such an important match, he is trying to spend time with me! Later, when you are dying, will you regret losing a fishing competition? Or will you regret not taking a walk with the Sword Sovereign in this beautiful place?”

“Obviously, losing at fishing. And the Sword Sovereign would probably prefer the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng to a walk too.”

It was just then, as Geom Mugeuk and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon laughed together.

“I caught one!”

At last, someone had caught the first fish. It was an unexpected person.

The one staring in astonishment at the fish flapping at the end of the line was the Soul-Capturing Demon Supreme.

Everyone had thought her spot was not particularly good, yet the first fish had been caught there. And it was a fairly large fish at that.

“Is this the start of an upset?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon answered flatly,

“It’s just a blind fish.”

Geom Mugeuk called out to her.

“Congratulations! That’s the first fish you’ve ever caught in your life, Soul-Capturing Demon Supreme.”

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

With a deeply moved expression, the Soul-Capturing Demon Supreme held up the fish.

Geom Mugeuk did not forget to tease the Demon Buddha.

“Even so, can you stop a beginner’s luck?”

He had been the one to say that, so long as he was here, there would be no such thing as beginner’s luck.

The Demon Buddha looked at Geom Mugeuk with a meaningful smile.

“Do you know the greatest enemy of beginner’s luck?”

“What is it?”

“Time.”

The Demon Buddha once again spoke like an enlightened high monk.

“If you wait patiently, beginner’s luck is bound to end. As it happens, today’s competition will continue until late, will it not?”

The Demon Supremes quietly listened to the two of them talk. It had truly been a long time since they had all gathered like this and shared such a conversation.

Geom Mugeuk did not return to his own seat. This time, he went to visit the Demon Supreme farthest from him. It was the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon.

“If you take off your mask, all the fish will gather here to see that handsome face of yours.”

Inside the mask, the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon’s eyes smiled. The two stood side by side by the river and talked.

The Demon Supremes watching them wondered how the Young Cult Leader had managed to become so close to none other than the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon. Once again, they found human relationships impossible to predict.

“Is the Master of Cheonhwaru doing well?”

“Yes. She is happy, saying that at last she can focus on her own work.”

Having been caught up in this affair, she must have neglected matters at Cheonhwaru.

“When I go out to the Central Plains, I should visit and pay my respects.”

“She will be pleased if you come, Young Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk suddenly grew curious. Perhaps the Master of Cheonhwaru knew about his future. Had she received a new prophecy related to the unification of the martial world?

“You intend to take off your mask today.”

The Extreme Evil Smiling Demon had understood the moment he heard about the wager between the two of them. Today, at this fishing spot, the Young Cult Leader had made his decisive move.

Yes. Perhaps today, when all Eight Demon Supremes were gathered, was the most fitting moment.

“But how did you predict who would take first place?”

He asked because he thought that if Geom Mugeuk had made such a wager, he must certainly have a plan.

“I did not make this wager because I predicted anything.”

“Then why?”

“If I cannot change Father’s mind before the competition ends, I plan to leave it to one last bit of luck.”

It was half joke, half truth.

Whether it was a joke or the truth, the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon believed in him.

“You did well. When it comes to luck, Young Cult Leader, you are not one to lose to anyone, are you?”

“I am quite lucky.”

The two looked at each other and smiled.

“If it were you, Smiling Demon, whom would you choose?”

After thinking for a moment, the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon chose an unexpected person.

“If it were me, I would choose the Cult Leader.”

“Father?”

“Yes.”

The Extreme Evil Smiling Demon did not give a reason, and Geom Mugeuk did not press him.

“I’ll come bother you again later.”

As Geom Mugeuk was returning to his place,

“I caught one!”

Someone caught the second fish. This time too, it was an unexpected person. It was the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign. Looking at the fish dangling from the line and flapping, she shook her head.

“You truly are foolish. Of all days, why bite my bait when this is my first time fishing?”

Looking at her, Geom Mugeuk spoke loudly.

“How can one help being drawn to beauty? Congratulations, Sword Sovereign!”

“Thank you.”

Geom Mugeuk went straight to the Demon Buddha.

“What happens when there are many beginners showing their luck? Might even time fail to handle it?”

Knowing that Geom Mugeuk had come to tease him, the Demon Buddha once again became an enlightened high monk.

“So long as beginners are fighting fish, they cannot defeat us. Are we not people who fight our own hearts?”

Geom Mugeuk crouched beside the Demon Buddha.

“When did you go fishing so much?”

“I, too, like time spent alone, just as you do.”

“I wander around a great deal, unlike you, Demon Buddha.”

In this respect, the Demon Buddha was similar to him. He seemed to meet many people and enjoy those meetings, yet he was the sort of person who liked being alone more than anyone.

“What do you think of my choice? In your eyes, Demon Buddha, was it a good choice? Or a doomed one?”

“That will depend on whose name you write.”

“If it were you, Demon Buddha, whose name would you write?”

The Demon Buddha turned his head slightly and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Have you not already written it in your heart?”

“No. If I had, I would not have given Father one shichen. I would have asked him to write it immediately.”

“You caused such a major incident with no plan? How like you.”

“You should be saying, ‘How unlike you’!”

After pondering for a moment, the Demon Buddha pointed to one person. Since he had been confident in fishing, Geom Mugeuk had expected him to say himself. But the answer that came from his mouth was the same as the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon’s.

“If it were me, I would write the Cult Leader’s name.”

“May I ask why?”

After a brief pause, the Demon Buddha answered.

“Because you are not as desperate as the Cult Leader.”

“!”

The Demon Buddha’s gaze turned toward his father. Geom Mugeuk understood. The one who had read his father’s longing for the unification of the martial world most accurately might very well be the Demon Buddha, who had once stood at the forefront in pursuit of power.

“If you thought the Cult Leader merely had a vague desire to unify the martial world, that would be a grave mistake.”

The Demon Buddha was telling him that his father’s longing was far stronger than Geom Mugeuk imagined.

Just then, another fish was caught. The beginners’ luck continued.

This time, the one who pulled it up was the Poison King. His face was full of joy. It was the first time Geom Mugeuk had ever seen the Poison King look so happy about anything.
