“Demon Buddha, at this rate, he may stop going to the mountains to dig for poisonous herbs and start diving for poisonous fish instead.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, the Demon Buddha laughed.

“I’ll come again later.”

After parting with the Demon Buddha and returning to his own place, the fourth fish was caught.

The one who caught the fourth fish was the Poison King again.

As the beginners caught fish one after another, a change occurred there.

The golden radiance around the Demon Buddha deepened as he cast his line farther, and the Drunken Demon drank anxiously.

“Look at him getting restless, unable to wait leisurely like you, Elder.”

When Geom Mugeuk turned to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, he had unfolded the fishing rods he had brought and was choosing another one. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was blaming his equipment.

It was not only the trio of boasters who changed.

Geom Ujin slowly walked toward the Poison King. Everyone’s attention focused on him.

Naturally, the most nervous person was the Poison King.

Geom Ujin held out his hand.

Ssssss!

With one light gesture, a large pit formed beside where the Poison King stood. When he moved his hand again, a narrow channel was made from the river to the pit. After filling the pit with water, he placed the fish the Poison King had caught inside. The two fish swam in the puddle.

“From now on, put the fish you catch here.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

“How does it feel, now that you have caught some?”

“It seems to suit my aptitude well.”

He meant it sincerely. The Poison King loved nature, and even in ordinary times, no one among the Demon Venerables here could surpass him when it came to sitting around in a daze.

“I thought as much.”

Geom Ujin walked back to his seat. The Poison King already wore the delighted expression of a man who had practically won. The fact that the Cult Leader had personally come over for his sake was a visit more precious than ten-thousand-year snow ginseng.

“Surely you aren’t thinking of choosing the Poison King, are you?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geom Ujin spoke with an inscrutable smile.

“Why do you think I wouldn’t? Can’t I choose anyone here?”

Watching his father return to his seat like that, Geom Mugeuk let out a light sigh.

“Now you’re even deploying confusion tactics.”

* * *

At last, one shichen had passed.

Geom Mugeuk reported the state of the fish as though he were giving live commentary.

“The Poison King has three, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable has two, the Drunken Demon has one, and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable has one. Seven fish in total have been caught. They’re all about the same size, so there’s nothing to fight over yet.”

The rest—Geom Ujin and Geom Mugeuk, the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon and the Demon Buddha, the Extreme Evil Young Demon and the Fist Demon—had yet to catch even one.

After giving his report, Geom Mugeuk stood before his father.

“Have you decided who today’s winner will be?”

Geom Ujin nodded.

The eyes of all the Demon Venerables were fixed on the two of them. From this moment on, the fate of the Murim might be divided according to the names written down on those slips.

The two each wrote a name on a piece of paper. Geom Ujin wrote without hesitation and folded it so it could not be seen.

Geom Mugeuk, who had written his name later and was holding it out, suddenly shouted again.

“Ah, this isn’t it.”

Fwoosh.

The paper burst into flame in Geom Mugeuk’s hand. After burning it with Fiery Yang Qi, Geom Mugeuk wrote the name again. Geom Ujin and the Demon Venerables considered even this fuss part of Geom Mugeuk’s strategy.

Just who had the two of them written down?

Geom Mugeuk entrusted his father’s paper and his own to his older brother.

“Hyung, you hold on to them.”

Geom Muyang asked Geom Mugeuk as if he found it unexpected.

“What will you do if, for Father’s sake, I write down a different name and then take out the winner’s name?”

“As if you would. Let’s say you did. As if Father would say, ‘I won,’ based on a name you fabricated.”

He was right. Geom Muyang smiled, accepted the papers, and put them into his breast.

Geom Mugeuk asked his father loudly enough for everyone to hear.

“Can’t we just live like this, all fishing together and enjoying ourselves?”

“How long do you think this enjoyment will last? It is an enjoyment that will end when the sun sets.”

“The next day we can go hunting, the day after that on a picnic, the day after that drink, and the day after that go out to punish bad guys. There are so many things to do.”

Geom Ujin gave no answer to his son’s words.

“Father.”

Instead of overturning the drinking table, Geom Mugeuk sat down across from his father at it.

And at last, he asked the question he had not asked even once in all this time.

“What kind of Murim is it that you dream of, Father?”

Chapter 856: You Just Killed the Heinous Little Demon
-----------------------------------------------------
All the Demon Venerables held their breath.

It was a question no one had dared ask until now.

What kind of murim was the one Geom Ujin dreamed of?

“I am curious, Father.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze went past his father’s shoulder to the Poison King.

Have you ever asked the Cult Leader about the unification of murim he dreams of?

He had now used the antidote the Poison King had told him about.

Geom Ujin silently stared at his son. Geom Mugeuk did not avoid his father’s gaze.

Geom Mugeuk knew because he had heard it from the Fist Demon. The reason his father had resolved to unify murim was because of his mother.

Father, is it still because of that reason?

How, truly, did his father regard this murim? Did he hate it? Was that why he wanted to sweep it all away? Or did he, unexpectedly, dream of a peaceful murim?

At length, Geom Ujin spoke in a low voice.

“To dream of anything when it comes to this murim.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled as he spoke. It was a moment that suited something his father used to say often.

“Cheap sentimentality just begging to get you killed, isn’t it?”

But Geom Ujin did not laugh.

“This murim has no such value.”

With those words, there was no need to hear more to know what his father thought of murim.

Is it because of Mother?

Geom Mugeuk could not bring himself to say those words. He felt that though he could speak of anything else, he had to be cautious when it came to matters involving his mother. She had been the most precious person to his father.

“It may hold no value to you, Father, but countless people in this murim must be living while assigning it values of their own. I want to protect those values.”

Geom Ujin’s expression twitched slightly. The tension between the two of them rose even higher.

“Fine. Then let me ask you one thing.”

This time, Geom Ujin asked his son.

“If I go to war despite your persuasion, what will you do?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk answered.

“I will stop you.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk asked.

“If I stop you, what will you do, Father?”

Geom Ujin read a certain intention in his son’s provocation, though there had been no need for him to add it. A request to show him, just once.

“Blood Heaven Blade Demon, listen.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon respectfully cupped his fist and prepared to receive the order. Soon, the Cult Leader’s command came down.

“Suppress the Young Cult Leader’s internal energy and make him kneel before me.”

All the Demon Venerables were shocked. The cheerful, pleasant atmosphere from moments ago chilled in an instant. It truly felt as if a flowing river had frozen over.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon answered without hesitation.

“I shall obey your solemn command.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon slowly began walking toward Geom Mugeuk.

Yes, he had expected that if an order like this were given, it would come to him rather than to the Fist Demon. The Cult Leader would know better than anyone that it would be far more effective that way.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon stared at Geom Mugeuk.

You fool, what will you do now? Surely you were not unaware that a moment like this might come someday. Hurry and beg the Cult Leader for forgiveness.

Geom Mugeuk must have read the emotion contained in his gaze, yet he did not move as the Blood Heaven Blade Demon wished. Rather, he acted in the opposite manner.

“Geugak Soma.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

“Stop the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.”

“I shall obey your command.”

Geugak Soma stepped out from the other side and blocked the way between Geom Mugeuk and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon revealed a cold aura.

“Step aside.”

“I cannot.”

“Do you not know what you are doing right now?”

To obstruct the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, who had received the Cult Leader’s command, was an act that could rightly be called rebellion. It was a situation where there would be no room for excuse even if beheading were ordered.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon could not understand it. There was no way Geom Mugeuk did not know that.

In a situation where even stopping Geugak Soma from stepping forward would not have been enough, he had instead given him an order?

Geugak Soma bowed respectfully to Geom Ujin.

“My apologies, Cult Leader.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon sent a sound transmission to Geom Mugeuk.

—If you don’t want to get the masked one killed, hurry and make him withdraw.

But whatever he was thinking, Geom Mugeuk did not call Geugak Soma back.

At that moment, Geom Ujin gave another order.

“Ilhwa Sword Venerable, listen.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

“Assist the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and suppress Geugak Soma.”

“I shall obey your solemn command.”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable walked forward. Her gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk, who stood behind Geugak Soma.

Young Cult Leader, just what are you doing?

Because she knew better than anyone that Geom Mugeuk was wise and clever, she too could not understand his current actions.

Tension flowed among the three Demon Venerables facing one another. A situation was unfolding in which the tacit rule that Demon Venerables absolutely did not fight one another might be broken.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Drunken Demon.

Mugeuk, hurry and fix this. At this rate, Geugak Soma might really die.

No, Geugak Soma was not the issue. This was an action that might get him expelled from the position of Young Cult Leader.

But rather than fixing it, Geom Mugeuk made the problem bigger.

“Drunken Demon, please help me.”

The words, “You mad bastard! Is now the time to ask me to help you?” rose all the way to the Drunken Demon’s throat.

But the Drunken Demon answered respectfully.

“Young Cult Leader, I cannot do that.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned without hesitation to the Demon Buddha.

“Demon Buddha, please help me.”

The Demon Buddha could tell that Geom Mugeuk was doing this with some intention. He was curious what that intention was. Truly, when one stayed by the Young Cult Leader’s side, there was never a moment to be bored.

“I’m sorry, but I refuse.”

When the Demon Buddha firmly refused, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned this time to the Poison King.

“Poison King, please help me.”

The Poison King silently watched the three fish he had caught swimming in the puddle.

He knew. The Young Cult Leader could not block the Cult Leader’s will with Geugak Soma alone.

What if he helped? He would be able to become the strongest force for him. Of course, he would forever fall out of the Cult Leader’s favor.

The Poison King slowly shook his head, showing his refusal. Even without this puddle the Cult Leader had personally made for him, he would have refused; today, he would not be barking like a dog together with the Young Cult Leader.

Next was the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable.

“Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable, please help me.”

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable’s circumstances were different from the other Demon Venerables.

From now on, it’s the Six Demon Venerables!

Geom Mugeuk had asked whether it was all right to make such a joke to his father.

This is not a joke, is it?

Even so, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable was truly about to step forward and say he would help. But before he could, Geom Mugeuk’s sound transmission came flying in.

—Soul-Seizing, stay where you are. Today is not the day.

Believing he must have something in mind, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable followed Geom Mugeuk’s will.

“I am sorry, Young Cult Leader.”

And so the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable, too, respectfully refused.

Now Geom Mugeuk looked at the last remaining Demon Venerable. Even though this was the one person who would absolutely never turn his back no matter if all the other Demon Venerables did, he did not give up.

“Please help me, Master.”

As expected, the Fist Demon merely looked at Geom Mugeuk in silence. As if it was nonsense from the start, he did not even express refusal.

Having been refused by everyone, Geom Mugeuk looked at his father.

Geom Ujin asked in a low voice.

“You have just killed Geugak Soma for the crime of rebellion. Is this what you want?”

“No.”

He had expected as much, of course.

There was no way this clever son of his had expected any Demon Venerable other than Geugak Soma to help him.

If he had been confident that even a few of the Demon Venerables would step forward to help, he would not have created a situation like this. At the very least, he was not a son who would drive him into a corner in that manner. Geom Ujin’s faith in his son was that strong.

“Then why did you tell Geugak Soma to stop him?”

The gazes of all the Demon Venerables turned to Geom Mugeuk. They were all curious. Curious about why he had made him step forward and stop them like this.

“Because it is something that will not happen in reality.”

Since none of them could understand what he meant, they all wore puzzled expressions.

“That is why I wanted to see it once. The sight of Soma stepping forward for my sake. The sight of the other Demon Venerables not defying Father’s command no matter how close I have become with them.”

“Something that will not happen in reality?”

“Yes. Something like this will never happen. Soma will never become a traitor.”

That astonishing reason flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“If Father starts a war, I will follow Father’s command.”

“!”

He had felt the same when he promised his father that five-year period. Today, Geom Mugeuk was making that feeling clear to his father in concrete terms.

“When that time comes, I will stand at the vanguard as promised. I will not resent you for casting aside my will, Father. I have no intention of scheming to ruin Father’s war either. I will lead that war to victory without fail.”

Everyone could feel it. Geom Mugeuk was speaking from the heart.

“Of course, I will work to minimize the sacrifices caused by the war. It will not be easy, but I will also do my utmost to save the lives of the alliance leaders and my friends.”

There was a reason Geom Mugeuk had created this situation today.

“That is why I am trying so hard to stop it. Because the outcome if I fail to stop it has already been decided like this. Today, I will stop it until the tournament ends, and if I fail today, I will keep trying to stop it until the very last day of the promised five years.”

Geom Mugeuk asked his father for forgiveness.

“I deliberately created this situation to properly show you what is in my heart. So please, forgive Soma.”

Just from the fact that Soma had not stepped forward on his own but had been called out by his son to block the way, it was clear this had been a deliberately staged situation.

And he already knew that Geugak Soma had given everything for his son. Rather, it would have been a problem if he had not stood in front of his son, and Geom Ujin bore no ill feeling toward him for defying his order.

Geom Ujin sent a sound transmission to Geugak Soma.

—Thank you for faithfully keeping my request.

Geugak Soma understood what those words meant. In the past, the Cult Leader had once entrusted the Young Cult Leader to him. Geugak Soma had even told Geom Mugeuk about it.

As if proving that he had not spoken lightly, the Cult Leader remembered the matter precisely.

—I will keep it until the day I reach my death.

Geom Ujin nodded with a satisfied smile.

Geom Mugeuk walked over to Geugak Soma’s side.

“In the future, there will never be a time when you must stand between me and Father like this. And thank you for today, Soma.”

Behind the mask, Geugak Soma’s two eyes were smiling pleasantly.

Watching that sight, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable felt relieved inwardly, yet also disappointed.

He understood why Geom Mugeuk had kept him from stepping forward. Since this had not been a real situation, it must have meant there was no need for him to fall out of his father’s favor. If two people had defied the Cult Leader’s will, the atmosphere would have become different from now.

But seeing Geom Mugeuk and Geugak Soma like that, he could not help feeling that he, too, wanted to be standing in that place.

Lastly, Geom Mugeuk expressed his gratitude to his father.

“Thank you very much for generously overlooking your son’s immature behavior.”

Geom Ujin felt it. By revealing his true feelings, his son had declared war.

As always, his son did not hide his sincerity and leave that space to be filled by imagination. This clever fellow knew that such imagination was always cold.

It was then.

Thud.

The fishing rod set up beside them fell over.

“Ah! That’s my fishing rod!”

A fish had bitten the bait on the fishing rod Geom Mugeuk had set up. Geom Mugeuk ran over and hauled the fish up.

“This is the first fish I’ve caught!”

With an excited face, Geom Mugeuk held the fish up high.

“Did you see my skill? Catching a fish even without holding the rod? Even when I stay still, the fish rushes toward me… No, where are you going? I’m telling you to look at my fish! Doesn’t it look the biggest?”

Geom Ujin cast his fishing line back into the river.

The Demon Venerables who had been in confrontation returned to their places, and the remaining Demon Venerables picked up their fishing rods again.

Geom Mugeuk spoke loudly to the Demon Venerables who had resumed fishing.

“Now, the tournament continues. From this moment on, please catch as many as you can! Especially those whose names I wrote down, please catch even more diligently!”

The atmosphere, which had been so heavy and serious, brightened back to its original state.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon said to Geom Mugeuk,

“I clearly told you not to write my name down.”

He had deliberately spoken loudly for the Cult Leader to hear. He had told Geom Mugeuk that he did not want to become the reason the Cult Leader’s will was bent.

Though he had yet to catch a single fish, it seemed he was still confident he could win.

“What will you do if Father wrote down your name?”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon glanced sidelong at Geom Ujin.

“That won’t happen.”

“You never know. Father might have picked you, sir. You can’t fail to take first place.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon stared at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“You picked me, didn’t you?”

“That, too, is hard to say.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and started walking again.

“Where are you going now instead of fishing?”

“I’ve caught one, while you, sir, haven’t caught a single one yet.”

“I’m someone who’s strong in the second half. And that fish got caught all by itself!”

Geom Mugeuk moved on, this time walking over to the Drunken Demon.

Without a word, the Drunken Demon poured liquor into the Ice Palace Sacred Cup hanging at his waist.

Geom Mugeuk drank. The liquor had already been delicious, but drinking it from the Ice Palace Sacred Cup made it taste truly sublime.

“This is good liquor.”

“You really are a madman.”

He had not shown it earlier, but he had truly been anxious beyond words. He had worried that the relationship between father and son might be completely ruined.

“You told me not to overturn the drinking table and to sit down, didn’t you? I followed your advice, Brother.”

“You fool, a drinking table needs good snacks. If you start by openly downing hard liquor like that, how could the mood possibly be good?”

“I was too hasty, wasn’t I?”

“Does that even need saying? Find some snacks even now.”

“Right, I should. This is why we came out like this today, to avoid creating a situation like that. I’ll find them!”

For a while, Geom Mugeuk stood beside the Drunken Demon and gazed at the flowing river.

“Brother, is it arrogance for me to believe I can change Father’s heart?”

The Drunken Demon gave no answer. If it had been anyone else, he would have answered firmly that it was.

But because he knew the things Geom Mugeuk had done until now, and the effort he had put in, the words would not come.

Looking down at the liquor bottle in his hand, the Drunken Demon said,

“You know something? Until now, liquor has never once tried to change me. Liquor is simply liquor. And yet I, after drinking it, keep changing. I feel good, I grow gloomy, I get angry, I become lonely.”

As he poured more liquor into the Ice Palace Sacred Cup, the Drunken Demon continued.

“If your heart is set on changing the Cult Leader, then this will be the most difficult thing in the world. But if instead you simply become good liquor and make the Cult Leader drunk? That would be worth trying.”

The words struck home, but a natural worry followed.

“What if he wakes and regrets it?”

“Everyone regrets it the day after getting drunk.”

The Drunken Demon drank deeply, then added,

“But if the liquor is Geom Mugeuk, he’ll regret it pleasantly.”

Chapter 857: Why Would a Drunkard Worry About a Hangover
--------------------------------------------------------
The Drunken Demon was right. He had no need to worry about his father’s hangover. Whether his father regretted it or not, if he took even those feelings into account, he would never be able to stop this matter.

“I’ve said it before, but the more I see you, the smarter you are, brother. Out of all the Demon Venerables, you’re the smartest.”

Praise for being smart always put him in a good mood, so the Drunken Demon could not hide the smile that naturally bloomed on his face.

“You’re no ordinary drunkard, brother. You’re an extraordinary drunkard.”

With a pleased expression, the Drunken Demon poured wine into the Ice Palace Holy Grail. As Geom Mugeuk drank, he returned the words the Drunken Demon had once said to him.

“If the wine is called the Drunken Demon, I would gladly be drunk on it all night!”

Just as the two were drinking, someone caught a fish.

Splash!

It was the Extreme Evil Young Demon. It was the first fish he had caught today.

A reaction completely opposite to his indifferent one burst out.

“Lord Soma!”

Geom Mugeuk shouted toward the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

“Congratulations! At last, our Lord Soma has caught his first fish! That fish is probably the handsomest fish in this river!”

Seeing Geom Mugeuk jump up and down, rejoicing as though it were his own achievement, the Drunken Demon, standing beside him, expressed his dissatisfaction.

“Isn’t your reaction too different? You weren’t this happy when I caught one.”

“Because you’re not Lord Soma.”

“Don’t discriminate right in front of someone. That’s the worst thing you can do.”

“If you hated this, you should have stepped forward when I shouted, ‘Help me, Drunken Demon!’”

“I was going to.”

Geom Mugeuk looked into the Drunken Demon’s eyes. Faced with that steady stare, the Drunken Demon could not bring himself to lie.

“No. I wouldn’t have been able to.”

Defy the Cult Leader’s order and step forward? For the Drunken Demon, that was absolutely impossible.

“You have to admit what needs admitting. The most I could have done for you was pour wine over your grave.”

“It’s fine. If it was wine from you, brother, it would have tasted good. I’ll pour your favorite wine over your grave too.”

Then the Drunken Demon said something unexpected.

“Don’t pour wine over my grave. I’ve spent my whole life drinking like that—why would I need wine even after I’m dead? When I walk the road to the Yellow Springs, I plan to go sober.”

As if to say they should drink diligently while they were alive, the Drunken Demon drank, then filled the holy grail again and handed it to Geom Mugeuk. Looking down at the cup, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“What’s the drinking snack of your life, brother?”

The Drunken Demon thought for a moment.

“You. Watching you is fun and entertaining.”

“Other than me?”

The Drunken Demon could not answer easily.

“What about Warrior Yeo?”

“Yeobin isn’t a drinking snack to me… she’s strong liquor.”

At the Drunken Demon’s half-serious, half-joking reply, Geom Mugeuk laughed and asked again.

“Isn’t it about time you drank that liquor?”

The Drunken Demon looked at Geom Mugeuk with a face that said, Surely not.

“I mean, the two of you should get married.”

“Out of nowhere?”

“You have to think of Warrior Yeo too.”

He did not know her exact age, but since she had risen to the place of one of the Three Great Drunkards, she could not be young.

The Drunken Demon drank. Seeing the worry in his eyes, it seemed he was not entirely without such thoughts.

“What does Warrior Yeo say? Hasn’t she asked you to marry her?”

“She isn’t the type to bring up such words.”

“Who would easily bring that up when the other person is a Demon Venerable? You have to bring it up first, brother. Don’t you have some wine you’ve saved to take out when you get married? Bring out any sweet wine on a nice day and ask her to marry you.”

The Drunken Demon merely emptied his cup in silence.

“Why? Do you dislike Warrior Yeo?”

“Dislike her? Where else would I find a woman like that?”

“Then why?”

Looking down at the empty cup in his hand, the Drunken Demon let out a light sigh.

“I only know how to get drunk. I don’t really know how to sober up.”

It seemed he was worried whether a life spent doing nothing but drinking could take responsibility for someone else.

“Good grief, they say a monk can’t shave his own head. You give me all sorts of advice, but you’re this bad with your own affairs.”

Geom Mugeuk filled the empty cup in the Drunken Demon’s hand and said,

“Warrior Yeo won’t be any different.”

“What won’t be?”

“She’ll be worried too. If she rose to the place of one of the Three Great Drunkards, then she’s lived her whole life drinking as well. Whether because of martial arts or not. Do you think she wouldn’t worry? ‘Can someone like me live well with the Drunken Demon?’”

The Drunken Demon emptied his cup without answering.

“She knows wine better than anyone, has watched you longer than anyone, and likes you more than anyone. So consider her a lifelong drinking companion and marry her. Drink together in the evening, wake up in the morning and regret it together. Take turns quitting alcohol and live like that.”

The Drunken Demon looked at Geom Mugeuk and smiled. Once in a while, this very young fellow acted like someone who had lived an entire lifetime.

“They say men who live alone die the fastest.”

“What would I do with a long life?”

“You have to drink and play with me even when we’re old. You need someone like Warrior Yeo by your side to watch over you, brother. So catch her before she runs off somewhere.”

He would have liked to drink while talking about this all day, but there was something he had to do.

After emptying his final cup, Geom Mugeuk looked at the Fist Demon standing far away.

“Master would know Father best, wouldn’t he?”

“I suppose he would.”

As Geom Mugeuk walked that way, he added,

“What kind of drinker worries about a hangover?”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been walking toward the Fist Demon, stopped for a moment. Far off, he could see his father teaching his older brother how to fish.

All at once, he thought that perhaps, while teaching his eldest son to fish, his father might be recalling his own dead older brother. He did not know why that thought came to him.

Geom Mugeuk started walking again and stood beside the Fist Demon.

“Fishing isn’t fun, is it?”

The Fist Demon gave no answer, but that silence was already answer enough.

“If you keep scowling so frighteningly, the fish will be too scared to come near.”

“Seeing how relaxed you are, it seems you at least didn’t write down your own name.”

“Whose name I wrote down is a top secret.”

Geom Mugeuk stood beside the Fist Demon and looked at the river.

“Why are you wandering around like this instead of fishing at a fishing tournament?”

“I’m looking for a drinking snack.”

The Fist Demon was about to ask what that meant, but soon turned his head back toward the river. How could he ever understand the things Geom Mugeuk did?

Just look at what had happened earlier. The Young Cult Leader did things that did not even exist in the Fist Demon’s mind.

“I thought long and hard about what Father likes most. Ah, excluding people, of course. If we include people, wouldn’t it be me?”

Seeing the confident Geom Mugeuk, the Fist Demon wore an expression that seemed to ask whether that was really so.

“After becoming closer to Father, I learned a few things. He likes go, he likes cooking. He likes fishing too. But those are all hobbies, aren’t they? Surely he doesn’t like working from the Grand Preceptor’s chair.”

Geom Mugeuk let out a soft sigh and said,

“I tried so hard to become close to Father, yet I realized I don’t know what he likes most.”

Thinking about it, was it only his father? He did not know what his older brother liked most. What was Ian’s favorite thing?

He did not know what the Extreme Evil Young Demon, who had willingly offered up his life a moment ago, liked most. What was it that the Drunken Demon, with whom he had been drinking until just now, liked most?

The Fist Demon looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Did you come because you thought I knew?”

“You don’t?”

He had thought that the Fist Demon, who had been with his father since their youth, might know.

“I don’t.”

It was not an answer given because he did not want to tell him. The Fist Demon truly did not know.

“Even if you asked me that question about myself, I would have to think for quite a while before answering. And even then, the answer might change tomorrow.”

The Fist Demon looked at Geom Mugeuk and asked,

“Then what do you like most?”

Geom Mugeuk could not answer readily either.

Yes. When he did not even know what he himself liked most, how could he know another person’s heart?

Thus the two of them looked at the river in silence. If there was comfort in the chatter he shared with the Drunken Demon, then there was comfort in this silence when he was with the Fist Demon.

How much time had passed? The Fist Demon suddenly said,

“I have not forgotten that promise.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. Long ago, he had made a promise with the Fist Demon. If his father ever decided to start a war, he would tell the Fist Demon first. And at that time, the Fist Demon would let him know in advance. The Fist Demon had willingly made that difficult promise.

“I hope I never have to keep that promise.”

It was the first time the Fist Demon had revealed his feelings about it. Geom Mugeuk bowed respectfully to express his gratitude.

“Thank you, Master.”

It was at that very moment.

“It’s a whopper!”

The one who had caught the first big fish was one of the three loudmouths, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. It was twice the size of the fish caught so far.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been utterly quiet with the Fist Demon, changed completely.

“The current first-place fish has appeared!”

As Geom Mugeuk ran toward the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, he said what the man himself wanted to say for him.

“As expected! Incredible! Your skill as Father’s fishing teacher has not yet died! At this rate, aren’t you going to win, elder?”

“If you wanted to win the bet, you should have picked me.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon became thoroughly triumphant.

“You told us not to pick you, didn’t you?”

“You must overcome adversity to achieve victory.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon lifted the large fish to show the One Flower Sword Venerable. The sight looked as though he had won and was asking her to take the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng together with him.

Just as his father had made one for the Poison King, this time Geom Mugeuk made a pool of water.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon placed the fish into the pool.

Squatting in front of it and looking at the fish, Geom Mugeuk suddenly said,

“You were very shocked earlier, weren’t you?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s hand, which had been putting a new worm on the hook, paused briefly before moving again.

“When has you startling me ever been a matter of just a day or two?”

“I’m sorry.”

“So? How do you plan to persuade the Cult Leader?”

“I’m searching for what Father likes most.”

Then the Blood Heaven Blade Demon said something unexpected.

“What are you going to do after finding out what he likes? Try to butter him up with it? I’m sure the Cult Leader would love that.”

In that instant, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes flashed. He was right. He realized that the very approach of finding out what his father liked most so he could get on his good side and improve his mood had been wrong from the start.

“Then what should I do?”

If it was about his father, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon would feel slighted if he were placed after the Fist Demon.

“You need to find out what he hates most.”

“!”

“What someone likes can be easily dressed up, but what someone hates is not so easily disguised. If you want to know someone deeply, you should look at what they hate rather than what they like.”

Geom Mugeuk cried out in admiration.

“As expected! Elder, you are the smartest! Even if we include all the Demon Venerables throughout history, you may be the smartest!”

Geom Mugeuk shouted loudly enough for everyone to hear.

Then someone immediately reacted.

“I’m quite sure I heard just a moment ago that I was the smartest.”

Rather than resolving the Drunken Demon’s grievance, Geom Mugeuk added another.

“You are drunk, Lord Drunken Demon. How could you be the smartest when Lord Blade Demon, who reads so many books, is here?”

The Drunken Demon looked at Geom Mugeuk with an incredulous expression, then soon drank as though giving up.

“Ah, I’m drunk.”

Even though it had been turned into drunken rambling, the Drunken Demon was in a good mood. At moments like this, it was enjoyable to be able to exchange such jokes.

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father. What did his father hate most?

As far as he knew, he disliked poison arts and also disliked soul-bewitching arts. He disliked assassins as well.

But those were merely general things.

What was it that his father truly hated?

The answer to this problem might lie in that very thing—something he had never once seriously thought about until now.

* * *

Shaaaaaa.

Everyone was beneath the tent, looking at the sudden shower that had begun to fall.

When the rain poured down, the guards of the Heavenly Demon Hall had just finished preparing lunch, so they had paused the fishing for a while and gathered under the tent.

“Fishing in the rain is the best kind of fishing.”

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s words, those who had done some fishing all nodded at once.

Of course, the Fist Demon, the One Flower Sword Venerable, and the Extreme Evil Young Demon did not agree at all, while the Poison King and the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable, who had newly taken an interest in fishing, only wanted to finish their meal quickly and go back out to fish, rain or no rain.

At present, the Poison King was still first in number caught, and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was first in fish size.

“This is the first time, isn’t it? All of us gathering like this to eat outdoors.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, everyone nodded. Truly, who would have thought a day would come when all the Demon Venerables would gather together in such good spirits and eat while watching the rain fall?

“Father, please accept a cup from your son.”

Geom Mugeuk poured wine for Geom Ujin.

After receiving the wine, Geom Ujin poured for his two sons.

The Demon Venerables shared wine with those seated beside them.

The Drunken Demon first poured wine for the Fist Demon seated next to him.

“It has been a long time since I drank wine like this.”

It truly had been a very long time since the Drunken Demon had shared a drinking table with the Fist Demon.

With his large hand, the Fist Demon filled the Drunken Demon’s cup.

“I drank well the wine you sent through the Young Cult Leader.”

At those words, the Drunken Demon understood. Geom Mugeuk had divided the wine he had given him among the Demon Venerables as gifts. And kindly, he had told them it was wine from him. How could he not like him?

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon shared wine with the One Flower Sword Venerable.

“Are you confident you can win?”

“It would be strange if I couldn’t in this round.”

Now the sight of the two of them together was utterly natural. They did not bother hiding that exchange of feelings. The last journey had brought a great change to their relationship.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable felt pleased to receive wine from the Extreme Evil Young Demon. This feeling was less a romantic sentiment than a kind of admiration.

There was an atmosphere unique to the Extreme Evil Young Demon that he could not feel from Cult Leader Pungcheon or Geom Mugeuk. An atmosphere unique to him that made one think, I want to become someone who gives off this kind of feeling.

The Poison King poured wine for the Demon Buddha.

“Thank you.”

The Poison King knew that when the Demon Buddha had stepped forward earlier and questioned why he had been the first to come out, it had been for his sake.

“The Young Cult Leader is worried. He says you may now have to search not for poisonous herbs, but poisonous fish.”

The Poison King did not deny it and smiled faintly. Then his eyes met Geom Ujin’s, and the way he slightly bowed his head was far more natural than before. It was a great change created by one small pool of water.

Shaaaaaa.

Looking at the falling rain, Geom Ujin said softly,

“How wonderful.”

Chapter 858: First Place Has Changed!
-------------------------------------
The gazes of all the Demon Venerables turned toward Geom Ujin.

It was the first time. The first time the words “how nice” had come from the Cult Leader’s mouth.

Could he have said it because he liked the rain falling outside? Surely it meant that he liked that they were all together.

The Demon Venerables exchanged glances, then looked at the person who had made this unbelievable thing possible.

Geom Mugeuk, the person in question, was looking out at the scenery his father was gazing at.

“The view of a riverside in the rain really is nice. I always feel better when I watch the rain like this.”

He had not said it expecting an answer, but Geom Ujin responded to his son.

“I like the rain too.”

“You do, Father?”

“Why are you so surprised?”

“I just thought you would say rain was dreary and unpleasant.”

A faint, wistful emotion passed through Geom Ujin’s eyes as he watched the rain fall.

But soon that emotion vanished without a trace.

The countless circles forming on the river, the smell of wet earth, the sound of rain striking the tent, the water mist rising in the distance. As all of these things came together, the spirits of those watching grew brighter.

Geom Ujin slowly turned his head and looked at the Demon Venerables.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon watching the rain together with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, the Drunken Demon drinking with the sound of rain as his side dish, the Poison King who no longer avoided his gaze, the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable who certainly exuded a young and vivid vitality, the Demon Buddha who moved between the Blood Buddha skilled in calculation and a Buddha who found the world easy, the Little Demon of Ultimate Evil who had already proven that his loyalty to his son was as pure as that snow-white mask, and even the Fist Demon, who would spring to his feet at the word “Let’s go” without asking where.

There had been a time when he had doubted and distrusted the Demon Venerables. Looking back now, there had been no reason for it at all. There had been a period that was inexplicably dark and gloomy.

But now, Geom Ujin trusted the Demon Venerables more firmly than anyone.

The Drunken Demon noticed that Geom Ujin’s cup was empty. He could endure anything else, but not this.

“Allow me to pour you a cup.”

“Let me drink from that cup again after so long.”

In the past, when Geom Mugeuk had returned after finishing matters with the Ice Palace, Geom Ujin had once drunk from the Ice Palace Holy Chalice.

The Drunken Demon poured wine into the Ice Palace Holy Chalice and handed it to Geom Ujin.

“It tastes good, as expected.”

“I shall pour you another.”

“That’s enough. You take a cup.”

As Geom Ujin poured wine into the chalice, he asked,

“What became of the wine essence in the Great Drunken Forest?”

Startled by the unexpected question, the Drunken Demon asked with surprise,

“You knew the wine essence had gone bad?”

Geom Ujin nodded. As expected, there was nothing within the cult that the Cult Leader did not know.

“After we repelled the Heavenly Killing Star, the wine essence became clear again.”

“That is fortunate.”

The Drunken Demon had thought the Cult Leader would regard using the corruption of the wine essence to divine the safety of the cult as superstition. For him to ask about it like this made him truly grateful.

Geom Ujin’s attention turned next to the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable.

“How is your martial arts training going?”

“I am doing my utmost.”

Geom Ujin took a small wooden box from his robes and held it out.

“Take it.”

The Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable went to Geom Ujin and respectfully received it.

When she opened the box, what was inside was, astonishingly, a Demon Essence Pill.

“Take it with you and consume it.”

With a deeply moved expression, the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable bowed politely.

“Thank you, Cult Leader.”

When she first received it, she had wondered if he was bestowing one upon all the Demon Venerables, but Geom Ujin did not grant any elixirs to anyone else.

Only then did she realize it. The Cult Leader had bestowed an elixir upon her alone. And in front of everyone, no less. So overwhelmed was she that the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable’s eyes and face trembled slightly.

Geom Mugeuk was not one to let such a sight pass without comment.

“Is that an elixir bestowed only upon the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable?”

Geom Ujin nodded.

“Why give it to just one person and make the other Demon Venerables feel left out?”

“Because I wanted to.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable and said,

“Soul-Subduing, it seems Father has noticed. That you were about to come over to my side.”

If she followed her heart, the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable would have liked to make this joke.

—If this is how things are, I may have to reconsider becoming the Young Cult Leader’s first Demon Venerable.

Of course, she did not. She merely smiled faintly and looked down at the box containing the Demon Essence Pill with emotion.

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said to Geom Ujin,

“Why have you been hiding this affectionate side of yourself until now? I wondered why I was so full of warmth, but it turns out I take after you, Father.”

As Geom Ujin quietly gazed at his son, astonishing words flowed from him.

“You take after your mother.”

For a moment, Geom Mugeuk was startled. He had not expected his father to mention his mother in front of the Demon Venerables.

Not only Geom Mugeuk, but the Demon Venerables were equally surprised. Bringing this up was even more astonishing than his earlier words that this was nice, or his consideration toward the Drunken Demon and the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable.

“Then I suppose I take after you in surprising people.”

He said it like a joke, but his father denied even that.

“That is not from me either.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell that his mother had often surprised his father while she was alive.

Geom Mugeuk conveyed what he had felt from his father’s eyes as he looked at the rain.

“It must have been Mother who liked the rain too. And you came to like it because of her.”

Geom Ujin did not say that was not true.

Since his father had brought it up anyway.

“What kind of person was Mother?”

Instead of answering, Geom Ujin turned his head and looked at the rain, which was now gradually beginning to let up.

The one who answered in his stead was the Fist Demon.

“The Demon Empress was truly a remarkable person.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Fist Demon. He was not someone who said pleasant things to curry favor with others, so his mother truly must have been a remarkable person.

Thinking about it, he realized that all the Demon Venerables except the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable might have seen his mother. The Ilhwa Sword Venerable had clearly remembered her, after all.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. Her gaze as she looked down at her wine cup had deepened. She had said she did not like his mother. Why did the Sword Venerable dislike his mother? If she had been such a remarkable person.

Was it because he knew the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s feelings? Though he seemed like he might say something, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon seated beside her said nothing.

Geom Mugeuk did not want the atmosphere to sink because of talk of his mother, so he immediately changed the subject.

“Now, now, returning to the fishing tournament, let us speak for a moment with the Demon Venerables who are the talk of the event.”

The first target was the Poison King.

“Poison King, you are currently in first place for the number of fish caught. How do you feel?”

The Poison King’s eyes poisoned him with the question, Must you really ask me that in a place like this? Then he answered calmly.

“This is the first time I’ve realized fishing could be this enjoyable.”

That answer was sincere.

“It wouldn’t have been enjoyable if you hadn’t caught even one, would it?”

As he said that, he glanced subtly at his father.

“Our father, who has yet to catch a single fish, would know that feeling best. Father, if you keep acting so relaxed, you won’t even win a thousand-year snow ginseng.”

It was the thousand-year snow ginseng he had decided to give as a commemorative prize for catching even one fish. It was almost no different from a participation award.

“You caught one by luck and now you are full of confidence.”

“I haven’t even started in earnest yet.”

“The same goes for me.”

For some reason, Geom Ujin was full of confidence.

“I will win the bet.”

Curious at his father’s confidence, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Just who did you write down? At first, I thought you must have chosen yourself, of course, but you are far too relaxed right now. You didn’t really write down the Poison King, did you?”

At that moment, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon subtly cut in.

“You should have written my name.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke loudly again.

“Currently leading in the largest catch category, we have the Blade Demon. How do you view today’s fishing tournament?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon said confidently,

“Do you want to win the bet? Then even now, ask the Cult Leader to change it to my name.”

Geom Mugeuk did not forget to tease the Demon Buddha.

“Likewise, Demon Buddha, who has yet to catch even one fish, please say a word.”

The Demon Buddha was still composed.

“It is about time the beginners’ luck begins to fade.”

“Then shall we go check?”

Before they knew it, the rain had stopped. As if the heavens had briefly bestowed a gift so they could all enjoy the atmosphere and dine together.

The riverside scenery after the rain was clearer and cleaner than ever.

“Now, the afternoon fishing tournament begins! Anyone who wishes to move spots may do so at any time.”

However, everyone went back to their own spots and cast their lines.

Since it was now afternoon, everyone focused on fishing.

Geom Mugeuk also stopped wandering around and fished standing in place. As if he truly possessed skill, the first fish of the afternoon was caught by Geom Mugeuk.

“Now I’m going to show you what I can do!”

Though he seemed excited on the outside, Geom Mugeuk’s mind was seized by one thought.

What did Father hate most?

Leaving aside this bet or the five-year promise, he was personally curious.

He could simply ask directly, but then he would not be able to hear an accurate answer.

Because that answer might have to be drawn from that pitch-black hole everyone carried somewhere in their heart.

What do I like most?

What do I hate most?

As Geom Mugeuk asked himself these questions, he cast his line forcefully toward the river again.

It seemed the fish had realized that the true match had now begun as well. They were caught more often than before, and the next fish caught had great significance.

“It’s a whopper.”

The one who caught the whopper was none other than Geom Ujin.

“It is the largest fish caught so far. You are finally showing your true skill! Congratulations, Father!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon quietly went to where Geom Ujin was.

“You caught a big one. Congratulations.”

His words were congratulatory, but his expression was different. Is it really the biggest one? As he was measuring it by eye to see whether it was larger than the one he had caught—

Geom Mugeuk, who had arrived behind him at some point, said,

“It’s definitely bigger, isn’t it?”

“Doesn’t it look about the same?”

“It’s bigger. Definitely bigger!”

In truth, even to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s own eyes, the one Geom Ujin had caught was larger.

Geom Mugeuk said loudly to everyone,

“First place in the whopper category has changed.”

Geom Mugeuk suddenly flinched.

“Father, you didn’t write down your own name, did you?”

Geom Ujin gave an unreadable, strange smile.

“This is an emergency!”

Geom Mugeuk ran back to his spot and picked up his fishing rod.

“Now, this is no time to play. Fish, come to me!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon, now pushed down to second place, grew even more anxious.

“You brat, if you make that much noise, all the fish will run away.”

The fish that fled went straight over and bit the bait the Fist Demon had cast.

A fairly large fish flopped at the end of the Fist Demon’s rod.

“Because Master’s hands are large, it looks small, but that fish is not small either. It’s the third largest.”

Even from a distance, Geom Mugeuk judged the sizes with ghostlike accuracy.

“Demon Buddha, it seems the beginners’ luck has not ended yet.”

Since the Demon Buddha had still not caught a single fish, an anxious gleam was radiating from him. At this rate, he truly might fail to catch even one.

Of course, he had not yet forgotten to imitate an enlightened high monk.

“One must not forget that fishing is a time to converse with nature and, furthermore, with oneself.”

“Please don’t forget that this gathering today is a fishing tournament!”

“Empty your mind, Young Cult Leader.”

This time, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon reeled one in. Had his intense desire to win been conveyed to the fish?

After that, he continued catching them one after another. In terms of whopper size, he was still in second place, but in number caught, he also rose to second place.

“Elder, if this goes well, you may take both ten-thousand-year snow ginseng roots!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s eyes sparkled as he steeled his resolve.

On the other hand, after waiting all this time, when a fish that finally bit escaped with only the bait, the Demon Buddha at last exploded in frustration.

“You little bastards! I’ll wipe out your entire line!”

Geom Mugeuk laughed as he looked at the Demon Buddha.

“The Blood Buddha has returned.”

The Poison King exerted his beginners’ luck to the very end. Whenever the Blood Heaven Blade Demon caught up close, he pulled away; when he caught up again, he pulled away again.

The difference between the Poison King and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was only one fish.

The difference in size between Father’s whopper and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s was about a handspan.

As they caught and missed fish like that, the time they had set at the beginning came to an end.

A warrior from the Guard Hall came and said,

“It will soon be time to finish.”

At this rate, Father and the Poison King would win. From afar, the Little Demon of Ultimate Evil looked at Geom Mugeuk with worried eyes. Whose name did you write? Is it all right if it ends like this?

The atmosphere of the fishing tournament could not have been better, but responsibility would have to be taken according to the result of the bet.

If the Cult Leader had written his own name and won, they would have to grant that wish. Not only the Little Demon of Ultimate Evil, but the Demon Venerables were certain. That wish would undoubtedly be related to the unification of murim.

The guard warrior said loudly,

“We will count to ten from now and end the tournament.”

The Fist Demon stretched as if this tedious fishing was finally over. The Ilhwa Sword Venerable also murmured that this would be her last fishing.

Fishing was now far from the Soul-Subduing Demon Venerable’s mind; she was thinking of the Demon Essence Pill she held at her chest. Would she even be able to consume something so precious?

On the other hand, there were those for whom, even with only ten counts left, the tournament was not yet over. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon prayed inwardly.

‘Please! Just one last whopper! Even one small fry will do!’

He wanted to win and boast to the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

The Poison King also stared at his float until the very end, trying to catch even one more.

Geom Ujin and Geom Mugeuk likewise did not take their eyes off their floats until the last moment.

The warrior began to count.

“Ten, nine, eight……”

It was at that very moment.

Fwoooosh.

A bright radiance began to shine beneath the water, then spread in all directions. Gradually, that light grew larger and clearer.

Pwaaaaaang.

A fish that had bitten the bait shot up into the air, emitting a crimson radiance. It looked just like a small dragon ascending to heaven.

“Whoa!”

The moment they saw the fish, exclamations burst from everyone’s mouths.

It was larger than any fish caught so far. Its size alone was enough to make the person who had caught it the winner, but it was not merely a large fish.

Golden pupils revealed themselves to the world, along with long whiskers; from its body it emitted a flame-like red aura, and every one of its scales bore the traces of long years. Its spread fins were dazzling, like a phoenix unfurling its wings.

Radiating brilliant light, the fish flung itself toward the owner of the fishing rod it had bitten.

Chapter 859: The Coolest Madman I Know
--------------------------------------
The one who had reeled in the fish was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

As everyone’s eyes focused on him, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked with a startled expression,

“Surely that isn’t a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon had once seen an illustration of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp in the Illustrated Compendium of Spiritual Creatures. It certainly resembled the fish Geom Mugeuk was holding.

Geom Ujin and the other Demon Venerables had likewise never seen a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp in person. They had only seen it in pictures or roughly guessed at its appearance from stories handed down since ancient times.

Geom Mugeuk looked down at the fish in his arms and answered in a trembling voice.

“Yes, I believe it is.”

Geom Mugeuk was certain that the fish he had caught was truly a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp. After all, he had caught one once before.

The Demon Venerables were swept up in shock. They were men who had weathered every sort of hardship and were not easily surprised by most things, yet to think they would witness a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp being reeled in with a fishing rod right before their eyes.

Geom Ujin, too, could not hide the astonishment on his face.

“How did you catch a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp?”

At his father’s question, Geom Mugeuk answered in a quivering voice.

“I don’t know either.”

He had not caught it by deliberately trying to. The Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp he had caught before his regression had not been caught here, nor had it been caught at this time.

In his life before regression, he had gone through unimaginable hardship to catch a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp.

A Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp was not a spiritual creature one could catch in an ordinary river like this. He had caught it deep beneath a fiery cavern, in a truly dangerous and dizzying place.

And yet a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp had been caught at a fishing tournament? By him, no less?

Was it his fate, regardless of regression, to catch a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp at least once in his life? If not that, then—

Geom Mugeuk thought of the old man who had sent him back.

‘Is this a gift for me?’

It might not be a gift. If he ended up winning, he might lose the bet with his father. Geom Mugeuk had not written down his own name.

The Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp in Geom Mugeuk’s hands thrashed powerfully once. As if bidding farewell, it spread its large fins wide, and at that moment, a sunset-colored radiance began to pour from its scales. A light more beautiful than an actual sunset began to shine, mingling with gold.

The brilliant light that had been streaming forth gathered into a single point.

Swaaaaaang.

When the light vanished, the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp was gone.

In its place, a large crimson inner core remained in Geom Mugeuk’s hand.

The inner core of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp.

Alongside Kongqing Stone Milk, one of the two supreme peaks of all spirit medicines.

Its fragrance spread through the air.

Subtle yet clear, the scent alone made one feel as though the mind were being purified. Aside from Geom Mugeuk, none of those present had ever smelled such a fragrance in their lives.

Geom Mugeuk looked down at the inner core in his hand with eyes full of emotion. All the countless efforts he had made to obtain this in his life before regression flashed past him.

Everyone stared at Geom Mugeuk in utter astonishment. Geom Mugeuk had shocked them countless times before, but to think he would astonish them even at a fishing tournament to this extent.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to the martial artist of the Heavenly Demon Hall who had been counting down to end the tournament.

“Aren’t you going to continue counting?”

The martial artist, who had been staring in a daze, finally came to his senses and resumed the count.

“Three, two, one. The fishing tournament has ended.”

The martial artist loudly announced the winner. There was no need to measure its size.

“The winner of the First Heavenly Demon Cup Fishing Tournament is the Young Cult Leader!”

They had also decided to give Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng to the person who caught the most fish, but the winner of the fishing tournament was, after all, determined by who caught the largest fish. And the fish that had led to victory was none other than a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp.

Geom Mugeuk held the inner core of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp high and shouted,

“I won!”

He did not know how the bet with his father would turn out, but for now, Geom Mugeuk was happy. It wasn’t just any fish, but a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp that had fallen into his arms and given him victory, so of course he ought to be happy.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk received a prize even greater than Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng.

Geom Ujin burst into loud laughter. It was the first time he had ever seen his father laugh so loudly. It was simply pure laughter, carrying no intent whatsoever.

When his father laughed, the Demon Venerables also laughed loudly with him. The laughter came naturally. As they laughed together with the Cult Leader, the Demon Venerables thought,

It was only natural that their connection with this young Young Cult Leader had changed them. He was someone born under such a grand fate—how could they not be influenced?

It was also the first time the Cult Leader and the Demon Venerables had all burst into such hearty laughter together.

When the laughter echoing along the riverbank subsided, Geom Mugeuk asked his father for permission.

“May I keep this inner core?”

“You caught it, so of course it is yours.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and checked once more.

“You truly won’t feel disappointed if I don’t give it to you, Father? ‘This is a tournament I hosted, so hand it over, you brat!’ You would even have a solid justification.”

A faint smile formed on Geom Ujin’s lips.

“This is a tournament I hosted, so hand it over, you brat!”

“Now you truly sound like the Martial Cult Leader.”

After playing along with his son’s joke, Geom Ujin spoke calmly.

“I cannot say for other spirit medicines, but there is no martial artist in this world who would call you an undutiful son for not giving away the inner core of a Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp.”

Geom Ujin, and of course the Demon Venerables as well, believed that treasures and precious spirit medicines like this had destined owners. They also believed that coveting a treasure that was not meant for you would instead bring harm.

“Consume it at once. I will stand guard for you.”

It was the most exalted protection befitting the most precious of spirit medicines.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk said something no one had expected. The day’s surprises were not yet over.

“I will not be consuming this. There is someone I intend to give it to.”

He wasn’t going to consume the inner core of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp? Just who was he planning to give it to?

Under everyone’s puzzled gazes, Geom Mugeuk held out the inner core to one person present.

“Take it.”

The person Geom Mugeuk offered the inner core to was none other than Geom Muyang. Of all the people there, Geom Muyang himself was the most surprised.

“You’re giving that to me?”

When Geom Mugeuk nodded, Geom Muyang widened his eyes in astonishment and asked,

“Why?”

“Why else? Because you’re my brother.”

Geom Muyang refused at once.

“No.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Why?”

Geom Muyang’s reason was the same as the answer Geom Mugeuk had given.

“Because I’m your brother.”

Geom Muyang had absolutely no intention of accepting it.

“You consume it. This Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp came to you.”

“It came to me so I could give it to you.”

“What nonsense!”

Geom Mugeuk had expected in advance that his brother would refuse. But he had a trump card that could defeat that stubborn brother of his.

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father with eyes asking for help.

Geom Ujin had an idea of why Geom Mugeuk wanted to give that precious inner core to Geom Muyang.

After being lost in thought for a moment, Geom Ujin said to Geom Muyang,

“Muyang.”

“Yes.”

“Consume it.”

“Father!”

For the first time since he was born, Geom Muyang refused his father’s command.

“I don’t want to.”

As if understanding his son’s feelings, Geom Ujin spoke again in a calm tone.

“Consume it.”

Geom Muyang could no longer defy an order repeated twice. He could not continue refusing the Cult Leader’s command in a place where all the Demon Venerables were watching.

“Yes, Father.”

Geom Muyang accepted the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp that Geom Mugeuk held out. The brothers exchanged their feelings through their gazes.

You bastard! Are you really going to do this?

Save that look for when someone tries to steal the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp inner core from you.

The Demon Venerables were witnessing with their own eyes an unbelievable scene: Geom Mugeuk yielding the inner core of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp to his brother. It was also a moment that reaffirmed just how vast Geom Mugeuk’s vessel was.

“Then I will consume it.”

Geom Muyang sat cross-legged on the spot and consumed the inner core.

The inner core of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp melted in his mouth. It was the sweetest taste he had ever put in his mouth in his entire life.

An immense internal force began to circulate through his whole body, and Geom Muyang focused on circulating his energy and regulating his breath.

Geom Ujin placed a hand on his back and infused a stream of internal force. He was helping him absorb all the energy of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp.

Geom Mugeuk silently watched the scene. Then his eyes met those of the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon standing across from him. The two eyes behind the mask were smiling brightly.

Splendid, Young Cult Leader.

Beside him, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon nodded, and the One Flower Sword Venerable smiled beautifully.

The Drunken Demon filled the Ice Palace Holy Cup with liquor, raised it as if offering a toast, and emptied the cup.

The Poison King watched Geom Ujin and Geom Muyang without letting his attention wander elsewhere, and the Demon Buddha also looked at Geom Muyang with a pleased smile.

The Fist Demon clenched both of his enormous fists, and the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable bowed his head in respect.

At last, all the energy melted into his body. With Geom Ujin’s help, Geom Muyang absorbed every last bit of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp’s energy.

Now his dantian was filled with internal energy of a purity incomparable to before. In terms of internal energy, he had gained strength that would not fall behind anyone except Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Muyang opened his eyes. A clear, deep light in them revealed his achievement. It was truly the moment he had obtained the greatest fateful opportunity a martial artist could attain.

“Thank you, Father.”

After first expressing his gratitude to Geom Ujin, Geom Muyang stood before Geom Mugeuk.

Back when they had agreed to have a succession struggle without killing each other, he had never even dreamed that such a moment would come.

Geom Muyang said to Geom Mugeuk,

“You have given me both of the most precious spirit medicines in the martial world.”

Geom Mugeuk had also once put three drops of Kongqing Stone Milk into his mouth.

“You really are insane.”

Today, one more sentence was added to that.

“You’re the most splendidly insane man I know.”

It was the highest praise Geom Muyang could give his younger brother.

“You refused the Kongqing Stone Milk, and you refused the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp too. Don’t forget that you’re no less insane yourself, Brother.”

Geom Muyang smiled brightly, and Geom Mugeuk smiled along with him.

Geom Mugeuk said to Geom Ujin,

“Now then, you should hold the award ceremony. Poison King, come here! We have to receive our Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng.”

Geom Ujin personally presented the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng. First, he bestowed it upon the Poison King, who had caught the most fish.

“Congratulations.”

“Thank you, Cult Leader.”

The Poison King could feel it. The antidote Geom Mugeuk had made for him had taken proper effect. His relationship with the Cult Leader had changed from before, and he sensed that it would continue to change in the future.

Next, Geom Ujin held out the box containing Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng to Geom Mugeuk.

“Congratulations.”

“Thank you, Father.”

Geom Mugeuk held the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng high and shouted,

“I will keep this Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng safe and give it as a wedding gift to whichever of the Demon Venerables gets married first! If you’re going to do it anyway, don’t miss your chance!”

At present, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Drunken Demon were the strongest candidates.

The remaining Demon Venerables also received Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng, and only one person, the Demon Buddha, had not caught a single fish. Returning once more to the demeanor of an enlightened high monk, the Demon Buddha rolled his prayer beads and said,

“As the Buddha taught, one must cast aside greed.”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was not about to let such a perfect opportunity to tease him pass by.

“Demon Buddha cast aside greed, and the fish cast aside Demon Buddha.”

From the little giant’s body, a golden radiance burst forth, wishing for the second tournament.

Once the award ceremony ended as well, what had to come finally arrived.

Geom Muyang took two folded pieces of paper from his breast pocket.

“I will announce the result of Father and Mugeuk’s bet.”

Everyone’s attention focused on whom the two of them had each written down.

Geom Muyang unfolded the paper Geom Mugeuk had written first. After checking the contents, Geom Muyang held it up for everyone to see.

Father.

Surprisingly, Geom Mugeuk had chosen his father.

Geom Ujin asked with a curious expression,

“Why did you write my name?”

Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“I expected that you would write your own name, Father, and win. Since I also wrote your name, the bet would have ended in a draw.”

“Why did you think I would win? When you and I had a fishing wager before, you were the one who won.”

It was a question that meant, Wouldn’t it have been better to write your own name and win yourself?

“Because things are different now than they were then.”

The Demon Buddha had said it. He himself was not as desperate as his father. The Extreme Evil Smiling Demon had also advised that if it were him, he would have chosen his father.

“Isn’t it something Father has long yearned for? I thought I couldn’t win.”

Geom Mugeuk added with a smile.

“I meant for both Father and me to guess correctly and end in a draw, but we both got it wrong and drew that way instead.”

At that, Geom Ujin asked with an unreadable expression.

“Why do you think it was a draw?”

“Father, you must have written your own name too, didn’t you? Surely not?”

Geom Muyang unfolded the paper Geom Ujin had written on.

After checking what was written there, Geom Muyang’s expression flinched. Then he held it up for everyone to see.

Gasps burst out from all directions. The name written on the paper was—

Geom Mugeuk

Astonishingly, Geom Ujin had written his son’s name. The son had written his father’s name, and the father had written his son’s.

“I won the wager.”

Geom Mugeuk asked his father in surprise.

“Why did you choose me?”

“Because I thought you would win.”

“Why? Don’t tell me that Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp earlier was one you released, Father. How many are you secretly raising in the Heavenly Demon Treasury?”

At the absurd joke, the corners of Geom Ujin’s mouth lifted slightly. The reason he had written his son’s name was the same reason his son had written his.

“Because you will always accomplish what you set out to do.”

“!”

“I, too, thought you would write your own name and certainly win.”

Geom Ujin and Geom Mugeuk had thought exactly the same thing.

Geom Mugeuk could not understand it.

If it had been the old man who sent the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp, then he surely would have foreseen this result as well.

Did he want Father to win?

‘Was it the unification of the martial world you wanted?’

That didn’t seem likely. Or had it truly not been the old man, but his own turbulent fate that had sent him the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp?

“I have lost.”

Geom Mugeuk admitted defeat. The Demon Venerables looked at him. No matter how outstanding Geom Mugeuk was, there was nothing to be done about this.

Hyeolcheon Doma spoke as if consoling Geom Mugeuk.

“This is the will of heaven.”

“If I’d known I was going to lose like this, I should have written your name, Elder, and at least made a show of it.”

Chwima poured liquor into the Ice Palace Sacred Cup and held it out.

“Drink a cup of defeat wine.”

Geom Mugeuk drank it down.

“Bitter. So bitter!”

Geugak Soma said calmly,

“I will attend you.”

It was his resolve that, if Geom Mugeuk stood at the vanguard of the war, he would support him. They were words more reassuring than anything else in the world.

“Thank you, Lord Soma.”

Before Geom Ujin stated his wish, he asked Geom Mugeuk,

“If you had won, what wish would you have made?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk answered.

“There was something I very much wanted to hear from Father. I probably would have asked you that.”

Geom Ujin gazed at his son in silence. Yes. Even if he had won the wager, this was not a son who would wish for him to abandon his dream.

At last, Geom Ujin opened his mouth.

“My wish is……”

Stand at the vanguard of the Demonic-Orthodox Great War!

Everyone thought that wish would come. But instead, a wish flowed out that not a single person present had imagined. It was the very wish Geom Mugeuk had made to his father after winning the Divine-Demon Contest on the first day of his return.

“For the two of us to go hunting together.”

Chapter 860: An Even Tougher Fight to Return It
-----------------------------------------------
He could have gone without it being a wish.

If Father said the word—“Let’s go”—with just that single sentence, he could go tomorrow, the day after, every single day. It was merely that kind of thing.

He could hunt all year round. But Father had wished for this.

*I want to go hunting with you. Just the two of us.*

Was it because those words were so unexpected?

The moment he heard them, an indescribable surge of emotion welled up from the deepest part of Geom Mugeuk’s chest.

Since that emotion could not be concealed, the Demonic Lords who were watching Geom Mugeuk all felt that surge together. They all knew how hard Geom Mugeuk had worked for the Cult Leader, so they shared in that great joy.

Geom Mugeuk broke into a broad smile and spoke. He said exactly what his father used to say long ago. Because back then, Father had set the promise without a thought for his son’s circumstances.

“Departure is tomorrow morning!”

At that answer—so characteristic of Geom Mugeuk—the Demonic Lords laughed. Who in this world could say such a thing to the Cult Leader? It was quite amusing to see.

A smirk formed at Geom Ujin’s lips.

Geom Mugeuk had seen his father laugh heartily today, and now this smirk too. That was enough. He had seen all of Father’s smiles today.

“Very well.”

Geom Ujin accepted readily.

*Thank you, Father.*

Geom Mugeuk did not let the fishing tournament’s proceedings slip from his grasp until the very end.

“Now, now, before we conclude the fishing tournament, the Cult Leader has a few words.”

In truth, today’s tournament was a gathering for himself and his father, but also for his father and the Demonic Lords.

Geom Ujin looked around at the Demonic Lords. He met each of their gazes one by one, and his gaze was softer than ever before.

Especially the Poison King, the Drunken Demon, and the Soul-Seizing Demonic Lord were deeply moved by the Cult Leader at today’s tournament. Their hearts had been stirred by the small puddle, the question of whether the wine was all right, and the miraculous elixir bestowed only upon them.

Especially since those things came from someone who seemed like he would never do such things, the impact was far greater.

“I enjoyed today. If there is another opportunity, let us have such a gathering again. You have all worked hard.”

At the brief greeting, the Demonic Lords all cupped their fists and bowed.

“We enjoyed it as well, Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke in a loud voice.

“Then with this, I declare the first Heavenly Demon Cup Fishing Tournament concluded!”

And so, with unexpected results, the fishing tournament came to an end.

Geom Muyang packed up Geom Ujin’s fishing rod, and the Demonic Lords also returned to their spots to collect their rods. The fish they had caught were released back into the river.

Geom Ujin left that place with Geom Muyang.

The Demonic Lords held their fishing rods or slung them over their shoulders, then lined up behind him and walked. They could have returned slowly, or Hyeolcheon Doma and Ilhwa Geomjon could have gone for a stroll.

But they all followed the Cult Leader. The Demonic Lords felt deep respect for the Cult Leader at the sight of his refusal to force war upon Geom Mugeuk.

Of course, it was not all good for Geom Mugeuk. Because his father was such a man, at a single word of “Now is the time,” they would all leap onto the battlefield without hesitation.

Geom Mugeuk shouted from behind.

“Now, now, if anyone wants to ask the winner today about the secret to fishing, feel free to ask. Don’t you want to know the secret to catching a Ten Thousand Year Flame Carp? Honestly, aren’t you curious about the thrill in your hands when you hook one? No, where are you all going in such a hurry? Stay and hang out with me for a bit! You’re all free anyway!”

But none of the Demonic Lords looked back.

And so, his father and the Demonic Lords grew distant.

Geom Mugeuk watched until they disappeared from sight.

The sight of eight Demonic Lords following behind his father. That rear view of him walking with wings spread to perfection. It was also the sight he had most wanted to see when he proposed this fishing tournament to his father.

*You truly did well, Father.*

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned back to the river.

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes, having returned to their original state before he knew it, were calm and settled.

Geom Mugeuk stood there alone until the sun fully set and darkness fell.

***

The next dawn, Geom Mugeuk left the cult together with his father.

Geom Mugeuk came shouldering a large leather bag, just like when they had first gone hunting together. No, there was even more luggage than back then.

“You know, right? How many necessary things are packed in here.”

Geom Ujin climbed the mountain with his hands behind his back, and Geom Mugeuk followed behind him.

He had hunted with his father many times. There had also been hunts with his older brother, the three of them together.

But today’s hunt was more special than any other time.

“Uncle Hwi isn’t here today either, I see.”

Geom Mugeuk had now reached a level where he could clearly detect even Hwi’s concealment.

Geom Ujin nodded. He truly had not brought even Hwi.

“So it really is just Father and me, the two of us.”

It was truly an opportunity to share the heart-to-heart talks he had kept buried deep within his heart. Father had likely left Hwi behind for that very purpose from the start.

“Even if this tight-lipped son of yours talks a bit too much today, please understand!”

He said that, but Geom Mugeuk simply quietly followed his father’s back.

Sunlight shattering through the branches, the sound of grass crushed underfoot, the murmur of water flowing in the valley below, the scent of pine sap, the smell of earth borne on the wind, a squirrel climbing a tree with rustling sounds.

Geom Mugeuk savored the joy of walking through the mountain with his father. How long had they wandered the mountains like that?

“Tiger tracks.”

It was quite a large tiger.

“Come, this way.”

If he used martial arts to chase it, he would find it in an instant, but his father did not. He did not release his inner energy to figure out where the animal was either.

His father purely enjoyed the hunt itself. Yesterday he had been an angler; today he had become a hunter.

Geom Mugeuk felt that his father’s heart was the same as his own when he had first regressed and asked his father to go hunting.

He just wanted to become close with his father, purely and simply.

Because he wanted to spend time with his son.

That was why, as if by some unspoken promise, they did not speak of the unification of the martial world.

“It seems it discovered prey here and ran.”

His father could tell what situation the tiger had been in just from the tracks.

How long had they tracked it like that?

They spotted the tiger beneath a rock in the distance.

Geom Mugeuk’s bowstring was pulled taut. But in the end, he did not shoot the arrow. In that time, the tiger concealed itself somewhere.

“Why didn’t you shoot?”

Geom Mugeuk gave an absurd reason.
