“It looked like the cub of that tiger Brother saved back then.”

At that time, Geom Muyang had been unable to release his bowstring upon seeing the cubs around the tiger.

“If it had been the firstborn, I would have shot, but this one is clearly the second child.”

At that silly joke, Geom Ujin moved toward the sound of water, speechless.

The two slaked their thirst with the flowing stream water.

“Since we lost it anyway, shall we rest a moment?”

The two sat on a rock beside the stream. The sound of flowing water was so clear and cheerful that simply sitting there brightened their moods.

Then Geom Ujin momentarily turned from hunter to angler.

“Now that you’ve caught a Ten Thousand Year Flame Carp, how was the thrill?”

It was a question asked purely out of curiosity.

“It was incredible. The moment it bit, I knew it was a huge catch. Lord Hyeolcheon Doma said so, didn’t he? That when you’re fishing, there are times you feel as if the whole world is being reeled in by you. That is exactly how it felt when I pulled up the Ten Thousand Year Flame Carp.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and subtly boasted.

“Honestly, you’re jealous, aren’t you?”

Geom Ujin could not say he wasn’t. Because he coveted the thrill of catching it even more than the Ten Thousand Year Flame Carp’s inner elixir.

“Lord Hyeolcheon Doma and Lord Mabul will likely have fish swimming before their eyes for several days.”

Hyeolcheon Doma had finished second in the number of fish and third in the largest catch. If he had also taken second in the largest catch category, he would have been truly disappointed.

Mabul was probably at that riverside even today, fishing and scolding innocent fish with, “You should have bitten like this yesterday!”

In fact, unknown to Geom Mugeuk, Geom Ujin had also gritted his teeth to take first place. It was only because he had fortunately won the bet that his regret at not taking first place had faded; his desire for first place was stronger than anyone’s.

“Host the second tournament next year as well.”

“There won’t be any Ten Thousand Year Snow Ginseng left.”

“We wagered Ten Thousand Year Snow Ginseng for the first tournament, so we should bet something else for the second. Don’t we have plenty of martial arts secret manuals in the Heavenly Demon Library?”

Geom Mugeuk naturally brought up the subject of the Demonic Lords.

“I felt anew just how much the Demonic Lords like Father.”

“Not you?”

“No, they like Father. You can feel it when you look at them.”

It was not something said merely to please Father’s ears, but words spoken from the heart.

“Thinking about it, wasn’t it because Father was here that I could run wild to my heart’s content? It is also thanks to that the Demonic Lords indulged me no matter how much I acted up. Thanks to Father, I have had my fill of a fox borrowing the tiger’s might.”

Geom Ujin stared at his son and asked.

“But why are you trying to give up this position?”

As expected, Father knew. Why he had given the Ten Thousand Year Flame Carp’s inner elixir to his brother.

It was because he intended to hand over the position of Young Cult Leader to his brother. No matter how much he thought about it, he did not have the confidence to live his whole life guarding the Taesa.

He had felt it keenly when he served as temporary Cult Leader. That path was not his path. He wanted to roam the Central Plains freely.

“It is not because I dislike it.”

If he disliked the position his father had protected his whole life, there could be no greater disrespect to his father.

“It is because Brother suits it more.”

Geom Ujin quietly stared at Geom Mugeuk. Though he was his own child, he thought him truly inscrutable. To give up this position so easily was by no means an easy thing.

“Of course, I am quite responsible myself, so I would lead the cult well. I am prone to worry and prepare in advance, so I would overcome crises too.

But if someone asked me if I would be happy doing so, I do not think I could answer yes. On the other hand, if you asked Brother, he would answer that he is happy. You can tell just by watching him handle the duties he has taken on within the cult now.”

Brother handled affairs so well you could almost call it perfect.

Geom Ujin silently nodded as if agreeing with that opinion.

“Brother becomes Cult Leader, and I live freely. Both Brother and I will be happy. Rather than a choice that makes us both miserable, it is a choice that makes us both happy.”

Geom Ujin neither denied nor refuted Geom Mugeuk’s words. As Geom Mugeuk said, his eldest son was the one who suited the Cult Leader’s position better.

The problem was this.

“Even if I permit it, Yang will never accept your will. I can feed him the Ten Thousand Year Flame Carp, but I cannot seat him in your position.”

Father knew his eldest son better than anyone. Would his brother, who had lost the power struggle, sit in a position yielded by his younger brother? His brother’s pride would never accept it.

“Father, how can I convince Brother? Should I just run away? What could you do if I ran away?”

Of course, he was not speaking from the heart.

If he did, his brother would live an unhappy life even after ascending to the Cult Leader’s position.

“I fought so hard to snatch that position in the succession struggle, and now I have to fight an even harder battle to give it back!”

Geom Ujin, who had been quietly watching his son’s theatrics, said abruptly.

“You have the confidence to convince your brother, don’t you?”

How quick-witted he was.

“Yes, actually, I have the confidence to convince him.”

“Why are you so confident?”

“Because I know Brother’s weakness.”

At the word “weakness,” Geom Ujin started. And that weakness was something Geom Ujin had never imagined.

“Because Brother truly loves the cult.”

“!”

“In that regard, Brother is exactly like Father.”

After a brief pause, Geom Ujin asked.

“If you could hand over that position as you wish, what do you intend to do?”

“I plan to travel the Central Plains with Ian. I promised to take him out long ago.”

Famous sights, all of them; scenic spots, all of them—we said we would see them all. Famous dishes, I promised to let him taste them all.

“Now I must keep my promise.”

There was another purpose as well.

“This time, I will help Ian find his true self.”

Though it might have been incomprehensible, Geom Ujin nodded.

Having lived as a bodyguard for many years, there would inevitably be parts of him bound to his son. He was likely saying he would break those bonds.

“If I break the barrier that binds Ian, Ian might leave me.”

Geom Ujin said something cryptic.

“The opposite could also happen.”

“The opposite?”

“That child might hold onto you. As a man.”

“He doesn’t have that kind of personality.”

At that, Geom Ujin smiled a strange smile. An expression asking if that was really so, mixed with an expression asking if he even knew anything about women.

A conversation about women with Father? With none other than Father? This was more precious than a Ten Thousand Year Flame Carp.

Geom Mugeuk was thrilled. Seeing this side of his father, and having such a conversation, was unfamiliar yet wonderful.

“Perhaps when you were young?”

Perhaps guessing what words would come out, Father rose from his seat.

“Come, let’s go.”

“Acting like this makes it even more suspicious!”

Geom Mugeuk raised his voice as he followed.

“Is it because I inherited Father’s blood that I want to form a band of the Four Handsome Men and leave the cult?”

***

A bonfire blazed on the mountainside where the sun had set early.

They had come to hunt in earnest, but the two failed to catch a single beast. They were capable of catching every beast on the mountain if they put their minds to it, but today’s hunt had come up empty.

For some reason, Geom Mugeuk did not want to see blood today, and Father too—perhaps reading his son’s heart—did not catch any beasts.

“I prepared for this. A feast for failed hunters.”

Geom Mugeuk took food from his leather bag. Though they were dried foods, the superb side dishes—and Father’s favorite liquor—were far too excellent to be called hastily packed.

“Please have a cup.”

Geom Ujin received the liquor and then filled his son’s cup.

The two emptied their cups pleasantly.

Geom Mugeuk felt comfortable and happy in this moment alone with his father. Yet he also felt a strange tension. It was a day when anything might be said.

After drinking several cups like that, Geom Ujin asked something as if he had just remembered.

“If you had won the bet.........”

His son had said there was something he wanted to ask him.

“What was it you wanted to ask?”

Chapter 861: Do You Think You Can Escape So Easily
--------------------------------------------------
There were two questions he wanted to ask his father.

I’m curious what Father hates most of all.

But in this pleasant atmosphere, drinking and talking alone with his father, he did not want to ask that question. So he asked the other one.

“I want to know about Mother.”

His father nodded, as if he had expected as much.

But then words Geom Mugeuk had not anticipated flowed out.

“Do not try to learn more.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father in surprise. His father’s face flickered red in the campfirelight. It was weighty advice from a father to his son.

“The dead do not remain only in the past.”

How could Geom Mugeuk not understand what he meant? The more he learned, the more he would miss her.

In his life before regression, he had felt the sorrow of losing everyone more keenly than anyone. Those deaths had ruled him for his entire life.

“Leave it as it is now, as a vague longing.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. His mother’s briefly shining life still shone forever in his father’s heart.

A love that had lost its place to go remained in his father’s heart under the name of longing.

“Isn’t it hard, remembering her all alone?”

At those unexpected words, Geom Ujin flinched and looked at his son.

Given his father’s personality, he surely had never spoken of his mother to anyone.

Not to Hwi, not to the Fist Demon, not to Sama Myeong. His father would not have told anyone. He must have endured the pain of that loss alone. This blunt, taciturn man surely had.

In his life before regression, Geom Mugeuk too had borne all that pain alone. Looking back, that never had a good influence on anyone’s life.

I miss them. I long for them. I am sad. I am angry.

If only one could utter even a single word of it to someone, the heart would ease a little.

As he himself had been before his regression, his father too had never had such an opportunity. That saddened him.

“I want to rejoice together with Father, long for her together, and grieve together.”

Geom Ujin was staring at his son with trembling eyes. He had not known his son had asked for such a reason.

“Father, I want to hear about Mother, who liked floral patterns.”

Geom Ujin drank in silence. Looking down at his empty cup, he then raised his gaze to the night sky.

Geom Mugeuk asked no more and quietly refilled his father’s cup.

At length, after a long silence, Geom Ujin opened his mouth.

“I met your mother when I was about your age.”

It was a story his father had buried in his heart for a very long time.

“She was a woman of the orthodox faction.”

Geom Mugeuk understood why his father had said he resembled his mother. He also understood why he was walking the path of a Demon Hero so naturally. It was because her blood flowed in him.

It seemed his father might tell him more, but after finishing his drink, he lay back down. Now that he had actually tried to speak, it seemed he could not hide the emotion welling up within him.

“Let’s sleep.”

Whenever he looked at his father’s back, many thoughts came to him. But the back he looked at tonight felt different from any he had seen before.

Geom Mugeuk asked no more. If he pressed, his father would probably say a few more words, but he did not.

Even without hearing it, he could imagine how arduous the process must have been for a woman of the orthodox faction to become the Demon Empress. There must have been many things he could not easily speak of to his child, enough to fill several books if written down.

What mattered was that his father and mother had overcome that entire difficult process, fulfilled their love, and given birth to his older brother and himself.

“You went through so much, Father. Thank you for bringing me into this world.”

For a moment, he felt Geom Ujin’s back tremble faintly. But in the end, his father said nothing.

Geom Mugeuk also lay down and looked up at the night sky.

‘Father, someday, please tell me Mother’s story.’

***

“You’ve come?”

The Demon Buddha, seated in the dharma hall chanting sutras, spoke without looking back.

The person who had entered the dharma hall was Geom Mugeuk. The Demon Buddha greeted him calmly, as though he had expected Geom Mugeuk to come find him as soon as he descended the mountain.

“You came down sooner than I expected.”

“I missed you so much I could hardly bear it, Demon Buddha.”

The Demon Buddha, who had been facing the Blood Buddha, turned and sat facing Geom Mugeuk.

“As always, I suppose you’ve come because you want something from me.”

He had guessed why Geom Mugeuk had come to see him.

“The Cult Leader would not have permitted it, would he?”

The Demon Buddha had read Geom Mugeuk’s thoughts the moment he gave the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp’s inner core to Geom Muyang.

“He has not permitted it yet. He is convinced my brother will not listen to me.”

The Demon Buddha shared his father’s view.

“He is seeing it correctly.”

“That is why I came to seek an answer from you, Demon Buddha.”

“Then you have come in vain.”

“What do you mean?”

The Demon Buddha spoke with certainty. If it was the Geom Muyang he knew—

“You know what sort of person your brother is, do you not? He will never accept that position.”

The Demon Buddha asked with an expression of incomprehension.

“Why are you suddenly trying to run away? Did that man Ak Gunhak put the wind in your heart?”

“That man has now made up his mind to settle down.”

“Then why?”

It was not a thought that had suddenly appeared. From long ago, his heart had been split in half. He had thought of becoming a truly extraordinary Cult Leader, and he had also thought of giving the Cult Leader’s seat to his brother and leaving it all behind.

But what had influenced his heart this time was this.

After Hwa Mugi died, he obtained the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp’s inner core?

Geom Mugeuk wondered if this might be fate bestowed by heaven. At the very least, it could not be a gift heaven had sent because his internal energy was lacking.

“Is it because of the Eldest Young Master?”

Geom Mugeuk could not answer that it was not.

“Listen, Young Cult Leader.”

“Yes, Demon Buddha.”

“I know well what sort of person you are.”

That was why he could predict it. This was a guess close to certainty.

“If it is you, you may find a way to persuade the Eldest Young Master. Perhaps you may even succeed.”

The Demon Buddha, rather, feared that success.

“I hope you do not.”

Because he could feel that the words were sincere, Geom Mugeuk asked him carefully.

“May I ask the reason?”

The Demon Buddha’s gaze sank coldly.

“Because if that happens, the Eldest Young Master’s life will become unhappy.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk flinched in shock and could say nothing. Because the Demon Buddha knew his brother better than anyone, these were not words he could take lightly.

“After the Eldest Young Master ascends to the Cult Leader’s seat, he will have to fight endlessly with himself. Whether he is truly someone suited to that position. Moreover, he will endlessly suspect others. Whether the Demon Venerables truly follow him. Whether they are not reminiscing about you.”

“Would you not be at my brother’s side, Demon Buddha?”

The Demon Buddha slowly shook his head.

“My presence would make things even harder for the Eldest Young Master. I was the one who shared his defeat in the succession struggle. Every time he sees me, he will be reminded of the ghosts of the past. Listen, Young Cult Leader. The human heart is not so strong. Not everyone can have a heart like yours.”

Geom Mugeuk could give no answer. He had vaguely thought that if it was his brother, he would do well, and that if the Demon Buddha helped him, it would be all right. But the Demon Buddha was seeing the outcome of this matter far more realistically and accurately.

“Having intervened so deeply in everyone’s lives, did you think you could run away so easily?”

Feeling that the Demon Buddha’s words were right, Geom Mugeuk could offer no excuse.

After being lost in thought for a while, Geom Mugeuk readily acknowledged his mistake.

“My thinking was shallow.”

Because he had focused only on whether his brother would accept or not, he had not thought deeply about his brother’s life after accepting. The brother who had been in the succession struggle back then no longer existed now.

“It was also a decision that wronged those who have trusted and followed me until now.”

The stiffness in the Demon Buddha’s expression eased. Yes. Because Geom Mugeuk acknowledged what needed to be acknowledged and did not cling to useless stubbornness, he could offer him advice that came from the heart.

“It is not that there is no way at all.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Demon Buddha with shining eyes.

“Please tell me.”

As though he had already pondered it, the Demon Buddha spoke calmly.

“If there is a way, there is only one.”

“What is it?”

One word flowed once more from the Demon Buddha’s mouth.

“Legitimacy.”

When Geom Mugeuk had previously asked what he should do to prevent his father from unifying the murim, the Demon Buddha had given him the same advice.

“The Eldest Young Master needs legitimacy to inherit the Cult Leader’s seat.”

“Is there a way to create that legitimacy?”

He had asked without expecting much, but surprisingly, the Demon Buddha had an answer.

“There is. But it requires a condition.”

That condition was something Geom Mugeuk had not expected.

“You must become the Cult Leader.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk waited for the Demon Buddha’s next words with a startled expression.

“If you hand over that seat without ever becoming the Cult Leader, that is merely yielding it. But if you become the Cult Leader, lead our cult, and then appoint the Eldest Young Master as your successor, that will be a formal succession.”

The Demon Buddha added calmly.

“Try leading our cult in your own way. While doing so, continue to entrust the Eldest Young Master with important duties—matters only the Cult Leader can entrust.”

His brother would probably handle those duties better than anyone.

“Through those matters, the Eldest Young Master will prove for himself what outstanding abilities he possesses. That will become his legitimacy to become Cult Leader.”

Because it was a matter related to his brother, the Demon Buddha advised him more earnestly than ever.

“Try it this way, try it that way. If it does not seem right, seek another method. As the years pass, you, the Eldest Young Master, and I—everyone’s thoughts will change.

Perhaps the worries you have now will be nothing by then. Perhaps you may be telling me, ‘If I had yielded the Cult Leader’s seat back then, I truly would have regretted it.’”

The last thing he said was this.

“There is a proper time even to run away. When fate opens the back door just a crack, that is when you run.”

Listening in silence, Geom Mugeuk recalled the conversation he had once had with the old man.

—You asked me how I would live, but my life from now on may disappoint you. I have used up almost all the admirable things I can do.

—There is no need to worry. The people around you, whom you made admirable, will lead you to become even more admirable.

Geom Mugeuk sprang to his feet and bowed respectfully to the Demon Buddha.

“You are right. Thank you.”

With wise and grateful teaching, the Demon Buddha had led him away from the wrong path and back to the right one.

“You have set both my life and my brother’s life straight. Today, you have landed two big catches.”

To that, he added one remark he simply could not hold back.

“If only you had caught them like this that day.”

The Demon Buddha, who had been wearing the smile of an enlightened monk, gently closed his eyes and turned his prayer beads with enough force to shatter them.

“When did they say the next tournament would be held?”

***

“Now, here is the list of supplies we need.”

Geom Muyang held out the documents to Gwak Yeong.

The place where they stood was Maga Village. Reconstruction work was in full swing there, and Geom Muyang was the overall supervisor of the project.

Gwak Yeong had come there on an errand from the Iron Workshop.

“Then I will see you again.”

Gwak Yeong bowed politely and turned around. Out of gladness, she wanted to make even a joking remark, but now that they had met at a place where he was working, she could not dare open her mouth.

Then Geom Muyang’s voice came from behind her.

“When will you give me the treasured sword you promised?”

Gwak Yeong turned back with a smile.

“At this rate, by the time I bring you the treasured sword I have made, you may have already forgotten me, Eldest Young Master.”

It was half a joke, but it was true that she worried about when on earth she would reach a position where she could make a treasured sword.

Even now, she was only making iron swords at the Iron Workshop. No matter how skilled a craftsman she was, her history of having surrendered from the Murim Alliance was holding her tightly by the ankle.

“Why do you think I would forget you?”

“Because you are a noble and busy person, Eldest Young Master.”

“Could it not be the opposite? You may become a famous artisan of the Iron Workshop, while I remain an unremarkable Eldest Young Master.”

Gwak Yeong smiled.

“Seeing you engage in self-deprecation that doesn’t suit you, it seems you need a friend to drink with. If you feel like drinking later, come to the Iron Workshop. I’ll buy you a cup!”

After bowing, Gwak Yeong had walked some distance away when she looked back at Geom Muyang and said,

“Of all the demonic practitioners I’ve seen, you are the smartest. Don’t forget that.”

After rattling off the words as if pouring them out, Gwak Yeong bounded away into the distance.

Geom Muyang, who had been watching her, turned around, only to find Geom Mugeuk standing behind him.

Seeing Geom Muyang start in surprise, Geom Mugeuk put on a displeased expression.

“Brother is the smartest? You saw me and still said something like that!”

If he responded to that, it was obvious Mugeuk would tease him about Gwak Yeong, so Geom Muyang changed the subject.

“When did you come down the mountain?”

“Earlier.”

“And Father?”

“He blamed me for coming up empty-handed on the hunt. Said next time the three of us should go together, with you too.”

Geom Muyang struggled to hide the smile that was about to form.

“Why did you come here?”

“I missed you so much I couldn’t stand it.”

“You say that to everyone you meet, don’t you?”

“You really are smart. Not as smart as me, though.”

Geom Muyang stared hard at Geom Mugeuk, then spoke seriously.

“I know what you’re thinking. Don’t even dream of it.”

As if he was not merely saying it, Geom Muyang’s eyes gleamed sharply, and firm resolve filled his tightly pressed lips.

“I shouldn’t have taken the inner core until the very end.”

He had taken it in a daze because of their father, but afterward, he regretted it.

“I know what you’re misunderstanding. Sorry, but you’re the one who shouldn’t dream of it. Do you know what I went through to claim this position?”

Geom Muyang looked at Geom Mugeuk suspiciously.

“This position I stole from you—I’m going to keep it until I die of old age. Don’t come begging me later to let you try it for just one day. Ah, I’ll let you sit in the Taishi chair once.”

Even at his younger brother’s joke, the suspicion still did not leave Geom Muyang’s stiff face.

“Then why did you give me the inner core?”

“I gave it to you for practical reasons. It’s more effective for you to have it than me. If the benefits gained from taking the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp’s inner core amount to ten, then, judging coldly, it would only be worth about two or three to me. But if it’s you, Brother, you can take all ten.”

It was not wrong. To begin with, Geom Mugeuk’s internal energy was so pure and vast that even adding the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp’s inner core would not produce a particularly noticeable effect. Geom Muyang, however, was different.

Geom Muyang’s life now could be divided like this: before taking it, and after taking it.

Geom Mugeuk added an emotional reason to that rational calculation.

“You’re the one who’s going to become the cult leader in the future, Brother, so you need to be strong too. That way, wherever I go, I can say, ‘That man is my brother.’ This is because of my own vanity, so don’t be too grateful!”

“You’re certain?”

“I’m certain.”

Only then did Geom Muyang’s expression soften.

Seeing this brother of his, he thought anew that Mabul’s advice had been right.

“I’m sorry, Brother.”

“For what?”

For thinking only of myself and acting selfishly.

“I cursed you again when you weren’t around.”

Geom Muyang laughed as if dumbfounded, and Geom Mugeuk laughed along with him.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the site where construction was in full swing.

“They really are building it quickly.”

Because the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had mobilized an enormous amount of manpower and resources to build it with care, the structure was rising day by day.

Looking up at the building going up on the site of the Pungryu Tavern, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Our proprietor will be pleased.”

Chapter 862: I Didn’t Pack Pajamas
----------------------------------
Geom Ujin remained in the Heavenly Demon Hall until late.

Though it was an hour when he would ordinarily have been asleep, he sat alone in the grand armchair, lost in thought.

Then the doors of the Heavenly Demon Hall opened, and Geom Mugeuk entered.

“Father, I’m here.”

After greeting him loudly, Geom Mugeuk walked down the Path of Blood toward the grand armchair.

Everything looked the same as usual, yet for some reason, tonight both his father and that chair felt different.

Geom Mugeuk stopped below the chair and looked up at his father. Amid the inexplicable tension flowing through the Heavenly Demon Hall at this deep hour of night, he spoke provocatively.

“That seat is mine now.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s declaration-like words, Geom Ujin asked with a sneer,

“Is this rebellion?”

The solemn atmosphere loosened, and the midnight revolt came to an end.

“Of course not. I meant it’ll be mine in about thirty years.”

Geom Ujin could tell that his son had changed his mind about handing over the position of Young Cult Leader.

“Why did you change your mind?”

“When I thought about it, it felt unfair. What’s so lovable about him that I should give such a fine position to my brother?”

After starting with a joke, Geom Mugeuk spoke honestly.

“Demon Buddha scolded me.”

That explanation was enough. Geom Ujin also knew well that the person who understood his eldest son best was Demon Buddha. It seemed Demon Buddha had persuaded him not to do it.

From this, Geom Ujin could tell that Demon Buddha still liked his eldest son.

If he did not, he would have tried by any means to make Muyang the Young Cult Leader.

“Father, you know how much I like to play around, don’t you? If I become Cult Leader, rumors like this will spread through the jianghu. Did you hear? The Demon Cult Leader got drunk and sang and danced. If you don’t want to see that disgrace, please hold on to that seat for a long, long time. I’m sure I’ll grow more sensible when I’m older.”

“How long do you plan to make this father of yours suffer?”

“Until I’m worn out from playing and beg you to please step down.”

Geom Ujin stared quietly at his son, then suddenly said,

“You’ve thought well.”

Because the words were unexpected, Geom Mugeuk was inwardly surprised. Hadn’t his father secretly wanted his brother to become the successor?

He had thought that might be the case, but now his father seemed pleased, as if this had been the right decision.

Father, do you truly want me to become Cult Leader? Even though I am trying to stop your dream by any means I can?

“Demon Buddha is dreaming of a revenge match.”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes fell on the fishing rod propped against the wall behind the grand armchair. Seeing that it had not yet been put away.

“Ah, there was one more person here as well. If I see Demon Buddha, I’ll tell him. That the revenge match may take place in the not-too-distant future. Then, rest well.”

Geom Mugeuk bowed respectfully and left.

Once his son had exited the Heavenly Demon Hall, Geom Ujin turned his head and looked at the moon outside the window. Gazing at the bright, full moon, he spoke to Hwi, who was concealed nearby.

“Bring out a good bottle of wine.”

***

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was reading a book in the courtyard of his residence.

He had planted the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber in the ground and was leaning comfortably against it as he read.

On nights when the moonlight was bright like tonight, he would often come out into the courtyard and read.

Then he sensed someone arrive behind the saber.

Judging by the level of martial arts required to reveal one’s presence only after coming this close—or rather, considering who would come to his residence at this hour—there was only one person it could be.

“You brat, why have you come again at this hour instead of sleeping?”

There was no answer, but the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, still keeping his eyes on the book, asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Did you have a good time hunting with the Cult Leader?”

Whether he was planning some prank or not, no reply came. Regardless, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon simply said what he had to say.

“No matter how clever you are, how could you understand a parent’s heart? The Cult Leader has been holding himself back a great deal, given his nature. You can’t even imagine what his true temperament is like. So be good to your father.”

When there was still no answer, only then did the Blood Heaven Blade Demon raise his head.

“Why are you so quiet, you brat? I’ll say this in advance—if you didn’t bring pajamas, don’t even think about climbing into my bed!”

Then a low voice came from behind the saber.

“I did not bring pajamas.”

In that instant, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was so startled that he sprang to his feet.

“Cult Leader!”

The person who had come was none other than Geom Ujin.

“My apologies for coming at such a late hour. Did I startle you?”

Did he startle him? He was startled enough to wonder whether he had ever been this surprised in recent days. He had never imagined the Cult Leader would come looking for him in the dead of night.

“What brings you here?”

Worried that something might have happened, he asked, but Geom Ujin held up the bottle of wine he had brought.

“I came to have a drink with you.”

“As it happens, I was just thinking of wine myself. Please come inside.”

“The night breeze is pleasant. Let us drink here.”

“Then please wait a moment.”

“Take your time.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon went inside and gathered wine cups and a simple snack to go with the wine. As he was about to come back out, his gaze caught the scene beyond the window.

Geom Ujin was sitting with his back against the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber, looking up at the moon.

Over that sight overlapped the image of Geom Ujin in his youth, laughing heartily as he leaned against the great saber and drank.

In the past, when they drank, Geom Ujin had liked to lean against his saber while doing so. He said it somehow put him in a good mood.

Was it because of Geom Mugeuk? These days, whenever he looked at the Cult Leader, old memories kept coming back.

Geom Ujin, who had been looking up at the moon, turned this way. Their eyes met, and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon grinned before stepping outside.

“There is nothing much suitable for snacks.”

“It is fine.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon poured the wine first, and Geom Ujin, after receiving it, filled the Blade Demon’s cup.

The two emptied their cups together. It had been truly a long time since the two of them had drunk alone like this.

“Did the hunting trip go well?”

“We came down empty-handed.”

A faint smile touched the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s lips. If the two of them had truly set their minds on hunting, the beasts of Mount Daecheon would have been wiped out.

“Did you speak much with your son?”

“What do you mean, my son? He’s that brat of mine.”

At Geom Ujin’s joke, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon laughed.

“He asked about his mother.”

The hand of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, which had been pouring wine into Geom Ujin’s cup, paused for a moment.

“It is about time he did.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon filled Geom Ujin’s cup to the brim.

“He did not ask because he missed her.”

“What do you mean?”

“That insolent brat asked because he was worried I might suffer.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon drank with an expression that said, If it’s Geom Mugeuk, he’d certainly do that and more.

After emptying his cup, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon brought up something he had never once said to the Cult Leader until now.

“When I look at the Young Cult Leader, I sometimes think of the Demon Queen.”

Since the Demon Queen’s death, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had never mentioned her to the Cult Leader. This was the first time.

He believed that the Cult Leader coming to him tonight, and bringing up the Demon Queen first, meant he wanted to speak of her.

Geom Ujin silently nodded.

It was a wordless response, but the Blood Heaven Blade Demon could feel that the Cult Leader missed the Demon Queen.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon knew well how much the Cult Leader had loved her. He had watched it from the very beginning.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon recalled that day again.

I must have this murim.

What had been contained in Geom Ujin’s eyes when he said those words to him, face full of heat, had not been a conqueror’s lust for power. It had been the resolve of a man whose love had just begun.

Over the face of that young Geom Ujin overlapped the Geom Ujin of the present.

“I have confidence that I could bend the world under heaven to my will, yet when it comes to my own child, I have no confidence I can make him follow it.”

“You can make him do as you wish.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon knew Geom Ujin’s heart well.

“You simply do not wish to.”

Holding out his wine cup, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon said,

“Family are one’s enemies. Among them, a child is the greatest enemy of all.”

Geom Ujin laughed, clinked his cup against his, and emptied it.

After ascending to the seat of Cult Leader, he had shown only a strong side all his life. Yet today, he kept reminding the Blood Heaven Blade Demon of his younger days.

That was why what Geom Ujin needed now was likely someone who would stand on his side.

“Summon him tomorrow and scold him like this: You brat, stop running wild like a reckless child and follow your father’s words!”

Geom Ujin laughed pleasantly. It was the sort of smile that, had Geom Mugeuk seen it, he would have said, Please smile like that for your children too.

“He is the sort of fellow who would say, ‘Yes, I will do so.’ That is why I cannot.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon agreed with those words. If he were to simply rebel and stubbornly insist that his own thoughts were right, it would actually be easier to break him.

But Geom Mugeuk held a deep respect for Geom Ujin. That was why this fight was difficult.

It was a difficult fight for the child as well, but for the parent it was even harder. After all, it was a fight waged while carrying the great weakness that no parent can defeat their child.

After they drank several more cups, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon conveyed the words he had been keeping in his heart.

“If a parent is the bow, then a child must be the arrow. Even if that arrow aims in a direction the parent does not want...”

As he filled the cup, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon added,

“When the time comes, you must entrust it to the wind and release the string.”

Geom Ujin silently looked down at his own face reflected in the cup. Whether he had released the string within his heart, no one could know.

And so the two of them shared their cups together until the moon set.

***

Bang! Baaang! Bang!

Fireworks burst in the sky.

At last, the construction of Maga Village had been completed, and the day had come for the residents to enter their own homes.

The people gathered in Maga Village rejoiced. Their old houses had been transformed completely into new ones. In place of collapsed mud walls stood stone walls, and thatched cottages had become tiled-roof houses.

One-story homes had become two stories, and two-story homes had become three-story houses. Moreover, the interiors were filled with furniture better than what they had owned before, and clothing, daily necessities, and even enough food to live on for the time being were piled up in the courtyards.

They had truly never imagined that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult would do this much for them. Beyond saving their lives, they had bestowed upon them a great favor.

“Mother! Is this house ours now?”

“Yes, it’s our house.”

The children rushed excitedly inside, and the women shed tears as they stroked the sturdy pillars. The men looked around their homes and shops with hearts full to bursting.

“Maga Village has come back to life!”

At someone’s cry, everyone shouted in joy.

“Long live the Cult Leader!”

“Long live the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult!”

As much as the joy of receiving new homes, what delighted them was this.

The fact that they had not been abandoned.

Among those rejoicing was Jo Chunbae. He was looking up at his tavern with deep emotion.

The old two-story building had become a magnificent three-story structure. It had been built splendidly, a fine tavern that caught the eye of anyone passing by at a glance. He had never dreamed that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult would build it so wonderfully for him.

Even the kitchen was fully stocked with ingredients, and the storeroom was piled high with fine wine. They had prepared it so he could open for business that very day.

The other houses were the same. The fruit shop was filled with fruit, and the fabric shop was filled with cloth. They had not merely built homes; they had not neglected such consideration either.

Then cheers rose from far away.

Everyone looked in that direction, and the people worthy of those cheers were approaching.

They were Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang. Those following around them were Geom Mugeuk’s bodyguards.

Now, their momentum was in no way inferior even compared to the Cult Leader’s guards. Considering their relatively young ages, it was truly remarkable progress.

Of course, even amid this, Jeok Yeon, the captain of the guards who led them, was driving his subordinates mercilessly. Because the Young Cult Leader was growing stronger far faster than they were.

The people of Maga Village all bowed their heads and greeted them respectfully.

“We greet the Young Cult Leader!”

“We greet the Young Master!”

Geom Mugeuk asked with a bright expression,

“How do you all feel?”

“Wonderful!”

Geom Mugeuk gave the credit for today’s result to his brother.

“The reason Maga Village could be rebuilt so quickly is thanks to the efforts of my elder brother here. My brother sacrificed sleep to oversee the construction.”

The people of Maga Village bowed their heads to Geom Muyang and expressed their gratitude.

“Thank you, Young Master.”

“Thank you! Young Master!”

Geom Mugeuk glanced at Geom Muyang and said,

“Brother, say a few words.”

Geom Muyang declined, saying it was all right, but Geom Mugeuk forced him to do it.

“These are the houses you built for them, aren’t they? You should say something.”

In the end, Geom Muyang stepped forward. He would not have been nervous even before a thousand martial artists, yet for some reason, standing here under the gaze of the ordinary people of Maga Village felt more burdensome.

“We did our best to build it as quickly as possible. In return, we built it sturdy, so please don’t worry. I hope you all live well here.”

It was a brief greeting, but applause and cheers burst out from all sides.

Seeing how delighted the people were, Geom Muyang felt pleased as well. All their efforts, working day and night to finish the construction even a day sooner, had been worthwhile.

As if he had forgotten, Geom Muyang belatedly added one more thing.

“Ah, and the reason this matter proceeded like this is because the Young Cult Leader holds all of you in special regard. Do not forget that.”

Geom Muyang was turning the credit that had come to him back over to his younger brother.

With a shameless expression, Geom Mugeuk waved his hand at the residents of Maga Village.

“You heard him, didn’t you? You mustn’t forget that this is thanks to me!”

The residents laughed good-naturedly. Even if Geom Muyang had not said it, they all already knew.

The elders of Maga Village would often say this: that in the entire history of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, Maga Village was now living in its most blessed era. And they all knew that it was entirely thanks to the Young Cult Leader.

“Young Cult Leader.”

Jo Chunbae, who had been buried in the crowd, came running over belatedly and greeted him.

“Owner, how does it feel?”

“It’s wonderful. I’m so happy.”

Jo Chunbae looked up at the tavern.

On the signboard hung largely above the entrance, the characters 風流酒店 were written in a powerful, elegant hand.

“That calligraphy is especially magnificent.”

He did not know who had written it, but the characters were truly splendid.

“I don’t know which master calligrapher it was, but please be sure to tell them. I would like to treat them to a drink, at least.”

“Let’s do that. I’ll tell my father.”

“Thank you.”

Jo Chunbae, who had answered without thinking, flinched. A chill ran down his spine, and his heart began to pound.

“What did you just say?”

“I said I would tell my father.”

“Why the Cult Leader?”

“Because my father wrote those characters.”

Jo Chunbae, stunned, began trembling like an aspen leaf.

“The Cult Leader wrote that signboard? How could such a thing happen!”

“I asked him to.”

The residents of Maga Village nearby also looked up at the signboard, all with astonished faces.

“Oh my, such an exalted person…”

Jo Chunbae did not know what to do. Just imagining the Demon Cult Leader writing the name of his tavern left him swept up in shock beyond mere emotion.

This was even more moving than the letters that had disappeared earlier along with the wall.

Tears welled in Jo Chunbae’s eyes.

“I don’t know how I can ever repay this grace.”

“You can repay it with that unchanging taste of yours, of course. Now then, everyone, gather at Pungryu Tavern!”

To Geom Mugeuk, the mere fact that these people were living well in front of the main cult held great meaning. No matter what anyone said, his own Demonic Path was right here in Maga Village.

“I’ll be the one to open the new business. Owner, set out the drinks!”

Chapter 863: Because I Want to Remember What You Were Like Then Too
-------------------------------------------------------------------
* * *

A person he had not expected was standing before the door with his back turned.

“Young Cult Leader!”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been looking up at the sky, turned to Jang Ho and smiled brightly.

“Lord Jang, I came hoping to get a cup of tea.”

At this hour in the morning? And all the way to his residence? Wondering if something had happened, Jang Ho inwardly tensed as he welcomed Geom Mugeuk.

“Please, come in.”

“Then pardon the intrusion.”

Geom Mugeuk stepped inside. The interior of the house was neatly arranged, just like Jang Ho’s personality.

“Is it right for a house where a man lives alone to be this clean?”

“I’m hardly ever home, so there’s no time for it to get messy.”

While Geom Mugeuk looked around the house, Jang Ho brewed tea and brought it over.

“I’m not sure if the tea will suit your taste.”

“It’s just right.”

“Has something happened?”

He did not think Geom Mugeuk would have come in the early morning merely to drink tea. And indeed, there was a reason Geom Mugeuk had come.

“I came to ask a favor of you, Lord Jang.”

“Please tell me.”

How could there be only one? He could grant ten, no, a hundred favors.

If he were asked to give his life, he would give it.

“Draw my face for me.”

“Pardon?”

“I want to leave behind a painting of myself as I am now.”

Jang Ho asked with a puzzled expression.

“Are there not painters of exceptional skill within the cult?”

“I want to leave behind a painting drawn by you, Lord Jang.”

Jang Ho looked troubled.

“You drew my back before, so now you should draw me from the front.”

In the past, Jang Ho had drawn Geom Mugeuk’s back as he stood on a cliff and given it to him as a gift. He had said that when his skill improved, he would draw him from the front as well, and Geom Mugeuk had not forgotten those words and had come to him like this.

From the look in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes as he gazed at him, Jang Ho could tell that he was asking sincerely.

“My skill is lacking, but I will do my best.”

Jang Ho led Geom Mugeuk to another room. It was the workroom where Jang Ho painted. On the table were several paintings he had drawn, each one displaying a level of skill that went beyond a mere hobby.

There was also a painting of Ian that he had not yet had the chance to give, and one of Seo Daeryong as well.

“Why haven’t you given these to the two of them?”

“They’re just practice pieces.”

“They’re far too fine to be called that. It’s a shame to leave them sitting here.”

In truth, Jang Ho had also wanted to give them as mementos. But when it came time to actually do so, he felt embarrassed. He did not think they were fine enough to give as gifts.

“Please sit there.”

Geom Mugeuk sat in the chair, and Jang Ho began drawing from a short distance away.

As he painted with Geom Mugeuk before him, that day naturally came back to him. Whenever he painted, that moment would sometimes surface in his mind. The image of Geom Mugeuk holding out a brush, paints, and paper to him.

Since that day, painting had greatly affected his life.

Living the life of the Demon Army Lord, with danger and missions following one after another, the moments he spent painting were his only time of rest.

At first, he had consciously painted only things far removed from murim. The moments he painted were when he could briefly leave murim behind and rest.

But now, he painted freely. He painted the subordinates of the Demon Army, the demon statues at the main headquarters, martial artists training in the drill grounds, and even subordinates who had died in battle. Painting, which had begun as a hobby, had now become his solace.

At last, Jang Ho completed the painting.

“This is far too beautified!”

“It is because you are handsome, Young Cult Leader.”

Looking at the painting, Geom Mugeuk grinned broadly.

“Never draw my brother!”

“May I ask why you asked me to draw this?”

“Won’t this painting sell for an incredible price? After all, it’s a painting of the Young Cult Leader drawn by none other than the Demon Army Lord of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. It’ll be worth whatever price one names.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Jang Ho laughed.

Of course, that was not the reason.

“I have killed the Heavenly Slaughter Star, and now I am trying to live my own life. I wanted to leave behind what expression I wore at this time, and what kind of gaze I looked at people with.”

The meaning contained in the painting was heavier than Jang Ho had thought.

“You entrusted such an important painting to me?”

“That is why I entrusted it to you.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the painting again, pleased.

“I like it very much.”

“I will wrap it for you.”

Jang Ho wrapped the painting in a thin leather cloth so it would not be damaged, folded it carefully, and handed it to Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk kept the painting close to his chest.

“I’ll be going. Don’t see me out. Thank you for the painting!”

As he left the workroom, Geom Mugeuk added one last thing.

“When I later ascend to the seat of Cult Leader, draw me then too. Sitting properly in the grand chair.”

“Yes! When that time comes, I will draw you even more handsomely!”

After Geom Mugeuk left, Jang Ho happened to turn his head, then flinched in surprise before smiling with an expression that said there was no helping him.

Before he knew it, the paintings of Ian and Seo Daeryong had vanished.

* * *

“Do my eyes droop this much? They don’t, do they?”

At Seo Daeryong’s words, Geom Mugeuk drew his sword and reflected his face in the blade.

“I think they do.”

After seeing his own reflection in the blade, Seo Daeryong let out a self-deprecating sigh.

“When did I get this old?”

Clack. The blade returned to its sheath.

“If you don’t like the painting, I’ll take it back.”

As if afraid it might be snatched away, Seo Daeryong pulled the painting toward himself.

“I like it. I really do.”

It was a painting drawn by none other than Jang Ho. On top of that, the goodwill of a drinking companion had been properly put to use, making him look far more handsome than he was in real life.

“Lord Jang’s painting skill is amazing.”

“Keep it safe and leave it to your son as a family heirloom. Later, it’ll sell for an enormous price.”

“As expected! The only one who looks out for my purse is our Young Cult Leader!”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the mountain of documents piled on the desk in the office of the Yellow Springs Pavilion. Just by looking at those documents, he could tell that Seo Daeryong was still suffering under an excessive workload these days.

“How have you been lately?”

“I work, train in martial arts, and when I go home, I play with the baby. It is frighteningly identical every day. Yesterday is today, and today is tomorrow. It feels as though I’ve fallen into a formation.”

Seo Daeryong reached out desperately from within the formation.

“Are you not leaving the cult? It would be even better if you were going out to play.”

“Why?”

“So you can take me with you when you go!”

He had tossed it out like a joke, but Geom Mugeuk readily agreed.

“Fine, let’s go.”

“Are you serious?”

“Go say goodbye to your son. Tell him his father is going out to play, so he should keep growing well. Tell Lady Dan as well to raise the child diligently on her own. If you’re abandoning your wife and child to go out, you might as well enjoy yourself without regrets.”

Seo Daeryong let out a sigh and shouted.

“When am I supposed to get to have fun?”

“Look forward to your next life.”

Though he spoke that way, Seo Daeryong’s face was full of joy.

“In truth, I’ve been so happy these days. So much that I wonder if it’s all right for me to be this happy. And because of that… sometimes I’m afraid this happiness might vanish in an instant.”

After having a child, that fear had grown even stronger. It was not his work being hard that made things difficult.

It was the fear that some misfortune might come, that something might happen to his child or his wife.

How could his inherently gloomy nature go anywhere? His growing happiness tore flesh from his own body to feed and raise his unease.

Geom Mugeuk knew that about Seo Daeryong better than anyone.

“You’ve forgotten who you are.”

“Who am I?”

Geom Mugeuk cleared his throat as though preparing to give a speech.

“A cold-hearted, power-oriented man, yet a real man who loves only one woman; a rebellious spirit who raises his hand alone when everyone else remains silent, and at the same time a pacifist; the owner of an impulsive nature who made a life-changing decision over a drink at an inn; the champion of the martial competition with a keen eye besides; the next Demon Saber who has opened the Conception and Governor Vessels; and my right-hand man, now with even a son, who possesses a clear soul.”

The moment he heard those words, Seo Daeryong choked up.

“You still have all of that memorized?”

“Of course.”

Several expressions had even been added. In particular, the words about possessing a clear soul were something the Mistress of Cheonhwa Pavilion had said, and he had remembered and added those as well.

“You were there at my beginning, so you will be there at the end too. You will adorn the final moment with me, so do not be anxious.”

Seo Daeryong smiled brightly.

“Hearing you say that makes me truly happy.”

“Now your anxiety has vanished completely, hasn’t it?”

“No. Now that my happiness has grown, I’ve become even more anxious.”

At Seo Daeryong’s joke, Geom Mugeuk laughed, and Seo Daeryong laughed along with him.

“I’m going.”

Geom Mugeuk was about to leave the room when Seo Daeryong suddenly asked from behind him.

“Why did you come today?”

“To give you the painting.”

“Is that truly all?”

Somehow, it did not feel as though he had come merely to deliver the painting.

The look in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes as he gazed at Seo Daeryong was gentle.

“I wanted to remember what all of you were like at this time.”

Leaving behind those words whose meaning was unclear, Geom Mugeuk walked out of the room.

“Remember what?”

When he called out, Geom Mugeuk’s answer came from far outside.

“Your drooping eyes!”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk was leaving the Yellow Springs Pavilion when someone was waiting for him.

Surprisingly, it was the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest.

“There is something I wish to ask you, Young Cult Leader, so I came to see you.”

From the trembling look in his eyes behind the mask, Geom Mugeuk could tell that he had mustered great courage.

“Speak.”

“I heard that the assassins of the Ghost Shadow Unit will all be leaving soon.”

As Cha Iran departed, the assassins under her had also decided to leave. Cha Iran had allowed her subordinates to choose as they wished. They could leave her and live new lives, or they could follow her to the end.

The problem was Sipchilgyo, with whom he was in love.

“I wish to continue living as a Faceless Guest from now on. If I tell her how I feel, I fear that it may force a choice upon her.”

“Are you afraid she will leave?”

The Crescent Moon Faceless Guest shook his head.

“No. If that is her choice, I intend to send her off with the utmost courtesy.”

What the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest was worried about now was not the possibility of rejection, but the possibility that she would accept.

“What if she lives here with me as a Faceless Guest, and later becomes unhappy?”

Seeing that he was already worrying that far ahead, it seemed he truly liked Sipchilgyo.

“Why are you asking me that?”

“Somehow, I thought that if it were you, Young Cult Leader, you would know what I should do.”

During the operation for the selection of the greatest beauty under heaven that they had carried out together, he had confirmed just how outstanding and remarkable Geom Mugeuk was.

Moreover, at that time, the Young Cult Leader had even arranged a separate opportunity for him and Sipchilgyo.

“If you had not let us meet that day, we would never have come this far.”

“So you want me to take responsibility?”

“No!”

The Crescent Moon Faceless Guest panicked, and Geom Mugeuk enjoyed the reaction.

Yes, since you have bravely come this far, I should tell you something that will help as much as possible. But Crescent Moon, when it comes to women, you and I are not so different.

“You said you were afraid you might force a choice upon her?”

“Yes.”

“This time, force the choice.”

The Crescent Moon Faceless Guest stared at Geom Mugeuk in surprise.

“Say, ‘I cannot give up either one, so only your choice remains.’ Force it on her like that. But tell her in advance. ‘Right now, I am the one forcing you, but later, you must force me to choose.’”

“What choice do you mean?”

It was a choice the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest had not expected.

“Let’s run away together!”

“!”

“Tell her not to endure and endure a difficult life until she reaches a point of no return, but to make sure she tells you before that happens. ‘Life as a Faceless Guest is too hard and too sickening for me, so I’ve decided to run away. Only your choice remains.’ Since you chose before, this time she should choose.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled as he spoke.

“I made it sound complicated, but what I mean is that you should live as Faceless Guests once without regrets, then run away together.”

The Faceless Guest’s eyes smiled like crescent moons. Now Geom Mugeuk understood why this Faceless Guest used the crescent moon design.

He bowed his head respectfully and expressed his gratitude.

“Thank you very much, Young Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk watched him as he turned and walked away.

Yes, how could parting itself be what one cannot endure? What leaves regret and lingers in the heart is having it end without even being able to say anything, without being able to choose, without being able to do a single thing properly.

* * *

Ian was lost in thought in her room.

She thought of Cha Iran, who had come to see her earlier.

She had come to bid farewell, saying that she had decided to leave with the Sword King.

—I’ll come visit the inn next time.

—It’s an expensive inn, so earn plenty of money.

Cha Iran, who had been about to leave like that, turned back and said,

—May I say something not as your subordinate, but as another woman?

These were the last words Cha Iran left behind.

—Isn’t it time for that heart to truly start beating?

Ian knew very well what she had meant by those words.

‘Easy for you to say. When you know perfectly well whose heart it is.’

The words that followed actually came out of her mouth.

“When you know how many people’s lives and deaths depend on the owner of that heart.”

Just then, a voice came from behind her.

“Who depends on it?”

Startled, she turned around to find Geom Mugeuk standing behind her before she knew it.

“Young Cult Leader!”

“I could empty the whole house and you wouldn’t notice. What are you thinking about so deeply?”

Flustered, she blurted out whatever came to mind.

“You don’t know that a troop leader has more work to do than a young cult leader, do you? There are mountains of things I have to mind. Oh, right, Martial Artist Ak and Martial Artist Cha are leaving soon.”

“I heard from Brother Ak.”

“Ah, you met him?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“He said he’d contact me once he opens the inn. Normally, I’d go with him to look for a location and help him build the place, but since Miss Cha is going with him, there’s no need to worry.”

When Ian thought of the Sword King and Cha Iran, she felt that the bonds between people were truly impossible to fathom.

They had once been enemies, then met as master and disciple, entered the contest for the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven together, became friends and subordinates, and now were leaving as strangers once more.

“Let’s go.”

“Where?”

“To find the real Ian.”

“Ah, the Pungnyu Tavern just opened, right? I’ll need a drink before I can take off my mask again.”

“No, farther than that.”

For an instant, Ian’s eyes sparkled as if wondering, Could it be?

“Let’s go get some air in the Central Plains.”

“Just the two of us?”

“Yes, the two of us.”

Ian’s face lit up brightly. It was an unconcealable joy, one that made the person who had asked her to come feel twice as pleased.

“Pack your things.”

Ian ran off and opened the wardrobe.

Slide.

Inside lay a small leather bag she had already packed.

“You had it all ready?”

“You always tell me to go all of a sudden. So now I keep everything prepared in advance.”

“Let’s go. Give me your bag.”

For some reason, Ian asked for a little time.

“How much time can you give me?”

“Half a shichen?”

“Please wait outside!”

Leaving her as she moved like an arrow, Geom Mugeuk went outside.

Half a shichen later, Ian emerged from the room.

Dressed in the splendid clothes she had bought during the contest for the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven, with makeup on as well, she was truly beautiful and alluring.

“It’s been a while since I went out, so I dressed up a little.”

Under Geom Mugeuk’s fixed gaze, she asked cautiously.

“Is it too much? Should I change and wear a bamboo hat?”

“No. I told you to live with that face, didn’t I? You’ve been saving that face far too much until now. Let’s go and put it to proper use this time.”

Ian shrugged for no reason and said,

“Will you be all right? This is the face that made it to the finals of the Greatest Beauty Under Heaven. No getting jealous later.”

“Don’t you get jealous when all the righteous heroines line up saying they like me.”

The two looked at each other and smiled broadly.

Walking out with her, Geom Mugeuk said with vigor,

“Let’s go, to the jianghu.”

Chapter 864: Wipe Your Mouth First
----------------------------------
* * *

“Disappointing.”

Geomugeuk and Ian set down their chopsticks at the same time.

“It doesn’t suit my taste either. And we aren’t the sort of people to leave food we paid for.”

They had not even eaten half the meal.

“Are you sure this is the place? There isn’t an inn with the same name in the next village, is there?”

“No, this is the place.”

Geomugeuk and Ian sat side by side, looking down at a booklet.

A Gourmet Tour of the Central Plains.

It was the booklet he had bought long ago at the bookstore the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had led him to with his father. He had bought it so he could take his father there later, but since they were passing by, he and Ian had come first to check it out.

“I think our expectations were too high.”

“I know not every place featured in a book can be delicious, but I thought it would at least have the basics down.”

“Still, that place last time was good.”

Geomugeuk drew a long line through the name of this inn.

“Good thing we came in advance. If I’d brought Father here, he might have used the cooks as sacrificial offerings and started the Demonic Orthodox War right here on the spot.”

Ian laughed at Geomugeuk’s joke.

The two of them, having left the cult, were enjoying themselves in the truest sense of the word. They sought out inns and restaurants famed for their food, and made sure to stop at every place known for its scenic beauty.

When they camped outdoors, they fell asleep beneath the stars, and they swam together in clear waters.

Geomugeuk showed off his skills by cooking for her, and in kindhearted villages, they spent the night and helped with farm work.

Geomugeuk did not hurry. He traveled leisurely with her. He did not worry ahead of time about what might be waiting at the end of it.

Ian could feel it. Geomugeuk was turning this journey into one meant for her. That made her happy, but at the same time, it left her uneasy. Because she had never once enjoyed herself so leisurely with Geomugeuk. Because she had never been this happy in her life.

Just then, a waiter came over and set down dishes and wine.

“We didn’t order this.”

You bastard! We said it wasn’t good, so why bring more? Just as he failed to bring himself to say it, the waiter glanced toward a seat in the corner and spoke.

“The guest over there sent this dish especially for the young lady.”

When they looked that way, a middle-aged man was staring at them.

Ian dipped her head slightly in thanks, then said to Geomugeuk as if showing off.

“See? How many times has this been now? Eight?”

“Six.”

Indeed, wherever Ian went, gazes gathered on her. It was not the first or second time someone had ordered food for her like this.

Ian said it was bothersome and that she would go around wearing a bamboo hat, but Geomugeuk simply had her go without one.

“Enjoy it. Enjoy it to your heart’s content.”

“Are you not the one enjoying it more, Young Master?”

“Was it obvious?”

In truth, Geomugeuk was enjoying it more. Gazes would go to the beautiful Ian, and then inevitably come to him next. Whenever that happened, Geomugeuk wore a proud expression. An expression that said, I’m the man traveling with a beauty like this.

“Oh, that man is coming this way.”

The man who had sent the food was walking toward them.

He was a rather handsome middle-aged man. At a glance, his clothes were expensive, and he wore accessories adorned with gold and jade.

Geomugeuk quietly observed him, then looked at Ian. His eyes seemed to ask, Do you know? Ian smiled and nodded.

“You can recognize them even from afar now. You’ve improved a lot.”

“You can tell at a glance.”

While they were saying this, the man came right up to them.

“Is this your first time in this town?”

Geomugeuk smiled brightly, raised the booklet he was holding, and said,

“As you can see, I am traveling with my junior martial sister.”

The man introduced himself.

“I am a man named Damho.”

“I am Geom Yeon. Yeon as in smoke, not yeon as in affinity.”

Ian introduced herself as well.

“I’m Ian.”

Because Geomugeuk’s name was known, he used the name Geom Yeon, but Ian simply used her own name. Her face and her name, both as they were.

“Seeing you two made me think of the old days, so I sent wine and food. Since meeting like this is also fate, let us share a table. After all, in the murim, is there anything more meaningful than making a new connection?”

Damho did not even wait for their permission and sat down across from them. Both his words and actions were full of confidence.

“Today’s wine and food will be on me.”

“I had a good dream last night, and it seems it was because such a happy event was going to occur.”

One might have expected him to dislike having their time alone interrupted, but for some reason, Geomugeuk accepted him without resistance.

Damho looked at Ian and exclaimed in admiration.

“I don’t believe I’ve ever seen such a beautiful young lady before.”

He did not hide his feelings.

“What is the relationship between you two?”

“She is my junior martial sister and my friend.”

At those words, Damho asked,

“Does it not make you uneasy, traveling with such a beautiful junior martial sister?”

“What would make me uneasy?”

“I mean, does no one come and pick a fight? All sorts of rabble are bound to swarm around a beauty.”

Though he was hardly one to say such a thing, he spoke with perfect confidence.

Geomugeuk said with a smile,

“My junior martial sister is good at fighting, so it’s all right. Now, now, let us drink to our hearts’ content! Ah, since you are buying anyway, could you perhaps buy it at the inn next door?”

* * *

A group of martial artists had formed ranks in front of the Chu Family Sword Sect.

They were martial artists of the Murim Alliance. They had surrounded the place on all sides and were standing guard so that no one could enter or leave.

One man arrived there leading a group of martial artists. The martial artists all bowed their heads toward them in unison. The one who had arrived leading the Demon Extermination Squad was none other than the Young Alliance Leader, Jin Hagun.

“What happened?”

At Jin Hagun’s question, a Murim Alliance martial artist replied.

“The second son of the Chu Family Sword Sect has been taken hostage by unidentified individuals.”

Since ordinary branch martial artists could not resolve the matter, they had urgently requested help from the Demon Extermination Squad. Fortunately, the Demon Extermination Squad had been carrying out an operation not far away.

“They staged a hostage situation in broad daylight? Who on earth?”

“We have yet to identify them.”

The fact that they had not planned a perfect crime meant—

“There is a high possibility this is a matter of personal grudge.”

The martial artists of the Demon Extermination Squad nodded as if they shared Jin Hagun’s thoughts. Otherwise, there was no reason they would be staging a hostage situation like this.

But there was one strange point.

“Was it the Chu Family Sword Sect that contacted the Alliance from the start?”

The Chu Family Sword Sect was a famous orthodox sect. They had many masters of their own, so they would not have asked them for help. Moreover, as far as he had heard, the sect master of the Chu Family Sword Sect was famous for his pride.

“It seems their rescue operation failed.”

“The Chu Family Sword Sect failed?”

“The retainers barely managed to stop the sect master from going in to rescue him personally.”

The Chu Family Sword Sect he knew had quite a few renowned masters. If they had failed despite that, it meant the opponent’s martial arts were considerable.

“It seems things are difficult because his son is being held hostage.”

At his subordinate’s report, Jin Hagun nodded and asked again.

“What are their demands?”

“They requested delicious wine and food.”

“Wine and food?”

Jin Hagun, as well as the martial artists of the Demon Extermination Squad, wore expressions of incomprehension. When hoodlums staged hostage situations, they often demanded wine and food.

But these were masters skilled enough that the Chu Family Sword Sect had failed to rescue the hostage, and they had demanded wine and food? Something did not quite add up.

“That over there is Sect Master Chu.”

The Chu Family Sword Sect Master, Chuyeom, walked over to Jin Hagun. The two exchanged courteous fist salutes.

“It has been a long time, Young Alliance Leader.”

“Sect Master.”

Given the situation, Chuyeom’s complexion was poor.

“I regret that we meet under such circumstances.”

“Right now, your son’s life and safety are more important than that.”

Jin Hagun cautiously asked Chuyeom,

“Are there perhaps any people who might hold a grudge against you?”

Chuyeom shook his head.

“My son, especially my second son, is truly clever and kind. He has never once caused trouble until now.”

In Jin Hagun’s experience, when a child caused trouble, he had almost never seen parents who properly knew what sort of person that child was. For that reason, parents’ testimony was often of little help.

“Where are they?”

“They have taken over the detached building in the rear garden.”

“We will take charge from here.”

“I leave it in your hands.”

Jin Hagun led the Demon Extermination Squad into the rear garden. As befitted the Murim Alliance’s finest elites, they moved without a sound.

After having the Demon Extermination Squad guard the rear garden like an iron wall, he led only a dozen or so members of the squad into the building.

Even as they entered, there was no reaction from inside.

Jin Hagun, who had completely erased his own presence, silently approached a window.

He peered inside through a narrow gap in the window.

Far away, he saw a young man kneeling. He had been beaten so badly that his face was swollen. It was undoubtedly the second son of the Chu Family Sword Sect.

‘Fortunately, he is not dead yet.’

Through the gap, he could also see martial artists lying collapsed. They were likely the Chu Family Sword Sect masters who had carried out the rescue operation. From the feel of it, they did not seem dead yet either. From here, he could not see the ones staging the hostage situation.

‘We must hurry.’

Jin Hagun gave instructions to his subordinates with hand signals.

—We all enter at once.

The martial artists of the Demon Extermination Squad, who had approached different windows, all nodded. One person slipped into the ceiling.

Jin Hagun did not hesitate over the operation. The moment his signal fell—

Craaaash.

Jin Hagun and the martial artists of the Demon Extermination Squad entered simultaneously through the door, windows, and ceiling.

Shiiiiing.

The martial artist who dropped from the ceiling wrapped his arms around the man kneeling on the floor and retreated, shouting,

“Hostage secured!”

Before those words even came out, they should already have launched their attack.

But Jin Hagun stood there blankly, staring ahead without any reaction. The other Demon Extermination Squad martial artists also stood dumbfounded, their swords halted in midair as they were about to attack.

The Demon Extermination Squad martial artist who had come down from the ceiling and saved the hostage belatedly looked ahead.

‘Gasp.’

He, too, froze like a stone statue. Before their eyes was a sight that left them all no choice but to stiffen.

Ahead of them, two people were looking this way. The young man holding a chicken leg in one hand and a wine cup in the other was someone he knew.

It was none other than Geomugeuk.

With oil glistening on his lips, Geomugeuk said to Jin Hagun,

“You’re here?”

This was the first time they had seen each other since Geomugeuk stopped the Heavenly Killer Star. Jin Hagun had been wondering why no word had come from him, though it seemed likely he would contact him at least once.

But staging a hostage situation, holding a chicken leg, and then saying what with those greasy lips? You’re here?

He was so shocked and dumbfounded that at first, he wondered if this was someone who merely looked like him. He could not have imagined that the person staging a hostage situation with the son of the Chu Family Sword Sect would be Geomugeuk.

But Ian was drinking wine in front of Geomugeuk. She, too, was firmly holding a chicken leg and tearing into it.

Ian quickly put down the chicken leg, took a handkerchief from her robe, and wiped her mouth.

Then she bowed politely to Jin Hagun.

After bowing to her in return, Jin Hagun turned around.

Chujung, the second son of the Chu Family Sword Sect, spoke with difficulty.

“...Please save me.”

Not only was his face swollen, several of his teeth were broken as well.

When Jin Hagun looked back at Geomugeuk,

“I didn’t do that. Ian did. She said she absolutely couldn’t endure it. She said she probably wouldn’t get the chance to hit him later.”

When Geomugeuk tattled on her, Ian wore a dumbfounded expression. Her face seemed to say, No, you’re telling on me for that? Even what I said? But she could not deny that she had hit him.

“Same for that one over there.”

A man was hanging on the wall behind them. He hung there as if he were clothing hung on a wall, and astonishingly, he was Damho, whom they had met at the inn. He, too, was battered from head to toe, his head hanging low.

Judging by this scene alone, they looked exactly like a wicked man-and-woman duo.

Just then, the door opened and Sect Master Chuyeom entered. Behind him came the Demon Extermination Squad martial artists who had been guarding the entrance. They, too, were all shocked by the situation inside.

Chuyeom ran to his son.

“Jung-ah. Are you all right?”

Seeing the state of his son’s face, Chuyeom’s gaze turned cold.

“What are you doing? Why have you not seized them at once?”

Jin Hagun asked quietly,

“What did you do?”

Chuyeom flinched in surprise.

Jin Hagun had not asked the ones staging the hostage situation. He had asked his son.

Chuyeom asked in a cold voice.

“Just what do you think you are doing, Great Lord?”

Earlier, when they had exchanged greetings, he had been called the Young Lord, but now the form of address had changed to Great Lord. That much indicated his displeasure with the current situation.

How could Jin Hagun not know? He knew exactly what kind of people Geom Mugeuk and Ian were. That they had struck him like this meant he had committed an act truly deserving of a beating. If Ian, in particular, had been unable to endure it and struck him, it meant he had done something truly wicked.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk's voice came from behind.

“It’s no use asking. That brat will never be able to speak. He committed something unspeakable.”

Chuyeom, who had been listening, burst out in anger.

“Silence! Just who are you to spew such words so recklessly?”

Ian took out another handkerchief from within his robes and handed it to Geom Mugeuk.

“Wipe your mouth first.”

“Thanks.”

Geom Mugeuk wiped the grease from his mouth.

Chuyeom was further enraged by this behavior. Who had ever dared to act in such a manner before him?

“The crime of daring to take my son hostage and kill the warriors of the Chu Family Sword Gate is to make an enemy of the entire Orthodox Murim!”

At that moment, Chuyeom's eyes met Geom Mugeuk's.

In that instant, Chuyeom's heart sank. The torrent of words that had been about to pour forth was swallowed back down.

Even without releasing any killing intent or pressure, Geom Mugeuk overwhelmed his opponent with nothing but a gaze.

“You are wrong on both counts.”

“What?”

“First, all of those people who entered earlier are alive.”

The Demon Extermination warriors nearby examined those who had fallen. Just as Geom Mugeuk had said, they were all alive.

“No one is injured. They have only had their mahyeol and ahyeol points suppressed.”

At the Demon Extermination warrior's report, Chuyeom's expression hardened. He knew full well that subduing them without injury was far more difficult than killing them.

‘How on earth?’

He could kill them by ambush, but suppress the mahyeol of those experts? And that too without injury? It was absolutely no easy task.

“Nor did we take your son hostage.”

Geom Mugeuk's gaze turned toward Jin Hagun.

“We were merely waiting for the Demon Extermination Great Lord to arrive.”

Jin Hagun asked calmly.

“Why were you waiting for me?”

As everyone watched, Geom Mugeuk answered with a serious expression.

“Because I wanted to see you?”

This madman! Was now the time for jokes? Jin Hagun conveyed precisely that sentiment in his eyes and expression. Unfortunately, the Demon Extermination warriors standing behind him did not see that expression—one they would never normally get to witness.

Soon, Geom Mugeuk revealed the true reason.

“There was an incident recently that caused a stir in the murim, was there not?”

In an instant, Jin Hagun's expression completely stiffened, and the name of an organization spilled from his lips.

“Tanmibang!”

Chapter 865: It's Because You Haven't Been Dragged Away Yet
-----------------------------------------------------------
* * *

Tammi Bang.

That was the name of the organization Jinhagun had recently been investigating. Contrary to its name, which suggested a desire for beauty, it was an utterly hideous organization.

The first time Jinhagun learned of them was while investigating a series of disappearances.

When women of the martial world began disappearing one after another, Jinhagun and the Demon-Slaying Unit began digging into the matter in earnest.

In the process, they discovered a name.

Tammi Bang.

It was a secret organization, not an official martial sect. They abducted women from across the martial world and sold them off—a human-trafficking ring.

They primarily abducted beautiful women, but their heinous crimes extended to young girls as well as adults.

After pursuing them, they managed to capture the men employed to abduct the women, but they were nothing more than underlings acting on orders from above.

They were an utterly compartmentalized organization. The men received only money and written orders, and did not know who was giving them their commands.

Once the low-level members were arrested, the bastards stopped abducting women. They intended to wait for the Demon-Slaying Unit’s investigation to end.

Jinhagun was furious and despairing.

The Demon-Slaying Unit had other matters to deal with besides them, and if things continued this way, they would eventually have to bury the case and move on to other business.

They would be unable to catch the bastards until they committed another crime. Moreover, they would move even more covertly, meaning countless more victims would have to appear before a clue emerged that would allow them to track the organization again.
