*We should have caught them at the very beginning, no matter what.*

And yet Geom Mugeuk had brought up Tammi Bang.

Then, even more astonishing words flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“The master of Tammi Bang is in this room.”

“!”

Clack-clack-clack-clack.

Every member of the Demon-Slaying Unit raised their qi and pointed their swords in all directions, going on guard.

They had followed Jinhagun and experienced enough of Geom Mugeuk to know him well. Even when he said something absurd, they all knew he was not the sort of person to joke or play around about matters like this.

Jinhagun’s gaze turned toward the man hanging on the wall.

“Is it him?”

He naturally assumed it had to be that man, but Geom Mugeuk slowly shook his head.

*Not him?*

It could not be the master of the Chuga Sword School or his son.

Just as Jinhagun was wondering if it might be one of the fallen sword-school warriors—

*No.*

Jinhagun suddenly realized that Ian had beaten the second son, Chujung, mercilessly. She had even said there would be no chance to beat him later.

“Don’t tell me…”

At Jinhagun’s astonished words, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

Jinhagun’s gaze shifted toward Chujung, who stood behind them. At last, the answer to his earlier question—*What have you done?*—had emerged.

Jinhagun and all the members of the Demon-Slaying Unit were stunned. The son of a prestigious family was the master of Tammi Bang?

Naturally, the person most shocked was his father, Chuyeom.

“You lunatic!”

Just as Chuyeom reflexively drew his sword and tried to step forward—

Click.

The sword, half-drawn, slid back into its sheath.

Someone was pressing down on the end of the sword hilt with one finger.

It was Ian. In the instant she flashed forward, she had closed the distance and was now standing right beside Chuyeom, gazing at him as she calmly spoke.

“I understand how you feel, but you are not in a position to point your sword at anyone.”

Chuyeom realized that this young woman was far more skilled than he was. He had been agitated, certainly, but even so, there was no way he should have allowed her to approach this close so easily. Furthermore, despite her pressing down on the hilt with one finger, he had been unable to draw his sword.

“Who are you people?”

At Chuyeom’s trembling question, Ian took a small step back and replied,

“Who we are isn’t important. What matters right now is who your son is.”

She was completely different from the woman who had been tearing into a chicken leg moments earlier.

Jinhagun could tell. The woman she was now was different from the woman he had seen the last time.

*She’s become much stronger.*

Moreover, while traveling, she had continued discussing martial arts with Geom Mugeuk whenever they had the time, and her cultivation realm was still rising.

Jinhagun gave a signal with his eyes, and two members of the Demon-Slaying Unit rushed in from either side and sealed Chuyeom’s acupoints.

“What are you doing?”

He shouted belatedly, but his acupoints had already been sealed. It was for his own sake. If he flew into a frenzy because of his son, he would only be the one to suffer in the end.

Jinhagun calmly spoke to him.

“Please remain calm. I will uncover the truth of this incident.”

Chuyeom flinched, then looked at him suspiciously.

“Don’t tell me… Is the Martial Alliance plotting to destroy our school?”

It was an understandable misunderstanding. An unidentified pair of male and female experts had abducted his son, while the commander of the Demon-Slaying Unit, who had come to rescue him, appeared to know the culprits.

Understanding this, Jinhagun did not become agitated despite the rude accusation.

“If the school master has committed crimes heinous enough to warrant his disappearance at the hands of the Demon-Slaying Unit, then ultimately, your words will be correct. But if that is not the case, then watch a little longer.”

Chuyeom said nothing more. His words just now had clearly been a slip of the tongue. He had insulted the Alliance, the Demon-Slaying Unit, and even the Vice Alliance Leader.

Jinhagun looked toward Geom Mugeuk once more.

*School Master Chu, how could you know? Whenever that friend before you does something like this, all preparations have already been completed.*

“Do you have evidence that Young Master Chu is the master of Tammi Bang?”

“Of course.”

“Then do you also know where the women he sold are now?”

In truth, this was the most important question to Jinhagun. Rescuing the women was their highest priority.

He assumed that Geom Mugeuk would certainly have discovered the answer.

“Not a single one of them was sold.”

“Not a single one?”

In response to Jinhagun’s bewilderment, Geom Mugeuk revealed a shocking fact.

“Tammi Bang never existed in the first place.”

“!”

One astonishing statement after another continued to emerge.

“Tammi Bang is a fictitious organization created to throw you into confusion.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward Chujung.

“When you began investigating the disappearances and closing in on him, he created the fictitious organization known as Tammi Bang. He paid several men and made use of them as though they were members of a compartmentalized organization. He had them actually abduct the women, then allowed you to capture them.”

“Why would he do that?”

“As long as you believed that the perpetrators were a human-trafficking ring, you would never catch him.”

It made sense. No one could have imagined that the heir of a prestigious orthodox sect—especially a young successor known for being kind and intelligent—would be operating such an organization.

But there was still one part Jinhagun could not understand.

“Even without creating such an organization, you would never have suspected that Chujung was capable of such things.”

As though it were a fitting question, Chuyeom also nodded and waited for the answer.

The next answer was just as astonishing.

“He didn’t create it to protect himself. He created it to protect his master.”

At the word *master*, Chuyeom shouted,

“It’s becoming clear that this is all nonsense. I am Jung’s master.”

Jinhagun, however, realized who Geom Mugeuk was referring to.

His gaze naturally shifted toward the man hanging on the wall.

“That man is his master.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded and extended a hand forward.

Whoosh.

When Geom Mugeuk used his art of seizing objects from the air, the man who had been hanging his head raised it of his own accord. He was still asleep, but he no longer had the handsome face of the middle-aged man they had encountered at the inn.

He had become a much older elderly man. He had revealed his true appearance.

At that moment, Jeong Gyeong of the Demon-Slaying Unit recognized who the old man was.

“It’s the Lustful Demon.”

Everyone was shocked by his identity.

He was a notorious lecher. He had been declared a public enemy of the martial world for assaulting countless women. He was infamous for murdering women after draining away their vital essence.

The Martial Alliance had pursued him relentlessly, and last year had even driven him into a corner by laying a net inescapable as heaven and earth. Yet they had ultimately failed to capture him.

And now the Lustful Demon had been caught here.

The Lustful Demon had approached Geom Mugeuk and Ian at the inn by buying them alcohol and food.

Earlier, when they saw him walking toward them, Geom Mugeuk had asked Ian with his eyes whether she recognized him. Ian had nodded to indicate that she did.

He had actually been asking whether she had recognized the Face-Retaining Art the man used to make himself look younger.

Geom Mugeuk had reached a level where he could recognize practitioners of the Face-Retaining Art at a glance, and he had taught Ian how to identify its unique aura and characteristics as well.

Jeong Gyeong of the Demon-Slaying Unit belatedly recalled something and spoke.

“Come to think of it, the place where the Martial Alliance lost him was near here.”

Jinhagun’s expression darkened. If the Lustful Demon had been involved in this incident…

“Then all the abducted women must be dead.”

The Lustful Demon Jinhagun knew never left his victims alive.

Just as he had expected, that was indeed the case. Geom Mugeuk sighed and said,

“They all died after having their vital essence drained.”

Jinhagun and all the members of the Demon-Slaying Unit let out groans. They had wanted to rescue the women who had been sold away. Naturally, their gazes grew fierce.

Now, the last thing Jinhagun wanted to know was this:

“How did you capture that man?”

“I didn’t capture him. He came looking for me of his own accord.”

Jinhagun could tell that the Lustful Demon had been drawn to Ian’s beauty and had attempted to commit lewd acts before being captured. He had chosen the wrong target in spectacular fashion. The two of them were natural disasters to lechers like him.

“We caught him when he tried to drug us, and discovered that he was the Lustful Demon. Truthfully, until then, I had no intention of handing him over to you, despite how busy you are. I was going to punish him and kill him. Then the bastard said this: if we would please end his suffering, he would tell us who the master of Tammi Bang—the organization causing such a commotion in the martial world these days—was.”

Only now could Jinhagun understand the entire truth of the incident.

“You can hear the rest directly from him.”

Geom Mugeuk sent a bolt of finger qi and woke the man, whose acupuncture point had been sealed to keep him asleep.

Upon awakening, he blinked and looked at the people standing before him.

Even when faced with Jinhagun and the assembled members of the Demon-Slaying Unit, he showed no fear. Instead, a sneer instinctively rose at the corners of his mouth.

Jinhagun asked him,

“Did you threaten Chujung and take him as your disciple?”

The Lustful Demon sneered.

“What nonsense are you spouting now?”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk spoke from beside him.

“Answer properly.”

The Lustful Demon absentmindedly turned his head and met Geom Mugeuk’s eyes.

After blinking for a moment, he was suddenly terrified.

“Urrrgh!”

The composure in his eyes vanished, replaced by fear. Only then did he remember what had happened before he fell asleep.

“Shh.”

When Geom Mugeuk told him to be quiet, the Lustful Demon clamped his mouth shut like a clam.

“He is the Vice Alliance Leader of the Martial Alliance. Answer respectfully.”

At the words *Vice Alliance Leader*, the Lustful Demon’s eyes widened, but the person he continued to fear was Geom Mugeuk.

“I understand.”

It was the first time anyone had ever seen the Lustful Demon being so obedient.

“I’ll tell you everything honestly, so make sure you keep your promise.”

The Lustful Demon reminded him of their agreement once more, and Geom Mugeuk readily nodded.

“What did you ask me just now?”

“I asked if you threatened Chujung and took him as your disciple.”

The Lustful Demon’s gaze turned toward Chujung.

“That child rescued me when I was dying from severe injuries within the net inescapable as heaven and earth.”

The next part of his testimony was even more shocking.

“Even after learning who I was, he wanted to become my disciple.”

Everyone there was stunned by the revelation that Chujung had not been forced to become his disciple. In fact, the opposite was true.

“The moment I saw that child, I knew. He was born with a lustful heart and a vicious heart stronger than anyone’s. He had not a trace of mercy in him. He was a child who would one day surpass me. What a pity.”

Of course, Chujung vehemently denied it.

“That’s a lie. He’s slandering me to frame me.”

The Lustful Demon sighed as he looked at him.

“It’s all over, disciple. We… picked the wrong targets.”

At the Lustful Demon’s lament, Chuyeom shouted furiously,

“Shut your mouth! What sort of filthy nonsense is a lecher like you spouting? Vice Alliance Leader, surely you don’t believe the words of that lowlife lecher? Everything is merely his testimony, is it not? There is no evidence. You cannot brand our son a criminal based on this.”

At that moment, the Lustful Demon spoke to Chuyeom with a cold gaze.

“The energy of the lust technique he learned from me is flowing through your son’s body. You can tell immediately by checking his internal energy. He has already drained the essence from nine women.”

At the mention of nine, Chuyeom was stunned.

“You could have forcibly infused it into him!”

Leaving aside the fact that it was absurd, what Jin Ha-gun wanted to know was this:

“Then why didn’t your son say that first? That the man hanging there on the wall had forcibly made him his disciple.”

Chu Yeom flinched. It was true. If he were innocent, the first thing he would have said was that he had been forcibly made a disciple and that the man had forcibly infused his body with energy.

“He must have been too flustered to think of it.”

Geom Muguk drove the nail home.

“Since Tammi Bang was run as a cell-based organization, there must be evidence recording its activities. If we search that man’s residence, there should be a secret safe, and evidence related to the organization will be there. We can determine the truth by comparing the handwriting.”

“They must have planted forged evidence there, too!”

As Chu Yeom desperately tried to protect his son, Jin Ha-gun asked him,

“Master Chu, will you be able to handle your son from now on?”

“!”

Silence descended upon the place.

Chu Yeom slowly turned toward his son.

“I will never let you be falsely accused⋯⋯.”

At that moment, Chu Yeom could not continue.

His son was not looking at him. His furious gaze was fixed on the Lewd Fiend, and it was different from the anger of a man who had been falsely accused.

As though he had decided there was no longer any point in hiding it, Chu Jung revealed his true nature.

“You fucking idiot! I told you not to make a move for the time being! You couldn’t even endure a few measly days, and you ruined everything!”

Chu Yeom was startled by the icy gaze and tone. The sight was so unfamiliar that he could hardly believe this was his son.

The Lewd Fiend burst into loud laughter.

“Disciple, even if I was going to die, how could I simply stand by and watch such a beauty?”

The Lewd Fiend spoke to Geom Muguk as though he had finished everything he had set out to do.

“You must keep your promise.”

Geom Muguk waved his hand, and the Lewd Fiend’s pressure points were subdued once more, putting him to sleep.

“What promise did you make to him?”

Surely Geom Muguk could not have promised to set such a villain free.

“I promised not to drag him back to the Main Church. I frightened him a little while punishing him. I told him I would drag him to the Main Church, continue treating him so that he would remain alive, and make him suffer this pain for the rest of his life.”

At times like this, Jin Ha-gun felt something beyond anger—a sense of his own limits. Whenever he saw a villain who scorned justice and chivalry, yet could not withstand the fear inflicted by an even greater evil, flames surged up in his chest.

“I tried to at least find out where the women’s bodies were buried, but apparently only that man knows the location.”

Jin Ha-gun walked over and stared fixedly at Chu Jung.

“Where did you bury the bodies of the women you killed?”

Chu Jung’s eyes were empty, with only a single point of malice embedded within them.

The moment Jin Ha-gun saw the strange smile forming at the corners of Chu Jung’s mouth, he knew. The man would never reveal where the bodies were buried.

No matter how much they interrogated him—even if they tortured him—they would never get the answer they wanted. Instead, he would enjoy one final amusement by refusing to reveal it. He was someone born this way.

Geom Muguk, standing behind him, seemed to feel the same.

“Send him to us.”

Jin Ha-gun turned around and looked at Geom Muguk.

Just as Chu Jung had revealed his true nature, in this moment Geom Muguk, too, was revealing exactly who he was.

“It’s because none of you have been dragged into the underground of the Main Church yet.”

Chapter 866: Wake Up Secretly at Night. Got It?
-----------------------------------------------
Chujung glared at Geom Mugeuk.

It was a gaze filled with malice, enough to make the person facing him shudder.

But his opponent was Geom Mugeuk, who, when it came to villains, had experienced everyone from the Twelve Zodiac Kings to the Heavenly Killing Star.

Receiving that malice-filled stare as if it were laughable, Geom Mugeuk said to Jin Hagun,

“Not only will we find where the bodies are buried, we’ll make him into a whole new person before sending him back.”

He said it like a joke, but they both knew it was no joke.

Yes, if it was the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, they would make the words “I was wrong” come out of that bastard’s mouth on their own. Just as the Lust Demon had become so respectful earlier.

But the Murim Alliance could not handle its affairs according to personal feelings.

“Leave what comes next to us. We may not be as fiery as you, but we too have methods we have built up over many years of dealing with evil.”

With an expression that said he understood the decision, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“Do that.”

Just then, Jin Hagun’s voice transmission flew to Geom Mugeuk.

—If we fail, could you help us then?

Geom Mugeuk knew how difficult that request must have been for him. It was also a matter tied to the honor of the Murim Alliance.

Even so, the reason he asked was surely because of his resolve to find the bodies of the dead women no matter what.

—Why wouldn’t I? Those terrifying people in our underground sometimes get dispatched too.

—Thank you.

Even so, as if it still weighed on him, Jin Hagun added one more thing.

—It is something that should not be handled that way, but…

—It can be.

Cutting off Jin Hagun’s hesitation, Geom Mugeuk was firm.

—If he is someone who killed nine innocent lives, it would be fine to handle him a hundred times more cruelly than this. And above all, the bodies must be returned to their families.

Jin Hagun silently nodded.

In the past, he would have been ashamed of the fact that he had not captured them himself.

The Demon Extermination Unit should have caught them. And now, when they had not even managed to capture them, he was asking them to find out where the bodies were buried?

He would have wasted his emotions on such useless thoughts.

But not anymore. He intended to attach a tassel to his sword, perhaps, but not useless pride.

“Take these men away.”

At Jin Hagun’s order, several warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit subdued the Lust Demon and Chujung and took them away.

Chuyeom staggered out of the place, looking as though he had been struck by a tremendous shock.

Jin Hagun started to say something to him, then stopped. What could he say in this situation? Whatever he said, would it even reach his ears? To escape from this shock—no, he would never be able to escape from it.

Jin Hagun expressed his gratitude to Geom Mugeuk.

“Thank you. Thanks to you, we prevented many sacrifices that would have happened in the future.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk gestured with both hands as though presenting Ian, indicating that thanks should be given to her.

“Thank you, Martial Artist Yi.”

Ian waved her hands and said,

“I didn’t do anything. I was just standing still.”

Still making a gesture of reverence with both hands, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Even when you stand still, flowers lower their heads, the moon hides behind the clouds, and lechers rush in saying, ‘Take me away.’”

“Young Master! Please!”

At the two of them fooling around, the atmosphere loosened. Only then did they share the joy of their reunion.

“Did only the two of you leave the cult?”

At Jin Hagun’s question, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“We thought the two of us would quietly and leisurely look around the Central Plains.”

Jin Hagun gave a faint smile. As if that would ever happen. Would fate, would murim, ever leave him that way? Already, the Lust Demon had walked up to him of his own accord, and the Tammi House incident had been resolved.

“Let’s go. It has been a long time, so we should catch up.”

“Regrettably, I don’t think I can today. I have to deal with the aftermath of this incident, and there are other matters too.”

He was glad and happy to have met Geom Mugeuk, but because of that, he was even more reluctant to go. Because if he was with Geom Mugeuk, he would end up laughing.

Jin Hagun sincerely wanted to save the victims who had been abducted. But knowing that they were all dead, he could not sit there laughing. At least not today.

How could the quick-witted Geom Mugeuk not understand his feelings? That was why he tried all the harder to drag him along. Because to him, a friend was more precious than the dead.

“You’re turning away a friend who walked a hundred thousand li through the night solely because he wanted to see you?”

“Everything you just said is wrong. Your original purpose probably wasn’t to come see me in the first place, you surely didn’t walk through the night, it certainly wasn’t a hundred thousand li, and I’m not turning you away.”

Geom Mugeuk glanced at Ian as if to ask what she was doing.

Ian quickly put on a sorrowful face and said,

“Young Cult Leader, it would not be proper to hold back someone so busy by force.”

But soon she let out a long sigh.

“Let’s go, Young Cult Leader. Let’s walk the hundred thousand li back again in tears.”

When even Ian joined in, Jin Hagun looked troubled. In the end, Jin Hagun nodded.

“Then just for a moment…”

Before he could even finish speaking, Geom Mugeuk and Ian grabbed Jin Hagun from both sides and pulled him along.

“We’re borrowing your commander!”

The warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit watched them with smiles. The elder masters of the Murim Alliance might still worry over this relationship, but the warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit, at least, acknowledged it. Because it was the only moment when Jin Hagun truly relaxed.

While pretending he could not resist and being dragged away, Jin Hagun gave various orders to his subordinates.

“Report to the Alliance first, take formal statements from those two…”

Gwanghyo, a warrior of the Demon Extermination Unit, said pleasantly,

“Leave the aftermath to us.”

With their arms linked with Jin Hagun’s on either side, Geom Mugeuk and Ian joked.

“Commander of the Demon Extermination Unit, you are under arrest too. You, of all people, need to taste the underground of our cult.”

“If you’re going to take me away, at least tell me the charge.”

“The charge is workaholism! You workworm.”

“Calling your friend a worm!”

Watching them noisily grow distant, Gwanghyo said,

“The Young Cult Leader has left the cult, so murim is going to become noisy again.”

***

The three of them sat facing one another at a teahouse in the marketplace.

Jin Hagun had firmly declined alcohol, so they decided to drink tea.

Geom Mugeuk first asked after Jin Paecheon.

“The Alliance Leader is well, I trust?”

“He is well.”

After the battle with the Heavenly Killing Star, his grandfather had been devoting himself to martial training. It was the first time he had ever seen his grandfather train so diligently. The battle with the Heavenly Killing Star had changed him.

“Grandfather believes the Great War between the Orthodox and Demonic factions may break out.”

The reason Jin Hagun had recently devoted himself even more to the Demon Extermination Unit’s work was partly because of that inexplicable tension.

At Jin Hagun’s honest words, Geom Mugeuk answered honestly as well.

“My father still has not given up.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk added,

“I have not given up either.”

Because he knew that meant stopping his father, Jin Hagun gave a slight nod.

A brief silence flowed between them.

Ian, savoring the scent of the tea, lightened the heavy atmosphere.

“Try drinking it. The fragrance is lovely.”

Jin Hagun could sense that she had become brighter.

“Martial Artist Yi seems even better than before.”

“Do you mean I’ve become prettier?”

When she asked so directly, Jin Hagun was slightly flustered.

“At times like this, you are supposed to answer, ‘That is so.’”

“That is so.”

When he answered as instructed, only then did Ian smile and say,

“I was originally this bold. I just lived hiding it while serving someone all this time. Don’t be surprised! From now on, the tails I’ve been hiding will come out one by one!”

What Jin Hagun had meant by saying she seemed better was that she had become brighter. In the past, she had not treated him this brightly.

It was probably because she had come out with Geom Mugeuk, just the two of them. This journey must be making her brighter and happier.

‘This journey may decide the fate of the two of them.’

Jin Hagun glanced at Geom Mugeuk.

‘What are you hesitating over so much?’

With such a beautiful woman at his side.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk took a sip of tea and said to Jin Hagun,

“You look tired. Work while resting a little.”

Recently, as Jin Hagun had immersed himself even more in the work of the Demon Extermination Unit, he had been in a constant state of tension.

“I would like to as well, but the number of incidents requiring the Demon Extermination Unit keeps increasing.”

Brutal incidents that ordinary Murim Alliance warriors could not resolve.

“I don’t know why, but I get the feeling people are growing more and more insane.”

Jin Hagun, who had been looking down at his teacup in thought for a moment, raised his head. Geom Mugeuk and Ian were staring at him intently.

“I’m sorry. I made the mood heavy for no reason.”

Fortunately, sitting in front of him was an expert capable of making a heavy mood infinitely light.

“You ruined the atmosphere, so you pay for the tea.”

A person who could make someone worrying over a world going mad burst into laughter.

As Geom Mugeuk quietly gazed at Jin Hagun, a certain resolve flashed through his eyes.

“Hey, friend.”

“What?”

Unexpected words came from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“Come with us.”

“Where?”

“It’s a journey where we go wherever our feet take us. We meet people we want to see, and if the timing doesn’t work out, we simply pass by. Truly, nothing is decided.”

How could he dislike a journey with Geom Mugeuk? He wanted to see Bi Sain and Han Seol too, and he could not even remember when he had last seen Haryeong.

“I have too much work. I can’t.”

“That’s exactly why you should go. From the look of you, you’re doing nothing but work.”

Above all, the reason he could not do so lay here.

“You two came out to spend good time together, didn’t you?”

“Who said we have to stay together until the end? We travel together, then part when the time comes. Who knows? You might meet a woman who says she likes you. When that happens, don’t even dream of leaving us behind! I’ll chase you all the way to the Alliance Leader’s Hall and watch the two of you kiss.”

Jin Hagun waved his hand with an expression of disbelief, but Geom Mugeuk had already made up his mind.

“Take a vacation!”

“Where does the Demon Extermination Unit have vacations?”

“Are the Demon Extermination Unit not people?”

“What about the incidents that happen in the meantime? Evil never rests. Don’t you know that?”

“Announce it to murim. The Demon Extermination Unit is going on vacation. Any bastards who cause trouble during this period will be chased all the way to hell and punished twice as hard. I’ll send a letter to our cult too telling them not to cause trouble. Let’s declare it Murim Peace Week.”

At the words “Murim Peace Week,” Jin Hagun laughed out loud. Even when he tried not to laugh, he made him laugh with such absurd nonsense.

“If they were the sort not to cause trouble because of that, there would be no need to worry in the first place.”

If they had punished so many villains, it seemed like it should have become peaceful by now, but as if it were a rule that the empty spaces left by villains would automatically be filled, villains kept appearing.

“Did you say evil never rests? You people are not evil, so you should rest.”

“What?”

“Your work is to find where the bodies of good women who were abducted and killed are buried, and to stand beside a father who has learned that his son became a lecher and killed women. You are enduring such work with nothing but righteousness and loyalty.”

As Jin Hagun wore a bitter smile, the decisive persuasion flew in.

“What about you aside, what about your subordinates?”

“!”

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Mugeuk. With no smile on his face, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“Do you think they are as strong as your willpower? I don’t think so. They are struggling to follow you at their limits.”

At those words, Jin Hagun recalled a conversation he had recently had with Jeonghyeok, one of his subordinates in the Demon Extermination Unit.

—How old is your son?

—Six years old.

—When did you last see him?

—I saw him last autumn and have not seen him since.

—……

—……

—He must have grown a lot since then.

—I suppose so.

He and the warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit had willingly sacrificed their own lives and charged forward. Of course, there was also an indescribable sense of reward. They were punishing evil and saving innocent lives.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been gazing at Jin Hagun, looked outside. After watching the people passing in front of the teahouse for a moment, Geom Mugeuk spoke calmly.

“If we go mad along with a world gone mad, then in the end, we lose.”

***

All the warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit assembled.

At their commander’s sudden summons, they were all tense.

Jin Hagun stood before them and looked at everyone. At his serious expression, they all assumed something had happened.

Jin Hagun handed out what he had brought to his subordinates. They were envelopes, one for each warrior of the Demon Extermination Unit.

The expressions of the Demon Extermination Unit warriors hardened. It had to be a life-risking operation.

He must be handing them out so they could write wills to leave for their families.

When they went out on dangerous operations, they wrote wills before leaving. But what kind of operation was it that required every single one of them to mobilize?

The moment they took out what was inside the envelopes, everyone was startled. Within were vouchers for a considerable sum. Jeong Hyeok asked on everyone’s behalf.

“What is this money for?”

The identity of the money was something they had never expected.

“Vacation expenses.”

“Pardon?”

“Starting today, you’re all on leave for a month.”

All the warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit looked shocked. It was more surprising than the news that the Orthodox-Demonic War had broken out.

“Take care of your families, visit your hometowns, go traveling. Do whatever you’ve each been putting off.”

Their many confused emotions unified into one.

Someone broke into a bright smile, and as that smile seemed to spread through the group, cheers erupted.

Jin Hagun had not expected his subordinates to be this happy. They were far happier than he had imagined. He had thought at least one of them might step forward and say it was all right,

and he had even prepared words to persuade them then, but no such person appeared.

Geom Mugeuk had been right. Though they did not show it outwardly, it seemed they had all been struggling to keep up.

“May we ask the reason?”

“I thought it was about time we rested as well.”

To that, one more truth was added. Jin Hagun did not bother dressing up the reason as though it were because he had been thinking of them.

“I was taken in by the Young Cult Master’s coaxing.”

At that honesty, all the warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit laughed.

“When you return, we’ll have to move busily for a while. So rest well before coming back.”

The warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit answered in thunderous voices.

“Thank you, Commander!”

“You should rest well too, Commander.”

The warriors of the Demon Extermination Unit knew just how hard Jin Hagun had worked until now. Because he was the Young Alliance Leader of the Murim Alliance, he had willingly taken on even tasks he could have avoided.

That was why they, too, had endured, and to them, this vacation was truly like sweet rain in a drought.

Once they had all dispersed, Geom Mugeuk and Ian appeared there.

Geom Mugeuk walked over, pretending to banter with Ian.

“A success. At last, we’ve disbanded the Murim Alliance’s elite organization for a whole month.”

“What’s the next step?”

“We lure the Young Alliance Leader into the swamp of pleasure until he says, ‘I don’t want to return to the Demon Extermination Unit!’”

“When you’re playing around, it may feel burdensome at first, but there’s nothing quite as fun as it once you get into it.”

Jin Hagun shook his head with a look of disbelief. If they had not rehearsed it behind his back, how could they be so perfectly in sync?

Of course, Jin Hagun had his counterattack as well.

“Now that there are three of us, the cozy atmosphere between you two is over.”

He had kept worrying that he might become a nuisance, but Geom Mugeuk spoke as if it had turned out for the better.

“When a common enemy appears, we’ll only unite more closely. We’ll secretly get up at night. You know what I mean, Ian?”

“What do I know!”

No matter how much of a joke it was, that was one remark she found difficult to accept, and Ian’s face turned red.

Seeing that, Jin Hagun laughed. Yes, if he was with these two.

Since he was going to rest anyway, he would do it properly. It would be the first and last carefree journey of his life.

“Don’t try to stop me now. From this moment on, I’m putting down both the title of Demon Extermination Unit Leader and Young Alliance Leader.”

Jin Hagun strode ahead first.

“Where are you going?”

“You said it was a journey where we go wherever our feet take us, didn’t you? Stop grumbling and follow me.”

Ian was delighted as she watched him. Already, she could sense vitality from Jin Hagun, who had looked so exhausted and weary.

‘You did well, young master.’

Geom Mugeuk walked after him together with Ian and shouted.

“Now, clear the way! The wayward Young Alliance Leader is coming through!”

Chapter 867: It's Not Your First Time Having Street Food, Is It?
----------------------------------------------------------------
A cart drawn by an ox was passing along the road that ran between the fields.

Riding in that cart were Geom Mugeuk, Ian, and Jin Hagun.

The three of them had been walking when the old man driving the cart offered to take them as far as the village.

Jin Hagun sat perched on the end of the cart, looking back, while Ian sat leaning against it. Geom Mugeuk had simply lain down in the cart with his eyes closed.

Jin Hagun had never sat in an ox-drawn cart before. It was also the first time he had ever traveled this slowly.

Creak, creak.

Each time the cart swayed, his dangling feet swayed with it. Beneath his feet, the dirt road they had traveled stretched farther and farther behind them.

The past two days had been so much fun.

The three of them had hunted together, roasted meat over a campfire and eaten it. They had admired magnificent scenery, then gone to an inn famous for its food and tried the dishes there. At night, they had fallen asleep while looking up at the stars.

Those two days had passed so quickly he hardly knew where they had gone.

But no matter how enjoyable it was, if the companion with him had not been Geom Mugeuk, he might already have resolved to stop playing around and return to martial arts training.

As Geom Mugeuk had said, it seemed a workaholic was a workaholic after all.

Jin Hagun glanced behind him.

Geom Mugeuk had fallen into a soft, even sleep, and Ian was looking down at him. There seemed to be many thoughts in the depths of her gaze.

Sensing Jin Hagun’s eyes on her, Ian turned her head. When their eyes met, she smiled brightly.

At that kind smile that lifted a person’s spirits, Jin Hagun gave a faint smile in return, then looked behind him again.

The last beauty just before the sun set dyed the world red.

At the blowing wind, the grasses bent low, then raised themselves again. It looked almost like flames spreading.

Like a boat crossing a sea of sunset, the cart continued onward.

By the time they arrived at the village, it was already dark, so they looked for an inn first.

“There are two rooms left.”

Three people, two rooms.

“Please have a look at the rooms first.”

The innkeeper guided the three of them to the rear courtyard where the guest rooms were.

“If you happen to be husband and wife, then this spacious room should suit you.”

This experienced innkeeper did not presume to guess who the husband was.

“Do we not look like husband and wife no matter who sees us? Come, Ian. Let’s use this room.”

Before Geom Mugeuk could take Ian away, Jin Hagun stepped forward first.

“I will sleep in this room with this friend, so please rest comfortably.”

“Interloper!”

Geom Mugeuk tried to shake off his hand, but Jin Hagun dragged him along.

Ian smiled and bowed politely.

“Thank you.”

She could tell that Jin Hagun was not interfering, but respecting and considering her. No matter that the other party was Geom Mugeuk, they were not yet husband and wife, so it was only proper to offer Ian a separate room.

In truth, Ian had stayed in the same guest room as Geom Mugeuk several times before. During the contest to select the greatest beauty under heaven, for instance, since their opponent had been an assassin, they had stayed in the same room.

“Ian, you know, right? Later, when the night grows deep…”

Jin Hagun forcibly dragged Geom Mugeuk into the room.

The room was spacious, with a large bed and even a separate small bed, so there seemed to be no inconvenience at all for two people to sleep there.

Jin Hagun opened the window wide. Lamps lit the darkened rear courtyard, making for quite an elegant scene.

“How does it feel on the third day?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Jin Hagun answered while looking out the window.

“I’m still not sure.”

He still felt as though he was wondering, Is it really all right to play around like this?

“Do you regret it?”

“No.”

More than regret, it felt unfamiliar. Already, for three days, he had done nothing. He wondered if there had ever truly been a time when he had lived so thoughtlessly. What was even more surprising was that there was nothing set for him to do tomorrow either.

“Grandfather must have been informed by now.”

Since this was something he had done without even receiving permission in advance, he was concerned about his grandfather, if no one else.

“The Alliance Leader would instead say you did well. He would say, ‘My grandson has finally found some ease.’”

It was probably something said to make him feel better, but hearing it still put his mind at ease.

“In that case, I should use the large bed.”

“There is absolutely no correlation there!”

But Jin Hagun was already throwing himself onto the large bed.

***

The next morning, Jin Hagun opened his eyes.

When he turned his head, Geom Mugeuk was not in bed.

Jin Hagun rose from the bed and drew back the curtain. The moment he looked outside, he was startled.

He had thought it would be dawn or early morning, but the sun was already high in the sky.

‘Have I ever slept this late in my life?’

Aside from when he had been very young, this was the first time.

He could not believe he had slept until so late. His nerves were always on edge, and he had never slept properly; he would wake several times a night.

To think he had slept so deeply, oblivious to the world! If someone had launched a surprise attack, he would have died.

Just then, the door opened and Geom Mugeuk came in.

“You’re awake?”

“Why did you not wake me?”

“I just woke up too.”

Geom Mugeuk did not forget to make a joke about this late morning.

“I slept without waking even once too. Last night, the successors of the Murim Alliance and the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult nearly changed! My elder brother almost shouted hurray!”

Jin Hagun realized he could not tell anyone about this. If the people of the righteous faction found out, they would call him mad. How could he fall so sound asleep while sleeping beside the successor of the Divine Cult?

Then Ian’s voice came from outside.

“If you’re awake, let’s go look around the village. I went scouting, and as for food, let’s eat outside.”

Jin Hagun merely washed his face and left the inn with the two of them.

The marketplace at lunchtime was bustling with people.

Was it because he had not eaten breakfast? The place where their footsteps naturally came to a halt was a cart selling street food. Meat and vegetables had been grilled and skewered, and the aroma whetting their appetites was downright violent.

After reaching an agreement through glances with the other two, Geom Mugeuk ordered three.

Jin Hagun pulled the meat off the skewer with his teeth and marveled at the taste.

“It’s delicious!”

He had seemed like he would eat in a dignified manner, but after finishing faster than anyone else, he ordered and ate one more.

“Don’t tell me this is your first time eating street food.”

“It is.”

Geom Mugeuk was not the sort to let this pass.

“You look like you were raised terribly rough, but you were more pampered than you seem.”

“What about me looks that way?”

Jin Hagun wiped the sauce from his mouth with his sleeve. If he had been with his subordinates, it would have been an unimaginable act.

It was an intentionally deliberate deviation, but that alone gave Jin Hagun a great sense of liberation.

Of course, it was even more fun because there were people beside him widening their eyes and saying things like this.

“Ian, it seems we brought the wrong person. The next Murim Alliance Leader sleeping late, gluttonizing, and wiping his mouth with his sleeve?”

“What should we do? It seems we were deceived.”

Pretending not to hear, Jin Hagun paid for what they had eaten.

“I will pay for the skewers.”

Geom Mugeuk shouted.

“And one more thing! Now he’s showing off too!”

Jin Hagun moved on. There was much to eat where the carts were lined up.

This time, it was noodles.

It was also Jin Hagun’s first time eating noodles while standing on the street. It was also the first time he learned that such delicious noodles could be so cheap. Even if Geom Mugeuk teased him about having been raised pampered, he had nothing to say.

After thus taking a simple meal, they went to the stalls and looked at the goods for sale.

‘They sell all sorts of things.’

He had passed through countless marketplaces, but now that he thought about it, he had never once been curious. What they sold there, how much the prices were. He had had no interest to begin with.

Whenever he walked down a road, he thought only of his destination. He thought only of the people he would meet there, and focused only on how he would resolve the matter.

The only time he paid attention to the roadside was when there was a possibility of an ambush.

He had ultimately worked so hard in order to protect these people, yet he felt that he knew nothing about them.

Just then, Jin Hagun’s eyes caught sight of the swindlers.

“Come, come! Guess right and get three times your money! Guess wrong, and we just take what you bet! Three times!”

When Jin Hagun showed interest, Geom Mugeuk came over and urged him on while pretending to stop him.

“Don’t tell me you’re going to gamble now?”

“Why would I not?”

Jin Hagun walked over there.

The swindlers ran their shell game with their backs to an alley from which they could flee at any time.

Jin Hagun stood among the onlookers and carefully watched the swindler’s hand movements.

Then he placed a coin on the plate at the far right.

“I will win and treat you to dinner too.”

That confidence lasted only a moment.

When the plate was lifted, the die was in the middle.

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Mugeuk with a startled expression.

“It was definitely on the right.”

“The better your eyes are, the more you get fooled. You have to know the principle behind the trick.”

Jin Hagun shook his head as if amazed. Geom Mugeuk smiled and said in a low voice.

“I want to tell them. Do you know how historic a day this is in your swindling lives?”

Jin Hagun bet two more times, but failed both times.

“If I keep going, I’ll go bankrupt.”

Seeing him leave so readily, Geom Mugeuk asked.

“You’re just going?”

“If I do not?”

“You scoundrels! You are deceiving good people with trickery! You won’t punish them?”

“A fool who falls for that is the foolish one.”

“Ooh! Just now, I thought you were of the unorthodox faction.”

Geom Mugeuk turned and said behind him.

“Ian, this friend is already starting to fall. Even if we don’t bother tempting him…”

Ian was up ahead, busy choosing items at a street general store. She held up a small comb and waved it.

“Please buy me this.”

“Buy it with your own money.”

“No, Young Master should buy it for me.”

“I refuse.”

Then Jin Hagun spoke as if frustrated.

“Just buy her one. How much could that cost?”

Geom Mugeuk shouted to Ian.

“Hagun orabeoni here says he will buy it for you.”

Jin Hagun flinched in confusion.

“Orabeoni?”

“You said you were putting down all titles like Alliance Leader and unit leader, did you not? Then you are orabeoni. What else should she call you? Mr. Jin? Young Jin? Young Hero Jin?”

Hearing it, it was not exactly wrong.

“Do not treat this warrior carelessly. Telling her to call me orabeoni is rude to her as well.”

“You have returned from the unorthodox faction to the righteous faction?”

At those words, Jin Hagun could not hold back and let out a small laugh. Yes, no matter how deliberately he tried to deviate, could his nature change so easily?

Jin Hagun went to Ian and paid for the comb.

“Thank you. I’ll have to leave this as a family heirloom!”

“It is nothing.”

Ian repaid the price of the comb with a piece of advice.

“When you date a woman later, you must not do everything she asks you to do.”

When he looked at her with eyes asking why, Ian answered.

“If you do too much for her, she’ll come to think of it as natural. Somehow, I feel like Young Alliance Leader would do everything.”

She had seen him well. If he were to date a woman, he felt he would most likely grant nearly all her wishes.

“I think being good at refusing is actually a way to protect love.”

Perhaps embarrassed after saying it, Ian smiled bashfully.

“I just picked that up from somewhere. Honestly, I don’t really know either. Ah, I’ll go look over there.”

Ian went into a weapons shop, and Geom Mugeuk went into the clothing store across the street.

“Do you perhaps have floral-patterned pajamas here?”

Watching them, Jin Hagun shook his head.

“Look here, you two have switched places!”

There was nothing new about the marketplace, but being here with those two made it a new world. It was so much fun.

The place Jin Hagun ambled toward was an open lot in the middle of the marketplace. There, medicine peddlers had gathered people and were selling their wares.

“Come, come! If children take this medicine for just three months, they will shoot up tall!”

Jin Hagun stood among the people and watched their performance.

Just as Jin Paecheon had once enjoyed the amusement of sitting in a tavern where no one recognized him, Jin Hagun likewise savored the pleasure of blending in among people like this, with no one recognizing who he was.

“Then what happens if an adult takes it?”

A muscular man tightened his abdomen, and the wooden club the medicine peddler swung at him snapped in half.

“For children, it all goes into height, and for adults, it all goes into muscle.”

Jin Hagun laughed.

‘That is an effect not even the Murim Alliance’s spiritual medicines can produce.’

As he was watching, someone spoke from behind him.

“Has some mysterious force disguised as medicine peddlers appeared?”

When he turned around, Jin Haryeong was standing there.

“You found me well.”

Her elder brother had sent an urgent message saying to meet him here. She had rushed over, startled by the sudden message, but his expression looked more relaxed than she had expected.

“I came with Young Cult Leader Geom. Warrior Ian is with us as well.”

At the news that Geom Mugeuk and Ian had come, Jin Haryeong’s eyes widened. And she was even more surprised by what followed.

“I took leave. The entire Demon Extermination Unit is on leave.”

“You did, Brother? Truly?”

She looked at him in disbelief, then soon nodded. Geom Mugeuk must have persuaded this stubborn brother of hers.

“I thought you’d want to see him too.”

As Jin Haryeong watched him showing off his muscles and then suddenly spinning a plate, she abruptly said,

“Aren’t you worried? I still haven’t completely given up my feelings for him.”

“I am worried.”

“Then why did you call me?”

She had been surprised by the fact that her brother had personally sent the message. If anyone were to contact her, it would be Geom Mugeuk, not her brother.

“Because that’s how people’s hearts are. The more you stop them, the more they want to do it; the more they don’t see someone, the more they want to see them.”

“You mean I should meet him and then give up?”

Jin Hagun could not answer that it wasn’t so. How could a human relationship be severed simply because people didn’t see each other? You had to see them in order to cut it off.

“Well, if I end up with him, you’ll be plagued by all sorts of rumors for your entire tenure as Alliance Leader.”

For those of the righteous path, especially the old masters and eminent seniors, it was something absolutely unacceptable. Political enemies would even arise, accusing Jin Hagun of colluding with the Demonic Cult.

“If I’d met him as someone of ordinary status, you wouldn’t have opposed it then, would you?”

Since her brother liked Geom Mugeuk this much, she had expected he would have actively supported them.

Unexpectedly, Jin Hagun shook his head.

“You would have opposed it?”

“Yes.”

“Why?”

“Because I think you would have suffered.”

Jin Hagun conveyed to his younger sister the feelings he had never spoken until now.

“You must feel it too, but that friend was born with the fate of becoming the protagonist of the murim. He was born with a destiny to save the murim rather than to be devoted to his woman. I want my sister to live as the protagonist of her own life.”

Jin Haryeong found a flaw in her brother’s persuasion.

“Then what about Ian? Is it all right if she isn’t the protagonist?”

She thought that, given her brother’s personality, he would be flustered.

“Martial Artist Lee is not my sister.”

Because she could tell he meant it sincerely, Jin Haryeong was touched, yet at the same time surprised.

“Are you sick or something? Do you have some fatal illness?”

At Jin Haryeong’s half-serious, half-joking words, Jin Hagun calmly conveyed the rest of his feelings.

“You never received your parents’ love, Grandfather was busy as the Alliance Leader, and this brother of yours was busy with the affairs of the Demon Extermination Unit. I’d like it if at least your husband were someone who would always stay by your side and take good care of you.”

Because if it was his sister, even if it wasn’t Geom Mugeuk, she would surely be able to meet a truly good man.

“Not because I’m selfish and only think of myself?”

“There is that too.”

“You’re even saying things like this to me. Brother, you really have changed a lot.”

The two looked at each other and smiled.

“If you really can’t manage it, then I’ll just give up being Alliance Leader.”

“Where are the two people saying things they don’t even mean?”

“Over there, at the weapons shop and the fabric store.”

Jin Haryeong strode off toward that place.

As he watched her enter the weapons shop, Jin Hagun said quietly,

“That’s not the right one.”

Chapter 868: Not a Bear Doll but a Wolf Doll
--------------------------------------------
Ian was looking at an iron sword in a weapon shop.

The iron sword she was looking at was not one of those expensive, ornate blades with decorated scabbards displayed in the center. It was one of several swords propped up behind the featured merchandise like mere part of the backdrop.

The shopkeeper cautiously approached.

“Does this sword suit your fancy? You’ve been looking at nothing but this one for a while now.”

Having run a weapon shop for many years, he had encountered countless martial artists. He had already intuitively grasped that she was a master who did not belong in a place like this.

“For some reason, this sword has caught my eye.”

Perhaps it felt as though the sword placed behind the flashy ones was looking at her.

To say nothing of Geom Mugeuk, most of the people she met while traveling with him were flamboyant ones.

The young leaders of the Murim Alliance and the Demonic Alliance, demon lords, the Wind Heaven Cult Leader, the Demon Monarch, the Yellow Heaven Pavilion Master—even the subordinates she met, people like Cheongmyeon and Chairan, and even her enemies were all fearsome individuals.

And yet she herself, who had been a bodyguard martial artist, was like that sword standing behind the flashy ones.

“Why don’t you draw it?”

“I feel as though I know what kind of sword it is even without drawing it.”

The shopkeeper’s gaze turned to the sword she wore at her waist.

“It seems you already carry an excellent sword.”

Her sword was the Ilwol Sword, a gift from Geom Mugeuk.

“A gift from an excellent person, is it not?”

Just then, someone spoke from the entrance.

“Might that excellent person be the one across the street picking out pajamas?”

Standing at the entrance was Jin Haryeong. She had naturally entered expecting Geom Mugeuk to be here, but it was Ian. Geom Mugeuk was across the street at a fabric shop picking out pajamas.

Ian answered with a bright smile toward her friend she hadn’t seen in a long time.

“Probably.”

Jin Haryeong approached Ian’s side. The shopkeeper quickly returned to his place. What day was it? For such beauties to arrive one after another?

“What kind of trip is this, exactly? My brother used up all his vacation days.”

“According to the Young Cult Leader, it’s a journey to find himself.”

“He sure knows how to slap together excuses just to have fun.”

Jin Haryeong felt that Ian’s energy had changed from before. Not that she could tell exactly what the difference was.

*She’s gotten stronger again in that short time.*

Not only that, but her eyes and aura felt different somehow. A woman’s intuition that some kind of inner change had taken place.

Ian asked Jin Haryeong,

“Have you eaten?”

“No.”

“The skewers in this town are delicious. Let’s go, I’ll buy you some.”

Jin Haryeong grabbed the arm of Ian, who was about to walk out.

“I’m interested in the Young Cult Leader.”

Even though it was sudden, Ian was neither surprised nor flustered.

“I know.”

“Is that all you have to say?”

“What else?”

The two women’s gazes tangled in the air.

“You should be grilling me. The Young Cult Leader calls me his heart, and yet you, his friend, are interested in him? You should be calling me shameless.”

Ian, who had been silently staring at her, looked around and said,

“It’s dangerous to argue in a place with so many swords. Let’s go—when you’re hungry, your nerves are sharper.”

But Jin Haryeong had already made up her mind to speak.

“This isn’t something to talk about while smiling.”

There was something she had wanted to ask for a long time. Setting aside her own feelings for Geom Mugeuk, it was something she had been frustrated and curious about as a fellow woman.

“Why are you just staying as you are? That person calls you his heart. Do you think it’s a joke?”

“No. He may play all sorts of pranks, but he is not someone who plays with people’s hearts.”

“Then why?”

At the look in Ian’s eyes asking if she really had to answer, Jin Haryeong responded by not letting go of her arm.

An unexpected answer flowed from Ian’s lips.

“I’ve never just stayed as I am until now.”

“!”

“Since I first met the young master at seven years old, not for a single moment until now have I ever just stayed as I was.”

Jin Haryeong, who had been silently staring at Ian, let go of the arm she held.

Ian was the first to move her steps.

“Let’s go, you must be hungry.”

The two who had exited the weapon shop stood before it and looked across the street at the fabric shop.

Inside, they could see Geom Mugeuk talking with the shopkeeper. Jin Haryeong chuckled at the sight of the shopkeeper bursting into laughter.

“He can’t help himself. Chatting away over there too.”

At Jin Haryeong’s words, Ian looked at the other side of the street.

“Your brother is no slouch either.”

Jin Haryeong turned her head and was startled.

Jin Hagyun was helping medicine peddlers with a demonstration. He was holding a log together with a peddler—a log that had undoubtedly been pre-broken.

A muscular man ran in and headbutted the log they were holding.

*Crack.*

When the log snapped in half, the onlookers cheered.

Her brother mingling with medicine peddlers? It was truly unimaginable.

“What on earth is this trip? Did everyone decide to go crazy?”

“It’s the first time I’ve seen the Young Leader laugh like that.”

As Ian said, Jin Hagyun was smiling brightly.

“You’re right. It’s the first time I’ve seen my brother smile so brightly.”

Jin Haryeong, who had been watching for a moment, spoke to Ian.

“Tell him I left first. I’m busy; I only stopped by for a moment to see his face.”

As she was about to leave, this time Ian grabbed her arm.

“Come with us on this trip.”

Jin Haryeong glared at her and said,

“You must be confident. I’m Jin Haryeong, the greatest beauty of Hubei. The granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader and the sister of the next Alliance Leader. Do you have the confidence to handle me?”

“It’s not because I’m confident that I’m asking you to come.”

“Then why?”

An unexpected answer came from Ian.

“Because I want to travel with a friend.”

“!”

“Both the young master and your brother are so happy to meet friends. I want to do this trip with a friend too.”

Jin Haryeong said incredulously,

“This really is a trip where everyone decided to go crazy.”

Ian didn’t let go of her arm.

“The skewer vendor is that way.”

***

“Happy birthday.”

First Wolf of the Demonic Alliance’s Thirteen Wolves held out a small box to Bisain.

“This is a gift we prepared.”

“I told you I don’t need gifts.”

“It’s nothing much. Just a small token of our regard, so please accept it.”

When he opened the box, it was an accessory for clothing—a symbol of the Demonic Alliance in the shape of a wolf, made of gold.

“It’s impressive.”

Bisain pinned it to his chest.

“You didn’t have to pin it on.”

“I’ll wear it for today. Thank you for the gift.”

Bisain conveyed his gratitude to the other Thirteen Wolves standing behind as well. A heartfelt gratitude flickered across his fearsome, rugged face.

“Thank you, all of you.”

The Thirteen Wolves saluted in unison with cupped fists.

“Happy birthday.”

First Wolf reported to Bisain,

“By the way, I’ve reserved a VIP seat at Hwaryang Pavilion. I’ve also had them prepare the dishes you mentioned.”

Originally, a grand banquet would be held on the birthday of the Demonic Alliance’s Young Leader. However, Bisain had forbidden birthday banquets from being held several years ago.

He found it burdensome dealing with people who wanted to connect with him through expensive gifts, awkward to exchange greetings with those he could barely remember, and he did not desire having all attention focused on him at banquets.

He had intended to spend his birthday quietly at Hwaryang Pavilion today.

It was precisely as they were walking toward Hwaryang Pavilion that it happened.

*Shwiiik.*

A throwing dagger flew toward Bisain.

*Chaeng.*

First Wolf struck down the flying dagger with his sword.

“It’s an ambush!”

Four of the Thirteen Wolves leaped in the direction the dagger had come from. The remaining nine surrounded Bisain to protect him.

The Thirteen Wolves who had gone to find the ambusher returned empty-handed.

“The culprit vanished without leaving a trace.”

First Wolf, who had deflected the dagger, could tell. It wasn’t an attack launched to kill Bisain. Because if it were, the attack was far too straightforward.

Sure enough, a letter was tied to the dagger that had fallen to the ground.

First Wolf took out a small medicine bottle from within his clothes and sprinkled a little on the dagger and letter.

“There is no poison.”

First Wolf unfolded the letter and brought it to Bisain.

Bisain was startled when he checked the letter. Just by looking at his expression, one could guess the contents were no trifling matter.

“What does it say?”

Bisain showed the contents of the letter to First Wolf.

—Come alone to the Jujube Manor twenty li to the west. If you do not come, Hanseol dies.

***

Bisain arrived at the manor.

Seeing the main gate tilted diagonally, fallen from its hinges, he could tell this place had long been abandoned.

He crossed the yard littered with scattered gravel and trash and entered the central building.

Upon entering the main hall, there were four people there.

A chair was placed in the center, and a woman sat upon it. Her face was covered with a black cloth sack, making her unrecognizable.

Three figures in black stood around her. They wore black martial uniforms and conical hats with gauze veils, making their faces unrecognizable. The man in the center spoke coldly.

“Bisain, Young Leader of the Demonic Alliance.”

His voice was an incredibly low bass—so low that it seemed almost impossible to produce such a tone deliberately.

“You really came alone to save a woman.”

The man in the conical hat let out a despicable laugh.

“So the rumors that the Demonic Alliance’s Young Leader is enamored with the young palace mistress of the Northern Sea Ice Palace were true.”

Bisain’s gaze turned toward the woman sitting in the chair. Even at rest, Bisain’s face was fearsome, but as it hardened, it became even more terrifying.

“Why is her face covered?”

“Because we have questions. It’s a question too difficult to answer while looking face to face.”

The man in the conical hat pressed a sword to Hanseol’s neck and asked,

“Hanseol, do you know that the Young Leader favors you?”

Hanseol, who had hesitated briefly, slowly nodded.

“Do you also favor the Young Leader?”

Though she pondered longer than before, Hanseol slowly nodded.

The man in the conical hat said with a laugh,

“What a beautiful love. A love where you offer your lives for each other.”

Just then, Bisain spoke abruptly,

“I wasn’t planning on giving up my life, though.”

A silence suggesting they might have misheard flowed.

They had not misheard. Because Bisain drew his sword without hesitation.

*Wuuung.*

Sword qi enveloped his blade.

“How dare you stage a kidnapping against me!”

The man in the conical hat pressed his sword even tighter against Hanseol’s neck and warned,

“Don’t act rashly. If you so much as twitch a finger, Hanseol dies.”

Bisain let out a long whistle.

Shortly after, the Thirteen Wolves of the Demonic Alliance who had been waiting outside stormed into the manor.

“If Hanseol dies, the Northern Sea Ice Palace won’t stand idly by.”

Bisain didn’t bat an eye at the threat. He even added more.

“Her death will be framed as the work of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

The man in the conical hat flinched in surprise. The other two men in conical hats also seemed startled.

“Kill them all!”

“Then Hanseol will die!”

But the Thirteen Wolves charged in unison at the command.

As they flew at them simultaneously, the masked figure standing in the middle shouted instead of killing the woman.

“Wait! We surrender! Stop the attack!”

The charging Thirteen Wolves froze with their swords pointed from all directions.

The man in the conical hat slowly removed the black sack that had been pulled over Hanseol’s head.

The woman inside was Ian. She said with a surprised face,

“......Happy birthday, Young Leader Bi.”

Soon after, the man removed the conical hat he was wearing. He was none other than Geom Mugeuk, who had been acting with a disguised voice. The other two removed their hats as well, and of course they were Jin Hagyun and Jin Haryeong.

Jin Haryeong said with a dumbfounded face,

“So we almost died trying to throw a surprise birthday party, is that right?”

Jin Hagyun also said with an incredulous expression,

“He attacked without even checking when there’s a hostage?”

Ian fanned her face with her hand.

“I thought I would die of heat wearing that thing.”

Geom Mugeuk shouted in anger,

“Are you crazy? Ian put her whole heart into this performance, are you crazy! What if something happened to Miss Han!”

Not only Ian, but he himself had thoroughly concealed his energy, disguised his voice, and even swapped the sword he wore for an ordinary iron sword.

Bisain smiled leisurely and revealed the truth.

“I knew it was you lot from the start. If it weren’t you, who would dare kidnap Miss Han and send me such a letter?”

Bisain had known it was something Geom Mugeuk had done.

His words about framing the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had also been to tease Geom Mugeuk.

“As if you could deceive me.”

Bi Sa-in smiled in satisfaction, as if he had pulled it off. The Sado Thirteen Wolves must have known beforehand as well, for their expressions were relaxed.

Naturally, Geom Mugeuk and the other three believed his words were true.

Because if he had truly thought Han Seol had been kidnapped, he would never have brought the Sado Thirteen Wolves with him. Because he was not the sort of person who would shift the blame for Han Seol’s death onto the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

How on earth had he known?

Before uncovering that, Geom Mugeuk did not let this fine opportunity to tease Bi Sa-in pass him by.

Geom Mugeuk’s counterattack began.

“Liar! Now that you’ve been caught, you’re making excuses and saying you knew all along!”

By now, when it came to Geom Mugeuk’s pranks, they understood him with the slightest cue. As if they had all been waiting, each of them chimed in.

“I’m disappointed!”

“So today we witness the heartless jianghu firsthand.”

Following Ian and Jin Haryeong, Jin Hagun joined in as well.

“Have we been deceived all this time?”

“It means all that affection was fake. Lady Han, you were fooled. This cold-blooded heir of the unorthodox faction had no such thing as love for you.”

Bi Sa-in, who had been smiling at ease, grew flustered.

“I told you, I knew! Don’t misunderstand for no reason.”

But they were not the sort to let go once they had sunk their teeth into prey.

The hunter leading the charge was Jin Hagun. He recalled something from Bi Sa-in’s past—when Bi Sa-in had secretly strolled through the marketplace with Han Seol.

“That bear doll!”

Jin Hagun had seen Bi Sa-in dressed in white, walking around with Han Seol while carrying a bear doll, and had realized the nature of their relationship.

Well said! Geom Mugeuk’s eyes flashed sharply as he revealed the true nature of the bear doll.

“It wasn’t a cute bear doll. Hidden inside was a terrifying wolf doll meant to seize the Northern Sea Ice Palace. Look over there! He’s even wearing one on his chest!”

Ian and Jin Haryeong added their voices.

“I think I’ll be frightened every time I see a bear doll from now on.”

“I should start by throwing away the bear doll at home.”

The two women looked at each other and smiled with their eyes. Living as martial artists, when would they ever get a chance to tease and attack the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance like this? When they were with Geom Mugeuk, they truly ended up experiencing things they could never have imagined.

Geom Mugeuk remembered something else.

“Ah, the sweets you bought Lady Han in the marketplace that day must have been poisoned!”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Jin Hagun with sincere eyes.

“I owe you an apology. Sleeping in late, getting food on your clothes, showing off, gambling—none of that was anything before this enormous evil. We failed to see the true great mass beneath the glacier.”

“I accept your apology.”

Listening to them, Bi Sa-in shook his head.

“How long are you going to keep this up?”

“We’ve only just begun! Until we strip away every bit of that hypocritical mask of yours!”

A triumphant smile returned to Bi Sa-in’s lips. As if he had been waiting for this very moment, his final counterattack began.

“If they had said they’d kidnapped you lot instead, this deception would have succeeded. Then I really would have come alone.”

At last, the reason was revealed.

The reason he had realized this kidnapping farce was a prank from the very moment he first received the letter walked out from among the Sado Thirteen Wolves.

The person he was supposed to eat with today at Hwayang Pavilion—Han Seol, dressed in pure white.

Seeing her awkward smile, Geom Mugeuk and Jin Hagun shouted at the same time.

No, you little—!

“You were together again, just the two of you?”

Chapter 869: Did I Make a Drunken Scene?
----------------------------------------
The four of them pressed in on the two.

Their target was not the hot and frightening one, but the cold and beautiful one.

“You didn’t even send word that you’d come.”

When Geom Mugeuk struck first, Ian fell in step with him.

“Even I didn’t know, and I’m your only older cousin.”

Jin Hagun, too, made a joke he never would have made to Han Seol under normal circumstances.

“Last time you went to the Divine Cult, and after that to the Evil Dao Alliance, so I thought you’d come to our Alliance this time.”

At Jin Hagun’s words, Jin Haryeong recalled how Geom Mugeuk had once asked Han Seol about her ideal type.

“This is all because my brother isn’t dependable and talks too much.”

Jin Hagun looked aggrieved. He had always been famous for being dependable and taciturn. And to think he had to hear that he talked too much in front of Geom Mugeuk, of all people.

“Now that I can’t let slide!”

With Geom Mugeuk as the vanguard, the other three followed behind and pressed in.

At that, Han Seol took one step back, and Bi Sain stepped in front of her. He had moved without thinking, but when he saw the mischief flicker across Geom Mugeuk’s face, he inwardly thought, Ah, damn.

“I’ve been mistaken all this time.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Jin Hagun standing beside him as he spoke.

“I thought you were the Young Alliance Leader of the Murim Alliance.”

Jin Hagun wondered what he meant by that.

“To anyone looking, we’re the villains, and that fellow is the righteous hero protecting a lady, isn’t he?”

Jin Hagun nodded and replied.

“Turns out I was with the Evil Dao Alliance all along.”

At her brother’s frivolous joke, Jin Haryeong let out a laugh. Hadn’t he put on a performance with a medicine peddler? And now he was making such jokes so calmly? Today, she was seeing many sides of her brother she had never seen before.

Geom Mugeuk cried out with solemn resolve.

“I never dreamed the first war of our era would be a war between the Demon-Righteous Coalition and the Evil-Ice Coalition.”

Bi Sain hurriedly waved his hands and said,

“Don’t misunderstand. It’s not what you’re thinking. It’s just…”

“Just what!”

Han Seol, who had been watching their antics, finally opened her mouth.

“It’s Young Alliance Leader Bi’s birthday, so I came to congratulate him. It’s been a long time, everyone.”

Han Seol greeted Ian separately.

“Unni, have you been well?”

“From now on, I’ll hear how you’re doing through Young Alliance Leader Bi.”

Everyone could tell that Han Seol also had an interest in Bi Sain. Would she really come all that way in person just to celebrate his birthday?

If she had no interest in Bi Sain, it would have been impossible.

And Han Seol made no particular effort to hide that fact.

“Young Alliance Leader Bi is the kindest to me.”

A slight blush crossed Bi Sain’s face. Spring had come to that terrifying face.

“I said you looked good when you smiled, but not right now!”

Geom Mugeuk became an obstruction to love and laid out his formation.

“Since I’ve become the villain anyway, I shall gladly become a perilous trial standing in the way of your affections. If you cannot find the method to break it, your love will never come to pass. I’ll follow you to the ends of the earth and interfere!”

At that, Bi Sain suddenly asked,

“But among the people here, is there even anyone who knows the method to break through love?”

When Geom Mugeuk could not offer a single rebuttal, Jin Haryeong became the fair judge.

“It seems the Young Alliance Leader found the method to break it at once?”

The trial was shattered, and Geom Mugeuk admitted defeat.

“Good grief! He’s not a bear, and he’s not a wolf. He’s a fox, a fox.”

At that, everyone laughed. Thus, the greeting that had been like a whole commotion came to an end.

Bi Sain asked them,

“What on earth happened?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Jin Hagun and said,

“Hagun took a vacation. Which means the chance has come for you and us to strike the Murim Alliance.”

Bi Sain looked at Jin Hagun. Normally, he would have asked, “Are you all right?” Even that would have been a question he could ask because they had grown close, but now Bi Sain said this instead.

“I’m envious.”

He was truly envious. Of the courage to put into action what he wanted to do.

“There’s still a place for you, too.”

Bi Sain could tell. The reason they had come to see him was not because of his birthday, but to ask him to travel with them.

Of course I should go.

The words rose all the way to his throat. But he could not say them.

He was not in a position where he could simply go just because he wanted to, and more than anything, Han Seol was here.

Bi Sain turned to Illang and said,

“My friends have come from afar, so please prepare the finest wine.”

***

Illang rented out the entirety of Hwayang Pavilion.

Originally, he had rented only one VIP room, but the situation had changed. This was a gathering of the successors of the three paths that would one day become the masters of the murim: Evil, Demonic, and Righteous.

Their safety was one thing, but there was also the possibility that some information might leak out.

The Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao guarded the area around it like an iron wall, and they even brought the wine and food themselves.

Bi Sain poured wine for everyone. He was so glad and happy that his friends had come.

“Thank you for coming like this.”

Everyone set aside their playfulness and congratulated him sincerely.

“Congratulations.”

“Happy birthday!”

Though he had not even drunk yet, Bi Sain already felt intoxicated.

The Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Young Alliance Leader of the Murim Alliance were together at the birthday celebration of the Young Alliance Leader of the Evil Dao Alliance? It was something that had never happened in the history of the murim, and likely never would again.

They were undoubtedly leaving a mark on the history of the murim.

“Now, to celebrate Sain’s birthday! Cheers!”

Everyone drank the first cup in good spirits. Since the finest wine in Hwayang Pavilion had been brought out, it went down smoothly.

After drinking a cup, Bi Sain offered his congratulations to Geom Mugeuk as well.

“Congratulations on repelling the Heaven-Slaying Star.”

It was the first time he had met Geom Mugeuk since the Heaven-Slaying Star had been repelled.

“It was only possible because the two Alliance Leaders helped.”

He attributed the credit to the Alliance Leaders, but Bi Sain knew that in the end, the ones who had killed and stopped him were the Cult Leader of the Demonic Path and Geom Mugeuk. After returning from the battle, Baek Jagang had called him and told him everything that had happened.

Since the subject had come up, Geom Mugeuk spoke honestly.

“The battle with the Heaven-Slaying Star is over, but I am still fighting.”

They knew whom he was fighting without him having to say it.

“No one can predict what situations will unfold from now on.”

As the atmosphere naturally grew heavy, Geom Mugeuk continued speaking. This was something he had intended to tell them before he got drunk.

“So I want to tell you one principle I’ve set for myself.”

At the word principle, everyone looked at Geom Mugeuk. And that principle was astonishing.

“No matter what situation arises, I will not attack you.”

Then came another astonishing line.

“Even if I die.”

“!”

They all understood. Geom Mugeuk was speaking from the heart.

“Ah, of course, I don’t mean you are more important than I am. My life is far more precious.”

Even so, the reason he said this was as follows.

“If our relationship breaks apart, it won’t be because we lack trust. It will be because of the situation.”

Geom Mugeuk gave an example of the most extreme situation among them.

“The lives of hundreds of demonic practitioners are at stake. If I don’t attack you, they all die. The reverse could also be true.

If that kind of situation arises, no matter how close we are, we’ll end up suspecting each other. Since the circumstances can’t be helped, Hagun will attack.

Sain will attack. That loud-mouthed demonic bastard will attack.”

Everyone listened to Geom Mugeuk in silence.

“That’s why I’m telling you this principle now. Even if such a situation comes, I will not attack you.

I’ll probably be trying to solve it another way. So don’t waste your time suspecting what I might do. Use that time to find another way yourselves.”

Only now did they understand clearly why Geom Mugeuk had spoken of such a principle. It meant they should not waste mental energy on useless concerns and should deal with things as wisely as possible.

And how could Geom Mugeuk be the only one who felt that way? Jin Hagun stated his own will in a calm tone.

“I, too, will never draw my sword against you.”

Bi Sain likewise conveyed his sincerity.

“I swear it as well. I will never attack you.”

No more words were needed. The three of them raised their cups high at the same time and toasted.

The three women watching could feel it. What they were seeing now might perhaps be the unification of the murim.

If not the Young Cult Leader and the Young Alliance Leaders, but the Cult Leader and the Alliance Leaders were to hold this exact same gathering, then at that time, true and perfect unification of the murim would be achieved.

A unification of the murim even more perfect than one where the strongest power destroyed the other two forces. The most perfect unification of the murim, where they respected one another, did not invade one another, and lived together.

Of course, even within this solemn vow, Geom Mugeuk did not forget to tease the main character of the day.

“Of course, if you go off secretly with a woman behind your friend’s back, then I might draw my sword.”

Bi Sain cheerfully accepted Geom Mugeuk’s joke.

“As long as it isn’t a matter of kidnapping that woman, I’ll never have reason to draw my sword either.”

The three of them laughed together and emptied their cups again.

***

Ian was outside, taking in the night breeze.

It had been a long time since she had drunk this much. Everyone had been drinking without expelling the alcohol from their bodies.

No one forced anyone to drink, but everyone had gotten quite drunk.

In particular, Bi Sain was in the highest of spirits today. It was the first time he had ever had such an exciting birthday banquet.

He drank because he felt good, drank because the wine tasted good, and drank simply because he felt like drinking.
