Even now, boisterous sounds were coming from inside. At drinking gatherings where only Geom Mugeuk’s voice had always been heard, Bi Sain and Jin Hagun’s voices were now properly playing their parts as well.

“It’ll be my first and last birthday banquet.”

At Bi Sain’s words, Geom Mugeuk and Jin Hagun shouted drunkenly.

“Last? Then what about my birthday banquet? Even if we quit, we have to do mine!”

“Sorry, but the next birthday coming up is mine. Mine.”

Listening to their conversation, Ian smiled. Then Han Seol walked up to her from behind.

Just from the fact that her pure white clothes were stained here and there with wine and food, one could tell what the atmosphere had been like today.

“Those people really picked the right day.”

Han Seol’s face, too, was flushed with drunkenness.

“It’s a birthday banquet, but it’s also a banquet to congratulate ourselves on defeating a great enemy.”

At Ian’s words, Han Seol nodded. She, too, had every right to take part in that banquet. She and the Northern Sea Ice Palace had also played a role in the battle against that great enemy.

“But did something happen between you two?”

“Nothing happened. I just came out to have fun with the Young Cult Leader.”

“No.”

The two people Han Seol was asking about were not Geom Mugeuk and Ian.

“I mean with Lady Jin.”

“What about Lady Jin?”

“You seem awkward with each other somehow. I happen to be quite experienced in pretending to be cheerful while talking with people I feel awkward around.”

Ian did not deliberately hide it.

“We decided to determine on this trip whether we would become friends or enemies.”

Of course, that did not mean she told her everything.

Han Seol looked unable to understand for a moment, then suddenly said to Ian,

“Then become enemies.”

Han Seol recommended it without even hearing what it was about. Was it because of the wine? Han Seol was speaking more than usual.

“I think that would be better for you, unni.”

Ian gazed quietly at Han Seol, then spoke words she had never once brought up before.

“I always told the Young Cult Leader that I had ten tails. But in truth, I don’t have ten tails.”

“Then how many do you have?”

Han Seol thought she would say she had far more, but the answer was unexpected.

“One.”

After a brief pause, Ian answered.

“I have only one tail, the single tail that deceives others into thinking I have ten.”

Han Seol asked Ian,

“Why did you lie and say you had ten tails?”

Ian looked up at the night sky for a moment.

“I suppose I wanted to say this. That even though I’m a guard warrior who stands silently like a folding screen, if you look closely, I’m someone with hidden charms.

That I’m not some frustrating person who’s only kind, but someone who knows how to take care of what’s mine. That I’m someone who knows how to take things too, not someone who foolishly yields.”

Of course, Ian knew she was not like that. The ordinary sword standing behind the weapons shop had felt like herself, she had gone to save that woman’s husband after receiving a single sip of water, and even Han Seol, who did not know her deeply, was worried that she might yield something.

“I thought you were more charming because you were someone without tails, unni.”

“What?”

“Those parts you dislike about yourself are things I could never obtain no matter how hard I tried my entire life. If anything, I’d have to act them out with ten tails.”

Pretending to be kind, pretending to know how to yield, pretending not to be selfish, pretending to be someone who could willingly sacrifice herself for another.

“To become like you, Sister, I need a tail. But you need a tail to become the opposite.”

The two women looked at each other in silence.

Just then, Jin Haryeong poked her head out of a third-floor window and called out.

“What are you two doing? Hurry up and come! Today’s star says he’s going to dance!”

Jin Haryeong, shouting like that, was also very drunk. And Bi Sain, who had stepped forward saying he would dance, was probably even drunker.

Ian shouted back to Jin Haryeong.

“We’re coming. I could miss anything else, but not that.”

By the time Ian finished speaking, Han Seol was already heading inside. Those hurried steps were clearly not the steps of someone going to enjoy the spectacle, but of someone going to stop it.

Ian, about to follow her in, paused and looked up at the night sky, then murmured softly.

“Seol-ah, tonight’s heat doesn’t look easy to cool, not even with that chill of the North Sea.”

***

Bi Sain woke up. His head throbbed with pain. It was the first time he had ever drunk so much without worrying about what would come afterward.

Then, from deep within his heart, Geom Mugeuk’s voice echoed back to him. The words made every fine hair on his body stand on end.

—Now, on a fine day like this, how could we leave out dancing!

Bi Sain’s eyes flew open.

He had sworn never to dance again in his life. So why did he remember himself rising from his seat while saying, Of course!

“No way, right? Surely not?”

Of course it couldn’t be, but what was this ominous feeling?

Bi Sain got out of bed and called for someone.

“Illang!”

“Are you awake?”

“Come in for a moment.”

Illang, who had been standing guard outside, opened the door and entered. Seeing Illang brought the memories back. He remembered grabbing the man’s hand and dragging him inside by force to drink with him.

Bi Sain asked without preamble.

“Did I dance?”

A smile flickered across Illang’s face.

“Do you mean which dance, and with whom?”

Damn it! Bi Sain shook his head and asked no further.

“No. Forget it.”

No, this he had to ask.

“Did I perhaps make a drunken scene?”

Surely he hadn’t made a drunken scene in front of Han Seol?

Fortunately, Illang shook his head as if to say no.

“It was the first time I had ever seen you look so happy. It was good to see.”

“By the way, where is everyone?”

At that, Illang took a letter from inside his robe and handed it to him.

“It is a letter Young Cult Leader Geom left behind when he departed with your friends.”

Bi Sain was startled. It seemed Geom Mugeuk had been unable to wait and had left first. Perhaps he had made room for him to spend time with Han Seol.

Still, even if he was going to leave, he could have had breakfast with him first. He could at least have said goodbye.

With a sense of regret, Bi Sain opened the letter.

—If you want to save Miss Han, follow the western main road. But come alone.

Bi Sain stared blankly down at the letter for a moment, then burst into loud laughter.

It was an invitation to travel together, in a style only Geom Mugeuk would use. Taking Han Seol with him was a pleasant bit of coercion, a hostage-taking that only Geom Mugeuk could pull off, telling him not to weigh this or that and simply come along.

Bi Sain showed the letter to Illang. Illang had already guessed its contents. After all, Geom Mugeuk had even taken Han Seol with him.

“Illang.”

“Yes, Young Alliance Leader.”

Bi Sain looked at him calmly and said,

“This time, I really must go save her alone.”

Bi Sain set off westward with his lightness skill.

Chapter 870: She Is Like a Fairy Banished from Paradise
-------------------------------------------------------
He ran like a child rushing to meet a friend.

When was the last time he had run with such an excited heart?

After running for some time, he found them waiting at a roadside inn with tables and chairs set up outside.

“Over here, over here!”

As if he had been certain Bisain would come, Geom Mugeuk waved and welcomed him.

“You haven’t had your hangover soup yet, have you? Owner, another bowl here, please.”

Bisain sat down.

Han Seol, sitting across from him, slightly bowed her head to Bisain. It was an apology for having gone along with Geom Mugeuk’s proposal without telling him beforehand. She had fallen for the argument that this was the only way Bisain could join the outing too, but sorry was still sorry.

Bisain smiled faintly and nodded as if to say it was fine. He was rather grateful she had given in. Thanks to that, he was able to go on a trip together like this.

“I came without even sending word to the Alliance Lord.”

Bisain had a comrade named Jin Hagun.

“So did I,” Jin Hagun said.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to the two of them.

“You know it’s more exciting and fun to play when you’re bracing for a scolding. But Illang let you go willingly?”

“I found it strange too.”

He had seemed like he would jump up and adamantly refuse, but Illang gave his permission.

Had he judged that the young alliance lord’s fate, so entangled with Geom Mugeuk’s, was impossible to prevent?

“Looks like you’ve prepared to get scolded together with me.”

At Bisain’s words, Jin Hagun smiled faintly. Listening to talk of the Sado Sipsamrang, he was reminded of his subordinates in the Demon Extermination Corps. He wondered if they were all enjoying their leave. No, forget it!

Just then, the tavern keeper brought Bisain a steaming bowl of soup.

“The food here is good. Eat slowly.”

It was delicious, just as Geom Mugeuk had said. Spicy and refreshing, it settled his stomach.

“So where are we going?”

Geom Mugeuk told Bisain about the trip.

“The principle of this journey is to follow where our feet take us.”

“I don’t think I’ve ever lived like that.”

The same was true for Jin Hagun.

“Same here.”

Geom Mugeuk first asked the two young alliance lords who were pushing their luck the most on this trip.

“Is there anywhere you particularly want to go?”

Bisain and Jin Hagun shook their heads. They might think of somewhere along the way, but nothing came to mind right now.

“Then shall we go where my steps lead first?”

Everyone nodded. They could tell that Geom Mugeuk had a destination in mind.

“Do you happen to like bugs?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Jin Hagun asked with a puzzled look.

“Who in the world likes bugs?”

Geom Mugeuk spoke with an inscrutable smile.

“You’ll come to like them this time.”

***

At the sound of insects in the distance, Chung-ui raised her head.

“The Mingyinchong have been singing since morning. It seems welcome guests will arrive today.”

Pyo Gigwang, who had been warming up with a sword in the courtyard, looked toward the main gate and spoke.

“Bring a whole bundle of money!”

“You brat, why is a young one like you going on about money?”

“Who do you think I learned it from? And young? I’m practically an old man now.”

“That’s a habit of flattery you picked up for your old master.”

Pyo Gigwang set down his sword and brought Chung-ui some water.

“Since you’re up, please have a glass of water first.”

“My throat isn’t dry.”

“Even if you don’t feel it, your body wants it.”

Pyo Gigwang made her drink it by force. Then he kneaded Chung-ui’s shoulders. This was the beginning of the daily routine.

“Oh, that feels good.”

“Drinking a glass of lukewarm water right after waking up is so good for your health.”

“You rascal, if you care so much about health, why did you ruin your own body like that?”

“My body was injured doing great deeds.”

Pyo Gigwang was a person Geom Mugeuk had met while fighting the Fighting King in the Sado Alliance. After being used by the schemers behind the scenes, he had been taken to Chung-ui by Geom Mumeuk and accepted as her disciple.

Since then, Pyo Gigwang had lived like a newborn, serving Chung-ui as his master.

“I told you to train with me.”

Chung-ui was a master of martial arts. Her medical skills were as outstanding as those of a demonic physician or divine physician not only because of her deep knowledge of poison bugs, but also because she herself was a peak-level expert who understood the body of a martial artist better than anyone.

“You do more. I’m old, so everything is a bother. My shoulders are good now.”

“Just a little more.”

Pyo Gigwang kneaded her shoulders more.

“But aren’t you going to maintain the formation anymore?”

Normally, the Geungrakwon was surrounded by a formation. One had to give the password to the boy at the village entrance and reveal who had sent them in order to enter.

But Chung-ui had removed that formation. She said it was no longer needed since she had a dependable disciple, but Pyo Gigwang knew his master’s heart.

He knew that his master, feeling time was running out as she aged, was saving even the money to maintain the formation so she could raise and teach even one more child.

“What? Are you scared because there’s no formation?”

“I am. The world is a dangerous place these days.”

“Is that something a vicious man who was once the Black Dragon Division Leader should say?”

After accepting Pyo Gigwang as her disciple, Chung-ui had completely shaken off the loneliness and depression that had often tormented her. Because life with this disciple was quite enjoyable.

Only after kneading a while longer did Pyo Gigwang return to the courtyard and swing his sword again.

Pyo Gigwang, who had once risen to the position of Black Dragon Division Leader in the Sado Alliance, had been severely injured during an operation and rendered unable to use martial arts.

But now, Pyo Gigwang was swinging his sword using internal energy.

He had given up on martial arts and devoted himself solely to serving Chung-ui, but she had healed him with a treatment method using poison bugs.

Though not to his prime, he had now recovered considerably.

But an even greater fortune for him was this.

“Your waist is sagging. Bend your knees more.”

Not only had Chung-ui treated him, but she had also passed on her own martial arts. The martial arts she had mastered were stronger than his. She had acknowledged Pyo Gigwang as her true disciple.

“Breathe deeper. Hold your breath, yes.”

While instructing Pyo Gigwang like that, Chung-ui spoke as if she had just remembered something.

“Gigwang.”

“Yes, Master.”

“If I die, release all the bugs here, and you go far away and live your own life. Don’t suffer trying to carry on my work for no reason. Just do what you want and live.”

The gaze of Pyo Gigwang swinging his sword deepened. His quick-witted master had read his heart completely.

“Why would taking care of the children be suffering?”

“It is suffering.”

“Do you regret it, then? Spending all the money you earned in your life on caring for orphans? Living a life called the Money Bug?”

Chung-ui raised her head and looked at the sky. Did she regret her life? Not a chance.

“Yes, I regret it.”

“Then in your next life, don’t live like this. Live splendidly. Earn money and buy a big tiled-roof house like a whale’s back. I’ll live like that in my next life too.”

Pyo Gigwang intended to continue her work even if Chung-ui died. That was why he was doing his best to learn her medical arts.

At that moment, the main gate burst open, and two martial artists entered without permission. It was clear they had not come with good intentions.

“It seems the Mingyinchong couldn’t tell who the precious guests were after all.”

Pyo Gigwang seated her in a chair positioned to face the courtyard.

“Please stay right here without moving. I will handle them.”

“I can’t stand it as I get older. My anger is boiling.”

“Don’t blame your age. It’s your temper.”

“You brat!”

Meanwhile, the two martial artists approached them.

The middle-aged man walking in the lead was Cao Tang, the sect leader of the Gongshengmen.

The man standing beside Cao Tang was his right-hand man and the foremost swordsman of the Gongshengmen, Huang Chun.

“Who might you be?”

At Pyo Gigwang’s calm question, Huang Chun stepped forward on behalf of his leader.

“We sent people here a few days ago.”

Pyo Gigwang rebuked him in a low voice.

“Since when have we met that you speak so rudely? Mind your manners.”

Huang Chun scowled.

“What use is age in the martial world? Skill is what matters.”

Then the sect leader, Cao Tang, stepped forward.

“Impertinent.”

At his rebuke, Huang Chun stepped back. It was clearly a perfunctory scolding.

If Cao Tang truly knew manners, his subordinate would not have spoken like that in front of him in the first place.

“I am a man called Cao Tang who leads the Gongshengmen. I should have come personally from the start. My apologies.”

“Welcome, Sect Leader Cao.”

Pyo Gigwang greeted him courteously.

Chung-ui sat in the chair as told, watching the courtyard.

“To what do we owe the honor of your visit?”

“I sent people here a few days ago.”

“I remember. If I recall, I politely conveyed that their proposal was refused.”

The reason Cao Tang had come was that he wanted to buy some land in another region.

Learning that the owner of that land was the master of this Geungrakwon, he had sent several martial artists under his chief manager, telling them to negotiate a price and buy the land.

The chief manager and martial artists returned with their whole bodies swollen red from bug bites. Of course, no deal was made.

Because it was an urgent and important matter, the sect leader had come in person.

“Since this is a meeting with an important person, I should have come from the start. My apologies.”

After apologizing, Cao Tang spoke with a pleasant face.

“I will buy that land at a price better than the market rate.”

Pyo Gigwang refused without even asking the price.

“I must refuse.”

“Deals always fall through because the amount doesn’t match. I’ll pay double the land price.”

He had obtained information that a large sect was planning to relocate near that land. If he could buy that land and sell it to the relocating sect, he would make many times the profit.

“It is not land for sale.”

“Very well. I’ll pay triple. I absolutely cannot go higher.”

“Even if you offered ten times rather than triple, I have no intention of selling.”

These guys? Had they heard the relocation rumors too?

“May I ask the reason?”

“Do you know what building is on that land?”

“I heard there is a place called the Qing’eryuan where orphans are gathered and raised.”

Cao Tang made an expression of disbelief.

“Surely you are not saying you won’t sell because of that?”

Whoever bought the land would undoubtedly tear down that unprofitable place first.

“Ridiculous.”

Cao Tang’s expression, which had been polite until then, turned cold.

“A mere Money Bug.”

Cao Tang revealed his true colors. He had come knowing that the master of the Geungrakwon was a physician called the Money Bug.

“A physician mad for money. Rumors about you are widespread. Are you perhaps selling those orphans at a high price?”

“Are you being this rude knowing our elder is Chung-ui?”

“Yes, I hear you use poison bugs well?”

Cao Tang had something to rely on. He took something from his bosom. What he held was Blood Poison. Not just any Blood Poison, but the highest grade.

Not only that, Huang Chun also took out Blood Poison. The two had come fully determined.

“If you know why only the two of us came, it would be best not to show useless stubbornness.”

It was a blatant threat. A threat that they might be killed if they did not sign the contract.

He took out a contract from his bosom.

“Stamp the contract. Don’t forget that a seal can be stamped even with a severed arm.”

Since he was officially buying it for three times the market rate, he would be able to make excuses later if problems arose. The Money Bug demanded three times the price, so he paid three times the market rate!

“You worms worse than bugs!”

At Pyo Gigwang’s anger, Chung-ui, who had been sitting still until then, spoke in a languid voice.

“You rascal, how dare you compare precious bugs to the likes of them?”

Chung-ui turned her head slightly to look at Cao Tang and Huang Chun and asked.

“You know, don’t you? There are bugs that go crazy for Blood Poison?”

Having said only that, Chung-ui groaned and struggled to her feet.

“Oh dear, my back.”

It was Pyo Gigwang who interpreted her words for the two men who did not understand what she meant.

“It means there are poison bugs that cannot be stopped by something like Blood Poison.”

***

Geom Mugeuk was walking alongside his friends.

“Now, watch closely.”

Geom Mugeuk, walking ahead and explaining, was in high spirits.

“Once you turn that corner, there’ll be a boy sitting on a bench. The next time you come, just say this to him: ‘Boy, go and bring me a bottle of liquor brewed from insects.’

Then the boy will ask who sent you. When he does, answer like this: ‘I came on the recommendation of the dashing and handsome Young Cult Leader Geom.’ Got it?”

When they turned the corner, there really was a bench there.

A boy was sitting on it, reading a book.

“You’ve grown so much.”

The boy, who had shot up in the time they had not seen each other, recognized Geom Mugeuk as well.

“It’s been a long time since you came.”

“Remember the people standing behind me, and if they come next time, guide them properly. They’re hard to forget once you’ve seen them, so you’ll recognize them right away. That scary-looking older brother there is actually kind once you get to know him, so guide him well too. Hey, friends. If not for me, you wouldn’t even be able to get in here.”

Just as Geom Mugeuk was putting on airs for all he was worth—

“You can reach Geungnagwon by going straight down this path.”

“What are you talking about?”

“The elder has removed the formation.”

The five of them passed by Geom Mugeuk as he stood there blankly. The scary-looking older brother said a word as he went by.

“Friend, bring me a bottle of liquor brewed from insects.”

“They said you don’t need the liquor anymore!”

Left alone, Geom Mugeuk gave the boy some pocket money.

“You’re giving me this much?”

“I’m giving it to you because I admire how you’re always reading. Buy lots of books and read them. That will surely become your way to live! Hey, friends, wait for me.”

Geom Mugeuk ran after his friends.

The boy, looking down at the banknote, bowed politely to Geom Mugeuk.

After they had walked for quite some time and Geungnagwon came into view up ahead, Geom Mugeuk introduced Chungui.

“You probably don’t know how remarkable Elder Chungui is. She is a noble person who has devoted her entire life to children. She is kind yet dignified, her medical arts are on the level of a divine physician, and she has learning and refinement besides.

Why do you think this place is called Geungnagwon? It means she is like a fairy banished from paradise. Elder, I’m here.”

Geom Mugeuk and his friends opened the main gate of Geungnagwon wide and stepped inside. They could not say a word at the scene unfolding within.

As it happened, Chungui was in the middle of trying to make a large insect in her hand bite into Jodang’s bare upper body. Perhaps he had already been bitten by other insects, for his entire body was covered in wounds.

Just as Chungui looked over with a delighted expression at Geom Mugeuk’s visit—

Chomp.

When the insect she was holding bit savagely into his body, Jodang screamed.

“Aaaaaaaaaaagh!”

At that scream, Hwang Chun, who had been lying collapsed, came to his senses and crawled toward the entrance, grabbing the leg of the person who had just entered and pleading. Unfortunately, that person happened to be Bisain, but Hwang Chun was in no state to tell whose leg he was holding.

“...Please save me!”

Whether he had been beaten or bitten by insects, Hwang Chun’s face and body were swollen so badly they were nearly unrecognizable.

Seeing Chungui looking their way with a blood-smeared face and smiling brightly, Bisain whispered quietly into Geom Mugeuk’s ear.

“Seems more like she was banished from hell, if you ask me.”

Chapter 871: Why Would People Like You Be Mere Physicians
---------------------------------------------------------
A lifeline descended for Jodang.

To him, who was being bitten and torn by unidentifiable venomous insects, six lifelines descended from the heavens.

Seeing Geom Mugeuk, Chung-ui removed the venomous insects biting Jodang.

Thinking this was his chance, Jodang mustered his strength. Though he was half out of his mind, his desire to survive was tremendous.

Jodang sprang up from where he sat and ran toward the main gate, shouting,

"Save me!"

He had thought they were guests who had come to this place. Surely they wouldn't kill everyone to eliminate witnesses. And indeed, Chung-ui did not stop him as he walked toward them.

Naturally, he had expected them to ask what had happened.

He had even thought of an instantaneous reply. That they had come to buy land, but that money-grubging bug had demanded three times the market price, and when they refused, she had tried to kill them.

But among the line of people, one man said something completely unexpected.

"If you want to live, you must choose well."

Naturally, the one who spoke was Geom Mugeuk. His gaze fell upon Hwang Chun, who was clinging to Bisain's leg, as he spoke.

"One could hardly say that person's choice was very good."

Bisain laughed. He had come to quietly—no, thoroughly—enjoy Geom Mugeuk's teasing.

Bisain smiled with delight, but to Jodang, that smile on such a fearsome face evoked the Grim Reaper of hell.

"Now then, will you be able to choose properly?"

Jodang stood in the middle of the courtyard and looked at them.

'What in the world is this situation? Aren't these people traveling together? Or are they trying to mock me?'

All manner of strange figures lived in this murim.

There was no room for choice. At this rate, his entire body would be devoured by the venomous insects and he would die.

His gaze swept over the six people.

There wasn't a single ordinary person among them. Every last one of them possessed an appearance that would make anyone turn their head for a second look if passed on the street.

Undoubtedly, the one who stood out the most was Geom Mugeuk, standing in the center and speaking.

When their eyes met, Geom Mugeuk spread his arms wide.

"The best choice, isn't it?"

Jodang quickly turned his head. No matter what, he wasn't so out of his mind as to choose that talkative, strange fellow.

Beside him was Bisain, with his fearsome face. Hwang Chun, who had been clinging to his leg, lay motionless, perhaps having fainted again.

Why would anyone choose a person like that? No, if going by appearance alone, that man looked the strongest.

But among six people, he decidedly did not wish to choose that face.

This time his gaze turned toward Jin Ha-gun. Integrity and chivalry radiated from his eyes. It felt as though if he asked for help, he would surely receive it.

That was precisely why his instincts refused.

Not him.

He thought that someone like himself, who had committed evil deeds, might bring down another calamity upon himself by asking help from a person of such upstanding character.

This time he looked at the women. First among them was Yi An, a woman so beautiful he wondered if he was seeing things.

The other two women were the same. Jin Ha-ryeong and Han Seol each revealed their own beauty.

Jodang excluded them all. In this murim, the people one had to be most careful of were such beauties.

His gaze naturally turned back toward Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk said with a smile,

"As expected, there's no one but me, right?"

Surprisingly, Jodang chose Geom Mugeuk.

"Please save me."

There was only one reason for this choice. He had sensed that Geom Mugeuk controlled the atmosphere at the center of the group.

"Did you see? Did you see him choose me? This dependability, this trustworthiness that you lot absolutely lack! This strength that looks like it would save anyone from any crisis!"

Jodang wanted to shout like this:

'It's not because of that! I chose you because you were the only one talking!'

After boasting to his heart's content, Geom Mugeuk spoke to his friends.

"Since he chose me, I should greet her last. Now, everyone, greet her first."

The first to move was Bisain, who wanted to detach the fainted Hwang Chun clinging to his leg.

Free from Hwang Chun, Bisain walked a few steps forward and greeted Chung-ui.

"I am Bisain, Young Leader of the Demon Path Alliance. I have long heard of your esteemed reputation."

Out of respect for his counterpart, he revealed his identity.

Even at the words "Young Leader of the Demon Path Alliance," Chung-ui was not surprised at all. She rubbed her arm and asked,

"The only reputation I have is as a money-grubbing bug, no?"

"Are there not also bugs that help the world more than humans?"

'Not bad, this one.' Chung-ui, looking at him with such a gaze, abruptly asked,

"Do you have a lot of money?"

"Yes, I do."

"Then buy a few bottles of Flower Bug Wine when you leave."

"Yes."

"Buy five bottles. You're not allowed to complain about the price."

Chung-ui's expression brightened. In this moment, she truly acted like a money-grubbing bug.

Meanwhile, Jodang, listening to the conversation, was horrified.

'He is the Young Leader of the Demon Path Alliance?'

To think he would meet the Young Leader of the Demon Path Alliance in a place like this? But the Young Leader of the Demon Path Alliance was out here without subordinates?

Could it be that the talkative fellow and the others were his subordinates? No wonder he was walking around with three beauties! If that was the case?

'I'm doomed! I should have chosen him!'

Then Jin Ha-gun stepped forward and politely cupped his hands in greeting.

"I am Jin Ha-gun, who serves as the Demon Annihilation Commander of the Murim Alliance. It is an honor to meet you."

At the words "Demon Annihilation Commander," Chung-ui's expression grew pleased. As someone who walked the righteous path, she held basic goodwill toward the Young Leader of the Murim Alliance.

Despite keeping a disciple from the Demon Path Alliance by her side, and being deeply connected to the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, she was a good person to the very bone.

"I have heard of your excellent character many times. Please continue to be a pillar of the murim in the future."

"You give me too much praise."

Because he had roughly guessed why she had tried to sell wine.

"I will also buy some wine."

"You're a fine one. It's too expensive."

She was openly stating that the price was inflated.

"I also have a lot of money."

"Then you buy just one bottle. Use the remaining money for other good deeds."

She was certain that Jin Ha-gun's money would be used for good deeds anyway.

Geom Mugeuk could not let this favoritism pass.

"Aren't you discriminating too much? It's unfair enough that I have a scary face, but to be overcharged on top of that!"

"Ripping off villains is the real taste of it. You get ten bottles."

"That's too much!"

Meanwhile, Jodang was startled by the words "Demon Annihilation Commander." He knew well that the Demon Annihilation Commander was the next Murim Alliance Leader.

'That person is the Young Leader of the Murim Alliance?'

So surprised was he that the places bitten by the insects no longer hurt.

But he soon realized this situation was absurd. How could the Young Leader of the Murim Alliance and the Young Leader of the Demon Path Alliance be together? And without even bringing subordinates?

'What the hell are these people!'

But why was that Chung-ui accepting this absurd situation as fact? Why was she believing their words so readily? To deceive him? But there was no need for that, considering his plight before they arrived.

This time, Jin Ha-ryeong greeted her.

"I am Jin Ha-ryeong, his younger sister. It is a great honor to meet you."

"You get along well with your elder brother. I can see you're traveling together like this."

"We are rather close."

Geom Mugeuk slipped in and teased Jin Ha-ryeong.

"You lost the succession struggle, didn't you? Does the loser have power? If you're told to go, you have to go."

Without even knowing why she had joined this journey, to crack such a joke without knowing another's feelings!

"Please lend me the venomous insect you're holding! I'll shove it right into his mouth!"

When Chung-ui actually thrust the venomous insect at her as if telling her to do it, Jin Ha-ryeong retreated in surprise. Seen up close, it was truly repulsive and terrifying.

Next, Han Seol walked forward. Even though she had not introduced herself, Chung-ui discerned her origins.

"You came from a cold place."

"I am Han Seol, from the Ice Palace."

"Is your mother the Palace Mistress?"

"Yes, she is."

"Then you buy a bottle too. It will completely chase away the cold."

"Yes."

Finally, Yi An greeted her.

"I heard of you from the Young Cult Leader. I am Yi An."

Chung-ui remembered the Yi An that Geom Mugeuk had spoken of.

"So you are the subordinate he cherishes like his own heart."

Geom Mugeuk had once spoken to Chung-ui about Yi An in the past. Chung-ui had not forgotten the conversation they had shared then.

Chung-ui quietly gazed at Yi An's face. The unmistakable goodness she felt from her was the temperament Chung-ui liked most.

"It's a waste to give you to the Young Cult Leader."

Yi An felt overwhelming emotion. It was the first time she had heard such words since birth. She had never even thought such a thing herself. She had always considered herself far too lacking for the Young Cult Leader. Because she had never once considered herself his equal.

She knew it was said merely to be nice, but to her, it became words that overwhelmed her heart.

Yi An forcibly swallowed her surging emotions and spoke calmly,

"It is thanks to serving the Young Cult Leader for so long that I hear such undeserved words. I will also buy wine."

Then Geom Mugeuk shouted,

"Don't buy it! Then our cult gets stuck with eleven bottles of overpriced wine!"

Jodang could not hear any of the other words being spoken. The word "Young Cult Leader" was shaking his heart.

Young Cult Leader? Just which cult's Young Leader?

The Young Leader of the Demon Path Alliance had appeared, and the Young Leader of the Murim Alliance had appeared, so there was only one place left from which to come.

Jodang slowly turned his head and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

'No way. It can't be, right?'

It was right at that moment.

"Young Cult Leader!"

The one who called out to Geom Mugeuk was Pyo Gi-gwang, who had been coming out from the rear yard carrying a jar of venomous insects. He set down the jar and ran to Geom Mugeuk.

"It has been a long time, Young Cult Leader."

"You look well."

Geom Mugeuk could tell through the sword hanging at Pyo Gi-gwang's waist that he had even recovered his martial arts.

"Thanks to you, Young Cult Leader, I am living well."

Pyo Gi-gwang could not hide his emotion. At that time, it had been a situation where he could not have resented Geom Mugeuk even if he had killed him.

But Geom Mugeuk had given him a second chance. The life he was living now was a life given by that very Young Cult Leader before his eyes.

He was not the only happy face.

Pyo Gi-gwang discovered Bisain among those who had come together.

"Young Alliance Leader!"

"Glad to meet you."

"Have you been well?"

Because Bisain had once saved his life, Pyo Gi-gwang's eyes toward him contained deep gratitude.

By the time everyone had finished greeting each other, Jodang was plunged into shock and confusion.

With a trembling heart, he asked Geom Mugeuk,

"Why do they call you Young Cult Leader?"

The answer he would rather not have heard flowed out.

"Because I am the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult."

"!"

The moment he saw those clear, deep eyes, Jodang could intuit it. That the man before his eyes truly was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

"Why in the world would people like you come to meet a mere physician like this?"

No matter how skilled this physician named Chung-ui was, they must have had far better physicians.

Geom Mugeuk stared at him intently and said,

"You are wrong. She is one who sacrificed her entire life bearing the stigma of a money-grubbing bug for the sake of orphans, and she is the one doing us insignificant fledglings the honor of meeting with us."

Demonic energy settled in Geom Mugeuk's eyes as he looked at him, turning them cold.

"We revere her in this way; what was it that you did?"

Listening, Chung-ui closed her eyes. Geom Mugeuk's words were ones that comforted and soothed her difficult, arduous life.

Yes, if she could receive such an evaluation from the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, how could she have any regrets in this life?

At Geom Mugeuk's rebuke, Jodang could say nothing and trembled violently.

He had lived a life diametrically opposed to his martial sect's name, Mutual Life.

Live together? No, he had lived only for himself. To fill his own selfish desires, he had committed all manner of evil deeds together with that Hwang Chun.

Until now, he had escaped undetected and safely.

But today, he had been properly caught. By the entire murim, both righteous and demonic.

"You chose me, so let us go to the cult together."

Jodang's face turned pale as he staggered backward. If he were dragged to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult in this situation, what would happen to him was as obvious as fire.

He looked at Jin Ha-gun and Bisain. They too looked at him with impassive faces. If they had been people who would help him, they would not have come together in the first place.

Jodang staggered back toward Chung-ui.

Standing before her, Jodang's gaze turned toward the venomous insect Chung-ui was holding.

Jodang slowly reached out and grabbed Chung-ui’s wrist, pulling her toward herself.

The poisonous insect she held touched her own chest.

*Crunch.*

“Uuaaaaaaargh!”

She was one who had chosen the agony of being bitten by a poison insect and dying over being dragged to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Geom Mugeuk nodded and said,

“That choice might be for the best.”

When Chung-ui gave a signal with her eyes, Pyo Gigwang came and suppressed the demonic and slumber acupoints of Jodang and Hwang Chun, then dragged them off to the backyard.

Only now did Geom Mugeuk formally reunite with Chung-ui.

“Elder! Divine Physician!”

Geom Mugeuk ran up with a delighted face and hugged Chung-ui tight.

“Our noona has grown even more beautiful!”

In the past, too, he had run up to hug her tightly and had joked by calling her noona.

Chung-ui welcomed Geom Mugeuk with a bright smile.

“I thought I’d die without ever seeing you again, yet here you are.”

Though Chung-ui was not one to easily reveal her emotions, she could not hide her joy now.

“Have you finished your task?”

“Yes.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. Chung-ui smiled pleasantly and patted his shoulder.

“You’ve worked hard.”

“It’s all thanks to you, Elder.”

It was thanks to Chung-ui that he had gained immunity to ten thousand poisons, and thanks to the fortuitous encounter of that poison immunity that he had been able to easily escape many dangers.

“But who were those people from earlier?”

“How quick you are to ask.”

Pyo Gigwang answered, walking out from the backyard.

“They’re the bastards who tried to steal the home of the children you sponsor.”

Not only Geom Mugeuk but the other friends’ expressions darkened as well. They were guilty of a crime that would earn them the same agony even if dragged to the Murim Alliance or the Sa Do Alliance rather than the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Geom Mugeuk volunteered to handle the aftermath.

“If we leave them in the backyard, people from the main sect will come and deal with them soon.”

Since it was a matter related to Chung-ui, he would have them thoroughly investigate what those bastards had perpetrated and see to it that the matter was settled without any loose ends.

“But what brings you here?”

“I brought them to introduce my friends.”

Everyone once again cupped their fists in unison and paid their respects.

How could they not know why Geom Mugeuk had brought them here? It was to show them firsthand that such a life existed—a life that left behind sincerity rather than fame, a life where fame was naught but the shed husk of an insect.

And Geom Mugeuk had one more reason.

“Did I not promise you?”

“What promise?”

From Geom Mugeuk’s mouth came words she had never imagined.

“That I would surely cure your illness, Elder.”

“!”

An astonishment akin to shock bloomed across Chung-ui’s face. She had never imagined Geom Mugeuk would come for such a reason. It was a promise even she had forgotten.

“Now, I think I can finally give it a try.”

Chapter 872: Another Thirty Years of Infamy
-------------------------------------------
Chungui was deeply moved.

For the words “we can give it a try now” meant that he had been thinking about her illness all this time.

On those nights when the pain was too great for her to sleep, this young cult leader before her must have been worrying about her somewhere.

Deep gratitude filled Chungui’s gaze as she looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“I’m grateful that you cared, but my illness cannot be cured.”

She had spent her entire life searching for and researching rare venomous insects. In the process, she had been poisoned by deadly toxins several times, and her body had been ruined. Ordinary medicine had no effect, so she was only barely holding on by using poison to counter poison.

“Please entrust it to me once.”

The look Jin Hagun gave Geom Mugeuk held respect beyond mere surprise. It was a moment that confirmed anew how Geom Mugeuk treated the bonds he had formed.

“There’s no need to waste your strength treating this old body. It’s already ruined anyway.”

Just then, Pyo Gigwang came running over and pulled her along.

“Master. At times like this, you’re supposed to say, ‘Fine, this body’s ruined anyway, so do whatever you like.’”

“You rascal, let go.”

“Being stubborn in your old age is what makes the young ones expend their strength. Now, hurry and go inside.”

“I told you to let go.”

Pyo Gigwang forcibly dragged her into the room. Geom Mugeuk followed behind them and said to his friends,

“It’ll take some time.”

Bi Sain nodded and said,

“Take your time and do it well.”

Bi Sain, too, knew the reason Geom Mugeuk had come here, and thought, As expected! If one simply said he had come to keep a promise, it might not seem like anything special.

But Bi Sain took it this way. The reason he had brought them all the way here was because he had wanted to say this.

Rather than for honor, live sincerely for someone else; once you make a promise, be sure to keep it; let us live like this from now on.

Geom Mugeuk entered the room Chungui had gone into.

Even after entering the room, Chungui was still arguing with Pyo Gigwang.

“I’m telling you, I’m fine. I know my own body best.”

“Have you ever seen a monk shave his own head? Even a physician has to seek out another physician when he’s ill. Sit down first.”

“I said I’m fine.”

Reluctantly, Chungui sat on the edge of the bed.

After seating her, Pyo Gigwang asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Is there anything you need?”

He did not say it aloud, but the look in his eyes showed just how desperate this old disciple was.

“It’s all right.”

“If you need anything at all, call me. I’ll be waiting outside the door.”

Before leaving, he whispered into her ear.

“Don’t be stubborn for no reason, and do exactly as the Young Cult Leader tells you!”

After he left, Chungui shook her head.

“If I was going to take on a disciple, I should have brought in a young one. He nags even more than I do.”

“These days, young people are terrified at the mention of venomous insects. Just think of that old child as the youngest one.”

Chungui, who had been wearing a faint smile, spoke with a serious expression.

“Listen, Young Cult Leader.”

“Yes, elder.”

“There is no need to overdo it. I mean that sincerely.”

She said it because she knew just how difficult it would be to fix her body.

“As I told you, I am in a state where poison is being suppressed by poison. As time passed, I developed a resistance, and I had to use stronger poison.

“But those poisons became entangled with one another and turned into a mess. It has reached a dangerous point where they will burst if touched even slightly.

“No matter who you are, you cannot handle it. So just go out and say you tried but failed. Stay and talk with me for a while, then leave.”

It seemed that had been her intention from the start when she allowed herself to be dragged in.

“If they’ll burst if touched slightly, then I just have to touch them even more lightly.”

Compared to when he had made that promise with her, Geom Mugeuk’s martial realm had risen beyond comparison, and his internal energy, which had once held the profundity of the Six Paths Primordial Qi, had become incomparably pure.

“Yes, I know very well how extraordinary you are. But even so, it is impossible.”

Chungui told him the reason.

“Even if my body could withstand the treatment process, it cannot be done alone. Separating the clumped poisons is impossible to begin with, but even if you did manage to separate them,

“those two poisons must be expelled from the body in an instant. One person can never handle that simultaneously.”

In other words, two people were needed.

After pondering for a moment, Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“Then I will have to call an assistant.”

Since that meant he intended to call one of his friends outside, Chungui shook her head as if it were out of the question.

“They cannot endure the poison dwelling in my body. Do you want to see that precious heart of yours, at such a young age, spending every day having his shoulders and legs massaged?”

Geom Mugeuk was fine because he was impervious to all poisons, but it was a poison that others could not handle.

At that moment, astonishing words flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“There is someone else who can withstand that poison.”

“!”

For an instant, Chungui’s eyes widened, her face stricken with shock.

“Poison does not work on my assistant.”

Chungui’s eyes opened wide.

“There is another besides you who is impervious to all poisons?”

She had used the materials she had gathered over a lifetime to make him impervious to all poisons, and yet there had been another such fortuitous encounter?

“You will understand when you see. But please don’t be surprised.”

Though he had warned her in advance, Chungui could not help but be surprised.

“Hah!”

Chungui’s eyes bulged. A sight more shocking than surprising unfolded before her.

A being stood behind Geom Mugeuk. It had appeared silently, without any indication of when it had arrived, and it was none other than the Heavenly Demon Soul descended in human size.

“Relax your eyes! She is someone precious!”

Geom Mugeuk eased the mood with a joke. Even though it had shrunk to human size, the presence exuded by the Heavenly Demon Soul was not something a person could easily withstand.

“It is the Heavenly Demon Soul manifested from my martial arts. No poison can affect it.”

It was the Heavenly Demon Soul she had only heard of in stories.

“As far as I know, I heard it was enormous in size.”

“It is originally, but my neck hurt from looking up while chatting with it.”

Geom Mugeuk turned to the Heavenly Demon Soul and said,

“Friend, I need your help.”

The Heavenly Demon Soul, which had been gazing at Geom Mugeuk, gave a slight nod.

At that sight, Chungui was even more shocked. He was exchanging intentions with a being born from martial arts.

Geom Mugeuk spoke honestly.

“To tell the truth, this is my first time performing a treatment like this together with the Heavenly Demon Soul, so I cannot be certain either.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his Heavenly Demon Soul.

“But if I am with my Heavenly Demon Soul, I feel that we can do it.”

From time to time, Geom Mugeuk would use the Sicheon Secret Art and spend time by the seashore with the Heavenly Demon Soul.

All they did was sit quietly, watch the waves, and feel the sea breeze, but he felt himself growing closer and closer to the Heavenly Demon Soul. Geom Mugeuk could clearly sense the Heavenly Demon Soul’s emotions and its potential.

“What do you say? Will you entrust it once to me—no, to us?”

Chungui’s hesitation did not last long.

He was saying he would treat her, even summoning the Heavenly Demon Soul. If the bond with this young cult leader was to continue so deeply.

Chungui, looking at Geom Mugeuk, gave her permission.

“Yes, even a physician must seek out another physician when she is ill.”

Delighted, Geom Mugeuk seated her in the middle of the bed.

“Now, please sit comfortably here.”

Chungui sat in the lotus position.

“It will be difficult if you remain awake. Please sleep for a short while, then wake.”

Chungui nodded and followed Geom Mugeuk’s will. Even if she were to die like this, the last thing she saw would be the state she had longed for: imperviousness to all poisons.

Yes, it could not be better than this.

After pressing her sleep acupoint and putting her to sleep, Geom Mugeuk said to the Heavenly Demon Soul,

“Now, the two of us are going to draw out every last bit of poison from the elder’s body. Do as I instruct.”

The Heavenly Demon Soul sent him a reassuring look. Together with the Heavenly Demon Soul, he felt confident that he could do it.

Geom Mugeuk sat behind Chungui and placed his palm on her back.

The Heavenly Demon Soul sat in front of Chungui and placed its palm on her chest.

“Now, let’s begin.”

First, a strand of Geom Mugeuk’s internal energy flowed into Chungui’s body. He slowly examined her interior. As expected, her blood vessels had been badly damaged.

‘This won’t do as it is.’

Geom Mugeuk’s internal energy gently wrapped around her blood vessels. He protected and regulated her blood vessels and vital energy first. Like reinforcing a collapsing embankment, he overlaid his own internal energy upon her blood vessels.

After slowly soothing her interior, Geom Mugeuk was able to find the poison deep within her blood vessels.

“So this is it.”

The poison was clumped together. Just as she had said, multiple poisons had piled upon one another, tangled like one great mass.

Geom Mugeuk approached the poison cautiously. If he touched this poison incorrectly and caused it to flare up, it would be the end.

Slowly, very slowly, he examined the poison.

As she had said, the poison was largely divided into two parts.

However, they were entangled with each other like a spiderweb, and separating them did not look easy.

It was too large to move as it was.

‘I have to untangle them and split them into two pieces.’

Geom Mugeuk sent a signal to the Heavenly Demon Soul, which was waiting.

—Now it’s your turn to help.

The Heavenly Demon Soul infused a strand of internal energy. He had only recently learned that the Heavenly Demon Soul could use internal energy. He did not know whether it had originally been capable of it, or whether his Heavenly Demon Soul was special, but the Heavenly Demon Soul could move its internal energy with caution, just like him.

When the Heavenly Demon Soul’s internal energy arrived at the location of the poison, Geom Mugeuk began separating the tangled poisons in earnest. Geom Mugeuk’s internal energy moved like the hand of a living person.

Untangling poisons entwined with each other using only internal energy—without Geom Mugeuk’s martial realm, it would have been impossible.

The Heavenly Demon Soul assisted in that work. Because it was the Heavenly Demon Soul born from Geom Mugeuk’s body, it knew what its master wanted without him having to say it. At that moment, the Heavenly Demon Soul was a better assistant than anyone else in the world.

Just as only a true master could display the aesthetics of patience, Geom Mugeuk worked so slowly that one could have asked, When on earth are you going to treat her?

Whenever poison tried to shoot out into the blood vessels while Geom Mugeuk worked, the Heavenly Demon Soul’s internal energy wrapped around it and calmed it again.

Geom Mugeuk was truly acting as the physician, and the Heavenly Demon Soul was fulfilling the role of assistant physician.

He could understand why Chungui had been unable to remove the poison. All sorts of unknown poisons were entangled together. It was to the point where one would not dare touch them, unable to tell what to do or how to remove them.

But Geom Mugeuk calmly untied one knot after another. He was someone who had spent an entire lifetime searching for the materials for the great art. No matter how tangled you are, could you be harder to unravel than my life before regression?

Like that, one hour passed, then two hours passed. Time continued to flow, but Geom Mugeuk’s concentration remained the same as at the beginning.

As he began undoing the last tangle of poison, he conveyed to the Heavenly Demon Soul,

—Now, get ready.

They had now reached the most important moment of today’s treatment.

At the end of those minute movements of internal energy, the instant the tangled poisons completely separated and broke apart.

Geom Mugeuk and the Heavenly Demon Soul instantly wrapped the poisons in internal energy, then began moving them along blood vessels in opposite directions.

—We have to draw them out before the poison runs wild.

Once the entangled poisons were separated, both the poison that had been suppressing and the poison that had been suppressed began to rampage. Both natures—the nature of continued suppression and the sense of release—had activated.

Geom Mugeuk’s and the Heavenly Demon Soul’s internal energies surrounded them and moved them as quickly as possible toward the blood vessels where their respective palms rested.

“Ugh!”

A short scream burst from Chungui’s mouth. Though she was asleep, she seemed to have sensed the poison expanding and trying to burst.

—A little faster!

The poison swelled the blood vessels and was on the verge of exploding.

Swaaaak.

At that moment, the half of the poison that arrived was sucked into Geom Mugeuk’s palm.

Just as the poison was about to spread in all directions from his palm.

Fwoosh.

Geom Mugeuk burned it away in an instant with Samadhi True Fire.

There was not only one flame.

Fwoosh.

On the Heavenly Demon Soul’s palm as well, the poison vanished together with flame.

The Heavenly Demon Soul, too, had succeeded in expelling the poison outside the body before it could flare up within.

“Whew.”

A sigh of relief escaped him on its own. He had truly succeeded by the narrowest of margins.

Geom Mugeuk expressed his gratitude to the Heavenly Demon Soul.

“If it had not been for you, I could not have done it. Thank you, friend.”

Just as a faint smile seemed to form at the corners of the Heavenly Demon Soul’s mouth, it disappeared without a sound.

Geom Mugeuk infused internal energy and examined the rest of Chungui’s body, and only after confirming that no poison remained did he wake her.

Chungui slowly opened her eyes. The light in her eyes had changed from before she received the treatment.

“I survived.”

She had not merely survived. She could feel that the poison that had been so terribly dominating her body had vanished cleanly.

As the poison disappeared, the pain that had made her whole body feel as if it were melting away vanished with it.

She could not even remember the last time her body had not hurt like this.

“You did it.”

Her voice trembled as much as her gaze.

“It wasn’t me. You cured yourself, Divine Doctor.”

“I did?”

“If you had not given me the fortuitous encounter of being invulnerable to all poisons, this treatment could never have been done today.”

Chungui wordlessly took Geom Mugeuk’s hand. These thin hands would no longer need to knead her shoulders and legs.

“I’m sorry I couldn’t treat you sooner.”

The hand gripping Geom Mugeuk tightened. Joy and emotion she could not hide overflowed across her face.

“Now you should be able to spread your ill fame for another thirty years.”

When the two came out of the room, everyone was waiting outside.

The moment Pyo Gigwang saw Chungui, tears welled up in his eyes. Just from the way Chungui walked, he could tell the treatment had succeeded.

“Master!”

Pyo Gigwang burst into tears. From the sight of him lowering his head and weeping, it was clear just how deeply he cared for Chungui.

Looking at her disciple, Chungui spoke spitefully.

“You brat, you’ll still have to massage me every morning.”

Wiping his tears with his sleeve, Pyo Gigwang replied.

“Then promise me. That you’ll be sure to drink a glass of water first.”

Jin Hagun and Bisa-in, Ian and Jin Haryeong, and Han Seol were all looking at Geom Mugeuk. The fact that it had taken this long meant the treatment had truly been difficult.

But as always, Geom Mugeuk had accomplished that difficult task.

“I’m starving to death. Don’t tell me you all ate first without any loyalty?”

Seeing him spout nonsense as always, everyone smiled brightly.

“You’re hungry?”

Chungui swung around the arm that, because of the pain, she had not even been able to lift.

“You shouldn’t move like that yet.”

“Shouldn’t, my foot. Wait here. I’ll cook for you myself.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk said something that sounded like a bolt from the blue to everyone else.

“Does this mean we’ll finally get to taste your insect cuisine, Elder?”

He had eaten it once before, around the time he had received the Great Art of Invulnerability to All Poisons.

Thinking of teasing his friends, Geom Mugeuk grinned from ear to ear.

“It looked disgusting, but it tasted all right.”

Sure enough, not only the women but even Jin Hagun and Bisa-in were startled. It had to be a joke, right? But if Chungui really offered it to them, they could hardly refuse out of courtesy!

Just then, Chungui shook her head.

“No?”

“Pardon?”

This was Chungui, who, because of the pain, had once torn the wings off poisonous insects and eaten their legs raw.

“With guests this precious here, why would I feed them bugs? Gigwang, go buy every ingredient you can find. Buy beef, pork, and chicken, all of it! Today I’m laying out a feast of delicacies from land and sea!”

Everyone was relieved, but Geom Mugeuk alone cried out with an incredulous look on his face.

“You fed them to me!”

“I didn’t think you’d actually eat them just because I told you to.”

Chungui turned to the others and said,

“Wait here. I’ll make you something delicious.”

“Yes! We’ll look forward to it, Divine Doctor!”

As everyone smiled warmly, Geom Mugeuk shouted,

“You said you had to know how to eat poisonous insects to know life!”

With an expression that said even thinking about it was revolting, Chungui walked off and said,

“Who eats bugs these days?”

Chapter 873: Isn't the Real Thing Better?
-----------------------------------------
Chungui was practically flying about.

Since she was like this even before she had properly regained her strength, it showed just how ill she had been until now.

“Gwang, go see if the pork’s done boiling!”

“Yes.”

“And hurry up and wash the vegetables.”

“I’m going!”

Pyo Gwang was excited too. Boiling, simmering, stir-frying—the yard was filled with delicious smells.

Ian said she would go and help, but Chungui absolutely refused to let her come.

Chungui wanted to serve Geom Mugeuk a proper meal with her own hands.

No matter how much her illness had been cured, she was old, and the other party was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. No one could guarantee whether she would ever see him again.

Watching her bustle back and forth so happily, Ian said with a smile,

“I’m not sure I could live like tha—”

Before Ian could finish speaking, Geom Mugeuk leaned his head against her shoulder.

Thump.

Ian turned her head to the side. Geom Mugeuk, who had been nagging and complaining to Chungui just moments ago, had fallen asleep before she knew it.

From the way he slept so softly, she could tell how much mental strength he had spent on the treatment.

‘Yes, rest a little now, Young Master.’

Ian did not move, in case Geom Mugeuk woke. Then she released her qi and blocked out the sounds from outside. She had now reached the level where she could create a barrier and shut out noise.

Han Seol, who had just come out of the building, saw Geom Mugeuk leaning against Ian and turned right back inside.

Just then, Jin Haryeong also came out of her room, and Han Seol said to her,

“There seems to be some time before the meal. Would you like to have a cup of tea in my room?”

“Sure.”

Han Seol took her into the room.

At that time, Bisain was looking at the poisonous insects displayed along one wall of the backyard.

Pyo Gwang had said that the insects that spewed deadly poison were stored separately, so he could look around as much as he liked.

As he was looking at the poisonous insects, Jin Hagun walked over.

“Fortunately, it seems we won’t have to eat those bugs.”

At Jin Hagun’s words, Bisain spoke to the insect he had been looking at.

“You’re lucky.”

Jin Hagun stood beside Bisain. The two had grown close through their shared suffering while dealing with their common enemy, Geom Mugeuk.

After watching the poisonous insects for a while, Bisain asked Jin Hagun,

“Aren’t you worried about the Alliance?”

“I was worried on the first day, but now I’m not thinking about anything.”

It was a remark that did not suit Jin Hagun, so Bisain smiled.

“What about you?”

“I wasn’t worried even on the first day.”

This time, Jin Hagun laughed. Considering the atmosphere when they had first met, it was hard to believe they were having a conversation like this.

“Do you remember? The day I asked Mugeuk what kind of demonic path he dreamed of.”

Bisain nodded. He had been there that day as well.

Jin Hagun continued.

“Mugeuk said then that the martial world he dreamed of was one where the righteous path, the unorthodox path, and the demonic path all got along.”

Bisain took up Jin Hagun’s words.

“In the end, he is keeping his word to the very end.”

“What a stubborn bastard.”

Bisain fully agreed with that. Because he was that stubborn, he must have dragged them all the way to this place today.

Jin Hagun asked cautiously,

“May I ask you something?”

“Miss Han?”

Jin Hagun nodded.

“She is a friend.”

Then came an addition that showed just how close he had grown to Jin Hagun.

“For now.”

A faint smile appeared on Jin Hagun’s lips.

“I hope things go well between you and her.”

“Thank you.”

Just then, Pyo Gwang’s voice rang out from the yard.

“The meal is ready!”

***

The next morning, Geom Mugeuk said farewell to Chungui.

“Stay and enjoy yourself a little longer! I’ll make you more delicious food.”

“No matter how much of a feast you laid out yesterday, the tastiest thing I ever ate here was still that bug from before! The bug that taught me the pain of betrayal.”

Chungui laughed at Geom Mugeuk’s joke.

Now Geom Mugeuk bid her farewell with a serious expression.

“Please stay healthy until I come to visit again!”

She wondered if there would be a next time, but after thinking about it, there would be. Because if he heard that she had died, Geom Mugeuk would come to see her.

“Very well. Stay well until then.”

Jin Hagun, Bisain, and the three women also said farewell to Chungui and Pyo Gwang.

After the farewells were over, Pyo Gwang separately expressed his gratitude to Geom Mugeuk.

“Young Cult Leader, you have saved me twice.”

Geom Mugeuk understood that he meant saving his master was the same as saving him.

“The two people I saved will go on to save two hundred—no, two thousand more lives.”

Pyo Gwang’s gaze deepened. When he had first met Geom Mugeuk, he had been living like a ruined man, but now he was living an immeasurably precious life. Thank you, Young Cult Leader.

“Go safely.”

After Geom Mugeuk had moved away from the main gate, he looked back. Pyo Gwang was entering the building with his master, massaging her shoulders as they went.

‘May the two of you live well too.’

When Geom Mugeuk turned back again, everyone’s eyes were on him.

“Where are we going now?”

At Ian’s question, Geom Mugeuk answered,

“Let’s go wherever our feet take us.”

***

What stopped their steps was a name.

“That’s an awfully brave name, isn’t it?”

At Ian’s words, everyone looked up at the signboard of the restaurant.

Cheonma Restaurant.

They wondered how it dared use the name Cheonma, but the characters were different.

天馬飯店.

It did not use the character ma for demon, but the character ma for horse. In any case, it was certainly a name that could catch the eyes of passersby.

“How could we pass by after seeing this name? We’re hungry too, so let’s have a quick meal here before we go.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, everyone nodded. They were curious as to who had given it such a name and for what reason.

They went inside. Perhaps the food was decent, because there were quite a few customers.

A young woman who looked like a newlywed came over and guided them to their seats.

“One bowl of noodles for each of us, please.”

Compared to the grand name, the taste was ordinary.

Since this visit had been more out of curiosity than for the food, once the meal was over, Geom Mugeuk asked the woman,

“Does the owner here like horses?”

As if she had received the question many times before, the woman immediately understood what he meant.

“Yes, he likes horses very much.”

Just then, a customer at the next table cut into the conversation.

“Why are you lying?”

He revealed the true reason.

“It’s because the owner here has met the Heavenly Demon in person.”

At those astonishing words, Geom Mugeuk and his friends were all startled. On the other hand, the customer who had spoken and his companions laughed aloud in amusement.

Just then, someone inside the kitchen said,

“I really did meet him!”

“Of course you did.”

Judging by the exchange, the customer seemed to be a regular who knew the owner shouting from the kitchen.

With this sort of conversation taking place, there was no way Geom Mugeuk would let it pass.

“The owner has truly met the Cult Leader of the Demon Cult?”

An answer came from the kitchen.

“That’s right.”

“Then tell us the story! Bring us a bottle of liquor too. And serve liquor to everyone else as well. I’ll pay for it.”

At that, all the customers cheered. The atmosphere heated up at once.

“It’s been a while since you told it, hasn’t it? Come on out and tell us.”

From the looks of it, most of the customers there had heard the story before. Worried that they might miss out on free liquor, someone else urged him on.

“Hurry up and tell it!”

“Come out!”

A moment later, a man walked out of the kitchen.

When he saw Geom Mugeuk, he flinched in surprise. It was not because the other party was a martial artist, nor because the people with him had an unusual air.

“Have we perhaps met before?”

When Geom Mugeuk shook his head, the man tilted his head. It was definitely a face he had seen somewhere, but he simply could not remember where.

“Now then, let’s hear the story of how you met the Heavenly Demon!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the cook’s eyes grew distant.

“When I was younger, I once worked as an investigator at a moneylending office. My job was to confirm whether the family of a borrower actually lived at the registered address and have them stand as guarantors.”

Amazingly, the man was none other than Deokchul, the investigator from the moneylending office who had once gone to collect money from Geom Ujin.

“It may be hard to believe, but at the time, the son of the Demon Cult Leader borrowed money from the moneylending office.”

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression that said, I can’t believe you. Beside him, Bisain nodded with a face full of certainty. Because it was such an absurd thing, he was all the more convinced that Geom Mugeuk would have done it.

The listening customers, however, were already laughing. To begin with, the story was unbelievable from the very start.

“If someone heard that, they’d think the Young Cult Leader was some reckless, immature brat.”

Another customer picked up the first customer’s words.

“If that were the case, he would have bought the entire moneylending office. Or taken it by force.”

To ordinary people, the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult borrowing money from a moneylending office was unimaginable.

As if he had experienced this reaction many times before, Deokchul continued speaking in silence.

“I went all the way to the front of the Demon Cult’s main headquarters, saw the Demon Supremes, and even saw the Eldest Young Master.”

How could anyone believe that? How could a mere investigator from a moneylending office have seen the Demon Supremes and the Eldest Young Master? But no one laughed yet. The real part was still to come.

“In front of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s headquarters, they were selling dolls of the Demon Supremes. At the time, I bought dolls of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Demon Buddha.”

As if this still was not the real part, everyone was holding back their laughter.

“Then, at a tavern there, I met the Heavenly Demon.”

As if by prior agreement, all the customers burst into laughter. Those who had already heard it laughed, and the customers hearing the story for the first time laughed as well.

How could he lie so calmly about something so absurd? The lie was so ridiculous, and he told it so shamelessly, that it was funny.

Deokchul’s wife, on the other hand, was distressed. Why did he keep telling that story when he knew he would be ridiculed like this?

“It was no ordinary tavern. Written on the walls of that tavern were words left behind by the Demon Supremes and the Heavenly Demon.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk and his companions knew that his story was true.

“I went up to the man who was drinking alone and told him to stand guarantor for his son.”

Geom Mugeuk asked with a smile,

“And what did he say?”

“He laughed loudly. After I persuaded him by saying there is no parent who can win against his child, he eventually signed.”

In truth, Geom Mugeuk had heard from Ju Yang of the moneylending office long ago that his father had met the investigator. The person he had met back then was the man standing before him now.

Deokchul expressed his deep gratitude toward the Demon Cult Leader.

“Although I committed a great discourtesy, the Heavenly Demon did not kill me. Instead, he bestowed upon me one thousand nyang and told me to buy his doll.”

At the part about telling him to buy his doll, laughter broke out again, and someone said that no matter how many times he heard that part, it was still funny.

“Why do you tell this story even though you know people don’t believe you?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Deokchul revealed the reason.

“Because it is true. That day, I promised the Cult Leader. I said I would return to my hometown and live as a new man. I came straight back to my hometown and opened this restaurant with the money he gave me. I married my wife too.”

It was right then, as his gaze turned toward his wife.

A deep sigh flowed from Deokchul’s wife.

“Stop now.”

Tears rolled down from her eyes.

Her husband was a good man. He was someone she could trust and live with for the rest of her life, except for one thing. She could not understand why he was so obsessed with this lie. She hated seeing her husband become a laughingstock to others.

“I’m sorry.”

At her husband’s apology, his wife was firm this time.

“Promise me you won’t do it anymore from now on.”

After hesitating for a moment, Deokchul nodded. He had known his wife was upset, but he had not realized she was hurting this much.

“I won’t.”

Deokchul looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“The people here will be the last ones to hear my story. I’m sorry, dear. I won’t do it again.”

“Thank you.”

His wife did not feel good about this either.

Just as Deokchul was trudging back toward the kitchen, Geom Mugeuk asked from behind him,

“So did you buy the doll?”

Deokchul stopped walking and looked back at Geom Mugeuk.

“Why do you ask?”

“Because I believe you.”

For an instant, surprise flashed across Deokchul’s face.

“You believe me?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded with a serious expression.

From that expression, Deokchul could tell. He was not mocking him. He truly believed him.

“Why do you believe me?”

“Because I think you told the truth.”

Deokchul’s face brightened. Until now, no one had ever believed his words.

They had said they believed him, but he knew even his wife did not. Yes, if today was going to be the last time he told this story anyway.

“Please wait a moment.”

Deokchul brought a box from the kitchen. Aside from his wife, he had never shown it to anyone.

Inside were dolls. There were dolls of the Eight Demon Venerables, of course, as well as dolls of Geom Ujin and Geom Mugeuk.

Since it was the first time the guests had seen the dolls, they crowded around them.

“Oh! They’re well made.”

“Who’s this supposed to be?”

“This Demon Venerable is so impressive!”

The dolls included every kind that had been sold at the time.

“Why did you buy all the dolls?”

He could have just bought the Cult Leader and Fist Demon dolls as extras.

Yet gathered there were dolls of Geom Ujin, Geom Mugeuk, and all of the Eight Demon Venerables.

“As for why I bought them all…”

Geom Mugeuk, who had asked the question, guessed the answer.

“Because they said they’d give you thirty percent off if you bought them all at once.”

Deokchul stared at Geom Mugeuk in astonishment.

“That’s right. How did you know?”

That was not the only thing Geom Mugeuk had guessed.

“The money the Demonic Cult Leader’s son borrowed—was it ten nyang?”

“That’s right. It was ten nyang. Hup!”

In that instant, Deokchul started in shock.

“How did you know that too?”

Now all the guests’ gazes turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“If you lent money to the son, would it do to go collect it from the father?”

Just as everyone was blinking in confusion, wondering what he meant,

Geom Mugeuk placed one of the dolls from the box on the table.

“Whose doll is this?”

“It’s the Young Cult Leader doll.”

Geom Mugeuk stood beside the doll. Then he struck the exact same pose as the doll.

“Isn’t the real thing better?”

Everyone looked once at Geom Mugeuk, then once at the doll. Their gazes went back and forth until—

“Aaagh!”

A scream burst from someone’s mouth when they realized the two looked exactly alike.

Soon, silence descended over the place.

When Geom Mugeuk raised his hand, every liquor bottle and cup there rose into the air and filled the cups. With this one move, he had shown that he was truly the Young Cult Leader.

Geom Mugeuk set down the cost of the food they had eaten, and beside it, ten nyang. It was payment for the liquor he had promised to buy them, but he deliberately gave ten nyang.

His final farewell was to Deokchul’s wife.

“Your husband is neither a liar nor a braggart. He is a fine man who kept the promise he made with my father.”

Deokchul’s wife’s face was filled with both apology and emotion.

Geom Mugeuk went outside with his companions.

Deokchul, his wife, and the guests all rushed outside to see them off.

They were deeply moved. To think they had seen the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult in person! To think he had bought them liquor! To think they had drunk liquor he had poured for them! It was an experience they would pass down to their descendants for generations.

As they watched them recede into the distance, Deokchul’s wife apologized first.

“I’m sorry, dear.”

“It’s all right.”

Not only his wife, but the guests apologized as well. Even after experiencing such a thing, their mouths were already itching to tell it; having gone through what he had, how could he possibly have kept silent?

“I’m sorry for all this time.”

“I apologize too.”

“Forgive us.”

Deokchul smiled as he looked at them. Since no matter how much they boasted to others, no one would believe them either,

“Now all of you have become braggarts too.”

At those words, the guests laughed.

When they turned their heads again, the Young Cult Leader and his companions had already vanished.

“If we tell people about this too, no one in the world will believe us, right?”

His wife accepted that sorrowful yet overwhelming feeling with a smile.

“Tell me. I’ll listen for the rest of my life.”

Chapter 874: You’ve Saved Up a Lot of Money, Right?
---------------------------------------------------
Where the water had receded, only mud remained.

Some houses had collapsed, while others had rooms filled with mud.

People were clearing away a place where they sank into the mud up to their waists.

Children and adults alike were shedding tears. The women were struggling to salvage whatever household goods they could from the mud.

Two people were watching from a short distance away, and the woman among them was Geum Arin of the Golden Dragon Clan.

“Last night’s flood broke the embankment and swept over the village. Several people died because they could not escape in time.”

At the report from the Golden Dragon Clan’s chief steward beside her, Geum Arin let out a sigh.

“Have new houses built for those who lost their homes.”

The chief steward asked in surprise.

“You mean all of them?”

“Yes, build them all anew. Prepare places for them to stay for the time being as well, and send relief supplies.”

Geum Cheonbang, the head of the Golden Dragon Clan, had entrusted the clan’s affairs to Geum Arin and withdrawn from the front line. Now, every decision regarding the clan was hers to make.

“It will cost more money than you expect.”

The chief steward looked troubled, but Geum Arin was firm.

“That doesn’t mean we can simply stand by and watch them suffer, does it?”

This village was not far from the Golden Dragon Clan.

“I will request cooperation from the Murim Alliance and handle it together with them.”

“This is our territory. We should be the ones to take care of these people.”

“Did you not spend as much as one hundred thousand nyang for people in another region last time?”

She understood the chief steward’s worries.

“Are you afraid I’ll squander the main family’s wealth?”

“That is not it, but…”

He said that, but in truth, he was worried.

“I’m someone who loves money too. But this is something that must be done.”

The chief steward looked at her as if asking why she had changed so much, but she did not tell him.

“See to it at once.”

“Yes, understood.”

She stood there for a while, watching the restoration work. Not only the villagers, but also warriors carrying swords had come to help with the recovery.

The men cleared away heavy debris, and the women carried household goods out of the houses. There were also people looking after the children. To one side, they lit fires and cooked food to feed the children. The little ones, too young to understand, ran about laughing merrily.

At that moment, someone spoke behind her.

“The Golden Dragon Clan must be making a great deal of money these days.”

When she turned around in surprise, a middle-aged man was standing a short distance away. His face and body were round and soft, but in contrast to that harmless-looking face and figure, the presence and aura radiating from him were exceedingly sharp.

Geum Arin was an expert. She had once led a secret organization operated by the Golden Dragon Merchant Guild. Although she had been neglecting her martial arts training lately after formally inheriting the clan’s work, she still possessed enough keenness to discern an opponent’s strength at a glance.

‘He’s an expert.’

He was an expert powerful enough to make even her tense.

And the moment she saw him, she knew. He had not come with good intentions.

Srrrk.

A man with snow-white hair emerged from concealment in front of her.

He was Im Hyeok, Geum Arin’s personal bodyguard. As she had intended to come here without causing a commotion, she had brought only Im Hyeok with her.

Im Hyeok warned Geum Arin through sound transmission.

—He is an expert. Be careful.

After rumors spread through the merchant world that her father had stepped down, many things had happened.
