Some tried to slash contract terms, thinking she knew nothing of the world, while others underestimated her and schemed in every possible way to profit from her.

There had been many tricks that did not work, but no one had ever come directly like this.

Im Hyeok drew his sword, but the man was not frightened in the slightest. Just from that, it was clear the man’s skill surpassed Im Hyeok’s.

Geum Arin signaled Im Hyeok to step aside. If the man had meant to kill her, he would not have appeared like this but would have ambushed her instead.

Geum Arin asked the man politely.

“May I ask Senior’s honored name?”

“People call me Blood Money Pouch.”

At the words Blood Money Pouch, Im Hyeok’s expression hardened. If the rumors of his skill were true, even if they attacked together, he would not be an opponent they could handle easily.

Geum Arin had also heard of Blood Money Pouch before.

Bearing the name of a pouch of bloody money, he was an extremely famous figure in the merchant world.

He was notorious as a vicious man who latched onto various merchant guilds throughout the Central Plains like a leech and extorted money from them.

One might wonder how much of an expert a man who did such things could possibly be, but in the murim, there were always exceptional experts.

They said money could make even ghosts obey, and the fact that he had survived while extorting money in the merchant world, where wealth overflowed, proved just how formidable an expert he was.

He liked exerting influence over the merchant world and enjoyed squeezing money out of them. The money he extorted this way was squandered on drink and gambling. There were times he scattered tens of thousands of nyang in a pleasure house in a single night.

“Someone of great fame has come.”

Blood Money Pouch replied with a broad grin.

“What is my fame compared to yours? Rumors are spreading everywhere that the Golden Dragon Clan has been saving the world these days.”

It seemed her recent acts of charity had reached this money-grubbing demon’s ears.

“There is a reason behind those good deeds.”

“What reason?”

“I made a promise. To live kindly.”

“With whom?”

Originally, she had not been the type to help people in need or make donations like this.

But after she became the successor of the Golden Dragon Clan, she had not spared money in doing good deeds.

She was living with the heart of repaying her past mistakes.

Following the will of one person who had helped make her the successor.

“There is someone whose name uses the character yeon, as in smoke. It is a promise I made with that person who vanished like smoke.”

Of course, Blood Money Pouch had no way of knowing what she meant.

“Why do you still keep someone who vanished like smoke in your heart?”

Because he had not been ordinary smoke. Even now, Geom Yeon came to mind from time to time. The astonishing things he had accomplished. It would be nice if she could see him even once more, but she would likely never see him again.

“Why have you come to see me?”

In truth, Geum Arin knew better than anyone why he had come, but she asked as if she did not.

“I have come to make a deal with you.”

“Please speak.”

He took a sheet of paper from his robe and held it out. When she read it, it was a contract stating that she would pay three hundred thousand nyang in exchange for protection from an enemy. It was not a small sum, but a demand for as much as three hundred thousand nyang.

“Who is the dangerous enemy written here?”

At Geum Arin’s question, he slowly approached her. Im Hyeok tried to stop him, but Geum Arin signaled that it was all right.

Since he had presented a contract, nothing would happen until she placed her handprint on it.

Blood Money Pouch came to her side and looked toward the place where restoration work was in full swing. In front of them, a man covered completely in mud passed by, carrying mud and broken household items in a handcart.

Watching him, Blood Money Pouch mentioned someone.

“According to the rumors, there is an exceedingly vicious fellow in the merchant world. They say that man is so greedy for money that he steals everything his opponent possesses and is not satisfied until he takes their life as well. I heard he is like the flood that swept through this place, a natural disaster merchants cannot stop.”

Blood Money Pouch turned his head and looked at Geum Arin as he said,

“I am saying I will protect you from that man.”

Geum Arin could tell. The vicious fellow he spoke of meant none other than Blood Money Pouch himself.

In other words, it was a brazen threat that if she did not hand over three hundred thousand nyang, he would steal all her money and kill her.

The reason he had prepared and brought a contract must be so that if the Murim Alliance later made an issue of this matter, he could claim it had been a legitimate transaction.

Geum Arin recalled an incident that had recently occurred in the merchant world.

“I heard a rumor that the guild master of the Nine Dragons Merchant Guild had one of his arms cut off not long ago. Was that also the work of that vicious man?”

It was known to the outside world that his arm had been lost in an accident. But looking at Blood Money Pouch, she felt certain he too had been victimized by this man.

As if her guess was correct, Blood Money Pouch defended the man.

“I think that vicious fellow showed mercy. After all, he ended it with just one arm.”

When her father had been the clan head, Blood Money Pouch had never once come to the Golden Dragon Clan. It was publicly known that her father was close to the leader of the Murim Alliance.

And now that she had taken charge of the clan’s affairs, he had come as if to show off. As if he had been waiting for this very moment.

Even so, the fact that he had come to her like this while her father was still very much alive meant that this man not only had the strength to back it up, but also possessed a boldness bordering on madness.

“I’m sorry, but I do not have that much money right now. If you come again later, I will give it to you then.”

“You have money to build houses for those people, though?”

He had overheard the conversation she had shared earlier with the chief steward. That was why he had said the Golden Dragon Clan must be making a great deal of money these days.

“Good. Let us do this, then. First, I will take only the money you planned to use to build their houses. As for the rest, I will consider it money I have lent you. I will charge only one-tenth in interest per day.”

He set conditions she could not accept.

He was someone who enjoyed this process. The amount of money was not what mattered; what mattered to him was the other person’s reaction.

When Geum Arin gave no answer, another threat flowed out.

“Ah, you probably have not heard that rumor. They say that before the head of the White Dragon Merchant Guild had his arm cut off by that vicious man, the guard protecting him had his neck cut off first. You had not heard that, had you? After all, the only thing you people care about is money. The head of the White Dragon Merchant Guild shouted that he would not pay because he trusted the guard protecting him. In the end, that guard was the only one who became pitiful. I hear he even had a family.”

It was a threat that he would kill Im Hyeok as well. He would steal their money and mock them too. Blood Money Pouch was acting with utter arrogance.

His threat led to the drawing of his sword.

Srrrng.

“I will ease our young family head’s worries as well. Originally, when a subordinate’s neck is cut off, decisions tend to be made quickly.”

As he moved to kill Im Hyeok, Geum Arin said coldly,

“If you lay a hand on us, the Murim Alliance will not stand by.”

But Blood Money Pouch was certain she would not be able to inform the clan head of this matter.

“You are the one leading the clan, yet you cannot handle your own affairs and will go complain to your father? Go ahead and try.”

She had put forward the Murim Alliance, but it did not work.

“Even if the Murim Alliance comes to investigate, I will give half of the three hundred thousand nyang as a bribe to the one who comes. Let us see whether that fellow chooses justice or money.”

Blood Money Pouch chuckled as he enjoyed the situation. There was nothing more pleasurable than the thrill of stealing what was most important to someone.

“I will kill this man and cut off one of your arms.”

Blood Money Pouch mocked her.

“Then again, even without an arm, someone else will count your money for you. They will feed you and wash you too. That is the good thing about being rich.”

Geum Arin’s expression hardened. What if there was no guarantee they could win if they fought him?

“I will place my handprint.”

She could not lose Im Hyeok over three hundred thousand nyang.

“If you kill him, then you will have to go to my father to receive that handprint.”

She meant that she would die here as well.

“Then I will take just one arm from this man.”

Just as Blood Money Pouch raised his sword to cut off Im Hyeok’s arm,

Geum Arin drew her sword and stood in front of him. He had guarded her since she was young.

“Not even a single hair!”

Im Hyeok stepped in front of her again and said,

“I am all right, Young Family Head.”

If only she could remain unharmed, he could offer not just an arm, but his life.

“When you act so tenderly toward each other, how can I not kill him? If only to see what kind of reaction you will have.”

Just as he raised his sword high,

Splat.

Something flew at Blood Money Pouch.

Thinking it was a hidden weapon, Blood Money Pouch looked down at himself in surprise, only to find that it was mud.

When he raised his head and looked, the young man who had been moving trash with a handcart had thrown mud from the cart at him.

“What are you doing? Everyone else is working, and you’re over here fighting!”

For a moment, Blood Money Pouch was dumbfounded. The one who had interfered was absurd enough, but had he really just let that mud hit him? It must have hit him because there had been no killing intent at all.

Blood Money Pouch looked at the young man. He wore a sword, but there was no trace that he had trained in martial arts at all.

Above all, the reason he felt no tension whatsoever was that the young man’s face and entire body were covered in mud. A true expert could not possibly be doing this kind of restoration work.

Blood Money Pouch laughed loudly, as if amused.

“Have you lost sight of everything because you lost your home?”

At that moment, Geum Arin’s eyes gradually widened as she looked at the young man.

“No way?”

At first glance, his face had seemed familiar. Since his whole body was covered in mud and his face was smeared with it, she had not recognized him at once. But when she heard his voice, she knew.

“Geom Yeon!”

Astonishingly, the person who had been passing by while pulling the handcart was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

It was the first time Geum Arin had ever been this glad to see someone. She was truly glad. She was so happy she almost cried out in delight.

“Why are you here?”

“I was on my way to meet you, but I couldn’t just pass these people by, so I was helping them.”

“You were on your way to see me? Why me?”

“Have you already forgotten? I said I would introduce you to my friends next time.”

That was right. Geom Mugeuk had said that. That he had friends who were pure despite their positions. That he would introduce her to people like that.

Geum Arin had interpreted his words like this: the position of young head of the Golden Dragon Clan was not so extraordinary, so do not make a fuss and live kindly.

She had thought that was what he meant, but he had truly come to introduce his friends.

Blood Money Pouch’s expression had hardened. It bothered him that this man knew Geum Arin. That meant he had approached intentionally.

Sure enough, Geom Mugeuk threw an unexpected question at Blood Money Pouch.

“You have saved up a lot of money, haven’t you?”

“Why do you ask?”

“My friends have such sharp ears, you see. They all heard. I also tattled a little.”

It meant they had heard the conversation between him and Geum Arin.

“They all want to cut off your arms.”

“What?”

“There are five people in my party besides me, so not only your arms but even your legs have all been reserved. It seems you’ll have to spend the rest of your life lying down and eating only the food someone else feeds you. I think you’ll need a lot of money.”

Blood Money Pouch’s expression stiffened at once. To dismiss it as the nonsense of a madman felt strangely wrong.

“One person in particular got very angry. Why did you have to bring up the Murim Alliance? I will try to stop him, but during this journey, he regained his true nature and has become very rough and violent. He may even switch over to the unorthodox faction.”

Blood Money Pouch could not understand what this mud-covered young man in front of him was talking about.

Geom Mugeuk turned to look behind him, where the restoration work was in full swing, and said,

“Now then, allow me to introduce my friends.”

Chapter 875: A Friend as the Murim Public Enemy
-----------------------------------------------
The water trickling from the edge of the roof looked as though it were shedding tears.

With a flash, Jin Hagun lifted the roof and checked if anyone was trapped underneath. Fortunately, no one had been crushed beneath it.

He was covered in mud from head to toe, except for his eyes, looking like someone who had completely fallen into a muddy pit and only just climbed back out.

Bisain was lifting a massive tree that had been uprooted and washed downstream from a short distance away, moving it aside.

He too was completely covered in mud, yet worked without a care in the world.

Bisain glanced at Jin Hagun. He could sense from Jin Hagun’s expression that he was deeply upset. Jin Hagun had also been the first to rush here when he first saw the state of this devastated place.

Hanseol had filled a large tub with clean water and was washing and treating the injured. She applied golden wound medicine to their wounds and wrapped them in clean bandages.

Beside her, Jinharyeong was boiling soup in a massive pot and cooking rice.

The people who had lost their homes had no appetite at all, but precisely because of that, they needed to eat. Everyone was so exhausted they were on the verge of collapsing, and above all, they had to feed the children.

Ian was looking after the children who had lost their parents. She embraced the crying children. She knew better than anyone what these children were feeling—the fear of knowing that there was no one left in this world to care for them.

And she also knew how the terror they felt and experienced in this moment would affect a child’s life forever.

“It’s okay, it’s okay. Everything will be okay.”

Ian embraced the children one by one.

Just then, Geommugeuk shouted from over there.

“Everyone, come this way for a moment.”

They stopped what they were doing and all walked over.

Geumarin watched them with a heart full of surprise.

‘He really brought his friends!’

What was even more surprising was that they were working alongside them on the recovery efforts here.

When Geumarin first saw Hyeoljeonnang, she had realized he was a master stronger than herself.

But she couldn’t sense the martial prowess of the five people standing before her now. They seemed like masters greater than herself, yet also like people who had never practiced martial arts at all.

If she hadn’t known of someone called Geommugeuk, she would have thought they were people who had never learned martial arts. Five young people had gathered, and it was impossible that all of them were masters so profound she couldn’t even detect their strength.

But since they were people brought by Geommugeuk, and judging by Geommugeuk’s martial prowess...

‘All of them are masters far greater than Hyeoljeonnang, not to mention myself.’

They were so young. Who on earth were they?

“That angry friend—I’ll try to calm him down somehow.”

Geommugeuk stuck close to Hyeoljeonnang as if they were on the same side and whispered to him.

“Especially that friend over there—he’s very angry. You can tell just by looking at him that he’s stubborn, right?”

The person Geommugeuk was looking at was Jin Hagun.

Hyeoljeonnang tried to gauge Jin Hagun’s strength, but he could tell nothing.

A true master wouldn’t do this kind of work with his own hands. Not while covered in mud, no less.

Because of this prejudice, Hyeoljeonnang didn’t think these young men before him were masters stronger than himself.

Still, just in case, he asked cautiously.

“What kind of man is he?”

“A friend who finally found freedom by breaking the chains that bound him.”

“Then shouldn’t he be happy?”

“This is his territory. He’s returned to being a workaholic, you see.”

How could Hyeoljeonnang know that this was the orthodox faction’s territory?

“Our old man here was trying to steal the money to build houses here. Not only that, he even said he’d bribe the Murim Alliance martial artists to cover it up, so it’s only natural he’s furious.”

Jin Hagun strode toward the two of them.

The momentum was so extraordinary that Hyeoljeonnang instinctively drew his sword.

But the blade was aimed at Geommugeuk’s neck.

“If I flick a single finger, this one dies.”

Without hesitation, Jin Hagun raised his hand and flicked a finger. Of the others watching from behind, none showed any sign of tension.

“Ridiculous. Did you think I would fall for such a trick? This one is undoubtedly your leader.”

At that, Geommugeuk laughed loudly and spoke to everyone.

“Even a villain like this thinks I look like a leader, doesn’t he?”

Hyeoljeonnang flinched at the word “villain,” but Jin Hagun poured fuel on the fire.

“If you can kill him, try it. No, please kill him. Let us watch how exactly that friend dies.”

What in the world were they saying! And why were those in the back all nodding their heads?

Hyeoljeonnang thought they were mocking him, believing he couldn’t kill the man.

“You wretches dare—!”

Slash! Chop!

Hyeoljeonnang’s blade cut through Geommugeuk’s neck without hesitation.

Clutching his neck, Geommugeuk collapsed right then and there.

“There, I killed him as you wished.”

If things had gone as he expected, everyone should have been shocked and appalled.

Yet none of those watching were surprised. Even one of them being surprised would have been understandable.

‘Was that talkative fellow a nobody to them?’

In any case, having easily killed one of their party, Hyeoljeonnang became convinced that they were people without martial arts.

If there had been a true master among them, they wouldn’t have stood by and watched him die.

“Who shall I kill next?”

After all, when he had cut Geommugeuk’s neck, he had resolved to kill them all.

Just then, someone spoke.

“Who else! You should kill that heartless wretch who asked you to kill his friend over there first.”

Hyeoljeonnang glared at the speaker with wide eyes. The man he had thought dead was lying on his side, propping his head up with one hand while resting his elbow on the ground.

“But I clearly cut his neck?”

He had even felt the sensation of cutting through flesh. Then how was he alive? If they had tricked him, how on earth had they done it?

Geommugeuk chose Bisain next after Jin Hagun.

“Next, let’s go with that savage fellow who didn’t try to stop his friend from getting his friend killed and just watched.”

Swoosh.

Hyeoljeonnang swung his sword at Geommugeuk, who was lying down.

But before he knew it, Geommugeuk was standing beside him. Naturally, he hadn’t seen how Geommugeuk dodged. It felt as though he were dreaming.

“Hey, what’s the deal? I’m on your side, remember? Old man, can that friend over there really stop him alone?”

Jin Hagun was continuing to stride forward.

This time, Hyeoljeonnang thrust his sword at Jin Hagun.

Shing! Shing!

He thrust his blade at the fastest speed he could muster, but Jin Hagun lightly dodged and counterattacked.

Tap.

With a single move, Jin Hagun subdued his vital point.

Only now did Hyeoljeonnang understand. They were all masters beyond any comparison to himself.

“Which arm did you say it was? The right one? Cut off just one!”

Hyeoljeonnang was terrified by Geommugeuk’s words. That’s right. Hadn’t they said they would cut off his arms and legs?

The image of himself with both arms and legs severed was truly horrific.

The words that burst out reflexively were these:

“If you touch me, you will have to answer to the Murim Alliance.”

Geommugeuk sighed from beside him and said,

“No, I told you not to bring up the Murim Alliance.”

Jin Hagun’s fist flew toward his stomach.

Crack!

Thud!

With Jin Hagun’s strike, his dantian shattered into pieces. For a martial artist, the pain of a shattered dantian was greater than having an arm cut off.

Hyeoljeonnang rolled on the ground and screamed.

“Uwaaaaaaah!”

The physical pain was unbearable, but the despair of losing all the inner strength he had built up until now and never being able to possess inner strength again forever brought him even greater agony.

“Damn it! Fuck! You bastard, I’ll kill you! Uwaaaaaaah!”

Looking down at him writhing and thrashing on the ground, Jin Hagun spoke coldly.

“Why cut off your arms and legs? I’ll keep your limbs intact and make you pay for your crimes. I won’t let you lie around and eat in comfort.”

He would be sent to the harshest dark prison among the many prisons of the Murim Alliance, where he would perform hard labor until he died.

Hyeoljeonnang’s eyes blazed with hatred.

“You bastard, I will have my revenge.”

Even with his dantian shattered, there was a reason he could speak so boldly.

“You have no idea who I’ve been bribing all this time. How great those people are. No matter how great a master you are, you cannot handle an enormous organization like the Murim Alliance.”

Hyeoljeonnang laughed like a madman.

“I’ll make you a public enemy of the martial world.”

Jin Hagun said, his eyes cold.

“You will meet them all in the dark prison as well. Everyone connected to you—I’ll root out every last one of them.”

“How could someone like you?”

Jin Hagun asked Imhyeok a favor.

“Take him to the nearest Murim Alliance branch.”

Jin Hagun revealed his identity himself.

“Tell them the Demonic Annihilation Lord Jin Hagun sent him, and have him transferred to the main headquarters.”

Imhyeok was startled, and so was Geumarin. There wasn’t a martial artist alive who didn’t know that the Demonic Annihilation Lord was the one who would become the next Murim Alliance Lord.

Hyeoljeonnang’s eyes went wide. That man was the Young Alliance Lord of the Murim Alliance?

“Nonsense!”

Geommugeuk squatted down beside Hyeoljeonnang, who had collapsed on the ground, and spoke spitefully.

“That’s why I told you not to bring up the Murim Alliance. Why did you keep talking about it?”

Looking into eyes full of disbelief, Geommugeuk added in a whisper.

“I’ll make sure that friend becomes a public enemy of the martial world! He’s gradually getting his true nature back.”

“...What the hell are you people?”

“We’re just youths who left home.”

That was the end of the conversation. Geommugeuk pressed his vital points and meridians simultaneously.

Geommugeuk guessed the reason Jin Hagun hadn’t cut off his arms and legs. It was because the children were watching from a distance. Lest the children see such a sight. That was the true nature of his friend called Jin Hagun.

And besides, the life remaining for Hyeoljeonnang would be far harder and more miserable than living with severed limbs.

“Now, everyone, introduce yourselves.”

Bisain introduced himself to Geumarin.

“I am Bisain of the Sa-do Alliance.”

Geommugeuk added from the side.

“He is the one who will become the next Sa-do Alliance Lord.”

Geumarin and Imhyeok’s eyes grew even wider. It truly sounded like a joke, but they could tell it wasn’t. That fearsome face standing silently as if confirming the words was far from joking.

The Young Alliance Lord of the Murim Alliance and the Young Alliance Lord of the Sa-do Alliance together?

Geumarin’s pupils trembled. He was introducing them as friends?

It wasn’t just them.

“I am Hanseol of the Ice Palace.”

As expected, Geommugeuk revealed her identity.

“She is the Young Palace Lord of the Northern Sea Ice Palace.”

Even the Young Palace Lord of the Northern Sea Ice Palace, whom she had only heard of in legends, was present. Her white robes were stained, but the cold energy dwelling in her eyes made it clear she spoke no lie.

She learned that Jinharyeong was Jin Hagun’s younger sibling and the leader of a gathering of younger-generation martial artists, and that Ian was the Ghost Shadow Lord of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Naturally, Geumarin’s gaze turned to Geommugeuk. You, who have all these people as friends? You, who could bring such extraordinary people all the way here?

“Who on earth are you?”

Geommugeuk looked at her and spoke calmly.

“I am Geommugeuk, the Young Cult Lord of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“!”

Geumarin was so shocked that her legs gave out.

She couldn’t believe this situation. The Young Alliance Lord of the Murim Alliance, the Young Alliance Lord of the Sa-do Alliance, and the Young Cult Lord of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?

The entire future of the martial world was standing before her eyes.

Geommugeuk had brought the entire martial world with him to find her.

People of such tremendous status were helping the people, covered head to toe in mud.

Masters of their caliber could have easily swept through everything using lightness skills. Like her, they could have simply issued orders to build new houses.

Yet they had all worked directly alongside the people, and cooked meals for the people and children.

Imhyeok sent a telepathic message to her, who stood blankly.

—Hurry and greet them.

Geumarin greeted them first, cupping her fists respectfully with a trembling voice.

“I am Geumarin of the Golden Dragon Clan.”

She looked at Geommugeuk and revealed her honest feelings.

“What right does someone like me have to be introduced to such great figures?”

Geommugeuk stood facing her. The playfulness had vanished from his face; he was more serious than ever.

“Young Family Lord, you were doing well. Far better than I had expected.”

By the time they arrived here, they had already learned of the good deeds she had done before them. They had also heard that she intended to spend a great deal of money to help the people here.

“I think I was right to choose you back then.”

Emotion surged up in her chest. More than any other words, those gave her strength.

“Now then, we’ve exchanged greetings, so let’s get to work!”

Geum Arin also took part in the restoration work. It was the first time in her life she had ever done such a thing. She carried loads as she was told and looked after children as well. She truly worked hard.

They ate there together, too.

When Geom Mugeuk first said they should eat, Geum Arin instantly thought of the finest restaurant where she could host them.

But what Geom Mugeuk had meant was that they should eat the rice cooked there, together. With everyone else.

At that moment, Geum Arin felt ashamed.

‘I still have a long way to go.’

It weighed on her all throughout the meal, so she confessed honestly. She told him she had harbored such a shameful thought.

Then Geom Mugeuk said,

“You should have said so sooner! The work was hard—I would have liked to eat somewhere nice.”

The Young Cult Leader soothed her shame with a joke.

After the meal, Jin Hagun looked toward the collapsed embankment in the distance and said,

“I didn’t know there was an embankment here. I only found out this time that there had been several floods.”

He was reproaching himself. This place was clearly within the Murim Alliance’s territory, and he wondered whether, if they had built the embankment stronger and prepared properly, they might have prevented innocent people from dying.

Geom Mugeuk comforted him.

“Is that the only thing we don’t know? We’ll have to go through countless things like this from now on.”

“Even so, I can’t help being angry.”

“That anger will lead you to become a good Alliance Leader.”

Jin Hagun looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“And not lead me to the Unorthodox Faction?”

It was Bisain who took his joke.

“You becoming unorthodox would be as difficult as Mugeuk becoming taciturn.”

The three of them looked at one another and smiled together. The longer their journey continued, the deeper their friendship grew.

After finishing the meal like that, they parted from Geum Arin.

The last words Geom Mugeuk gave Geum Arin were these.

“From now on, my yeon is not the yeon of smoke, but the yeon of connection.”

Geum Arin bowed respectfully.

“I will cherish the lesson you have given me for the rest of my life.”

Geom Mugeuk had not said a single word telling her how she should live. Instead, he had shown her with his own actions. How she ought to live.

Even these extraordinary people were living so passionately, seeking their own paths. Even this extraordinary person had come all this long way to keep a promise he had made in passing.

Leaving behind a great lesson, Geom Mugeuk and his companions departed.

Geum Arin and Im Hyeok stood there watching until they disappeared into the sunset.

“Uncle.”

“Yes, Young Family Head.”

“This isn’t a dream, is it?”

“No, it is not.”

A bright smile spread across Geum Arin’s face, and with it, a certain resolve flashed through her.

Today, Hyeoljeonnang’s dantian had vanished, but in its place, a radiant yeouiju had appeared in the heart of the Young Family Head of the Golden Dragon Family.

“The heavens giving me such an incredible connection must mean they want me to try living a magnificent life, right?”

Chapter 876: Even If I Become Cult Leader and Alliance Leader
-------------------------------------------------------------
The Tongcheongak Operations Room was busy again today.

As always, countless reports flew in, and the strategists analyzed and sorted through them.

And today, there was a visit from someone unexpected.

“Greetings, Lord!”

At Geom Ujin’s arrival, the strategists stopped their work and greeted him in unison.

Sima Ming, the Chief Strategist who had been in his office, hurriedly rushed out. The day shift strategists had already been replaced by the night crew, but Sima Ming had yet to leave his desk.

“What brings you at this hour, Lord?”

“I’ve come to share a cup of tea with you.”

“Please, come in.”

Sima Ming guided Geom Ujin into his own office.

“Please sit, Lord. I will brew the tea.”

While Sima Ming brewed the tea himself, Geom Ujin took a seat and looked around the office. It was filled with books and documents.

“Are you keeping up with your mental cultivation practice?”

“Yes, I practice every morning and evening.”

The mental cultivation method that Geom Ujin had specially taught him was likely an unparalleled technique that any martial artist would desperately desire.

The more he practiced it, the healthier he felt. His body grew lighter and more refreshed.

“You must sleep more. If you collapse, the main cult collapses with you. So take extra care of your health.”

“Thank you for your concern.”

Staying up late had been a lifelong habit for him, but these days, it was the Lord who had been sleeping late.

The Lord had always fallen asleep promptly at his usual hour.

Sima Ming personally brewed a tea he had been saving especially for when Geom Ujin or Geom Mugik visited.

“Please, have some.”

“Excellent flavor.”

How could Sima Ming not know why the Lord was so sleepless? At this late hour, the Lord had come to find him, his heart torn between love for his son and his own long-cherished ambition.

As he drank his tea, Sima Ming suddenly recalled the past. His youth, when he had dreamed of becoming Chief Strategist.

“Do you remember what you first said to me?”

Geom Ujin nodded.

“You told me back then: ‘If I lose this fight, you will die with me. Even so, will you become my strategist?’”

In those days, the succession struggle had been in full swing, and the atmosphere had been incomparably more brutal than that of Geom Mugik’s succession struggle.

Geom Ujin remembered that day as well.

“And you said, ‘I will make this young lord the Cult Leader.’”

Geom Ujin’s gaze upon Sima Ming was gentle.

“And you kept that promise.”

Regret flashed across Sima Ming’s eyes.

“I sometimes wonder. Was there not a better way back then?”

Watching Geom Mugik’s succession struggle made him think of it all the more. Surely he could have given better counsel.

Geom Ujin set down his teacup and spoke.

“That was the best we could do.”

He was nothing but grateful to hear the Lord say so. Back then, they had believed they could change the world. That single belief had brought them this far.

Geom Ujin looked at the strategic topographic map visible past the window and asked.

“What are those blue lines?”

Blue lines were drawn here and there across the map.

“They are the Young Lord’s movements.”

Geom Ujin asked with a surprised expression.

“Are you tracking all of his movements?”

At that, Sima Ming smiled and shook his head.

“The Young Lord sends word to Tongcheongak whenever he is able to contact us.”

Geom Ujin stepped out of the office and stood before the map. Sima Ming followed and stood respectfully a few paces behind.

“He has been traveling far and wide.”

Geom Ujin understood. If his son was sending word even when he did not need to, then he was showing this to his father.

*Father, this is the life I want.*

His son had told him as much, begging him to travel the Central Plains for leisure as well. Through this journey, the son was sending this message.

*Father, let us live like this.*

“Is there no movement from the Murim Alliance or the Sado Alliance?”

“No, there is none.”

Geom Ujin turned to Sima Ming and asked. It was quiet, even though Ma Jeongsa and the three successors were traveling together in the Central Plains.

“Has this martial world ever been so defenseless?”

A smile played at Geom Ujin’s lips as he asked, making it clear he spoke in jest.

“I can say with certainty that there has never been such a time.”

Geom Ujin’s gaze returned to the blue lines.

Sima Ming spoke to the subordinate strategists.

“Everyone, step outside and get some fresh air.”

The strategists in the operations room all went out.

Once they had left, only Geom Ujin and Sima Ming remained. Sima Ming manipulated a hidden device on the wall of the operations room.

*Rumble.*

The wall bearing the topographic map opened left and right, revealing a new map behind it.

The other walls did the same. As new walls were revealed, walls inscribed with different information appeared.

The map that had been filled with blue was now flooded with a red tide.

Red flags stood upon the map.

Originally, the Majongi banners had possessed their own unique colors.

Those various colors were unified into a single color.

Red flags.

Black characters written upon the flags:

Fist, Blade, Sword, Poison, Evil, Buddha, Wine, Soul……

They signified that individuals and organizations moved toward a single goal, setting aside their unique identities. It was the same for the flags of all organizations, not just the Majongi.

The flags were war banners, and the map was a wartime operations map.

It was as if Sima Ming were asking:

*Lord, which will you choose? Will you live freely, like those blue lines? Or will you live letting these red flags fly?*

“The Young Lord is desperately trying to stop the war, but the more he moves, the greater our chances of victory in war have become.”

It was a truly paradoxical result.

“The martial level of the Majongi has continued to rise, and the purge of corruption within the cult has continuously strengthened our main forces. Both you and the Young Lord have even achieved the Twelve Star Great Completion of the Guhwamagong. I can say with certainty that the main cult’s current strength is the greatest in its history.”

Unless something unexpected happened, the unification of Murim was an inevitable outcome. He need only make the decision to carve into history an unprecedented chapter.

Geom Ujin clasped his hands behind his back and silently stared at the wartime map.

Sima Ming could tell.

That as the Young Lord’s activities had raised the probability of victory, the Lord’s desire to go to war had diminished. And that his heart for his son had grown commensurately.

What was the probability now that the Lord would start a war?

Sima Ming could perform difficult calculations in any situation, but reading Geom Ujin’s heart was not easy.

Sima Ming manipulated the device again, and the walls returned to their original state.

Before them, the blue lines spread out once more in place of the red flags.

Gazing upon that blue journey, Geom Ujin asked.

“What is that boy doing right now?”

* * *

Splash!

Geom Mugik threw himself into the river.

He had been washing mud off in the flowing water at the riverbank when he simply dove right in.

Splish-splash.

At the sight of Geom Mugik swimming refreshingly through the water, Ian plunged in after him.

“I’m coming too!”

When Ian jumped in, Jin Haryeong jumped in as well.

“Me too!”

Seeing this, Jin Hagun shouted.

“You can’t!”

He shouted because wet clothes would cling to the body. The moon was bright, and others might see.

At that moment, Geom Mugik stopped swimming and stuck his head above the water.

“Ian can?”

Ian too stuck her head out of the water, playing along with Geom Mugik’s mischief.

“How mean, Young Alliance Leader.”

Jin Hagun was slightly flustered. He had blurted it out without thinking when his younger sibling suddenly jumped into the river.

“It’s not that….”

Geom Mugik looked at Bisain and spoke.

“Look at trusty Saine over there. He doesn’t say such things to Miss Han, does he?”

At that moment, Bisain spoke to Han Seol.

“You must not.”

He too was playing along with Geom Mugik’s prank.

Han Seol smiled faintly and said.

“I am the type to do it more when someone tries to stop me.”

Han Seol promptly jumped into the water.

Splash!

Han Seol, who had come from the Northern Sea, swam better than expected.

“I thought you wouldn’t be able to swim since you came from a cold place.”

Han Seol laughed at Geom Mugik’s prejudice.

“Have you forgotten who I am? Cold water is my element.”

Han Seol gestured for Bisain to come in. How could he stay out when she beckoned? Bisain too splashed into the water.

Jin Hagun was left alone outside. He had told his younger sibling not to go in, so it would have been awkward for him to enter; instead, he simply washed his clothes by the riverbank.

Then Geom Mugik struck the water’s surface with his palm.

*Splaaaash.*

He flicked the leaping droplets with his fingers.

*Pew!*

A single droplet flew toward Jin Hagun like a hidden weapon.

Jin Hagun slightly turned his body to avoid it.

“Stop it!”

*Pew! Pew!*

This time, Geom Mugik sent two droplets flying.

“Villain who only cares for his younger sibling!”

*Pew! Ping! Ping!*

After dodging several times, Jin Hagun finally jumped into the river.

“Why you… I said stop!”

“Finally, the true nature of evil awakens!”

Jin Hagun chased after Geom Mugik, who was swimming away.

“Why are you so fast? Have you mastered water arts?”

Jin Hagun chased after him with a happy smile. He had wanted to jump in and play too, and he was grateful to Geom Mugik for naturally drawing him in.

Geom Mugik ran across the water, and the real water play began.

“Ian, the Murim Alliance is attacking!”

“Haryeong, join forces and strike the Divine Cult!”

Jin Haryeong had never seen her older brother act so brightly and playfully. For the first time in a long while, she remembered how he had been as a child.

Geom Mugik and Jin Hagun employed every manner of movement art: Treading on Duckweed to Cross Water, Flying Over the Water’s Surface, Crossing a River on a Single Reed, then Stepping Through the Void and Soaring Across the Empty Sky. They played in the river, displaying all sorts of divine footwork.

“What, is this something to flee using the Heavenly Demon Flight Technique for?”

“That’s rich coming from someone chasing me so fiercely. Ian, request aid from the Sado Alliance.”

“Haryeong, we call upon the Ice Palace!”

At that moment, Geom Mugik suddenly stopped and spoke seriously.

“Take that back. Otherwise, those two will become star-crossed lovers.”

“Ah, I take it back.”

Geom Mugik smiled wickedly.

“So naive! Ian, the Murim Alliance has canceled the Ice Palace’s aid. Let’s continue our joint attack!”

There were no orthodox, no unorthodox, no demonic cultists there. There were only youths who simply loved their friends and loved to play.

* * *

That night, everyone sat around a bonfire.

They had played to their hearts’ content and enjoyed a late dinner with meat from their hunt.

After the meal, Jin Hagun announced a decision.

“I must return now.”

It was unexpected, and everyone’s gaze turned to him.

“It’s not that this journey hasn’t been enjoyable. Quite the opposite. I’m afraid if I continue, I won’t want to go back.”

In truth, there was a bigger reason.

It was because of those who had perished in the collapsed levee.

Perhaps because he had personally participated in the rescue and recovery, he kept thinking of the dead and the children.

Of course, it wasn’t that he would return to his main sect and start by rebuilding levees. He had realized there was simply too much he had to do.

Bisain looked at Han Seol. She read the meaning in his eyes and nodded. The two of them also had plans set in place.

“Then we shall take our leave as well. Since we’re out, we thought we’d visit the Northern Sea.”

He meant to escort Han Seol and pay his respects to the Palace Lord of the Northern Sea Ice Palace.

Geom Mugik asked subtly.

“Shall we follow?”

Bisain was about to agree, but Han Seol cut in firmly.

“We were allied with the Murim Alliance.”

She was refusing, drawing on the play from their water games.

Geom Mugik looked at Jin Hagun. Saine was already living under the thumb of that scary face! Jin Hagun nodded with an expression of agreement.

Geom Mugik had not ended his journey because he had considered that they might part ways midway like this.

“Ian, our journey continues.”

“Yes, Young Lord.”

An unconcealable joy flashed across Ian’s face.

Then she thought of one person.

“Friend, let’s go together.”

Though she knew Ian spoke sincerely, Jin Haryeong refused.

“I’m a busy person too.”

How could Jin Hagun not know his younger sibling’s heart?

“Why do you like this martial artist so much?”

He had never been the sort to ask such a question in his life, but he had asked it for his younger sibling’s sake. He had asked as if in jest, yet they were words he had struggled to speak. For the sake of a younger sibling who harbored feelings in her heart for someone unattainable from the very start. To cut it off cleanly today.

How could Geom Mugeuk not know? Why Jin Hagun was asking such a question.

Yes, now was the moment to make his own heart clear. For Jin Haryeong, and also for Jin Hagun.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Ian, who was sitting beside him.

Why did he like Ian?

Because all of this had been possible because Ian existed.

Because she had thrown herself to block Hwamugi’s sword qi, enabling him to survive.

But the reason he liked her was not merely because she had saved his life.

There were many other reasons, but he did not reveal them here. They were words he would tell Ian when they were alone someday.

“Isn’t she pretty?”

Even if Ian had possessed an ordinary face, or even if the side effects of the Whole-Body Petrification Technique had gone uncured, Geom Mugeuk would have chosen Ian.

At Geom Mugeuk’s answer, Jin Haryeong deliberately furrowed her brow.

“To judge a woman based on nothing but her appearance!”

“If it’s unfair, then you should have been born the greatest beauty under heaven instead of the greatest beauty of Hubei!”

“You awful man!”

“Don’t forget that we are the Demon Cult!”

Jin Haryeong was rather grateful that Geom Mugeuk had spoken this way. Because she could console herself that she had simply lost in looks. Because no one could rival that face.

“Brother, are you just going to watch while your sister is treated like this? You should tell him you’ll start the Great Righteous-Demonic War.”

Jin Hagun spoke cautiously.

“Honestly, this martial artist is quite beautiful, isn’t she?”

“You’re impossible! Truly!”

Jin Hagun, too, was grateful to Geom Mugeuk. Because only by cutting it off cleanly like this could his younger sibling begin a new love.

“So is this the last night?”

To Geom Mugeuk’s words, Bisain answered.

“We should part while we still regret it, so that our next meeting is all the more earnest.”

“You don’t look the least bit regretful right now!”

Hanseol, who was in the back, formed a faint smile.

Geom Mugeuk proposed one thing to the two of them.

“Let’s make a promise. Let’s set aside a date once every year and travel together, even if only for a few days. Even if we become the Cult Leader and the Alliance Leader.”

Jin Hagun and Bisain wondered if they could truly do so even after becoming Alliance Leaders, but they thought that with their hearts as they were now, it would certainly be possible.

“Let’s do that.”

“I promise.”

As the two made their promise, Geom Mugeuk raised his cup high.

“If you miss it, the penalty is one root of Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng. I’m not joking!”

Everyone toasted vigorously and drank.

“It’s always the one who makes rules like that who ends up missing them.”

“Who’s speaking such ominous words!”

They all had personalities as sharp as blades, but on this day of farewell, those blades did not obey.

Until morning broke, their farewell continued.

Chapter 877: The Young Cult Leader’s Direct Subordinate?
--------------------------------------------------------
When Taesu returned, his father's reaction was unexpected.

He must have known that Taesu had gotten involved in the Jangbodo affair, despite having told him not to.

So Taesu had expected a severe scolding.

"You're back?"

"Yes."

Scrape, scrape.

His father sat with his back turned, tending to his equipment.

Taesu had spent the entire journey home thinking that he should apologize to his father first.

But looking at that stubborn back, he grew angry again. Shouldn't his father ask if his son had been swept up in danger, or how he'd managed to escape? And all he said was that.

"Have you eaten?"

"Do you think a thief would starve himself?"

Originally, he'd intended to tell his father everything.

About meeting the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult while fleeing after obtaining the Jangbodo, and all those incredible things he'd experienced beneath the pond on Cheonmok Mountain.

He had wanted to tell him of this miraculous survival despite being entangled with the Demonic Cult.

Far from all that, not even a single "I'm sorry" came out.

As he tried to leave the room, his father suddenly spoke.

"There is a job. Something that will require time to prepare..."

Lately, he had always worked in a two-man team with his father.

"No. I have something I need to think about for a while."

Taesu did not ask what the target was or where.

"I see."

Even that answer made him angry.

"Make sure you eat properly."

He turned his back on his father and went outside.

He didn't know then. That he had inflicted a deep wound on his father. That while wounds parents leave on their children may scar, wounds children inflict on their parents leave not even scars behind—he hadn't known that then.

Afterward, Taesu wandered aimlessly for a time.

He thought about simply leaving to join the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Then, the words the Young Cult Leader had spoken came to him suddenly.

—When you are ready to take responsibility for your own life, come find me.

He realized he was not yet ready. If he couldn't even properly apologize to his father, how could he take responsibility for his own life?

His father was often away from home, and time continued to pass.

Taesu realized he was using his father as an excuse again.

It wasn't because of his father that he couldn't go to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

He couldn't go because he was afraid of the life that awaited him there.

The Young Cult Leader had said upon parting:

—Accepting you into the main cult is possible through my connection, but once you enter the cult, from that moment you must live a life unrelated to me. You may even find it difficult to see my face again. I will merely be the opportunity; it is you who must live the changed life. Do not forget, if you do not change even when your environment changes, nothing will be different.

As time passed, this part kept nagging at him.

You may even find it difficult to see my face again.

Could he really survive among those rough demonic folk without the Young Cult Leader by his side?

No, he wasn't confident. That was why he had made the excuse that he couldn't go because he hadn't reconciled with his father. He was merely postponing the reconciliation.

"I haven't changed at all."

When he had spoken those wounding words to his father, too, they had been an excuse.

—Is that principle of yours for the sake of good deeds? Or is it to justify your thievery?

He had no interest in his father's morality. He had simply been frustrated. When his father could become the greatest thief and steal everything, why did he only steal from villains, and why did he give away what he'd stolen with such effort?

He should have said, "I am tempted by greed," and received a lesson: "No, my son. At the very least, you must live like this." Instead, he had wounded his father.

"Damn it."

A messy stretch of time passed.

Yes, it was fine. He could speak words that wounded his father; he could step back from an opportunity to change his life. So what if he drank and wandered? He was still young.

But there was at least one thing he shouldn't have done. He shouldn't have left his father to face that place alone.

* * *

Im Ju, branch chief of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult's Sanyang branch, was silently staring at the Taesu before him.

"You're that man's son?"

"Yes, I am Taesu, son of the Great Thief Taegon."

Taesu's appearance was a mess. His hair was disheveled, and a thick beard covered his face. Only his two eyes shone like a wounded beast.

"What proof do you have that you're Taegon's son?"

At that, Taesu took a money pouch from his bosom and placed it down. When Im Ju saw it, his gaze sharpened. It was his own money pouch that had been in the branch vault.

He knew it was an act that would offend Im Ju, but there was no more certain way to prove he was Taegon's son.

"Why have you come to me?"

Im Ju asked, though he could guess the reason. As expected, the answer came.

"It's because of my father."

The region was in an uproar over Taegon right now.

The Great Thief Taegon had infiltrated the Blood Shadow Gate to steal and been captured. The martial world was abuzz that he, who had never once been caught, had failed at theft.

The Blood Shadow Gate was a sect notorious even within the Evil Path Alliance. They never suffered losses in their dealings, and they were ruthlessly cold to those weaker than themselves. That was how they had expanded their power so swiftly.

The Blood Shadow Gate announced they would publicly execute Taegon before a crowd.

"Was the public execution in two days?"

"Tomorrow."

"Why have you come to me?"

Taesu only learned that his father had been captured after the Blood Shadow Gate announced the public execution. Even then, he only learned of it days late because he'd been drowning in drink.

"It's because of me."

He believed his father had been captured because he'd been unable to concentrate due to Taesu.

He had tried every possible method to save his father.

He attempted to infiltrate the Blood Shadow Gate, but ultimately failed. Having announced the execution, they had set up an ironclad defense, expecting someone might come to rescue him.

Then he thought of asking the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult for help.

"I am close with the Young Cult Leader."

It was truly a life-or-death gamble. To dare speak of the Young Cult Leader? It was a situation where he could be cut down with a single slash.

Fortunately, Im Ju was not such an impetuous man.

"Close with the Young Cult Leader?"

"Yes."

His father always used to say:

—To become the greatest thief, you must know how to steal people's hearts as well.

To save his father, he had to steal the heart of the branch chief before him.

"I am scheduled to enter under the Young Cult Leader's command."

"Scheduled?"

Im Ju was bewildered as to how he should take this. The son of the Great Thief, who was to be executed tomorrow, had boldly come claiming to be close with the Young Cult Leader?

"He said he would make me his direct subordinate."

Perhaps the Young Cult Leader hadn't forgotten him after all.

Im Ju frowned at Taesu, who was staring back at him boldly.

What madman in the world would come to a branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and commit fraud while invoking the Young Cult Leader?

But this wasn't fraud. If it were fraud, he would have forged plausible proof of being a direct subordinate. "Scheduled to enter"? This was truly absurd. But it seemed clear that he did indeed know the Young Cult Leader.

"So? What do you want?"

"Please save my father."

Im Ju stared intently at Taesu.

"Why not ask the Young Cult Leader for help directly?"

"It happened too suddenly; there is no time to inform him. If you help, the Young Cult Leader will reward you greatly later."

The moment he finished speaking, Im Ju said coldly:

"You must be mad."

Taesu flinched.

"If I touch the Blood Shadow Gate based only on your word, and later the Young Cult Leader says he doesn't know you?"

"No. He truly knows me. If my words are false, I will accept death as punishment."

"Of course you would. The problem is that it won't end with just you dying."

Yet the reason he didn't immediately kick him out and was considering it was not because he feared repercussions if this brat truly was close to the Young Cult Leader. It was the opposite.

Because of one person in the rumors.

Gang Dal, the Jeongan Branch Chief.

The dream and hope of every branch chief in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

His success story of rising to the main cult through a connection with the Young Cult Leader was a legend among all the cult's branch chiefs.

"Has such fortune befallen me? Or is this the temptation of terrible misfortune?"

After careful deliberation, the conclusion he reached was refusal.

"I'm sorry, but I cannot help."

If it turned out difficult to believe Taesu later, he would at least keep his position, but if this person really had no connection to the Young Cult Leader, he would be relegated to some backwater stable keeper.

For the moment, the legend among branch chiefs still belonged solely to Gang Dal.

* * *

The next day, a large platform was erected in the open square before the main gate of the Blood Shadow Gate.

Countless people flocked there. To see Taegon, the legendary thief.

Taegon was dragged onto the stage with his vital points suppressed. His appearance was unspeakable, apparently from the hardships he'd suffered.

"Son of a thief!"

"Kill him!"

While some hurled curses, others looked at him with pity. For he was the one who had stolen from villains and helped the poor.

Taegon looked at the crowd surrounding him. There were people cursing, laughing, looking on with sad faces—all kinds of people. And the majority were looking at him with pity.

Then Taegon met someone's eyes, and his heart sank.

His son Taesu was standing conspicuously in the front row.

Taesu.

He was glad to see his son at the last moment, yet simultaneously felt uneasy.

He hasn't come to do something foolish, has he?

He was a smart boy; he wouldn't commit such folly. No matter how fast his movement arts were, he couldn't break through the countless Blood Shadow Gate warriors filling this place to save him.

Then a middle-aged man ascended the stage. He was the Blood Shadow Gate Lord, Geobaek, with a cold and heartless face. He was famous for his cruelly calm temperament.

Geobaek looked at the gathered crowd. No one met his eyes, and not a single breath could be heard around him.

Soon, he opened his mouth.

"Where are the luxuries you bought with the stolen money? You claim to only steal from villains? Then where are those who fed on that rotten meat? Where is the guard corps protecting the righteous thief?"

Silence hung over the place. Geobaek's gaze turned to Taegon.

"I heard you have a son. You've been thieving together?"

Geobaek's voice thundered out.

"Where is your son?"

Taesu merely stood silently in place. Taegon was looking elsewhere, afraid of being caught exchanging glances with his son.

"No doubt he is hiding somewhere here like a rat, watching his father die. If you were truly a courageous man, you would have come forth boldly and rescued your father. But you won't, will you? You're trash who only know how to steal."

Taesu did not come out despite his provocation. Taegon's expression brightened.

Well done, my son.

Please don't think of revenge; don't steal anymore either; live as you please.

As if to kill him personally, Geobaek drew his sword.

Taegon closed his eyes without looking at his son. If their eyes met for no reason, it would only hurt his son's heart.

At that very moment.

"Stop."

The owner of the voice echoing there was Taesu.

Taegon's face stiffened instantly.

No!

But Taesu was already walking forward and ascending the stage.

"I am that man's son."

Exclamations and sighs burst from all around. Some cheered and clapped, while lamenting sighs flowed from everywhere as well.

"Though he inherits the blood of a thief, his filial piety runs deep?"

Who knew how much he had agonized through the night. Should he detonate smoke bombs and escape in the confusion? Should he bribe one of their masters? Should he disguise himself as one of their subordinates?

But there was no method he could accomplish in just one day. The Blood Shadow Gate had too many experts.

This was the last method Taesu had thought of.

"I am a direct subordinate of the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult."

Before the branch chief he had merely been someone scheduled to become a direct subordinate; now he introduced himself as one outright.

Forgive me, Young Cult Leader. This is the last method I can use.

Geobaek's face twitched.

"You, a subordinate of the Demonic Cult's Young Cult Leader?"

"Is that so."

Geobaek had not even entertained the slightest possibility of that.

He laughed aloud. His subordinates on the stage laughed as well, and laughter erupted from all around.

Meanwhile, those who pitied him felt even greater pity. They understood his feelings. How could they not understand his desire to save his father, even if he had to resort to this?

In any case, one thing was the same. Whether laughing or pitying him, not a single person believed those words.

At Geobaek's signal, the experts on the stage rushed forward and forced Taesu to his knees.

"There is no greater punishment than watching your own child die first."

Taegon, whose vital points had been suppressed, thrashed about desperately. It was a plea to be allowed to speak. At Geobaek's signal, one martial artist released his vital points.

"Please, save my son. Please save him! I am the one who stole! If you spare my son, I will do anything you ask."

Geobaek, who had been watching him with scorn, made an unexpected proposal.

"If I spare your son, can you also steal from good people? Can you steal the entire fortunes of kind, good people?"

Taegon's eyes wavered. He could not say that he could.

"So you choose your pride over your son."

Just then, Taesu conveyed the words he had been unable to say to his father.

"Father, I am sorry. What I said to you then was not my true feelings. I love you because you are not someone who steals just anything."

Even if he died, he had to get these words out.

The distressed Taegon cried out.

"Why did you come? Why! What is so important about such words! What is so important about this old father of yours!"

Then Taesu saw. The tears falling from his father's eyes. They flowed ceaselessly. It was the first time he had seen his father cry.

At the sight of his father's tears, Taesu's vision blurred over.

Yes, he could have sent his father off alone.

But seeing those tears from his father, he thought that perhaps he shouldn't have stepped forward at this moment.

'But I could not send Father away without even trying.'

Geobaek grabbed Taesu by the collar and hauled him upright.

"A sniveling wretch like you, blubbering your eyes out, is a direct subordinate of the Demonic Cult's Lesser Cult Leader?"

Tears streamed from Taesu's eyes as well.

"Yes, I am… a direct subordinate of the Lesser Cult Leader… so release my father."

Some laughed, calling it pathetic, but most people watched the scene in silence.

They wanted to step out and protect him, but they could not. They had wished someone would step forward, yet no one could.

"If you are a subordinate of the Demonic Cult's Lesser Cult Leader, then I am the Demonic Cult Leader!"

Just then, a welcome voice called out from somewhere.

"Father!"

Suppressing the clamor and noise of the crowd gathered there, the words echoed forth. A single word filled with resonant internal energy pierced the ears of everyone present.

"Father!"

At the welcome cry heard again, the gazes of the assembled heroes turned toward the source.

The one who walked out, parting the crowd, was Geommugeuk.

Geommugeuk looked at Geobaek and spoke.

"Huh? You're not my father?"

Chapter 878: Today Was My First and Last Day as Your Subordinate
----------------------------------------------------------------
Blood Shadow Gate Lord Geobaek immediately realized that the young man who had appeared was no ordinary person.

He could tell simply from the internal energy carried in his voice.

In the silence that hung over the place, Taesu's trembling voice emerged.

“Young Cult Leader?”

Taesu was truly astonished. He had never dreamed he would see Geom Mugeuk here.

“Is it truly the Young Cult Leader?”

Everyone was shocked by the words “Young Cult Leader.” Earlier, he had said he was a direct subordinate of the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader, yet now he addressed him as Young Cult Leader? The crowd of heroes murmured with surprised faces.

Geom Mugeuk greeted Taesu warmly.

“Taesu, have you been well?”

At those words, Taesu’s heart swelled. Tears that had retreated from shock now threatened to pour forth once more.

“No, I have not been well. As you can see, I have not been well. I’m sorry, truly sorry.”

Even after hearing the words “Young Cult Leader,” Geobaek did not believe it.

“Who is this young hero?”

“How can a parent fail to recognize their own child?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s jest, Geobaek’s expression stiffened. It was the most unpleasant and unnerving joke in the world.

For some reason, Geom Mugeuk readily revealed his identity before all these people.

“I am Geom Mugeuk, the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

A silence born of shock and astonishment hung over the place.

Geobaek surveyed Geom Mugeuk’s surroundings. There was no one who appeared to be a guard. The people around were also looking at him with surprised faces.

Geobaek reached one conclusion.

“These swindlers have put their heads together.”

He believed it was a scheme cooked up by the son to save his father. They were impersonating the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader to escape this situation.

“Did you think if you put on airs as the Young Cult Leader here, I would gasp, say ‘Oh my, Young Cult Leader,’ and let them go?”

There was a decisive reason Geobaek did not believe his opponent was the Young Cult Leader.

“Would the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader wander around alone like this?”

He must have found some young expert from somewhere to impersonate the Young Cult Leader and rescue his father.

As the crowd of heroes murmured in agreement with Geobaek’s words, Geom Mugeuk raised a question.

“If I were planning a fraud of this level, wouldn’t I have brought subordinates as well?”

It made sense when one thought about it, but how could he believe the opponent was the Young Cult Leader?

“He must not have had time to find anyone daring enough to do such a thing.”

Many of the watching heroes felt frustrated. They truly wished the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader would come and save that father and son.

But even to them, it didn’t seem likely the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader would come to rescue a father-and-son pair of thieves. Nor would he travel alone like that. If the Young Cult Leader was making an appearance, wouldn’t he bring at least as many people as were gathered here?

Geom Mugeuk slowly moved his feet and ascended the platform.

The Blood Shadow Gate martial artists on the platform stepped forward, trying to draw their swords.

“If you draw those swords, there will be no turning back. Have your subordinates stand down.”

Geobaek misunderstood that kind warning in the opposite way.

‘He is definitely a fake.’

If he were the real Young Cult Leader, he would have displayed his strength.

That was why Geobaek had his subordinates step back.

“It is fine. Step back.”

Geom Mugeuk, having ascended the platform, walked toward Taesu and his father without paying them any mind. They were kneeling together.

Geom Mugeuk raised the two of them up.

“Has my direct subordinate here done any wrong to you?”

Geobaek was dumbfounded.

“You insist on playing the Young Cult Leader to the end?”

Fine, I’ll play along with that tune. But you won’t leave here alive today either.

This stage was his stage anyway. No matter how great an expert his opponent was, he couldn’t withstand their combined assault.

“You have the misfortune of having been born to the wrong parents.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to Taegon beside him. Taegon was looking at Geom Mugeuk with a surprised face. The moment he saw Geom Mugeuk’s clear and deep gaze up close—

‘He is no ordinary person.’

And he saw his son’s face looking at Geom Mugeuk from the side. He could tell from those eyes and expression full of respect and awe.

‘He really is the Young Cult Leader.’

His son was not someone who could make such an expression through acting.

‘Taesu truly became a direct subordinate of the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader?’

What on earth had happened? Even so, how had the Young Cult Leader come personally to rescue them?

Everything was confusing, but one thing was certain: there was goodwill in the Young Cult Leader’s gaze as he looked at him.

He thought it was because of Taesu, but Geom Mugeuk’s goodwill was actually because of him.

It has been a while, my friend.

In his previous life before regression, Geom Mugeuk had owed Taegon a debt. Back then, he had been running forward blindly, so he couldn’t fully repay that kindness. And fate had brought him before him once again.

“Have you been born to the wrong parents?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Taesu conveyed the feelings he hadn’t been able to express to his father.

“No. The greatest fortune in my life was meeting my father.”

At those words, emotion flashed across Taegon’s face. It was the first time he had heard such words from his son. They were words he would never forget for the rest of his life.

“Not meeting me?”

The nervous Taesu failed to realize Geom Mugeuk was joking and answered earnestly.

“No, Young Cult Leader, you are second.”

“I will let it slide because he is your father.”

Geom Mugeuk turned back toward Geobaek. Geobaek had been listening to their conversation with a scornful face.

“My subordinate does not seem to have been born to the wrong parents.”

“He was raised by parents who taught him thievery, so it doesn’t seem he was born to good ones either.”

“If I too have children, I will teach them to punish bad guys.”

In that instant, the corners of Geobaek’s eyes twitched. Before he could explode in anger, Geom Mugeuk rebuked him first.

“When you tried to kill my subordinate earlier, didn’t you say that? That watching your child die would be the most painful thing.”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head and spoke coldly.

“Even for the Demonic Path, this is excessive.”

Among those watching, everyone who had children empathized with those words.

Now Geobaek’s eyes were filled with killing intent. The more he looked, the more this brat dared to judge and scold him?

“Bring that bastard to his knees before me right now.”

The Blood Shadow Gate martial artists on the platform all drew their swords at once.

At that sight, the heroes felt regret. Even the youth who spoke rightly would die.

Then someone spoke again from among the heroes. This time, it was a woman’s voice, incomparably clear and beautiful.

“How dare you draw your swords on the Young Cult Leader!”

The moment the words ended, a woman from among the heroes soared through the air toward the platform.

“Protect the Gate Lord!”

The experts on the platform all drew their swords and rushed at the woman.

In a flash!

More than ten blade flashes gleamed.

The Blood Shadow Gate martial artists rushing from all directions stopped in their places, unmoving.

The watching heroes finally saw the woman standing among them, frozen like dolls.

Ian stood among them with her sword lowered.

She had unleashed sword qi to suppress their acupoints. Even the slightest misjudgment in force would cause flesh to be pierced by sword qi, so suppressing vital points with sword qi was a far more difficult realm than suppressing them with hands or finger wind.

Geobaek looked at Ian with a surprised face. The move she had just displayed was a realm that even he, the Gate Lord, could not imitate. If this was not a dream or an illusion—

‘…A Martial God!’

The watching heroes burst into cheers. They were amazed by her skill and admired her beauty.

Ian’s sword pointed at Geobaek’s throat. Geobaek had never offered his neck so helplessly before. She had suppressed his acupoints while subduing his subordinates.

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke with a smile.

“If I am not the Young Cult Leader, that would be an even bigger problem, wouldn’t it? According to your words, the experts of the Blood Shadow Gate were taken down by mere swindlers?”

Through this woman’s skill, Geobaek could understand.

“You truly are the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.”

She was not someone a mere thief could bring along to commit fraud.

“May I take my subordinate with me?”

Under normal circumstances, he would have had to let them go. But Geobaek could not back down like this in front of so many people.

“You may take the subordinate, but you cannot take his father. He is a man who entered to steal our gate’s treasure. I cannot simply release him.”

He had something he believed in. They couldn’t kill him recklessly because he belonged to the Four Paths Alliance. If that weren’t the case, they wouldn’t have merely suppressed his subordinates’ acupoints—they would have already killed them.

“Surely the Young Cult Leader of the Divine Cult isn’t trying to violate the laws of the martial world?”

“Do you wish to sort out right and wrong clearly?”

“Only then will all these people remember the Divine Cult’s Young Cult Leader as one who knows the ways of the martial world, will they not?”

Geobaek felt pleased inside. Today’s events would spread throughout the Central Plains, and there would be no one who didn’t know the story of the Blood Shadow Gate Lord who faced the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader with dignity.

“Don’t forget that our gate belongs to the Four Paths Alliance.”

“Ah, I was actually just about to contact the Four Paths Alliance.”

Geom Mugeuk gave Ian an order.

“Send word to the Four Paths Alliance right now.”

Just as Geobaek wondered what kind of word—

“Relay that the Blood Shadow Gate Lord has committed a grave sin against our cult and is being punished.”

Geobaek was startled.

“What nonsense is this? I committed a grave sin?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him with cold eyes.

“The sin of impersonating the Supreme Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult!”

“!”

Even more shocking words followed.

“I shall execute you for the crime of impersonating the Heavenly Demon!”

Geobaek was shocked out of his wits at the words saying he would be killed.

“Was that not said in jest?”

“A jest? You dare make the Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult into a subject of jest?”

Geobaek couldn’t argue back. If his opponent pushed like this, even the Four Paths Alliance wouldn’t be able to do anything. They might not care about other matters, but this involved the Heavenly Demon.

‘Damn it!’

Then Ian revealed her killing intent. It seemed she would kill him the moment the order fell.

Geobaek trembled in fear. He had never imagined he would die like this.

Then Geom Mugeuk’s voice transmission flew in.

—Do you want to live?

Geobaek didn’t dare act on his temper. He felt that if he pushed back here, they would really kill him.

Geobaek realized before he knew it that his acupoints had been released and he could use voice transmission. They were truly handling his body like a baby’s without the slightest hint of movement.

—I want to live.

—Sell the item that thief tried to steal this time and help people. There are many in this region who lost their homes to floods, so helping them should suffice.

From the moment he stepped forward, Geom Mugeuk had no intention of killing him. If Geobaek had committed excessively evil deeds, the Four Paths Alliance should punish him. That was courtesy toward Bisain.

Geom Mugeuk had Ian withdraw her sword.

“It was a joke. How could I kill someone of such renown in the Four Paths Alliance?”

Geobaek forced a smile as well.

“I was also joking. Since it is a request from none other than the Young Cult Leader, I should naturally comply. Please take them both.”

“Thank you.”

The two laughed together as if they had merely been exchanging jokes.

Geobaek spoke to the crowd of heroes.

“Since we have had such a precious meeting, I shall offer up the item the great thief tried to steal to help the victims who suffered flood damage this time.”

How could those watching not know? That Geobaek was performing a good deed because of the Young Cult Leader.

When one person clapped, everyone cheered as if taking it as a signal. It appeared they were cheering Geobaek’s act of charity, but most of the cheers were for the fact that he had submitted to the Young Cult Leader. It was a display showing just how excessive the Blood Shadow Gate’s usual evil deeds had been.

Geom Mugeuk bid him farewell.

“Well then, let us meet again.”

“Farewell.”

Without looking back, they knew what kind of gaze Geobaek was watching them with. Having survived this crisis, he would still commit evil deeds. He might even dream of revenge against Taegon and his son.

But he didn’t know. The flood that had befallen him hadn’t even begun.

This matter would naturally reach Bisain’s ears, and Bisain would not let this matter that he had involved himself in pass by. If investigation revealed that they had committed excessively evil deeds, Bisain would not let it go.

This, too, was the reason he had intervened by revealing his true identity rather than resorting to the sword.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and Ian came to a nearby forest with Taesu and Taegon.

“Thank you, truly thank you.”

The two expressed their gratitude to Geom Mugeuk several times.

After offering his thanks, Taegon swept his son into his arms. He held him like that for a long while without a word.

At last, Taegon spoke honestly to Geom Mugeuk.

“Everyone calls me a righteous thief, a great thief, but the truth is, I don’t deserve such words. I kept a portion of what I stole for my son. I stored it in the money houses of the Central Plains under his name.”

Taesu was startled. He had known it too—that his father did not give everything he stole from villains to help the poor. That was why he had said those hurtful words to his father.

But he had not known that his father had left that money for him.

“You need not make excuses to me. Each person has his own life.”

Taegon lowered his head.

Geom Mugeuk asked Taesu,

“Will you become my subordinate?”

As if his mind was already made up, Taesu shook his head.

“Today was the first and last time I served as your subordinate, Young Cult Leader.”

He meant that he would not throw himself into the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“I want to live with my father.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and wished him well in life.

“If you live remembering the moment you risked your life for your father, you’ll do well from now on.”

“Why are you being so good to me?”

No matter how he thought about it, he could not understand why the Young Cult Leader had come to save him.

Then Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“I didn’t save your father for your sake.”

“What?”

Geom Mugeuk had learned what was happening here because he had been keeping watch on Taegon through Tongcheongak.

Geom Mugeuk said something to Taegon that he could not understand.

“Long ago, I owed you a debt. With this, I will consider the debt I owed you fully repaid.”

Naturally, Taegon did not remember. When on earth could the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult have owed him a debt?

But Geom Mugeuk did not tell him the details.

“My direct subordinate, live well!”

Leaving those words behind, Geom Mugeuk and Ian launched themselves away and vanished from that place.

Taegon, too, had made up his mind.

“I intend to stop stealing from now on.”

Taesu looked at his father in surprise.

“What you said before was right. Leaving money for you was ultimately no different from leaving it for myself. I justified my thievery.”

That was why his son’s words must have hurt all the more.

“Father.”

“What is it?”

“Let’s steal properly from now on.”

“What?”

His father and he had spent their whole lives learning to steal. What happiness would there be in living some other life?

“Let’s become true great thieves now. Great thieves who don’t regret it and aren’t ashamed. I think what happened today was heaven’s will, telling us to live that way.”

Taegon did not agonize for long. Yes, this was the path they should have walked from the beginning.

Taegon patted his son on the shoulder and nodded.

The two of them smiled together as they looked toward the place where Geom Mugeuk and Ian had disappeared. They were already gone, but Taesu shouted belatedly. He had forgotten one person—the friend with such a good nose.

“Please be sure to tell Jihan this!”

That the next time he met him, he would smell good.

Chapter 879: Don't Go Elsewhere, Come to Us...
----------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk and Ian stopped walking.

Along the path they were walking, a drunkard sat collapsed on the steps in front of a building, singing. Ian thought to herself.

'What kind of story does he have, to sing so sorrowfully?'

Just then, Geom Mugeuk, who had been listening to the song as well, spoke.

“He sings terribly.”

At those words, Ian burst out laughing. Really, how could he judge the man’s singing skill at a time like this?

Courtesans came out and led the drunkard inside.

“You said you were going out for some fresh air. What are you doing here?”
