Then, from the opposite side, someone was shouting at the top of his lungs.

“Do you know who I am! Tell the master here to come out! Hurry!”

Watching the scene, Ian spoke.

“Does he even know who the master here is, acting like that?”

The place where Geom Mugeuk and Ian stood was none other than inside Cheonhwaru.

They had been traveling through the Central Plains and had come to meet the master of Cheonhwaru.

The drunkard who had been shouting picked a fight with the two of them as well.

“Hey! Who are you? What are you glaring at? Who are you?”

And does this man know who this person is either, acting like this?

“No, we’re leaving, we’re going. Sorry for staring!”

Ian pulled Geom Mugeuk along and moved on.

“Let’s go. Let’s not insert ourselves into that miserable life too.”

Behind them, the drunkard shouted loudly again, and courtesans came out to soothe him and lead him inside. Unless it was an excessive commotion, martial artists were not mobilized.

“Ugh, no matter how much money I made, I couldn’t run this business. How could I watch that every day?”

“If you actually did it, you’d probably do it the best.”

If it had been Geom Mugeuk, he probably would have sat down with that singing drunkard and sung along.

Geom Mugeuk and Ian entered the inner courtyard where the master of Cheonhwaru resided. Martial artists hidden throughout the inner courtyard could be sensed.

There had been those guarding this place when they visited before, but the ones hidden now were even greater experts.

Cheonhwaru too had overcome many crises and gradually built up its foundation.

When they opened the final gate of the inner courtyard and entered, two unexpected people greeted Geom Mugeuk and Ian.

It was two Faceless Guests who welcomed them.

Geom Mugeuk could tell who they were even without them removing their masks.

They were none other than the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest and Sipchilgyo. Seeing that Sipchilgyo was also wearing a Faceless Guest mask, it seemed she had become one as well. Beside the pure white mask she wore, “十七” was written.

“Welcome, Young Leader.”

The two greeted him with polite respect. Their gazes toward the Young Leader were exceedingly favorable. After all, Geom Mugeuk was the decisive person who had connected their love.

Especially when the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest had come to Geom Mugeuk to pour out her worries, his words had been of great help. She had been troubled over whether it was right to force Sipchilgyo to become a Faceless Guest with her.

—Try living as a Faceless Guest without regrets once, and then run away together.

After that, Sipchilgyo’s life as a Faceless Guest began. Contrary to what the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest had worried about, Sipchilgyo was enjoying it. Unexpectedly, the life behind a mask suited her well.

“What are you two doing here? Were you perhaps dispatched?”

He had thought Geukak Soma had sent them to protect the master of Cheonhwaru.

Then, from the building behind them, an unexpected person walked out.

“I was the one dispatched.”

Surprisingly, it was none other than Geukak Soma.

“Why, when did you arrive, Lord Soma?”

Behind his mask, Geukak Soma’s eyes smiled brightly.

“It’s been a while.”

They could tell that the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest and Sipchilgyo had followed to attend upon Geukak Soma.

“It seemed the Young Leader cherishes these children quite specially. I brought them out so they could get some fresh air.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the two and nodded.

“They’re a fine-looking young couple, aren’t they?”

Just then, someone else walked out from behind and spoke.

“As fine-looking as the Young Leader and this warrior?”

The woman who followed out was none other than the master of Cheonhwaru. Just as the two Faceless Guests were in their youth, Geom Mugeuk and Ian were also truly in their most beautiful years, from the master of Cheonhwaru’s perspective.

“Welcome, Young Leader.”

The master of Cheonhwaru, whom she was seeing after a long time, was dressed in splendid makeup and well-arranged clothes, but more than anything, her expression was bright.

“You’ve become more beautiful and brighter, Master.”

The master of Cheonhwaru glanced at Geukak Soma as she answered.

“How could I not improve?”

How could she not know that this meant she was happy because Geukak Soma had come?

Ian greeted Geukak Soma and the master of Cheonhwaru.

“It’s been a long time since I’ve seen you both.”

Geukak Soma nodded and accepted the greeting, while the master of Cheonhwaru actively fussed over Ian. The master of Cheonhwaru took Ian’s hand, delighted.

“This warrior, I’ve missed you. You’ve grown even more beautiful in the time we haven’t seen each other.”

Her outward appearance hadn’t actually changed. But even as she traveled, Ian’s martial arts prowess had risen, changing her aura and demeanor.

“Thank you, Master. I wanted to see you too.”

Geom Mugeuk stepped forward as if to say it wasn’t only to that degree.

“You have no idea how much she pestered me to come see you.”

Because it was true, Ian didn’t deny it.

The master of Cheonhwaru always said kind words to her. And because Ian knew it wasn’t simply because of Geom Mugeuk, the master of Cheonhwaru was a special person to her as well.

When the two first met, Ian had been unable to cure the side effects of the Petrification Art. Even so, the master of Cheonhwaru had recognized Ian’s true worth.

The master of Cheonhwaru was also the one who told her to value herself as someone who would accomplish great things.

It wasn’t necessarily because of that that she wanted to see her, but strangely, during this trip, she had felt she simply had to see her. This was the first time she had felt such an emotion.

The master of Cheonhwaru watched Ian with a gaze that seemed to already know her heart, and then—

“This warrior, shall we leave the men to play and go for a walk ourselves?”

“Yes, that sounds good.”

Geom Mugeuk shouted to the master of Cheonhwaru, who was walking away.

“Leave the men to play! That’s the thing we men most want to hear! Please say it often!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s shout from behind, the master of Cheonhwaru smiled.

“The Young Leader remains the same, doesn’t he?”

To Ian’s words, the master of Cheonhwaru gave an unexpected answer.

“He looks quite different to me.”

“The Young Leader?”

The master of Cheonhwaru nodded. But she didn’t explain what looked different. Instead, she asked about the trip.

“Is the trip enjoyable?”

Ian smiled and nodded. Who could fail to enjoy a trip spent with him?

Perhaps she had wanted to meet her because she knew that more than anyone, the master of Cheonhwaru would understand her heart. No, in truth, she had wanted to ask her.

‘What should I do?’

But those words didn’t leave her mouth.

“Strangely, from a few days ago, I started wanting to see you, Master. So I pestered the Young Leader to change our travel schedule to come here.”

Ian looked at the master of Cheonhwaru and added.

“I suppose I wanted to eat a home-cooked meal made by you.”

Ian had tasted the master of Cheonhwaru’s cooking skills back when she had been confined in the An household.

Then the master of Cheonhwaru rolled up her sleeves.

“I shall display my skills once more.”

The two women smiled at each other.

The master of Cheonhwaru’s gaze, which had been looking at Ian, turned toward the stars in the night sky. A dizzy spell swept through her eyes.

“This warrior is gradually drawing closer to her destiny.”

Ian flinched for a moment. From the first time they met, the master of Cheonhwaru had spoken as though she could see Ian’s destiny.

“I don’t really know what my destiny is.”

The master of Cheonhwaru smiled and asked.

“No, this warrior must be feeling it.”

Ian couldn’t say otherwise. When they had decided to travel, Geom Mugeuk had said,

—To find the real Ian.

Ian knew. Those words foretold a change in her relationship with Geom Mugeuk. Whatever it was, it would be related to her destiny.

The master of Cheonhwaru gave her courage.

“Believe in yourself. This warrior will play a greater role than anyone in the world.”

Ian smiled. It would be a lie to say such words weren’t burdensome, but—

“You have no idea how much strength these words have given me.”

“I am simply telling you what I see and feel.”

What exactly had she seen in her?

Ian raised her head. Far away, atop the roof of Cheonhwaru’s tallest building, Geom Mugeuk and Geukak Soma stood.

The two stood side by side, gazing at the night scenery spread out in the distance.

“Is your journey enjoyable?”

“Yes, it’s fun. I met past connections, and confirmed that more than half of the famous restaurants in the Central Plains food tour are overrated.”

Geukak Soma smiled at Geom Mugeuk’s joke. Then Geom Mugeuk added one more thing.

“Actually, I came on this trip to find an answer.”

Geukak Soma could guess what the problem was. Since it was a trip taken alone with Ian, it must be a matter concerning her.

“And did you find the answer?”

Geom Mugeuk slowly nodded.

“Yes.”

In truth, when Geom Mugeuk first regressed, he had tried to let Ian go. He had actually told her to leave. He had tried his hardest not to give her his heart.

He had intended to live this life alone as well. He had thought that loving someone while carrying that dark and difficult past life was not a courtesy to the other person.

But as he lived his life after the regression, his heart had gradually changed. His relationship with his father, his relationship with the demonic followers, his relationships with friends. As precious relationships increased, it seemed greed had sprouted within him.

The decisive reason was this.

Perhaps I could become a good father like my father too?

Though Geom Mugeuk said he had found the answer, Geukak Soma saw some hesitation in him.

“What troubles you?”

An unexpected answer flowed from Geom Mugeuk.

“Am I allowed to dream of happiness?”

It was not an answer fitting for someone who was allowed to dream of conquering the world.

Geukak Soma knew where this question stemmed from.

He could tell it was a question born from the abyss of unfathomable depth—the darkness he had often sensed in Geom Mugeuk, which felt so ill-fitting.

“What makes you uneasy?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk answered.

“I fear that by harboring excessive greed, a great misfortune like the Heavenly Slaughter Star will come again. The Heavenly Slaughter Star has already come, so an even greater misfortune may arrive.”

Geukak Soma did not ask why that was excessive greed. Instead, he said this.

“Tell it to come.”

“!”

“Are we not the people best suited to face that misfortune and darkness? Don’t let it go elsewhere—tell it to come to us.”

Geukak Soma had not said “tell it to come to the Young Leader,” but “tell it to come to us.”

Understanding that this meant they would stand together no matter what darkness came, a smile formed on Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“Therefore, the Young Leader becoming happy may once again become an act that saves the martial arts world.”

Could there be anyone who could say “you may be happy” in such a magnificent way?

Perhaps that was why he could speak words buried deep in his heart so comfortably.

“Then shall I trust you and try to be happy?”

***

“It’s here, the place the Master told us about.”

The master of Cheonhwaru had recommended that there was a beautiful place nearby, so the two of them should go for a walk.

It was truly a beautiful place.

Willows that seemed hundreds of years old let down their hair toward the lake, and moonlight streamed through the leaves.

There, the stars of the heavens seemed to have shattered and scattered, twinkling. It was because hundreds and thousands of fireflies flitted about the lakeshore where beautiful flowers bloomed.

Standing in that place filled with the scent of grass, water, and ancient trees, Geom Mugeuk and Ian were enraptured by the beautiful sight.

“It’s so beautiful.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded at Ian’s exclamation. A sight this beautiful was new to him as well.

When Ian reached out her hand, a firefly landed on her fingertip. Light appeared and disappeared as if it were breathing.

Ian, who had been quietly watching, suddenly asked.

“Young Leader, is traveling with me fun?”

“It is.”

“What about it?”

“Why? Do you think I’m traveling with you even though it isn’t fun?”

“Because you’re someone who always keeps your promises.”

When Ian gently raised her hand, the firefly on her fingertip flew away.

“If it’s because of me, you can go back now.”

“I don’t want to.”

“What?”

“I truly enjoy this journey. Even if you don’t, I’ll keep going because I enjoy it. If you feel it’s unfair, then you should have been born the Young Cult Master.”

Ian was truly grateful that Geom Mugeuk said this.

“I don’t feel the least bit wronged that I wasn’t born the Young Cult Master, but I do feel a little wronged that you were born as the Young Cult Master.”

Ian finally felt that it was time to ask this question.

“Why are you so good to me? This is the last time I’ll ask.”

Long ago, they had jokingly promised that she would ask this question only five times, and in the meantime, she had already asked four. In truth, it was not as though he had been good to her only five times, but now she was asking the fifth and final question she had saved and saved.

“I won’t ask again.”

Geom Mugeuk had once answered playfully that it was so he could work her for the rest of her life. Another time, as he was returning after fighting with his older brother, he had answered that she had suddenly come to mind.

And now he felt that the time had come to answer her properly.

“There are many reasons. Can you remember them all?”

Ian nodded.

“Even if I forget the oral formulae of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, I won’t forget what you’re about to say.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“The first reason is because you’re so good at joking like that.”

“It wasn’t a joke.”

“Because you’re someone who nods beside me no matter what I say and takes my side.”

For some reason, Ian refuted even that reason.

“If the other person is the Young Cult Master of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, even someone with a broken neck would nod and take his side.”

Geom Mugeuk gave another reason.

“You also have the patience to never give up once you set your mind to something.”

She had shown that in the process of learning the Flying Heaven Swordsmanship. Whenever he went to see her, she had been training in martial arts.

“Who would give up after obtaining the Flying Heaven Sword Art? Even the laziest person in the world would have lost sleep over it.”

Despite her continued rebuttals, Geom Mugeuk kept giving his reasons.

“More than anything, I like your goodness.”

“You’ve been thoroughly fooled.”

Ian told him something she had never once spoken of before.

“It’s because of the side effects of the Full-Body Petrification Art. To survive, I had to become good. That way, the Young Cult Master wouldn’t abandon me.

I wasn’t even ten years old back then. I’m ashamed to say it, but from that time on, goodness became one of my ways of surviving.”

Ian’s eyes trembled.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. She was trying to push him away, but this was not truly pushing him away.

She seemed to be putting up an ironclad defense, but in truth, she was doing everything she could to break down the iron wall standing between them.

If he gave a hundred reasons, she would offer a hundred rebuttals.

This was her effort to break down that wall.

“There aren’t any more, are there? Now that you actually have to give reasons, there aren’t that many, right?”

It was just as Ian looked back at the fireflies. Over the years, Geom Mugeuk had surprised her countless times, but never as much as in this moment.

The words that flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth were a single phrase.

“Miss Yi.”

Chapter 880: If the Heavenly Killing Star Had Invaded Today
-----------------------------------------------------------
Miss I.

Had she heard wrong? No. She had not heard wrong.

How could she not know what those words meant?

Ian understood instinctively. The person standing before her was no longer the Young Cult Leader, but a man standing before her as a man.

It was as if fireflies were all at once giving off light and sparkling around her. Ian’s heart pounded as though it would burst. Never in her life had she been this startled and this nervous.

But she could not simply be happy. Her subconscious dragged up a negative possibility. All the fireflies that had been lighting up her heart went out.

“Ian! Get a hold of yourself! It might be your misunderstanding.”

It could be one of the Young Cult Leader’s jokes, like always, couldn’t it?

But Geom Mugeuk’s gaze as he looked at her remained serious. If it had been a joke, he would already have made her laugh with some silly remark.

“Surely not?”

Ian’s surprise turned into unease. This unfamiliar side of him...

“Are you perhaps trying to send me away?”

After all, the Young Cult Leader had tried to send her away before. Had this final journey been for the sake of parting?

“That is so.”

Ian was shocked. Even if her father’s fist had struck her on the head, she would not have felt this dazed.

He had clearly been looking at her as a man, and yet he was trying to send her away?

Was he angry? She had spoken of her own strengths, yet refuted him at every turn; it would be understandable if he were angry.

But he was certainly not someone who would send her away for such a reason.

Though she was confused, there was one thing she had to make certain.

“I absolutely cannot leave the Young Cult Leader.”

Forcing herself to smile, she spoke as if joking.

“What are you saying? I told you, didn’t I? I’m going to stick right by Young Master’s side and live comfortably for the rest of my life.”

Ian did not think of pride or anything of the sort.

“I’ll cling to your trouser leg. You know, don’t you? When I say I’ll do something, I do it.”

But Geom Mugeuk was firm.

“You must leave.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s resolute attitude, she despaired.

“You said I was your heart, didn’t you? Can you live without a heart?”

Words that stabbed her heart like a sword flew toward her.

“I will replace it with a new heart.”

“!”

Even at this moment, he spoke to her respectfully, making Geom Mugeuk feel like a stranger. To make her leave so suddenly, and in such a beautiful place.

“Tell me the reason. If I can’t accept it, I won’t leave. You said I had patience that never gives up? No, I trained until that late at night so I could fight back at times like this.”

Geom Mugeuk revealed the reason.

“Because only then can we move on to what comes next.”

Ian could not understand what he meant, so she waited for his next words. But the answer that came was still incomprehensible.

“I did not mean I would send Miss I away.”

“What does that mean? Until just now, you were trying to send me away.”

“What I mean to send away is Ian, who calls me Young Master.”

Ian’s eyes widened.

“And Ian, who feels afraid of me.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk calmly added,

“I am sending away Ian, who thinks she and I can never be. Miss I, after those Ians have gone, will become my new heart.”

Only then could Ian know for certain. Just as she had first felt, Geom Mugeuk was standing before her as a man.

“It does not matter if you cannot make jokes, and it does not matter if you only forced yourself to sympathize with me because I was the Young Cult Leader. It does not matter if you lack willpower, or if your kindness was a survival strategy. I...”

Geom Mugeuk, who was usually as fluent as flowing water, was clearly nervous at this moment.

“Wait! Don’t! Don’t say anything!”

At her cry, Geom Mugeuk stopped speaking for a moment.

“You must hear it.”

“I don’t want to. No, I’ll hear it, so just a moment!”

Ian covered her ears and closed her eyes like a child.

She knew what he was about to say.

The moment those words were spoken, they would not be able to go back to how they were before.

She liked Geom Mugeuk.

From the day she had first stood before the Young Cult Leader as a little girl until now.

Geom Mugeuk had called her his heart and cherished her more than anyone. Nowhere, before no one, had he ever hesitated to call her his heart. Not once had he compared her to anyone else, nor had he been ashamed of her.

But both she and Geom Mugeuk had kept the line.

Now Geom Mugeuk was trying to cross that line.

Geom Mugeuk took Ian’s wrists and pulled away the hands covering her ears. Ian slowly opened her eyes. Geom Mugeuk gazed at her and said,

“I like Miss I, who stands before me now, as a woman.”

Ian’s body began to tremble. Joy, tension, fear, excitement, happiness... truly, all sorts of emotions mixed together and wrapped around her.

If she accepted his heart, she would become the wife of the Heavenly Demon.

She would become the Demon Empress.

Ian stood at the most important crossroads of her life. Her answer would change the life ahead of her. Hers, and Geom Mugeuk’s as well.

She was confident she could overcome any hardship that came from this choice. Because she sincerely liked Geom Mugeuk.

The only thing that made her hesitate was this.

“Will you not regret choosing me?”

Geom Mugeuk gazed at her and said,

“I am not as perfect a person as Miss I thinks. I am darker than I appear.

Though I seem to get along well with people, I like being alone, and I am the sort of person who buys worries I do not need to have. Because I have stained these hands with so much blood, misfortune may cling to me for the rest of my life.”

Geom Mugeuk returned the question she had asked him.

“Would you be all right with someone like me?”

The two of them looked at each other in silence. A single firefly passed between them.

At last, Ian conveyed her heart.

“I was entrusted to the Divine Cult when I was young, and I survived until now to face this moment. No misfortune will ever defeat my luck.”

Because those words meant she would accept him, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and said,

“Until now, you have protected me, so for the rest of our lives, I will protect you.”

Ian was the first to take one more step toward Geom Mugeuk. Gazing quietly at his face, she spoke in a low but forceful voice.

“No. My dark, worry-filled man is for me to protect.”

Her face drew closer to Geom Mugeuk’s. Her soft lips touched Geom Mugeuk’s lips.

Her heart pounded as though it would burst, and her face flushed bright red. She did not know where such courage had come from.

Yes, though she had always stood behind him until now, perhaps it was her will to step proudly forward from now on.

As she tried to retreat in embarrassment, Geom Mugeuk pulled her to him.

This time, Geom Mugeuk kissed her. It was a very sweet and deep kiss.

Around the two of them, the fireflies shone all at once.

The fireflies of that night did not go out for a long, long time.

***

The next morning.

The Cheonhwaru Master went to the kitchen early.

In the place where the Cheonhwaru cooks, who had been cooking until late at night, had left, she rolled up her sleeves.

She intended to show her skills after a long while for Ian, who had said she wanted to eat home-cooked food.

Just as she was setting a pot on the stove to boil water, Ian entered.

“Did you sleep well?”

“Why are you up so early? It seemed you came back late yesterday. Did you sleep well?”

“Yes, I slept well.”

But Ian had not slept a wink last night. Yesterday, she had experienced clearly that just as one could not sleep from being too troubled or worried, one also could not sleep from being too happy.

“How was that place?”

“It was wonderful.”

To Ian, that place had become somewhere she would never forget. How could she forget that place, and what had happened there? Ian was truly grateful to the Cheonhwaru Master for telling her about that beautiful location.

The Cheonhwaru Master could tell. Ian of yesterday and Ian of today had changed. She knew not because she was the Cheonhwaru Master who received prophecies, but because she was a woman.

“I’ll help too.”

“Would you?”

Ian brought the ingredients the Cheonhwaru Master told her to fetch from the storage room, then washed them in clean water.

As the two of them prepared the food, Geom Mugeuk suddenly poked his head in.

“I smell something delicious.”

The Cheonhwaru Master opened the pot lid and said to Geom Mugeuk,

“We haven’t boiled anything yet.”

“Do you think you can only recognize a master when they draw a sword? You can tell just by how they stand.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the ingredients sliced to one side and exclaimed in admiration.

“As expected! Our Ian truly works well!”

At that, the Cheonhwaru Master said,

“I did that.”

Geom Mugeuk sat down beside Ian, who was trimming vegetables, with an awkward smile.

“I’ll help too.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

He sat as he usually did, but it was not like usual.

Geom Mugeuk kept glancing at Ian. Ian sensed his gaze, but deliberately pretended not to notice.

“I have something to bring for a moment.”

When the Cheonhwaru Master briefly left her seat, Ian whispered to Geom Mugeuk,

“Why do you keep looking at me? No, why did you come here?”

“Why else? Because I wanted to help.”

“I don’t need it. Go outside and play with Lord Soma. And speak comfortably to me.”

“I refuse.”

Normally, in a secret romance, people spoke respectfully in front of others and casually when alone, but the two of them were the opposite.

“Ian, who was afraid of you, has already gone far away. Look, I’m calling you ‘you’ now, aren’t I? So treat me comfortably.”

“Then when we are alone, Miss I should treat me comfortably too.”

In truth, Ian had worried about this morning. When morning came, what if things became awkward? What if last night became as if it had never happened? She had worried about such things.

But it had been needless worry. That tension was continuing into the morning. No, rather, she liked this secret, hidden feeling.

“Do you really mean it?”

“I do.”

Ian almost spoke informally right away, but she could not bring herself to do it.

“This is the one thing I can’t do.”

“Then I cannot either.”

“Please do.”

Meaning that he should not be stubborn and should just do it, Ian lightly tugged at Geom Mugeuk’s sleeve. Then Geom Mugeuk was pulled toward her with a jerk like a doll.

“Did you want to embrace me that badly?”

“I only tugged lightly!”

Though Ian had not put any strength into it, Geom Mugeuk’s body jerked this way and that.

“Stop playing around. Even if the Heavenly Killing Star pulled you, you wouldn’t budge.”

The two of them laughed as they joked.

Just then, the Cheonhwaru Master returned carrying a small jar.

Before they knew it, Geom Mugeuk was washing vegetables in his original place as if nothing had happened. It was truly a lightning-fast movement.

‘He’s fast.’

Ian inwardly marveled that a person could move that quickly.

The Cheonhwaru Master looked at the two of them.

“Did you two perhaps fight?”

“If one dared to fight me, they would go straight to our cult’s underground prison. But why do you ask?”

“For some reason, there seems to be an awkward air about you.”

At that moment, the presence of their savior was heard outside.

“It seems Lord Soma has come out. I will go out and wait.”

Geom Mugeuk hurried outside.

The Cheonhwaru Master smiled and said casually,

“For some reason, you seem rather flustered today. Am I right?”

At the Cheonhwaru Master’s question, Ian did not answer. She was staring at the door through which Geom Mugeuk had disappeared, lost in thought. The Cheonhwaru Master looked at her and smiled.

“This side as well.”

Only then did Ian turn back to the Cheonhwaru Master and say,

“Yes? What did you say?”

“I said it’s a shame. If that Heavenly Killing Star had attacked today, we would have won.”

Sensing what she meant, Ian’s cheeks reddened slightly.

The Cheonhwaru Master did not embarrass her any further and began cooking.

“Now, bring the trimmed vegetables here.”

“Yes.”

She properly learned cooking from the Cheonhwaru Master.

Ian could originally make noodles and a few dishes fairly well, but she was no match for the Cheonhwaru Master.

She learned from the very basics. Though she had held a blade for so long, she even learned anew how to hold a kitchen knife. How to prepare ingredients,

when and in what order to add them, how to control the fire, and even how to adjust the final seasoning.

The Cheonhwaru Master’s cooking skills were excellent, and Ian learned earnestly.

A little while later, the food was laid out, and the four of them sat around it together.

Because Ian was present, the masked Extreme Evil Soma naturally only joined them at the table. Even if he did not eat, there was meaning in sitting together.

“Truly delicious!”

Geom Mugeuk exclaimed in admiration at the Master of Cheonhwaru’s cooking.

“You need to put out a revised edition of Culinary Tour of the Central Plains. You have to put the Master of Cheonhwaru on the very first page.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Master of Cheonhwaru laughed.

“When you include me, don’t forget this warrior as my assistant!”

“I only did as I was told. Would it taste like this if I made it on my own?”

“You have good hands, so you’ll learn quickly.”

“I’ll have to ask you sometime and properly learn from you, Pavilion Master.”

Just then, a breeze blowing in through the window stirred Ian’s hair. Geom Mugeuk, sitting beside her, absentmindedly raised his hand as if to smooth it back, then caught himself and let that hand move toward his chopsticks instead.

Geugak Soma looked at the Master of Cheonhwaru. The Master of Cheonhwaru gave a slight nod.

Behind his mask, Geugak Soma’s eyes smiled brightly.

“Allow me to pour you a drink.”

Geugak Soma first poured wine for Geom Mugeuk, then filled Ian’s cup next.

“You too, warrior. Please accept a cup.”

The look in Geugak Soma’s eyes and his manner toward Ian had changed. They had become more courteous, more gentle.

From that, Ian could tell. Geugak Soma had realized the nature of her relationship with Geom Mugeuk.

Ian spoke politely. She knew better than anyone the relationship between Geom Mugeuk and Soma.

“I look forward to your guidance from here on.”

“If you are the warrior I have seen until now, I believe you will become a splendid Demon Empress.”

“That is still more than I dare presume to bear.”

Ian bowed her head modestly. The weight carried by the words Demon Empress was the heaviest single phrase in her life.

For Geugak Soma to show her this much courtesy was also courtesy toward Geom Mugeuk.

After pouring wine for Geugak Soma and the Master of Cheonhwaru, Geom Mugeuk made the official announcement.

“Starting today, it is our first day together.”

This time, Ian lowered her head again out of embarrassment.

“Congratulations.”

“Thank you.”

They all celebrated and raised their cups in a toast.

The Master of Cheonhwaru said in good spirits,

“It’s an honor. To be able to share in this historic first day.”

One could feel that this moment was not merely changing the fate of the two of them. It was a moment that would have a great influence on the fate of the martial world as well.

Outside the building, standing guard, the Crescent-Moon Faceless Guest and Sipchilgyo looked at each other and exchanged moved glances.

To think they were present at this moment in history! At the very place where the Young Cult Leader, who had brought their own love together, was entering into love himself.

Geom Mugeuk asked the two of them,

“How in the world did you know? Was it obvious?”

When he asked in earnest, Geugak Soma and the Master of Cheonhwaru laughed together.

“It was beyond obvious. You had practically pasted it onto your face.”

The Master of Cheonhwaru added with a smile,

“It isn’t something you can hide just because you want to.”

Geom Mugeuk confessed honestly.

“To tell you the truth, I wanted to show it off.”

Ian said to Geom Mugeuk,

“Even though you possess such incredible martial arts, you never once showed them off.”

The four of them laughed together.

After breakfast, Geom Mugeuk and Ian left Cheonhwaru. Geugak Soma and the Master of Cheonhwaru did not hold them back and ask them to stay longer, nor did they ask where they were going.

“We’ll come visit again next time.”

“Come whenever you like. The doors of Cheonhwaru are always open to the two of you.”

He bid farewell to Geugak Soma by sound transmission.

—I’ll trust in you, Lord Soma, and become happy.

Geugak Soma was deeply grateful that Geom Mugeuk had accepted his advice.

—Trust only in me and be happy to your heart’s content.

The two looked at each other and smiled brightly.

Geom Mugeuk and Ian left Cheonhwaru.

Geugak Soma and the Master of Cheonhwaru stood side by side, watching them leave.

The Crescent-Moon Faceless Guest and Sipchilgyo watched while crouching side by side on the branch of a great tree that stretched over the wall of Cheonhwaru.

Once they had moved away from Cheonhwaru, Geom Mugeuk held out his hand to Ian.

A little shyly, Ian took that hand with a radiant smile.

Hand in hand, the two vanished as if melting into the dazzling sunlight.

Chapter 881: Living Well Is Your Revenge
----------------------------------------
"Is there any errand you need done?"

At the child's question, the middle-aged man who had been hammering in the smithy replied curtly.

"None."

The man, stripped to the waist, was thin and small of stature, but each downward strike of his hammer was filled with proper strength.

"Is there anything I can help with?"

"None."

It had been a few days since the child started coming to this small smithy on the outskirts of the village.

The child was a youngster who didn't look to be ten years old yet.

"Then I'll clean this place."

The one who truly needed washing was the child. Whether he slept outside or not, his clothes grew dirtier by the day.

The child began cleaning inside the smithy without being told.

He stood the hammers, anvils, tongs, and such upright, then cleared away the ash fallen beside the furnace. After sweeping and wiping the floor, he put the scattered tools back in their places.

Because his frame was small, he had to drag the large hammer using both hands and his body.

The man simply worked in silence without chasing the child away.

Kang! Kaang!

That was because he knew the child worked like this and endured each day on the food obtained from him.

If not here, the child would become a beggar, or fall into the hands of those one should never meet and be sold off somewhere.

Fortunately, this quick-witted and clever child knew full well how important each meal obtained here was, so he never crossed the man's nerves.

After cleaning diligently in his own way, the child admired the swords, knives, and daggers hanging on one wall.

This smithy made not only farming tools like hoes, sickles, and plows, but also various weapons used by martial artists.

There were so many diverse items that it was hard to believe the owner of this smithy made them all alone.

The child looked up at the concealed crossbow hanging on the wall. Perhaps because it was a dangerous military weapon, it was bound with a padlocked chain.

"Did you make that yourself too? If someone were hit by that, even a person trained in martial arts would die, right?"

As always, the man gave no answer.

"I want to make things like that too, later."

The man stopped hammering and asked the child.

"Why do you want to make them?"

Even though the child had come for several days, he had only given him food and never once asked him a question, so at this first question, the child answered clearly and deliberately.

"My father said a person needs a skill to make a living."

"Who?"

"My father."

The man did not ask about the father. Because the emotion visible on the child's face when he answered "father" was not hatred, but longing.

A father like that would never abandon the child like this, so he must be dead.

"Can I learn a skill like this too?"

"If it's to make a living, aren't there easier things?"

The child seemed to have gone to other places besides here.

"They said they don't need an errand boy at the village inn."

He was still too young; aside from being an errand boy on the streets, there would be no work he could do.

"Eat."

"Yes, thank you."

Now the child walked on his own to the table in the corner. Opening the cloth covering the basket, he found rice balls and dried meat inside.

The child ate the rice balls and meat slowly. He did not gobble them down and spill them. He tried his absolute hardest not to grate on the man's eyes. Even though he was very hungry, he did not eat everything there.

"Would you like to learn smithing?"

At the unexpected words, the child started in surprise and ran to the man.

"Yes! Please teach me!"

The man gazed at the child's sparkling eyes and nodded.

"Finish your meal first."

"Yes!"

The man began hammering silently again.

Kaang!!

From that day, the child began learning the work at the smithy in earnest.

He was still too young to do hard labor. He cleaned and ran petty errands while watching the man work. In fact, watching was the greatest lesson.

The child was smart and sharp beyond his years, so the man told him many things, even though he knew they were words given too early.

"Before you control iron, you must know how to control fire, and before you control fire, you must know how to control your heart. In the end, smithing depends on how well you control yourself."

The child did not yet know how tremendous a smith this man was. Nor that he possessed skills far beyond running a small smithy in such a remote place.

"If you fear fire, you cannot do this work. But neither can you disregard fire. Do you understand?"

It was still words he could not fully understand, but the child listened carefully to what the man said.

Thus, several months passed.

***

The child was walking a night path.

His face was taut with tension, as if being chased by someone, and in the child's hands was a crossbow. It was the very one displayed in the smithy. The crossbow was so heavy that he hugged it to his chest and walked with difficulty.

When someone walked up ahead, the child hid behind a tree. Once the person passed, the child came out again and continued walking.

Where the child's gaze was directed was a martial hall in the distance.

As the child carefully moved toward that place, someone blocked his way.

Recognizing the other party, the child was startled out of his wits. It was the owner of the smithy standing right there. He had clearly confirmed that the man had fallen asleep.

"......Mister."

The man asked with cold eyes. He had fed and sheltered him for the past several months.

"Did you come to the smithy from the very beginning to steal that?"

The child could not say no. After working for several months, he had figured out how to use the crossbow. He had also learned where the key to the lock binding it was.

The man reached out his hand, telling him to hand over the crossbow.

The child stepped back and said.

"I need this."

For the child, urgency outweighed guilt. The child had a reason he could not help but do this.

"That man killed my father. I'll kill him with this."

The child told his story while crying.

The child had lived with his father alone.

His father had done menial work at the Yongbaek Martial Hall, the largest martial hall in the area. As he was diligent by nature, he had no trouble feeding his son.

Then one day, his father returned home savagely beaten by someone.

His father, groaning in pain, had said he would be fine after sleeping, but in the end, he never woke up.

Listening to the neighbors who buried his father, the child learned that his father had been beaten to death by Zhong Chui, an instructor at the Yongbaek Martial Hall.

The reason was truly nothing much. It was because he had not placed training equipment where Zhong Chui wanted it brought, and even that had been Zhong Chui's own misunderstanding.

Zhong Chui had a foul temper even normally, but it worsened when drunk. Beating and tormenting people was an everyday occurrence for him, and he had been drunk that day as well.

He had caused trouble many times before, but because his martial arts skill was outstanding and his temperament vicious, everyone had hushed up and overlooked his evil deeds.

—Does a person die from being hit a few times? He must have had an underlying illness.

That was what the man had said upon hearing the news of the father's death.

The child spoke while crying. Even before coming to the smithy, the child had dreamt of revenge. Though he had told the man he couldn't work because there was no errand boy position, he had thought of saving money as an errand boy to get revenge.

"I heard guests talking. They said if you give money, an assassin will kill someone. But I would have had to work twenty years to save that money."

Of course, there was a high chance he would lose even that money to swindlers offering to introduce him to assassins.

"This is the only way I can take revenge."

Tears streamed from the child's eyes. The child had truly loved his father, and his father had been everything to him.

The man looked at the child without speaking. He was a smart child and a child of strong will. What child dreams of revenge upon his father's death, and what child would work for months in a smithy to steal a crossbow?

Heaven had given the child such specialness, but had also given him trials too great to bear.

The man calmly rebuked the child.

"So your theft is fine, and only that man's crime is a crime?"

The child could not answer.

"You cannot kill that man. The recoil on that crossbow is too strong; even if you aim and shoot, you won't hit."

The child looked down at the crossbow he had hugged to his chest despite its weight. It was not something he could hit with just by pulling the trigger.

"Even if you were lucky enough to hit him, you would also be guilty of killing someone. You would be imprisoned for life."

The child with his head hung low had clearly steeled himself for everything. Because of that, the man scolded him with different reasons.

"Not only you, but I will suffer hardship too."

"You, mister?"

"If a brat like you brought such a crossbow, wouldn't they trace where it came from? They'll think a child couldn't have done such a thing and assume I ordered it. Am I fine with dying for your revenge?"

How could this child have thought that far?

"I'm sorry."

To the point where one wondered how there could be so many tears in such tiny eyes, the child continued to weep.

"Would your father wish for you to be imprisoned your whole life while avenging him?"

The child said nothing.

"Living well is your revenge."

Just as the man was taking the crossbow from the child's arms.

The child asked the man.

"Then what about the man who killed my father?"

The man moved away without any answer.

"Come out tomorrow too."

The child flinched and looked at the man. He hadn't expected to be told to come out after stealing the crossbow.

"Didn't your father say so? That you need a skill to make a living."

***

Kang, kaang!

The man who had been hammering turned his head for a moment and looked at the table in the corner. The meal prepared every day for the child sat there still, covered by a cloth.

Even though the time to come had passed, the child had not come out.

Just as the man, having gazed at it for a moment, was about to hammer again, someone spoke from behind.

"Would you tend to this sword?"

The man answered without turning around.

"Take it elsewhere. I cannot tend to such a precious sword."

Then the guest from behind asked.

"How do you know it is a precious sword without even looking at it?"

"Because............ it is your sword."

The man turned around.

The person smiling before him was Geom Mugeuk. The owner of this smithy was none other than Myeongsin, who had been a subordinate of the King of Killers.

"I thought I had completely hidden my past, but it seems not."

"For that, you ought to have lived selling rice or clothes."

Parting with Geom Mugeuk, Myeongsin had said he would live running a smithy. There was no way Tongcheon Gak could not find the whereabouts of such a Myeongsin.

Myeongsin stared intently at Geom Mugeuk. He recalled what the young cult leader had said. It had been the answer to why she saved him.

—Because I don't want to sweep away people like you as I go along my path. Because I think it would be nice if somewhere in this world, there was a wonderful assassin who would take a single coin offered by a little one who unjustly lost his parents as commission to kill a wicked man.

That was why Myeongsin was glad to reunite with Geom Mugeuk. Because he was the only one who truly recognized him.

Geom Mugeuk looked outside and spoke.

"This young lady, please come in."

Ian, who had been outside, came in.

Geom Mugeuk introduced her as if boasting.

"This is my beloved."

His voice was thoroughly excited, and even his expression contained the emotion of "What do you think? Isn't she a wonderful woman?"

With a long-suffering expression, Ian shook her head. Truly, whenever Geom Mugeuk met someone he knew, he seemed ready to introduce her like that.

Ian politely cupped her hands in salute and greeted Myeongsin.

"I heard of you on the way. It is an honor to meet you."

"No matter what you have heard, I am a man far from honor."

Myeongsin had been the second assassin of the Myeongbu. At a glance, he could tell Ian's martial arts were superior to his own.

"You are one who has achieved great things at a young age. It is I who am honored to meet you."

Ian had been seeing many lives while traveling with Geom Mugeuk.

One vaguely resolves that this is how one must live, but she knew well how difficult it was to live a completely different life.

But as you can see, such lives actually exist.

So even such a great assassin lives running a small smithy like this.

After exchanging greetings with Ian, Myeongsin asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Why have you come looking for me?”

“Have you forgotten? Did you not say you would send me something whenever you made a fine piece? I waited and waited, but nothing came, so I came like this myself.”

Of course, that was not the reason. He had been curious about how Myeongsin was living.

“Were you really waiting?”

He was the Young Cult Leader who even now wore the Black Demon Sword, called the second greatest sword in the Demonic Path. Once he became Cult Leader, he would wear the Heavenly Demon Sword, the greatest sword in the Demonic Path. What fine weapon in the world could such a man need?

“I have yet to make anything good.”

“When you do, be sure to send it to our cult.”

Since it meant he wished to keep their connection alive, Myeongsin nodded. He was the one who should be grateful.

Geom Mugeuk had come to see Myeongsin because he had wanted to, but there was also something he absolutely wished to tell him.

“Live at ease from now on. The ghosts of the past will no longer follow you.”

Myeongsin understood. The organization he had once belonged to had vanished completely. Now he was perfectly free.

“Thank you.”

Their gazes met in midair. Geom Mugeuk bade Myeongsin farewell.

“Then let us meet again next time.”

“Let us.”

Ian looked at the two of them in astonishment. They were really leaving just like this? After only seeing his face? Without even sharing a meal?

Through this journey together, Ian had come to learn new things about the man named Geom Mugeuk. She had thought she knew him better than anyone, but that had been a misconception.

The road here had been quite long. Before, she had not known he was the sort of person who would come all that way for such a brief meeting. She had only thought him the sort to eat, drink, and say that since they had come all this way, they might as well see everything before leaving.

Which side of him was the real one?

Geom Mugeuk, talkative? When he was someone who could smile once like that and leave?

After parting with Myeongsin and walking out of the forge, they saw a child running toward it in excitement.

With a face as though he had gained the whole world, the child rushed into the forge and shouted,

“Mister! They say that bastard died last night. He was drunk and missed his footing…”

Myeongsin’s voice could be heard as well.

“You brat, you’re late. Hurry up and start cleaning.”

“I’ll really learn and work hard from now on!”

“Eat first.”

“Yes!”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been looking at that small forge, turned his gaze toward the sky. As he looked up at the sky, which seemed especially blue today, a smile formed at the corners of his mouth.

“So you will not accept even a single coin.”

Chapter 882: Tell the Master to Come Out!
-----------------------------------------
“Is that the place you mentioned?”

Shi Zhuying (史株映), the eldest son of the Beiyang Sect Master (北洋門主), asked as he looked at the building visible in the distance.

“Yes, Young Master.”

The one who answered was Li Ke (李客). Li Ke was highly skilled with the sword, but above all, he was a man with abundant experience in the martial world. That was why Shi Zhuying had made Li Ke his right-hand man and took him wherever he went.

“So they built an inn in our territory, bold as brass, and several months have passed without them coming even once to pay their respects?”

The Beiyang Sect was a sect that stood between the orthodox and unorthodox paths, and it was the largest sect in this region. There was no law saying one had to pay respects simply because one had started a business, but until now, most newcomers had done so in some fashion.

Those quick on the uptake came of their own accord, but in most cases, the Beiyang Sect sent someone to give them a hint. You are now within the Beiyang Sect’s sphere of influence, so you should pay your respects.

Each year, offer a certain amount as a token of sincerity. That way, if something urgent happens, you may receive the Beiyang Sect’s help.

“And he couldn’t even handle one thing like this.”

This was originally the sort of matter Shi Zhuying usually dealt with. But for the past few months, he had been away with Li Ke handling affairs in another region, so he had left it to his younger brother—and his brother had failed to take care of it.

The very next day after returning, he set out at once. It was the perfect opportunity to show his father his ability. As expected, the one who should inherit the Beiyang Sect was himself, the eldest son.

Just as Shi Zhuying was about to start walking, Li Ke spoke to him.

“From what I hear, that inn is no ordinary inn.”

“What about it?”

“I heard that if you want to enter that inn, you have to unfasten your sword at the entrance and leave it there.”

Shi Zhuying was startled.

“What does that mean? You have to unfasten your sword at the entrance of an inn?”

It was the first time he had ever heard such a thing in his life.

“What kind of fool would entrust his weapon at the entrance of an inn? Would you leave your weapon with some mere waiter?”

“I only heard the rumors myself.”

He too had been away with Shi Zhuying, so he could not have known about this inn.

And that was not all he had heard.

“On top of that, they say there’s a rule that no fighting is allowed inside that inn under any circumstances.”

Shi Zhuying let out a laugh of disbelief. As if making people leave their swords at the entrance were not enough, they weren’t even allowed to fight?

“It must be a ridiculous rumor. Who in the world would create an inn like that?”

“Business has been poor these days, so they must have tried some peculiar trick to draw attention. I also heard the food and lodging are outrageously expensive.”

The two walked toward the inn.

Seen up close, the inn boasted a tremendous presence. It truly seemed like the largest inn in all the Central Plains, and a huge signboard hung over the entrance.

英雄客棧.

“Hero Inn? Whoever named it was truly arrogant.”

“I wonder who wrote those characters.”

The brushwork contained the very momentum of a dragon ascending to the heavens.

“Even the name is like this. Whoever the owner is, he’s clever. He knows how to draw attention in every way.”

“If he were clever, he would have come to pay respects to us first.”

It could be considered fortunate that his younger brother had failed to handle the matter, but looking at this inn alone, it was truly infuriating. So they were acting boldly because the place was so large, were they? In the end, the owner was still nothing more than an innkeeper.

Fine. Now he would let them have a taste of a real hero.

The two entered the inn.

“Welcome.”

At the entrance, a man greeted them politely. He was not the sort of waiter seen in ordinary inns. He was dressed splendidly, like the chief steward of a great sect.

When they stepped through the door he opened, what greeted them inside was not the scent of liquor, but the fragrance of flowers.

The interior was splendid and beautiful. It was not merely large; the inside was truly luxurious as well. Dragons gilded in gold leaf were carved into the ceiling beams,

and ornate lamps hung everywhere. Famous paintings and poems adorned the walls, and beautiful patterns were engraved into the floor.

The tables were made of expensive hardwood, and the chairs looked exceedingly comfortable. The spaces between seats were wide, and the staircase leading to the second floor was, astonishingly, built in a graceful curve.

“Is this really an inn?”

He had visited many famous inns, but he had never seen one this splendid and elegant.

Even the waiters working inside were different from those elsewhere. They all wore matching uniforms as they carried liquor and dishes, yet not one of them ran. All of them walked lightly and gracefully, with refinement.

“They must have poured an enormous amount of money into this.”

A smile appeared on Shi Zhuying’s face. After all, that also meant there was plenty of money to squeeze out of them.

Guided by a waiter waiting inside, they arrived before a man. He was a ponytailed man with an easygoing face that gave off a free-spirited air.

“Welcome, honored guests.”

Shi Zhuying lowered his voice toward the man who greeted them warmly.

“I am Shi Zhuying of the Beiyang Sect.”

“It is an honor to meet you.”

After bowing politely, the ponytailed man asked,

“Will you be dining? Or lodging?”

“I came to see the owner.”

“May I ask why you seek him?”

He was asking why, when he himself had come? A mere employee at the entrance? He did not imagine for a moment that the inn’s owner would be dressed so casually with his hair tied up like that.

“I will speak to the owner directly.”

“Unless it concerns money, you may speak with me.”

Shi Zhuying’s expression hardened as he spoke firmly.

“I told you to call the owner.”

“He is busy at the moment, so you will have to wait a little.”

He even intended to make him wait? Fine, let’s see how far you go.

“I’ll wait.”

As he was about to go inside, the ponytailed man said,

“Before you enter, you must entrust your weapons to us.”

What Li Ke had heard was true.

“What did you just hear me say? I told you I came to meet your owner. I am Shi Zhuying of the Beiyang Sect.”

“Yes, Great Hero Shi. Even so, you must leave your weapon before entering.”

Shi Zhuying’s gaze sharpened.

“Have you ever seen a martial artist entrust his weapon to someone else?”

At that, the ponytailed man pointed to the writing carved into the wall.

“It is the principle of our Hero Inn.”

—Weapons may not be brought into this inn. If one is caught attempting to bring one in secretly, that weapon will be confiscated permanently.

—Fighting is absolutely forbidden within this inn. If this rule is violated, the offender must work at the inn without pay for a period determined by the owner.

—Murder is absolutely forbidden within this inn. If this rule is violated, the offender must pay with his life.

—No guest may stay at this inn for more than seven days in a month.

“Are you lunatics?”

The words burst from Shi Zhuying’s mouth of their own accord. The ponytailed man merely smiled, as if this was a reaction he had seen more than once or twice.

Shi Zhuying was dumbfounded. The first rule, that one had to leave one’s weapon before entering, was almost cute compared to the second and third.

“If we fight, we have to work here?”

“Yes, that is correct.”

“If the owner says work ten days, we work ten days, and if he says work for life, we work for life?”

“That is correct.”

“And has anyone actually worked here because of that?”

“Of course. There are people working here even now.”

“Surely they aren’t martial artists?”

“Would ordinary people have reason to fight? They are all martial artists.”

“And yet they work obediently?”

The ponytailed man answered with a meaningful smile.

“No. We made them work.”

Shi Zhuying shook his head with a look of pity. Just how much of a fool did one have to be to get caught by an inn and made to work?

In any case, that meant they had hired some fellow with a bit of martial skill.

“Then has anyone died for killing someone?”

The ponytailed man glanced around, then whispered,

“That is a secret known only to the owner.”

Fine, all this lunatic talk could be dismissed as bluffing. Shi Zhuying was curious about the fourth rule.

“Then why can one stay only seven days a month?”

“At first, there was no such rule, but some people began trying to use this place as a permanent refuge. So we set the limit at seven days.

And starting from the fourth day, the lodging fee doubles each day. It means we prefer guests to stay only three days before leaving.”

In any case, those words implied that for those seven days, one would be absolutely safe.

Shi Zhuying turned to Li Ke and laughed.

“Whoever it is, he used his head well. Once the rumors spread, wouldn’t anyone want to come here at least once?”

“Yes, that is so.”

Shi Zhuying looked back at the ponytailed man.

“And what if I refuse to leave my sword?”

Despite the provocative question, the ponytailed man answered calmly.

“In that case, you cannot use our inn. Naturally, you also will not be able to meet the owner.”

Shi Zhuying lifted the sword at his waist, scabbard and all.

“Do you know what kind of sword this is? It is a treasured sword passed down through generations in our family to the eldest son. If this sword were lost, even selling this entire inn would not be enough to compensate for it. And you still intend to take it?”

At that, the ponytailed man pointed to the writing posted behind him.

—If a sword entrusted to us is lost, we will compensate twice its value.

Shi Zhuying snorted.

“And just how would you compensate me? My sword has value that cannot be measured in money. In the end, you would simply shrug and say there’s nothing you can do.”

“Please do not worry. It will never be lost.”

At the ponytailed man’s certainty, Li Ke, who was behind him, asked,

“Where do you intend to store the swords? Have you perhaps installed a vault made of ten-thousand-year cold iron?”

Otherwise, there was no way he could be so confident.

At that, the ponytailed man stepped aside and indicated behind him.

“We store swords here.”

Behind the ponytailed man was a round barrel. Countless swords were stuck into it. They truly were stuck in there carelessly.

Shi Zhuying and Li Ke were at a loss for words. It was utterly absurd. As if people really had entrusted their swords, name tags were attached to the hilts.

Unable to hold back, Shi Zhuying shouted,

“Have you gone mad? You store precious swords like that? You just shove them into a barrel?”

At that moment, one of the weapons stuck there caught Li Ke’s eye.

It was a straight saber with a splendid flame pattern painted on its hilt.

The Spirit Flame Saber (靈火焰刀)?

That could not be. The one who used the Spirit Flame Saber was Ran Huoren (染火刃), a man whose temperament was cruel and ferocious, and he would absolutely never have entrusted his weapon to such a place.

Moreover, his Spirit Flame Saber was one of the rarest treasured sabers among sabers. It was far more precious than Shi Zhuying’s sword.

“Could these people be playing some trick? Putting fake weapons here?”

But that could not be. If they dared impersonate an expert like Ran Huoren, of all people, the entire inn would be slaughtered.

Just then, a middle-aged scholar came walking down from the second floor.

The ponytailed man smiled and greeted him.

“Did you have a comfortable stay?”

The middle-aged scholar answered with a satisfied smile.

“I stayed well. I have never seen such an excellent inn in my life.”

“I am glad you enjoyed yourself.”

Seeing the guest preparing to leave, Li Ke tilted his head.

‘Where have I seen that man before?’

He had definitely seen him somewhere, but he could not remember.

The middle-aged scholar said to the ponytailed man,

“Give me my sword.”

“Yes.”

The ponytailed man took a sword out of the barrel.

There was a name tag attached to that sword’s hilt as well, and the moment Li Ke saw it, his eyes widened.

Qing Jushi (淸居士)!

He remembered. Only now did he remember. This was Qing Jushi, a famed expert of the orthodox faction. Yes, that sword was his unique weapon, the Zhengming Sword.

He had met him once more than ten years ago. Qing Jushi had been surrounded by martial artists and showered with cheers, and Li Ke himself had been one of the martial artists in that crowd.

‘Qing Jushi entrusted the Zhengming Sword here?’

Even after seeing it with his own eyes, Li Ke could not believe it. His gaze turned once more toward the Spirit Flame Saber.

‘Don’t tell me that one is real too?’

An inexplicable unease came over Li Ke.

In any case, this was a place where Qing Jushi entrusted his sword.

“Today’s appointment is important, so for now, why not entrust your sword?”

“What are you talking about? What if it gets lost?”

“We can sit somewhere where this place is visible.”

Li Ke sent Shi Zhuying a voice transmission.

—The guest who just left was Qing Jushi.

—That was Qing Jushi?

—If an expert like him stays here, I think it would be best to meet the owner first.

The name Qing Jushi exerted its power. After all, he was famous enough that even Shi Zhuying had heard of him countless times.

In the end, Shi Zhuying entrusted his sword to them with a reluctant expression. Li Ke also entrusted his own sword.

“Instead, give me a seat with a good view of this spot.”

“Of course.”

He intended to sit there and keep watch over the place where his sword had been set.

Sa Juyeong warned the ponytailed man.

“I’ll say this clearly: if the sword is lost, or if so much as a small scratch appears on the scabbard, you’ll pay for it with your life.”

“Now, please look. I’ll keep it safe.”

Though he put it in carefully, in the end, it still went into that weapons bin.

The two of them took seats where they could see the ponytailed man.

After sitting down, Sa Juyeong pointed at the ponytailed man several times with his finger, signaling him to guard his sword properly.

Then, before even a quarter-hour had passed since they began waiting there, he could no longer hold back and shouted.

“Hurry up and tell the proprietor to come out!”

As Sa Juyeong’s voice rang out, someone seated beside them spoke in a voice like metal scraping against metal.

“Brat, be quiet.”

Turning his head, he saw an old man sitting there drinking.

The old man’s face was covered in blood-dark bruises and swollen all over, making it impossible to tell who he was. It seemed he had been beaten quite badly.

Just then, as the old man raised his cup, a tattoo in the shape of blazing fire could be seen on his arm.

The moment he saw it, Igaek was startled.

‘Yeomhwain?’

He had heard that Yeomhwain bore such a tattoo on his arm.

Just as the startled Igaek was about to send a sound transmission warning him to be careful, Sa Juyeong spoke first.

“And who might you be, old man, to speak so rudely?”

No! Igaek’s heart sank.

As if proving his guess correct, the old man’s gaze sank into an indescribable coldness.

‘This is bad.’

Igaek’s heart pounded as if it would burst. He had never heard of anyone who had offended Yeomhwain and survived. There were even rumors that he had burned someone to death with an extreme yang saber art simply because they had looked at him unpleasantly.

Just as Igaek was agonizing over how they could overcome this crisis—

A woman walked out toward them. She was a truly enchanting and sensual woman, one who drew everyone’s gaze. It was the first time either of them had ever seen such a beautiful woman in person.

She walked over lightly and spoke to the old man.

“I believe I told you not to drink while working?”

Her voice was gentle, but the old man had already sprung up from his seat in fright. At a loss for what to do, he trembled in fear.

“A customer left it behind, so I only had one sip.”

The old man hurriedly gathered the bottle of liquor and the dishes there onto a tray and cleared them away.

As he walked off in haste, as if fleeing, the woman spoke gently to him.

“You’ll raise dust. Don’t run.”

The old man flinched, then crept away and disappeared toward the kitchen.

Only then did the woman turn toward the two of them.

“You were looking for me?”

Chapter 883: No Need to Treat My Sword Specially
------------------------------------------------
“So the owner has finally come out.”

Strength filled Sa Juyeong’s voice. From that demeanor, Igaek could sense it.

‘He doesn’t know that old man is Yeomhwain.’

It was only natural. Even if he had been told through voice transmission, he wouldn’t have believed it.

His face was beaten to a pulp so he couldn’t be recognized, and he had even seen him flee in terror from a woman—how could he think he was Yeomhwain?

Seeing the Spirit-Subduing Flame Blade stuck in a container, and even the old man’s arm tattoo, he himself had felt uncertain. No, he had even had this doubt.

‘Could he be an old man hired to imitate Yeomhwain?’

It was unbelievable that Yeomhwain would have such a wretched appearance. That notorious Yeomhwain?

“I didn’t know the innkeeper was such a beautiful person.”

At Sa Juyeong’s words, the woman smiled. The owner of that enchanting smile was none other than Cha Iran.

She had been running this special inn together with Ak Gunhak, going through much trial and error.

It was something she couldn’t have imagined doing alone, but running it together with Ak Gunhak was still fun.

“Thank you for the compliment.”

Then Sa Juyeong’s eyes caught a red stain on her sleeve. It seemed to be sauce spilled while working.

Thinking that she had truly come from working, he relaxed slightly.

“Great Hero Sa, what business brings you to see me?”

“Did you know that this area is the territory of our Beiyang Gate?”

“No, I am not well-versed in the affairs of the martial world.”

In Sa Juyeong’s eyes, the woman did not seem to have learned martial arts.

Igaek felt the same. That was precisely the problem. She was the owner of the inn where Yeomhwain was working after getting beaten up.

‘She is definitely no ordinary woman.’

Sa Juyeong put on airs in front of Cha Iran.

“This entire area is governed by our Beiyang Gate.”

“Ah, a great person has come, and I failed to recognize you.”

“If any trouble befalls the Hero’s Inn in the future, we will help.”

“I am grateful just to hear those words.”

Sa Juyeong took a liking to this inn.

Judging by its scale, the money this inn must earn was enormous. Furthermore, it had such a beautiful woman as its owner.

He was in a position to control this inn.

“With a single word of mine.............”

Just as Sa Juyeong was about to let them know what kind of position he was in.

The inn door burst open roughly, and they heard someone enter urgently.

Everyone inside the inn directed their gazes there.

A middle-aged man entered with an injured body. Blood was flowing from his arm and side, and despite his serious injuries, one could tell he was a considerable expert just from his footsteps.

The man looked around the interior of the inn. Everyone’s gaze was directed at him, and it was clearly different from other inns. Eyes filled more with curiosity than fear.

“If you wish to enter, come this way.”

At Ak Gunhak’s words, the man walked a few steps closer and asked.

“Is it true that there is a rule here forbidding fighting one another?”

“That is correct.”

Ak Gunhak pointed to the wall where the rules were engraved.

“Please read them.”

The man frankly revealed his situation.

“Those chasing me will arrive here shortly. Will you still protect me?”

To those words, Ak Gunhak replied calmly.

“We are not protecting you.”

At the man’s questioning gaze, Ak Gunhak said calmly.

“We are upholding our inn’s rules.”

The man was certain that Ak Gunhak had not learned martial arts. It was inconceivable that a master capable of hiding their realm of martial arts from his eyes would be guarding the inn entrance.

‘Then what is this imposing feeling?’

Ak Gunhak looked at the man’s sword and spoke.

“If you wish to stay at our inn, you must first hand over your weapons.”

The man looked down at the sword he was wearing. If a fight broke out without this sword, he would die to his pursuers without being able to resist properly even once.

“If you cannot abide by the inn’s rules, you cannot stay at our inn.”

The man’s deliberation did not last long. He had come here knowing it was such a place.

The man untied the sword from his waist and handed it to Ak Gunhak.

“What is your esteemed name?”

The man hesitated to say his name.

“If you do not wish to reveal it, you may use an alias. I ask because of the weapon you are entrusting to us.”

But the man’s identity was immediately revealed.

The inn door burst open, and a newcomer rushed in, calling his name.

“Myeongilgeom! Is this the best place you could flee to?”

The man who had burst in was dressed entirely in black martial attire.

Someone sitting in the inn recognized him.

“Heuksurang!”

Heuksurang was a famous martial artist in the demonic faction’s martial world. He was so notorious that when a crowd gathered like this, there was always at least one person who recognized him.

Myeongilgeom, who had been chased here by him, was also an expert of the demonic path, and the two had originally been friends.

However, a few years ago, they had fought fiercely over a trivial matter, and as the conflict deepened, they became sworn enemies. This time, it had come to a life-and-death battle, and the result was as could be seen.

At the gazes concentrated on him, Heuksurang shouted.

“If you know who I am, all of you lower your eyes!”

But among the people in the inn, there was no one who averted their gaze. They seemed to have faith that no incident would occur here.

Only Sa Juyeong looked at Igaek with a nervous expression. He too had heard of Heuksurang.

—Can you subdue him?

Igaek shook his head firmly and sent a voice transmission.

—Heuksurang is not someone the two of us can handle.

Sa Juyeong’s pride was slightly wounded. Just moments ago, he had boasted that if any trouble befell the Hero’s Inn, they would protect it.

Meanwhile, Myeongilgeom sat in the seat closest to where he had been standing.

“Bring me a bottle of liquor here.”

With an injured body and his sword entrusted as well, his chances of winning against Heuksurang were nonexistent.

He had spoken as if to say, ‘This is how I feel right now!’ but a waiter actually brought him liquor.

Even Myeongilgeom, who had ordered the liquor, was just as bewildered as Heuksurang. Were they really bringing liquor in this savage situation?

“How much is it? I feel like I might not be able to pay.”

Then Ak Gunhak spoke from the entrance area.

“I’ve called for a physician, so please pay slowly after you’ve finished treatment.”

And he had Heuksurang read the rules engraved on the wall.

“Read this first.”

Heuksurang laughed mockingly after reading the rules.

“I intend to break all three of those principles. I will not hand over my sword, I will fight that bastard, and I will kill him. Are you putting on airs even after hearing who I am?”

Myeongilgeom, who was listening, spoke while drinking.

“The liquor tastes good. A drink before dying by a friend’s blade—this is superb.”

“Damn it! What friend!”

“That’s my line! You narrow-minded bastard!”

Then Yeomhwain caught Igaek’s eye. He was standing in front of the kitchen, having come out at some point.

Earlier, he had practically been chased away by the woman, but now his swollen, beaten face was wearing a bright expression.

What filled his two eyes was clearly anticipation.

‘Just what is he looking forward to?’

Igaek looked at where he was looking. He was looking at Heuksurang. Was he expecting Heuksurang to save him? Heuksurang was far weaker than him.

In the next moment, a single thought flashed through Igaek’s mind.

‘Could it be?’

That anticipation was precisely this.

‘He’s hoping Heuksurang will come in as his successor!’

As if to confirm it, the more Heuksurang disregarded the bristle-haired man, the happier Yeomhwain became.

“Today, I’ll kill every last one of you here!”

It was precisely when Heuksurang was emitting tremendous killing intent that someone entered behind him and spoke.

“Excuse me, I’ll pass through.”

When Heuksurang turned around, someone was entering carrying a large flowerpot.

“Phew, heavy, heavy.”

The one who put down the large flowerpot was none other than Jian Wuji.

Jian Wuji looked at Ak Gunhak and spoke in a loud voice.

“Congratulations on the opening, friend!”

A bright smile formed on Ak Gunhak’s face. The guests watching that sight were all surprised. Since staying at this inn, today was the first time they had seen that person smile so brightly.

“You came?”

Following behind Jian Wuji, Yi An entered as well.

“I came too. Congratulations!”

Gift bundles were also held in her hands.

At the appearance of the beautiful woman, everyone opened their eyes wide and stared at her. She was a peerless beauty who gave off a different feeling from the innkeeper here.

“You didn’t forget, right? The promise to let us use this place for free for life?”

At Yi An’s memory, Cha Iran smiled brightly and answered.

“Of course.”

Both Ak Gunhak and Cha Iran had seen all sorts of crowds since opening, but upon seeing Jian Wuji and Yi An, broad smiles came naturally to their faces.

On the other hand, Heuksurang was furious to the tips of his hair.

‘These bastards really!’

To carry on like this while he was standing right there! He should have been enraged, but he had missed the moment to be angry.

They wouldn’t bring liquor when ordered, and without reading the room, they brought a flowerpot to celebrate the opening.

Heuksurang exploded with killing intent.

But soon his expression faltered. He had raised his killing intent to the extreme, yet it could not be unleashed.

‘What is this?’

His internal energy was definitely intact, but killing intent would not arise. He had lived his entire life radiating killing intent—just what in the world was going on?

His acupoints had not been sealed, nor had he been poisoned. His internal energy circulated through his meridians without any abnormality, and his physical condition was fine.

Yet he did not feel like fighting.

At least one thing was certain.

‘It’s because of that young bastard.’

What technique did he use? He suppressed his surprised heart with effort and watched what Jian Wuji was doing.

Jian Wuji boasted to Ak Gunhak.

“This tree is no ordinary tree. It’s called the Myriad-Year Fortune Tree, a legendary tree known to keep a shop swarming with customers if placed there. To get this, I wandered through the poison mists of the Southern Wilderness and searched every nook and cranny of the Kunlun Mountains.”

Ak Gunhak spoke with a laugh.

“Lies. You probably got it just a moment ago.”

Yi An beside him nodded and spoke.

“Your lightness skill has gotten faster, so you retrieved it quickly.”

Jian Wuji placed the flowerpot next to where Ak Gunhak was.

“It’s perfect right here beside you. Brother Ak, you have to be happy for a myriad years.”

Gratitude filled Ak Gunhak’s eyes as he looked at the only friend in his life.

Jian Wuji spoke to Yi An.

“Now, we too must hand over our swords.”

Yi An handed over her sword first. Ak Gunhak received it carefully.

“The Sun-Moon Sword... it is the most precious sword I have received so far.”

Those who heard those words were startled. It was truly a treasured sword they had only heard of in legends.

Sa Juyeong looked at Igaek beside him. Is the Sun-Moon Sword truly that precious? He asked with his eyes, and Igaek nodded.

To the voice transmission asking, ‘More than my sword?’ he gave an exact evaluation that it was incomparable.

Sa Juyeong felt embarrassed. They entrusted such a precious sword so casually, yet he had made such a fuss over his own sword.

But the real surprise was yet to come.

Jian Wuji untied the Black Demon Sword he wore at his waist and gave it to him.

“I had something prepared for the day you’d eventually come.”

Ak Gunhak lifted something from below. It was a wooden sword stand.

He placed the Black Demon Sword on it as if displaying it.

“There’s no need to give my sword special treatment alone. Just put it in that container with the others.”

“You might be fine, but the Black Demon Sword won’t be.”

He wouldn’t have placed another sword together with it, but the Sun-Moon Sword was placed with the Black Demon Sword. With the thought that if it was Yi An’s sword, the Black Demon Sword would understand.

Igaek spoke with a shocked face.

“The Black Demon Sword is one of the Celestial Demon Cult’s Four Great Treasured Swords.”

Everyone froze in shock at the words “Celestial Demon Cult.” Of course, a demonic person could come to the inn, but that a demonic person would carry one of the Celestial Demon Cult’s Four Great Treasured Swords?

If it was a stolen sword, it meant that experts of the Demonic Cult could come rushing here.

Naturally, everyone’s nervous gazes concentrated on Jian Wuji.

“Make sure to put a name tag on my sword. If that thing goes missing, the Central Plains will be turned upside down. Write it big: Jian Wuji, Young Cult Leader of the Celestial Demon Cult.”

Silence flowed over the place. The Young Cult Leader of the Celestial Demon Cult? It was such an astonishing identity that only silence prevailed.

Ak Gunhak and Cha Iran could tell that Geom Mugeuk had deliberately revealed his identity.

His real opening gift had not been the Ten-Thousand-Year Lucky Tree.

Hero Inn was a place where even the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult entrusted his sword and rested.

Now this rumor would spread throughout the gangho. If even the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult had obediently handed over his sword, who would dare quibble over whether or not to entrust theirs?

It was the greatest gift he could have given their inn.

“The Cult Leader is doing well, I trust?”

“I suppose he is. It’s been quite a while since I’ve seen Father myself.”

Judging by the conversation alone, he was unmistakably the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

If it was true?

Sa Juyeong’s face turned pale. That ponytailed man he had looked down on seemed to be friends with the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. And close friends at that.

After entrusting their swords, Geom Mugeuk and Ian passed in front of Heuk Surang again.

“Now then, let us through. Why does this gentleman keep blocking the way?”
