Heuk Surang stood there without moving so much as a finger. He felt suffocated, as if his whole body had frozen solid. To exude such pressure without releasing even the slightest aura!

‘He really is the Young Cult Leader!’

Now he understood why his killing intent had not surged earlier. What killing intent could a rabbit emit before a tiger? He had not even realized he was being suppressed; he had simply been crushed beneath the other man’s presence.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been walking away, turned back and stood before Heuk Surang.

“I have sharp ears, so I heard on my way in. It seems you had a fight with your friend. If possible, make up with him. Even if he acts pathetic and gets on your nerves, having even one friend in life is a great source of strength.”

As he said that, Geom Mugeuk looked at Ak Gunhak and waved.

“Why are you looking at me while saying that!”

After teasing Ak Gunhak like that, Geom Mugeuk left again.

Without hesitation, Heuk Surang strode toward Ak Gunhak. His momentum was so urgent that it seemed as if he might draw his sword at any moment.

“Is this where I entrust my sword? You may simply toss mine on the floor.”

At the sight of him suddenly turning so docile, Yeom Hwain, who had been watching from a corner, let out a sigh so deep it seemed the ground might collapse.

With a disappointed expression, he trudged off toward the place where the dirty dishes were piled up.

After entrusting his sword, Heuk Surang sat down across from Myeong Ilgeom. He held out golden wound medicine for his injuries and said,

“We had a bit of a misunderstanding, didn’t we?”

“I was being narrow-minded.”

Who would refuse to make up when told to do so? It was the moment the two became forced childhood friends once again.

Geom Mugeuk took a seat with Ian and called out to Cha Iran.

“Finish your business quickly and come over here! Brother Ak, you come too!”

Geom Mugeuk looked as if he was itching to boast about his relationship with Ian.

Cha Iran turned back to Sa Juyeong and asked,

“Now then, what were you about to say earlier?”

To Sa Juyeong, she now looked like an entirely different person. Until just a moment ago, she had been an endlessly charming innkeeper, but now she seemed like a terrifying witch.

And he realized it too. What stained her sleeve was not sauce.

Sa Juyeong spoke to her with the utmost politeness he could muster.

“Ah, I was going to say there’s blood on your sleeve.”

Chapter 884: Did You Fall for That Signboard?
---------------------------------------------
“As you can see, I have honored guests.”

Cha Iran smiled and rose from her seat.

Indeed, if he was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, then he was the most honored guest Sa Juyeong and Igaek had ever seen in their lives.

“Don’t mind us. Go on.”

“Thank you for understanding.”

Cha Iran got up and went over to the table where Geom Mugeuk and Lee An were seated.

Sa Juyeong looked at Igaek. Igaek nodded once at him with an expression that said he had done well.

There were many times when Sa Juyeong acted without sense, but at times like this, he knew how to read the room.

Sa Juyeong sent Igaek a voice transmission.

—Do you think we’ll be able to get money out of them now?

Scratch that thought from a moment ago. He still had a long way to go before he could properly read the room.

—You mean take money from the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s friend under the pretext of protection fees? We should count ourselves lucky if they don’t take money from us instead.

Sa Juyeong stole a glance at Geom Mugeuk. Not only him, but every guest in the inn was secretly looking at the Young Cult Leader.

After all, the chance to see the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult in person, and at such close range, was something that might not come even once in a lifetime.

Since he had even handed over the Black Demon Sword according to the rules, surely the Young Cult Leader wouldn’t kill anyone in this inn, would he?

With that thought in mind, they pretended not to look while furtively stealing glances at him.

—Is he really the Young Cult Leader? He’s so different from what I imagined.

He had expected the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult to be truly terrifying and murderous. Someone cold and chilling, the sort of person whose eyes one would find difficult even to meet.

And yet, he was so bright.

And the person protecting him? Was that beautiful woman his escort? Somehow, she did not give off that impression. She seemed like a friend, and like a lover.

—The Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult wanders around like this without guards?

Igaek agreed with Sa Juyeong. Earlier, it had been Yeom Hwain, and now this Young Cult Leader too—there were certainly aspects that made it hard to tell whether they were real or fake.

But they could not possibly be fake. If they had impersonated not just anyone, but the friend of the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, would the Demon Cult leave them alone?

What was more, Geom Mugeuk even bought wine for everyone.

“Brother Ak, give a bottle of wine to everyone here.”

At that, the real waiters came and handed out wine. Ak Gunhak and Cha Iran could understand why Geom Mugeuk was buying wine.

To make the rumor spread faster.

How could anyone hold back from bragging that they had been treated to wine by the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?

Only then did Geom Mugeuk begin speaking with Ak Gunhak in earnest.

“You’ve decorated the inside of the inn splendidly.”

Lee An, too, looked around the interior in great admiration.

“This is the first time I’ve seen such a splendid and beautiful inn.”

“Miss Lee, let’s live here as freeloaders too. Surely they’ll give us a job.”

At that very moment, Cha Iran flinched.

“Wait!”

Cha Iran narrowed her eyes and sent him an interrogating look.

“Miss Lee? Did you just say Miss Lee?”

Geom Mugeuk pretended to be startled.

“Did I say Miss Lee?”

“And you even spoke politely.”

“Ah, what a mistake.”

Lee An shook her head. What awkward acting! Even his feigned fluster was a calculated performance meant to be seen through. He was doing it on purpose because he wanted to boast about their relationship.

Cha Iran looked at Lee An. She said nothing, but she could tell from Lee An’s expression.

“Congratulations to you both.”

At her words, Ak Gunhak also realized what was going on.

“Congratulations.”

“Thank you, Brother Ak.”

Geom Mugeuk conveyed his honest feelings to Ak Gunhak.

“I’ve been having a hard time holding it in since earlier because I wanted to boast. When I opened that door and came in, I wanted to shout it out.”

“It’s worth boasting about. Congratulations, Miss Lee.”

At his grateful words, Lee An bowed her head.

“Thank you.”

For her sake, Ak Gunhak told a story from the past. He was not originally the sort to say such things, but even so, he felt this was something he ought to tell her.

“When I was Mugeuk’s enemy, I once asked him about Miss Lee. I asked what sort of relationship he had with her. He answered that she was someone like his own heart.”

Lee An was inwardly moved. To think he had said such a thing to this remarkable Ak Gunhak when she was not present. It meant it had not simply been one of the jokes he usually made in front of her.

Cha Iran sincerely congratulated her. However, she knew better than anyone that this would not be an easy path.

“You’ve chosen a difficult road.”

Lee An returned those words to her.

“The same goes for you, Martial Artist Cha, doesn’t it?”

“Even so, my side is a bit easier.”

Geom Mugeuk denied the women’s conversation.

“I’m a much easier person than Brother Ak in every way!”

Not only Lee An and Cha Iran, but even Ak Gunhak shook their heads at the same time.

“Are you really saying I’m more difficult than that free spirit?”

Cha Iran nodded firmly.

“It’s more surprising that you didn’t know.”

After that round of congratulations, Ak Gunhak asked as if curious.

“Does the Cult Leader know?”

“Not yet. I’ll have to tell him when I return.”

Lee An’s heart pounded. Just imagining the moment they told the Cult Leader that the two of them were dating made her feel as though her heart would burst already.

And how would her father react?

Ak Gunhak said to the waiter,

“Go bring out the best wine.”

The waiter brought the most expensive wine in the Hero Inn.

“We ought to have a celebratory drink.”

Just as the four of them were about to raise their cups in a toast.

A man stepped into the inn.

He was a well-dressed middle-aged man with smooth skin.

Looking at him, Geom Mugeuk smiled an inscrutable smile and said,

“All sorts of things really do come here.”

It was a remark that saw through the other party at a glance. In that respect, Lee An had also learned everything, and she likewise grasped the man’s nature immediately.

“It must be very hard to run a business.”

Ak Gunhak sighed and said,

“Still, he is a guest, so we must receive him for now.”

Ak Gunhak rose from his seat, walked over to the man, and asked,

“Is this your first time at our inn?”

“It is.”

“Please read the rules over there first.”

The man read the rules. Seeing that he did not appear particularly surprised, it seemed he had likely come here already knowing about the rules of this place.

“I don’t use weapons, so that doesn’t apply. I have no intention of fighting or killing anyone, so that’s fine. I won’t be staying more than seven days, so that doesn’t matter either.”

“How many days do you intend to stay?”

“I plan to stay about two days. Before you give me a room, I’ll have a cup of wine first.”

“Please take any seat you like.”

The man walked into the inn.

Igaek, who had abundant experience, recognized who he was.

—Do not meet that man’s eyes.

—Who is he?

—He is the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost!

The Jade-Faced Yin Ghost was a notorious lecher in the martial world. He had raped and murdered countless women, and after being named a public enemy of the martial world, his whereabouts had become uncertain. Today, however, he had appeared here.

—If he’s a lecher, then he’s come after the owner of this place, hasn’t he?

—Most likely.

—We should tell them.

—You must be cautious. That man is famous for being petty and insidious, and for always taking revenge. He looks to be in his thirties, but in reality he is an old man well past seventy.

—He’s over seventy?

—His internal power is that profound.

What Igaek feared was that if they stepped forward needlessly, they might incur the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost’s grudge and put Sa Juyeong in danger. Such was the extent of the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost’s infamy.

—They have the Young Cult Leader with them, so let us watch for now.

Would they really be able to tell that the man was a lecher?

Indeed, the man was the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost Igaek had spoken of. Having heard the rumor that the owner of Hero Inn was exceedingly beautiful, he had come here.

After becoming a public enemy of the martial world, he had not appeared in such crowded places, but today he had taken the risk.

“It was worth it.”

The moment he saw Cha Iran’s face, he was so delighted he felt as if he might fly. For a woman as beautiful as that, he thought he would break his rule of not keeping a woman for more than a day and live with her for a while.

Just then, Lee An, who had had her back turned, looked to the side and revealed her face.

The Jade-Faced Yin Ghost’s expression brightened radiantly.

“Today will be the greatest day of my life.”

If possible, he resolved to abduct both women.

“Peerless beauties like these are bound to have guards.”

The Jade-Faced Yin Ghost glanced at Geom Mugeuk seated beside them. With his innocent and kind-looking face, he seemed like the scion of some prestigious family.

The ponytailed man who had made him read the rules did not look like an especially impressive master either.

“An inn that puts up rules like these must surely have prepared some expert.”

But no matter how he looked around, he could not see any expert protecting the women.

In any case, it did not matter. Once the flames of lust had been lit, whether someone protected them or not, he intended to abduct those women no matter what.

Having already been named a public enemy of the martial world, he had no way back anyway.

Cha Iran rose from her seat.

“I’ll go to the rear garden for a moment. There’s something I was handling earlier.”

She got up and walked toward the rear garden.

After looking around, the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost rose as if going to the privy and followed her out.

The Jade-Faced Yin Ghost’s heart pounded. It had truly been a long time since he had trembled like this while hunting a woman.

Passing through the corridor, he emerged into the rear garden.

She walked across the beautifully decorated garden, and the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost secretly followed behind her.

When he turned the corner, Cha Iran was standing there.

“Were you following me?”

Since he sensed no presence nearby, the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost revealed his true colors.

“It is your fault for being born beautiful.”

“And not your fault for being born ugly?”

Since her reaction was not one of fear, the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost’s gaze sharpened.

“Before that, I should finish what I was doing.”

When she stepped aside, the scene behind her was revealed. It was a sight he truly had not expected.

There was a deep pit there, and beside it lay a corpse.

Cha Iran walked over to the corpse.

Standing there, the Jade-Faced Yin Ghost asked coldly,

“Who is the dead man?”

“Someone who failed to keep the third rule of this inn.”

He was someone who had broken the rules in the inn and killed another person.

“Even if the inn out front is splendid and beautiful, there is bound to be a side like this as well. You would know that well, wouldn’t you? Like the ugliness beneath that smooth face of yours.”

The Jade-Faced Yin Ghost’s face twitched.

“I was called away earlier while I was working. This actually worked out.”

Looking down into the pit, Cha Iran added,

“It seems I won’t have to do the work twice.”

***

—That woman must have been done in.

Sa Juyeong, who had watched the two of them leave, sent Igaek a voice transmission.

—We should have warned her beforehand.

Then Igaek said something he had not expected.

—The woman will be fine.

—Why are you so sure?

—The three people over there all saw him follow her. Yet none of them even cared.

—That must be because they don’t know who he is.

Perhaps that was so.

But Igaek felt that was not the case. This inn was not the sort of inn where they could thoughtlessly interfere.

—Should we leave now? No, if we do that, we might end up being blamed.

All sorts of thoughts flashed through Sa Juyeong’s mind.

—If something happens to her, that Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult might kill us all. It’s right to leave, isn’t it? Right?

Just as he was worrying about every possible thing, Cha Iran opened the back door and came inside again.

As she walked, her eyes met Sa Juyeong’s.

“Great Hero Sa, are the wine and dishes to your taste?”

“They are excellent.”

At that moment, Sa Juyeong saw it. The blood on her sleeve had increased by quite a bit.

“...There’s more blood on you.”

At that, Cha Iran replied casually, as if it were an everyday occurrence.

“Don’t worry. It isn’t my blood.”

As he swallowed hard, Cha Iran added with a smile,

“I’m joking, joking. Then please enjoy your meal.”

Watching her return to her original seat, Sa Juyeong said to Igaek,

—Let’s hurry and get out of this unpleasant place.

—Let’s do that.

The two rose from their seats.

After settling the bill, they were about to leave when the inn door opened, and two men exuding a ferocious killing intent entered with their swords aimed forward.

“Back off! From now on, anyone who moves dies!”

Sa Juyeong and Igaek stepped back again.

—This damned inn!

One of the two martial artists who had entered leaped over to the place where Ak Gunhak always stood and said,

“Look at this.”

The man pulled the Spirit-Subduing Flame Saber out of the stand in the corner where the swords were stuck.

“Didn’t I say I saw it here?”

“Is that really the Jeryeong Flame Saber?”

the man said, drawing the Jeryeong Flame Saber.

“Look, it’s the genuine article!”

Then something new caught his eye.

“Look here! Here!”

He grew excited as he looked at the two swords on the rack.

“These are the Sun-Moon Swords!”

Watching him, Ak Gunhak let out a sigh and said,

“They’re making even more of a fuss than usual today.”

Since the inn had only recently opened, all manner of people had been coming by nonstop. But this was the first time incidents had broken out one after another like this in a single day.

Returning to her seat, Cha Iran looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“We do have someone here who brings storms wherever he goes, don’t we?”

At her words, Ak Gunhak rounded on Geom Mugeuk.

“You told me to run an inn like this just so you could watch me suffer like this, didn’t you?”

“This is how it’ll last. How could you people possibly run an ordinary inn?”

Cha Iran nodded.

“Thanks to that, we’re making plenty of money. Ah, he touched the Sun-Moon Swords. Hurry and go.”

Ak Gunhak walked over and said,

“Sir, please put down that sword first and read the rules written on the wall...”

Geom Mugeuk watched Ak Gunhak, annoyingly draining his cup.

“This place doesn’t need drinking snacks.”

***

That night, Geom Mugeuk closed the doors of the Hero Inn together with Ak Gunhak.

Looking up at the signboard, Geom Mugeuk said,

“My father must be fond of our Brother Ak.”

The characters on the signboard were none other than the handwriting of Cult Leader Geom Ujin.

“I went to pay my respects before opening the inn.”

He had thought it was merely a farewell before leaving the cult, but it had not been.

“I went to apologize because I could no longer keep my promise.”

“Promise? What promise?”

Ak Gunhak told Geom Mugeuk about the promise he had made with Geom Ujin.

“I promised the Cult Leader that I would stand on your side.”

“With my father? Why?”

“The Cult Leader wanted it.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk had not known his father had made such a promise with Ak Gunhak.

“I told him I was sorry I couldn’t keep the promise because I was opening an inn.”

“What did Father say when you told him you were leaving?”

“He told me to do good business and wrote those words himself.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled. It felt just like his father.

“Brother Ak.”

Ak Gunhak looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“On the day I promised to settle things with Father, come and take my side.”

Ak Gunhak looked up at the signboard, hesitating to answer.

“Why aren’t you answering? Don’t tell me that signboard won you over?”

Ak Gunhak smiled and replied,

“It’s too impressive, isn’t it?”

The calligraphy, and the Cult Leader who had written it.

“So find someone who will carry more weight than me.”

“Where would I find anyone stronger than Brother Ak!”

Ak Gunhak smiled meaningfully and said,

“There isn’t anyone yet, but I feel like there may be in the future.”

Chapter 885: Go Get Permission
------------------------------
“Construction isn’t finished yet.”

The next day, Cha Iran took Ian to show her around the rear garden behind the Hero Inn. Construction was in full swing there. The main building was magnificent enough, but they were even putting up an annex beside it.

“Our goal is to make it the largest inn in the Central Plains.”

“Your drive is incredible.”

“Try going into business with a leisurely man who waters water with water and wine with wine. If I hadn’t taken the lead, we still wouldn’t have decided where to begin.”

Ian laughed at Cha Iran’s words. She thought both Ak Gunhak and Cha Iran were remarkable. To start a new life so bravely like this.

“You look happy.”

“That is exactly what I should be saying to you.”

Among the workers at the construction site were some familiar faces.

“It has been a long time.”

The ones who greeted her respectfully were, to her surprise, Ilgyo and the other assassin women.

“Have you all been well?”

They had once been her subordinates, but now that Ian had left the Ghost Shadow Unit, she treated them with courtesy.

When Cha Iran left the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, all the assassin women except for Sipchilgyo left the cult as well.

Cha Iran had given her subordinates their freedom. She let each of them live a new life.

Even so, about a third of the assassins, including Ilgyo, remained by Cha Iran’s side. Cha Iran did not force even those women to leave.

Working at the Hero Inn was a new life, too.

Instead, she told them they were free to leave whenever they wished.

And as much as possible, she did not give them work that involved killing people. She handled it herself, and buried them herself.

Even when Ilgyo said every time that she would take care of it with her subordinates, Cha Iran would not entrust it to her. She told them to try living a life without staining their hands with blood.

That was why Cha Iran’s sleeves were adorned with drops of blood.

After looking around even the area under construction, the two of them began walking again. Up ahead at the front gate of the Hero Inn, Geom Mugeuk was talking with Ak Gunhak.

“Do you regret opening an inn?”

“I’d need time to regret it if I were going to regret it. You saw, didn’t you? Every day is so busy I can barely think.”

“When you look back on your life at the moment of death, won’t you regret the things you never got to do more than the things you did? So you did well. If it gets too hard, you can always quit.”

“Easy for you to say.”

The two looked at each other and laughed.

“Ah, I don’t want to go back! I want to stay here with you forever, Brother Ak.”

“Then don’t go.”

“Should I really live here for just a month? I’ll open the inn doors and close them myself!”

“I told you I’d give you the best room.”

Ak Gunhak could guess what Geom Mugeuk was feeling.

“Why? Are you scared?”

“Of what?”

“Are you afraid now that you’re going back and starting a new life?”

Geom Mugeuk flinched and looked at Ak Gunhak.

“You hand new lives to other people so readily, but now that you’re the one starting a new life, you’re scared, aren’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk did not hide his feelings from him.

“I’ve come to like her even more than before, and love her even more…”

“And you’re afraid you’ll lose Miss I then?”

Geom Mugeuk said nothing.

“Aren’t you afraid of losing her without ever having loved her properly?”

“!”

“You said people regret what they never got to do when they die, didn’t you? You just said it.”

Geom Mugeuk teased Ak Gunhak as he looked at him.

“How do you know so much? You’ve never even been in love!”

“Who says I’ve never been in love?”

The two laughed together.

“If you ever fight with Miss I later, run away here. At least you won’t be able to fight for seven days.”

At Ak Gunhak’s joke, Geom Mugeuk worried about him.

“Where will you hide if you fight, Brother Ak?”

After a brief pause, Ak Gunhak also revealed what was in his heart.

“I’m a far more selfish person than you. It’s because I’m selfish that I can go around barefoot. It’s because I’m selfish that I can decide to leave.”

But he would not leave anymore. Geom Mugeuk knew well just how strong Ak Gunhak’s sense of responsibility was.

“If that sickness of wanting to leave flares up, make sure you come to me.”

“And if I do?”

“I’ll tell you this. Where are you trying to run off to? Even if you go, there’s nothing there! If you can’t find it here, you won’t find it there either!”

Ak Gunhak knew those words were meant for him, and that Geom Mugeuk was saying them to himself as well.

Just then, Cha Iran spoke from behind them.

“What are you searching for so hard?”

When they turned, Cha Iran and Ian were walking toward them together.

Geom Mugeuk answered with a smile.

“Who else would it be? The only one I search for is Miss I.”

Cha Iran said to Ak Gunhak,

“You should watch and learn jokes like that.”

“A joke? That was my true heart!”

And so they exchanged farewells.

“Brother Ak, let’s meet again.”

“Go safely.”

“Then I’ll come visit again next time.”

At Ian’s farewell, Cha Iran smiled an inscrutable smile and said,

“I’ll be waiting for good news.”

“I’ll return those words to you as they are.”

And so the two of them left the Hero Inn.

As they were walking, a martial artist passed them together with a woman.

“It’s over there, my lady. If we can just reach that place, we can live.”

Though the man did not show it, Geom Mugeuk could tell he had suffered internal injuries.

The whispers between the two reached Geom Mugeuk’s ears clearly.

“Do you really think such an inn exists?”

“We can only hope the rumors are true. If we endure for a few days, people who can save us will come. Let us go, my lady.”

“You need to be treated first.”

“No, your safety comes first, my lady. I must not let it show that I am wounded.”

After they passed, Geom Mugeuk looked back. Beyond the two people walking with difficulty, he could see the Hero Inn.

In that place, someone will be waiting who will call a physician for you even if you do not say a word.

Geom Mugeuk began walking again with Ian and asked her,

“Miss I, how was this trip?”

Ian could tell that the time had finally come to return to the cult.

“It was wonderful. It was the most enjoyable trip of my entire life.”

This time, Ian asked,

“How was this trip for you?”

Since Geom Mugeuk had absolutely forbidden her from calling him Young Cult Leader or Young Master anymore, she called him you.

“It was a journey I, too, will never forget.”

The two looked at each other and smiled.

“Then shall we return now?”

Ian smiled brightly and nodded.

“Yes, let’s go back now.”

***

After returning to the main headquarters, Geom Mugeuk did not go straight to the Heavenly Demon Hall.

The place he took Ian was unexpected. It was the old training ground Geom Mugeuk had used a very long time ago, one that was no longer in use.

When the two of them opened the doors to the training ground for the first time in ages and stepped inside, time there had stopped.

Was this place always this small?

That was the first thought that came to mind. In his memory, it had seemed much larger than this.

It had been empty for a long time, but because it was the training ground once used by the Young Cult Leader, it had been kept clean and well maintained.

Against one wall stood wooden swords. They were the small swords Geom Mugeuk had used in childhood.

Geom Mugeuk picked up a wooden sword. Seeing the sword worn smooth by his hands brought back memories of training here as a child.

Beside them, wooden training dummies were placed throughout the room, their bodies gouged in many places.

Those marks were the traces of his yearning to become stronger, and the traces of his fear that he would die if he did not.

Ian had often come here, too. She had guarded him from outside the whole time Geom Mugeuk trained.

“It’s really been a long time since I came here.”

“Do you remember it?”

“Of course. You trained here a lot, didn’t you? Ah, back then, you were really…”

Adorable.

She could not bring herself to say it. But the little Geom Mugeuk who had held and swung a wooden sword really had been adorable.

Then Geom Mugeuk suddenly said something she had not expected.

“This is where I first saw you.”

How could she ever forget? The memory of that day was vivid even for her.

Over the figure of the present Geom Mugeuk, the Geom Mugeuk of childhood overlapped.

Into that place, her childhood self was entering together with her master.

—This is the child who will guard the Second Young Master from now on.

Thinking back on it now, she had been raised from that moment to be the Young Cult Leader’s shield. After learning the Full-Body Petrification Art, her fate was to remain always by the Young Cult Leader’s side, then throw herself forward at the final moment. She had been raised for that fate.

“I remember being so frightened. Back then, the Young Cult Leader said this to me.”

This was something even Geom Mugeuk did not remember. He only remembered seeing her that day.

“I’m Mugeuk. I’ll be in your care from now on.”

Before Ian’s eyes stood the young Geom Mugeuk of childhood. The moment he smiled brightly at her was also the moment she resolved to live for the Young Cult Leader.

Geom Mugeuk spoke the words she had said exactly as they were.

“I am Geom Mugeuk. I will be in your care from now on.”

Just as the two of them in childhood had stood facing each other, the two of them now stood in that place and faced each other.

Geom Mugeuk put into words the apology he had never been able to convey to her until now.

“Forgive me for not comforting you when you suffered the side effects of the Full-Body Petrification Art.”

He had not tended to her wounded heart. Even when he saw her come out after crying all night, her eyes swollen, he had not been able to ask if she was all right or comfort her.

Ian shook her head. This was not a matter of forgiveness or not.

“You were young then, too.”

Yes, he, too, had been young then.

“Then accept the apology of the me who has become an adult now.”

“You don’t need to apologize. You were never indifferent or cold to me.”

Dealing with his father and older brother, Geom Mugeuk had had no room to pay attention to anything else. Only Ian, who had watched him from beside him, knew that.

“In your childhood, you were fighting a harder battle than anyone.”

Ian stepped closer and embraced Geom Mugeuk tightly.

“If you have to apologize for that, then I have to apologize, too. Back then, I was too young, and so I couldn’t truly be of help to you. I’m sorry.”

The two conveyed to each other the apologies they had kept deep in their hearts for so long.

Held in his arms, Ian felt Geom Mugeuk’s heart begin to beat faster.

Slowly pulling away from her, Geom Mugeuk spoke the words that would change both his fate and hers.

“Miss I, will you marry me?”

With a thud, Ian’s heart dropped.

Her fingertips trembled, and the shiver that followed spread through her entire body.

Yes, perhaps she had hoped that something like this might happen.

But imagination born from vague expectation and reality were far too different. For the first time, she learned what it felt like when tears and laughter collided at the same time.

After standing there blankly, she let out a long breath as though she had forgotten how to breathe.

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk with trembling eyes.

If this trip had not happened, she would not have been able to accept those words easily. She would first have thought that someone like her did not suit such an extraordinary man.

And so she might have blurted out these words first.

How could I possibly marry the Young Cult Leader?

But Ian in this moment was not Ian, but Miss I. And because of that, she pushed all negative thoughts far away. Though countless words rose in her mind—

“Yes.”

It was permission in a single word, but in that instant, Geom Mugeuk raised both arms high and rejoiced.

He jumped all around the training ground. He was so genuinely happy that the thought came naturally: could he really like it this much?

It was a sight that did not suit Geom Mugeuk.

He had never once shown himself so delighted.

Ian could tell. Geom Mugeuk was expressing his joy with his entire body for her sake.

I’m this happy, so don’t think even a single negative thought.

Ian was happy. Not because she would become the Demon Empress. She was happy because she would become the woman of a man named Geom Mugeuk.

Watching Geom Mugeuk, who had been running around like a child, come stand in front of her again, Ian said bravely,

“Yes. I want to marry you, too.”

***

By the time they arrived at the Heavenly Demon Hall, that bravery had vanished.

“Wait a moment. I’m not ready yet.”

Ian leaned against a pillar because the strength had left her legs.

“Are you really going to tell him today?”

“You know, don’t you? That there are no secrets in our cult.”

If she had not been in this situation, Ian would surely have made this joke.

Weren’t most of those secrets let out by your mouth? You’re the one who made the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult a place with no secrets!

After taking several deep breaths, Ian nodded. Yes, if it was something she had to go through anyway, there was no point putting it off.

“Let’s go.”

They entered the Heavenly Demon Hall. Toward his father, visible far in the distance, Geom Mugeuk shouted louder than ever before.

“Father, I’ve returned.”

The two walked side by side down the path of blood.

Was it because Ian was there? The voice that greeted them was softer than usual.

“Did you return safely?”

“Yes, Father.”

“Yes, thanks to your concern, I have returned safely, Cult Leader.”

Normally, Geom Mugeuk would recount every little thing that had happened upon returning from outside, but he omitted it all for the moment.

“I have something to tell you, Father.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been gazing steadily at his father, spoke the most important words of his life.

“I wish to marry Ian.”

Silence fell for a moment.

Ian felt as though she could hear her own heart beating. She felt her ears burn and her face flush.

Just what would the Cult Leader think of her?

Even if her identity as the bloodline of Binggung was revealed, even if she had become Gwonma’s adopted daughter, to the Cult Leader she would be nothing more than the young cult lord’s bodyguard.

Geom Ujin, who had been looking quietly at Ian, rose from the high seat and began to walk down. With each step he took, it felt like a hammer striking Ian’s heart.

Eventually, Geom Ujin walked over to Ian.

“Ian.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

Answering with a trembling voice, Ian raised her head to look at Geom Ujin.

He was looking at her with gentle eyes. Astonishing words flowed from his lips.

“Would my worthless son be acceptable?”

“!”

Since it was something she had never imagined, a tempest of emotion surged through Ian’s chest.

The Cult Leader said nothing else.

He had simply granted permission with those words.

Ian fought hard to hold back her tears. Now was not the time to show tears, but to show her heart.

“That worthless person looks the best in my eyes.”

A smile formed at Geom Ujin’s lips.

That smile grew stern when directed at his son.

“Go and receive permission from the one who will be your father-in-law.”

“Yes, Father!”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Geom Ujin.

He could not even imagine how difficult his father’s love must have been, meeting his mother who belonged to the orthodox faction and even marrying her.

It was the moment when such a father readily permitted his son’s love.

Geom Mugeuk bowed his head politely to his father.

“Thank you, Father.”

Ian also bowed her head politely alongside him.

“Thank you, Cult Leader.”

Because of Geom Ujin’s deep love, she would never forget this moment for the rest of her life.

To his son, who was walking away into the distance with Ian, Geom Ujin spoke.

“That person might not give permission.”

“That would be troublesome. If the strongest fist in Murim opposes it.”

Geom Mugeuk added with a smile.

“Even if I take a beating from that fist, I will be sure to obtain permission and return.”

As the two left Cheonma Hall, Hwi’s voice was heard from thin air.

“Congratulations, Cult Leader.”

“Thank you.”

Geom Ujin had experienced many things in his life that he had never imagined, but he had not known a day like this would come.

“I truly never imagined I would become in-laws with that person.”

Geom Ujin briefly recalled the moment he first met Gwonma. Those young days felt as if they were only yesterday.

“Gwonma will come by later, so prepare the liquor that person likes.”

Chapter 886: Because You’re the One Who’ll Become My In-Law
-----------------------------------------------------------
“Please give me your daughter!”

A firm refusal came in response to Geom Mugeuk’s words.

“No!”

As though he had already expected it, Geom Mugeuk calmly asked,

“Why do you refuse? Please tell me the reason.”

“Because you will surely make my daughter suffer.”

“I will never make her suffer.”

“I can’t believe you. You’ll obviously spend your time going out and playing with the Young Leaders of the Martial Alliance and the Evil Alliance, and with the Demon Venerables as well. You’ll leave my daughter lonely every day.”

That problem was not difficult.

“Whenever I go out to have fun, I’ll always take her with me. You saw us traveling together this time. I like spending time with your daughter.”

“Still, I refuse!”

“Why do you refuse this time?”

The reason for this refusal had nothing to do with Geom Mugeuk himself.

“I refuse because of your father.”

“Because of my father?”

“How difficult would it be to have the Heavenly Demon as your father-in-law? I don’t want my daughter to walk such a difficult path.”

“My father truly cherishes and likes your daughter. He gave his permission at once! He didn’t oppose us like this!

And besides, isn’t having the Fist Demon as a father-in-law also difficult?”

“So? Are you complaining that your father-in-law is being fussy and asking too many questions?”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been momentarily flustered, waved his hands.

“No! It’s not like that.”

“She’s only an adopted daughter, so why are you being so stingy? Is that what you’re saying?”

“When did I ever say that?”

“No! I will never give my daughter to an insolent brat like you! Not until the dirt is in my eyes!”

Unable to hold back any longer, Geom Mugeuk shouted,

“Let’s settle this like men with our fist techniques! If I win, give us your permission. If I lose, I’ll give up!”

At that moment, he heard someone click their tongue from in front of him.

“You’re really going to do that? You fail, you bastard!”

The person standing before Geom Mugeuk was not the Fist Demon, but the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Before going to see the Fist Demon, he had stopped by the Blood Heaven Blade Demon to practice how he should obtain his permission.

Ian had said that she wanted to meet her father first, so he had sent her ahead and come to meet the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

The mock battle had ended in failure.

The Fist Demon had opposed him so realistically. He had been so infuriatingly inflexible.

“You idiot, you have to endure whatever he says.”

“He says he’ll oppose us even if he dies. How am I supposed to endure that?”

“And if you don’t?”

“I’ll elope with Ian in the middle of the night.”

“Your fist-fighting master will search for you all the way to the end of hell.”

Geom Mugeuk let out a long sigh and said,

“How can I obtain his permission?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon sighed along with him.

“You idiot! How would I know if you ask someone like me who doesn’t even have children?”

“But you’ve read so many books.”

“How can someone know life merely because they have a large study? Even if you read only one book, it becomes wisdom only when you live according to what you’ve read.”

“You’re the smartest person around me! Give me an answer already!”

Then someone else spoke from beside them.

“Young Cult Master, I think it’s important to understand the Fist Demon Venerable’s feelings first.”

It was the Sunflower Sword Venerable, who had been tending to the flowers and trees in the garden beside them. When Geom Mugeuk had come to find the Blood Heaven Blade Demon,

she had been cultivating the garden here together with him.

“Master, he must be very upset, right?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the Sunflower Sword Venerable nodded.

“Wouldn’t he be? He hasn’t had her as his daughter for very long, and now some black-hearted thief has come to steal her away. What would you do if you were in his place?”

As though the mere thought were horrifying, Geom Mugeuk clenched his fist.

“If it were me, I’d beat him half to death. No, I’d really kill him!”

“The Fist Demon Venerable’s feelings are no less intense.”

Geom Mugeuk thought of the Fist Demon. One only had to look at his father to know what sort of existence a person became once that steel-hearted man gave them his heart.

“What should I do? Master’s fists have grown even stronger, so I might die if he hits me.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon casually cut in.

“Send word to the physician beforehand. Tell him to prepare for when you fall into a coma.”

“This isn’t the time for jokes!”

“You idiot, do you really think he’ll kill you? He’ll beat you a few times and then give his permission. There’s no parent who can defeat their own child.”

“I’ll have to unleash the Heavenly Demon Guardian Technique to its limits for the first time in a while if I want to survive.”

Then the Sunflower Sword Venerable asked casually,

“But why did you decide to marry?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon also looked at Geom Mugeuk with curiosity.

Geom Mugeuk answered honestly. There were no words he could not say to the two of them.

“First of all, because I like Ian.”

There was another decisive reason that had made him want to experience marriage itself.

“I want to become a father like my own father.”

The two Demon Venerables had watched the relationship between Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin change over time, so they understood perfectly what those words meant.

The Sunflower Sword Venerable imagined the child born to Geom Mugeuk and Ian.

“If it’s your child, they’ll surely be born handsome and beautiful.”

And that was not all. The child’s paternal grandfather would be the Heavenly Demon, and the maternal grandfather the Fist Demon. Their mother would be the greatest beauty under heaven, their maternal family the Northern Sea Ice Palace—and most importantly, their father would be Geom Mugeuk.

And above all, what would be waiting for that child?

“There isn’t a single Demon Venerable with children, so the child will grow up receiving the full love of the Demon Venerables.”

***

Ian went to her father first and told him that Geom Mugeuk had proposed to her.

Despite the astonishing news, the Fist Demon was not particularly surprised.

*Had he expected this?*

When Geom Mugeuk had first teased her after she became the Fist Demon’s adopted daughter, he had said that she would never be able to marry because of that terrifying father-in-law. At the time, the Fist Demon had said,

*Then you should marry him.*

That joke had become reality and now lay before them.

After hearing that she had received a marriage proposal, the first thing the Fist Demon asked was this:

“What do you think?”

Ian hesitated for a moment before answering. She was not at ease telling her father the news. She could not know what he was feeling now.

“I’m happy.”

So Ian revealed not even a quarter of her joy. Perhaps that was why—

“There is only one thing I’m worried about.”

After a brief pause, the Fist Demon continued,

“Did you give your permission because he is the Young Cult Master, and you found it difficult to refuse him? Is your true heart elsewhere?”

Ian knew exactly what her father was worried about.

“No, Father.”

She honestly conveyed her feelings.

“I have liked him for a long time as well.”

“Then that settles it.”

“Are you giving us your permission?”

The Fist Demon nodded.

“If you’re happy, then I’m happy too.”

Through Ian, the Fist Demon was experiencing something new. He had not raised a daughter from childhood, but he had become a parent. Now he was experiencing what it was like to gain a son-in-law as well.

“I know I’m clumsy as a father.”

The Fist Demon conveyed feelings he normally never expressed to her.

“I don’t really know what I should say to you at a time like this. But there is one thing I can tell you with certainty.”

The Fist Demon looked at Ian and said,

“No matter what anyone says, you are my daughter.”

Even though she was not his biological daughter, even though he had not raised her from childhood, the Fist Demon regarded Ian as his own daughter.

“If he makes you unhappy, I won’t forgive him even if he is the Young Cult Master. So if anything troubles you, tell your father at any time. Don’t forget that I am behind you.”

For the sake of his daughter, the Fist Demon was a man who could fight the entire martial world alone.

Ian ran into her father’s arms.

She had held back when Geom Mugeuk had asked her to marry him, and she had held back when Geom Ujin had given his permission.

But at this moment, Ian could not hold back her tears. They streamed down from her eyes.

“Yes, I’ll be sure to tell you, Father.”

The Fist Demon patted his daughter’s back with his large hand.

“My daughter, never give up on your happiness, no matter what happens.”

***

Geom Mugeuk went to the Eastern Fist Sect.

Perhaps it was only his imagination, but the place was empty, and it felt heavier and more ominous than usual.

As though they had been waiting for Geom Mugeuk to arrive, all the Iron Fists of the Eastern Fist Sect had gathered in the training ground.

Even though they had seen the Young Cult Master, they did not greet him. Their fixed gazes were clearly hostile.

The Fist Demon stood among them with his arms crossed. His terrifying face was even more rigid than usual today. Ian stood a little way off with her head lowered.

Geom Mugeuk could already predict what was about to happen.

―I can’t simply hand over my daughter. If you defeat me with your fists, I’ll give you permission to marry her.

Could he really defeat his master with fist techniques? An enormous thunderclap unlike anything ever heard before would surely erupt here today.

Geom Mugeuk spoke loudly.

“I wish to marry your daughter.”

His voice rang through the training ground.

Then the Fist Demon’s stiff expression brightened.

“My son-in-law has arrived?”

Just as Geom Mugeuk stared in surprise—

Bang! Boom! Bang!

Firecrackers exploded behind the building.

Only then did the Iron Fists standing all around relax their expressions and shout in unison,

“Congratulations, Young Cult Master!”

They raised a joyous cheer.

“Waaaaaaah!”

The Iron Fists rushed forward at once and offered Geom Mugeuk their congratulations. They had once been fellow disciples, so they were familiar with one another and knew each other’s names.

“Congratulations.”

“Congratulations.”

Among them was Cheon Sohee, the future Fist Demon and his junior apprentice sister.

“Senior Brother, congratulations.”

“What is going on?”

Geom Mugeuk asked in surprise. Cheon Sohee was likewise astonished by the news of the marriage.

“That’s what I should be asking! What is going on, Senior Brother?”

Geom Mugeuk looked past the Iron Fists toward the distant Fist Demon and Ian, then answered with a joking tone,

“This is all thanks to the midnight training sessions, isn’t it?”

Cheon Sohee smiled brightly in agreement. At the very least, it was undoubtedly thanks to those gatherings that she had become the Fist Demon’s successor.

After receiving the Iron Fists’ congratulations, Geom Mugeuk walked over to the Fist Demon.

“Master.”

The Fist Demon strode forward and patted Geom Mugeuk on the shoulder with his large hand.

“You’ve made a great decision.”

“Are you giving us your permission?”

“Yes, I give you my permission.”

The Fist Demon was giving his permission so readily? Geom Mugeuk had even come prepared after practicing for his opposition.

“My son-in-law, you’ve lived freely until now, but your days of hardship are clearly ahead of you.”

*My son-in-law?* That was a phrase completely unlike the Fist Demon. Why was he acting like this? Was he planning to launch a surprise attack? Was this a trap?

“Shouldn’t you be opposing us, Master?”

The Fist Demon tilted his head and said,

“Why would I, when this makes me in-laws with the Cult Leader? What could be more glorious than this?”

He was not wrong, but still—

“Shouldn’t you at least say something like, ‘If you mistreat Ian, I’ll punish you’?”

Once again, the Fist Demon opened his eyes wide.

“You intend to mistreat her?”

“No, it’s not like that.”

“If you mistreat her, do you think the Cult Leader will simply stand by? It won’t even reach my turn. Don’t you know the Cult Leader’s personality?”

There was one more thing the Fist Demon trusted.

“It might not even reach the Cult Leader’s turn.”

“What do you mean?”

“Even if that child seems kind and gentle, she isn’t someone who can be pushed around easily. You know that, don’t you?”

“No, I only know that she’s kind and gentle.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Ian together with the Fist Demon. Ian smiled and said,

“I’m a soft and easygoing person.”

At her words, Geom Mugeuk flinched and looked at the Fist Demon.

“Could there be a side of her that she shows only at home, one that I don’t know about?”

Was there still a transformation left—from Miss Ian to Madam Ian?

“I don’t know why you willingly stepped onto this difficult path, but please take good care of my daughter.”

“You’re making me want to withdraw somehow.”

Only then did the Fist Demon raise his large fist before Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“Too late.”

The Fist Demon and Geom Mugeuk looked at each other and laughed. Geom Mugeuk was truly grateful that the Fist Demon had given his permission so readily and so cheerfully.

The Fist Demon did not bother telling him to treat her well. Nor did he make Geom Mugeuk promise to spend the rest of his life living for Ian.

What use would a promise to treat her well be? If someone truly intended to treat her well, he would do so without making a promise. And if he did not, he would fail to do so even if the Fist Demon beat him to death with those fists.

In the Fist Demon’s eyes, Geom Mugeuk was the kind of man who would treat her better than anyone.

If so, the wise choice for his daughter would be to welcome them as warmly as possible and rejoice for her.

Ian said to Gwonma,

“I’ll do better from now on.”

“I’m fine, so be good to your husband.”

Gwonma gazed at the two of them standing side by side.

“I know that the two of you are far smarter and wiser than I am. Don’t forget how you feel now, and live well.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. How could Gwonma not feel uneasy, no matter how cheerfully he sent them off? How could he not worry?

How could he not feel regret? There must still have been so many things he wanted to do with his daughter.

“Please don’t think of it as sending Ian away. Just as I gained another father, you have gained another son, Master.”

A bright smile spread across Gwonma’s face. That fearsome face was filled with joy today.

“Our daughter has brought home the finest son-in-law!”

At Gwonma’s thunderous shout, the Iron Fists cheered. Their cheers rang out to the farthest reaches of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

***

That evening, Gwonma visited Geom Ujin’s quarters.

As though he had known Gwonma would come, Geom Ujin had prepared a drinking table and was waiting for him.

“Have a drink from me first.”

Geom Ujin poured Gwonma a cup of liquor.

“Please take good care of my reckless son.”

After accepting the drink, Gwonma filled Geom Ujin’s cup.

“She’s a lacking daughter, but please take good care of her.”

The two of them toasted and drained their cups.

“I gave my permission because the children are happy, but I still wonder if this is truly all right. For someone like me to become related by marriage to the Cult Leader…”

He was, in every sense, the very embodiment of loyalty to Geom Ujin.

Geom Ujin looked at Gwonma and smiled warmly.

“I’m happy that the man who will become my in-law is you.”

Gwonma was deeply moved because he could tell that Geom Ujin was speaking sincerely, not merely saying it out of courtesy.

“I’m very happy that it’s you, Cult Leader.”

Gwonma honestly conveyed his feelings.

“I’m sorry to say this, but if the man who was to become my son-in-law hadn’t been Mugeuk, I might have opposed this marriage.”

Geom Ujin could understand how Gwonma felt. Could the position of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s heir’s wife possibly involve only good things?

She could be swept up in the Cult’s internal politics and caught in all manner of schemes and dangers.

But because of everything Geom Mugeuk had shown them until now, Geom Ujin could give his permission with a happy heart.

“Don’t you remember those days?”

At Gwonma’s question, Geom Ujin smiled.

“After going through that warlike ordeal, you had such a difficult time holding your wedding ceremony. And yet you’re giving your permission so easily?”

Geom Ujin laughed at Gwonma’s joke as he replied,

“I might enjoy teasing my son, but how could I make your daughter unhappy?”

“Thank you for approving this marriage.”

“Of course I should. She’s your daughter.”

Geom Ujin made no attempt to hide his affection and trust for Gwonma.

“Here, my in-law. Have another drink from me.”

“Thank you.”

“You should call me your in-law too.”

“Thank you… my in-law.”

The two men burst into laughter. It had truly been a long time since they had laughed so heartily together. It was laughter born of longing for those days when they had freely expressed every emotion—tears, laughter, anger, and joy.

Geom Ujin drained his cup and said,

“I intend to hold this wedding on a grand scale.”

Chapter 887: Avoided the Fate of That Cult Leader
-------------------------------------------------
When Geom Muyang returned to his residence, someone was waiting in the courtyard.

Sitting astride a rock and looking up at the moon was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

“Are you doing all the main sect duties by yourself? Why are you so late?”

“What brings you here at this hour?”

“I came because I missed you, Hyung. And there’s something I wanted to talk about.”

Geom Muyang knew what his younger brother was going to say.

“You’re getting married?”

“Huh? How did you know?”

“Who doesn’t know?”

“This main sect truly has no secrets whatsoever.”

News of Geom Mugeuk’s marriage was already the hot topic within the sect.

Geom Muyang walked over and sat down beside Geom Mugeuk. Sitting down next to him without being invited—something natural for brothers in other households—was deeply touching to Geom Mugeuk.

It meant that he and his brother had now become close enough to act so freely with each other.

“Sorry for cutting in line.”

“It’s fine. I have no intention of getting married anyway.”

“Neither did I.”

Geom Muyang looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“The next thing I knew, I was saying, ‘Please give me your daughter’s hand in marriage.’”

Geom Muyang smiled at his brother’s joke.

“You’ll do well in married life too.”

“You think so?”

“You’ll become a good husband and a good father.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze, which had been directed at the night sky, turned toward Geom Muyang.

“I think you, Hyung, would make a truly great father. Not so sure about a good husband, though.”

Geom Muyang looked at him with an expression asking why he thought that.

“You take after Father completely, don’t you?”

Did he really resemble their father? Mabul had always told him that he, not Geom Mugeuk, took after the Cult Leader. Come to think of it, the Martial Alliance Leader, Jin Pacheon, had said something similar too.

“Do you really think I take after Father?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“If you grow old just as you are now, I think you’ll become exactly like Father.”

“Father would hate hearing that.”

“He’d love it? I’m telling you, our father’s love for his firstborn—no one can stop it.”

A faint smile formed on Geom Muyang’s lips.

“Hyung, you should get married too so we can shower him with grandchildren. If you’re holding grandchildren in both arms, you can’t draw your sword, right?”

“Then you’d probably fight using the Igigeo Sword Technique while holding the grandchildren.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk laughed at Geom Muyang’s joke. It felt good to talk with his brother like this after so long.

Now, even sitting together in silence wasn’t awkward, so they spent a while moon-gazing together.

“You must be tired, so rest. I’ll be going.”

As Geom Mugeuk tried to leave, Geom Muyang called out from behind.

“Geuka.”

It was the first time he’d called him “Geuka” since they were little children.

“Congratulations.”

Geom Mugeuk turned his head. Under the moonlight, his brother looked more dependable than ever.

“Thank you, Hyung.”

***

When Geom Mugeuk went to visit him, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was packing his bags.

“Where are you going?”

When Geom Mugeuk asked in surprise, the Pungcheon Cult Leader gave an unexpected answer.

“I’m thinking of taking a trip on my own. Don’t worry. I’ll be back before your wedding ceremony.”

“By yourself?”

“Of course, by myself.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s lingering resentment burst forth.

“Wasn’t it you who said we should take a trip together later, you, me, and Gowol?”

“I remember.”

“Gowol is too busy to go, and you have a wedding to prepare now so you can’t go. What can I do? I have to go alone.”

He was actually thinking of visiting the Hero’s Guesthouse. Since he was the one who came up with the name, he ought to visit.

“So, what brings you here?”

“I came to ask a favor.”

Words truly unexpected came from Geom Mugeuk.

“Please officiate my wedding.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was startled. He was so surprised he thought he had misheard.

“Me?”

“Yes, please do it, Elder.”

“Aren’t there many Demonic Venerables close to you besides me?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader had expected that if Geom Mugeuk asked anyone to officiate, it would be Hyeolcheon Doma.

“Don’t tell me you asked Hyeolcheon Doma and got rejected.”

“No. I haven’t asked Doma Elder. You are the first person I’m asking, Cult Leader.”

The fact that he had come to him first filled the Pungcheon Cult Leader with emotion yet also bewilderment.

“Why me?”

“Is there a reason you can’t? Didn’t you tell me to give Father grandchildren and stop him from unifying the martial world?”

Yes, he had certainly said those words to Geom Mugeuk.

“I’m doing exactly as you ordered, Cult Leader. I’m getting married because of you. If I regret it later, you must take responsibility.”

“What nonsense is that! And what business does someone who has never even been married have officiating a wedding?”

“We martial artists aren’t bound by ordinary laws and customs. Didn’t Elder Doma officiate the wedding of the Hwangcheon Pavilion Master some time ago?”

“That’s because they were master and disciple.”

“Aren’t you also my teacher?”

They had agreed to treat each other as master and disciple within the Space-Time Reversal Art. But he had thought that was merely a joke.

“Thanks to the Space-Time Reversal Art and the Heaven-Soaring Secret Art, I was able to kill Cheonsal Seong. Not only that, but I also overcame various other crises.”

Geom Mugeuk expressed his gratitude to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“Just like Lady Gwonma, you are also my master, Cult Leader.”

Even though it wasn’t under the condition of “only within the Space-Time Reversal Art!” hearing the word “master” moved the Pungcheon Cult Leader deeply.

As if to hide his emotion, the Pungcheon Cult Leader put on a suspicious look for no reason.

“Tell me honestly. What are you scheming?”

“You threw everything away and came to me, Cult Leader.”

“That was because of Gowol, not you.”

“You also gave me precious divine artifacts without hesitation.”

“That was also because Gowol told me to.”

“You fought Cheonsal Seong for the sake of our main sect.”

“Because if that place fell, Gowol would die.”

Geom Mugeuk said with a smile.

“This is why. You are a wonderful person who does all these things for me that others would find hard-pressed to do even one of, and yet you never boast about it.”

“!”

An even greater emotion swept across the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s face. Hearing such words from Geom Mugeuk felt better than from anyone else.

“Just as you gave me my name, please also be the one to start my new beginning, Cult Leader. I respect you.”

How could he refuse when “respect” had even come out?

“Very well, I shall officiate.”

“Thank you.”

Geom Mugeuk bowed his head in gratitude.

Right after, the Pungcheon Cult Leader rushed straight to Eunwol.

He called Gowol out and began boasting.

“The Young Cult Leader asked me to officiate his wedding. He said he respects me!”

Watching the Pungcheon Cult Leader who looked like he could fly from joy, Gowol smiled.

“Are you that happy?”

“Of course. It’s not just anyone’s wedding—it’s the Young Cult Leader’s wedding. I’m sure both the orthodox and unorthodox factions will attend; it’ll be a wedding that the entire martial world attends. And I am to officiate that wedding.”

Gowol knew the real reason he was happy.

“Isn’t it because the Pungcheon Cult Leader from outside is coming?”

“!”

Surely his disciple and the current era’s Pungcheon Cult Leader, So Baekta, would also be invited.

“Don’t you want to show him?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader couldn’t deny it. How wounded his pride would be to see the master who had abandoned everything sitting among the crowd watching the wedding without any presence? Both his own and his disciple’s pride would be hurt.

“Yes, you brat! I want to show him I’m not dead yet.”

Gowol said to him with a smile.

“My Cult Leader.”

“What is it?”

“At this point, you’ve succeeded.”

“In what?”

“I may not have become the Young Cult Leader’s right-hand man, but at least I avoided that wretched fate of being the Cult Leader back then.”

“Same for you. Since the organization you built has entered the main hall of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult so openly, you’ve escaped those shackles from back then. We’ve succeeded at this level!”

The two looked at each other and laughed loudly in good spirits.

“This isn’t the time for this. What should I wear? Have I gained some weight lately? Should I have new ceremonial robes fitted from scratch?”

***

Finally, the day before the wedding ceremony arrived.

With guests coming from all across the Central Plains, Magachon was packed with people. All the inns were full, and the Divine Cult provided temporary lodgings for those who couldn’t find a place to stay in time.

To ensure no unpleasant incidents occurred, Magun personally stepped in to manage everything. Thanks to that, everyone controlled their anger well, and not a single unpleasant incident took place.

“There are so many people, I wonder if we’ll be able to see properly.”

The one who spoke worriedly was Yang In, wife of Jo Chunbae.

“We have to go even if we watch from afar. Whose wedding is it that we wouldn’t attend?”

The two had even bought new clothes to attend tomorrow’s wedding.

Then, one of the guests who had been listening spoke up abruptly.

“You people are attending too?”

As it was time to close the tavern, only the last few guests remained there. So they had spoken carelessly, but those words were heard.

The two at the table were guests who had come from afar: Hyeong Soyin, the sect leader of the Iron Claw Gate, which followed the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, and his son, Hyeong Dae.

The one who had just spoken was the son, Hyeong Dae.

“The tavern keeper? How? Is that even possible?”

Jo Chunbae explained politely.

“We were told that the residents of Magachon may freely attend tomorrow’s wedding.”

“By whom?”

“The martial artists of Hwangcheon Pavilion came and informed us personally.”

Hyeong Dae’s face stiffened.

“So what? They told you to go, so you’re going?”

He had an expression of utter incomprehension. At that moment, his father, Hyeong Soyin, who was with him, stopped his son.

“Dae, stop it.”

“No, isn’t that right? Does the tavern keeper even know what kind of occasion that is?”

Though he had stopped his son, inwardly Hyeong Soyin thought similarly. If it was a wedding where even a tavern keeper could attend so freely,

their status as invited guests felt diminished. They had boasted so much to those around them about being invited.

This was exactly what the residents of Magachon had worried about.

Not only were there too many people to see properly, but they were also afraid of getting into a quarrel with martial artists for no reason if they went.

A quarrel had broken out even before they went.

Jo Chunbae bowed his head. At times like this, he shouldn’t ramble on needlessly. He bowed his head as if admitting fault, but Hyeong Dae persistently demanded an answer.

“So are you going or not?”

If he said they weren’t going and then ran into these people there, it would be a disaster, so Jo Chunbae was at a loss, unsure what to do. Just then, someone spoke from behind.

“The tavern keeper will be attending.”

The one who entered was Geom Mugeuk.

“Young Cult Leader!”

At the words “Young Cult Leader,” the two from the Iron Claw Gate sprang up from their seats.

Jo Chunbae was startled because he hadn’t expected Geom Mugeuk to come here on the eve of the wedding.

“What brings you here, sir?”

“I came to see our tavern keeper’s face. And I have something to give.”

“But you must be busy preparing for the wedding.”

“What would I be busy with? It’s the people preparing who are busy and struggling. Ah, the bride couldn’t come today because she’s busy. She asked me to convey her apologies for not being able to come together.”

“Oh my, why such words.”

Geom Mugeuk also greeted Jo Chunbae’s wife.

“Have you been well too, Madam?”

“We greet the Young Cult Leader.”

In Yang In’s eyes as she looked at Geom Mugeuk were deep emotion and gratitude. He was the Young Cult Leader who had saved her husband and family. And in her husband’s life, the Young Cult Leader was his greatest pride.

After exchanging greetings, Geom Mugeuk looked at Hyeong Soyin and Hyeong Dae. They were holding their breath, heads bowed.

They had made a mistake toward someone the Young Cult Leader knew at the Young Cult Leader’s wedding?

Even at a glance, it was clearly no ordinary relationship. If they had misspoken, they had picked the absolute worst target.

*They’ll use us as blood offerings for tomorrow’s wedding!*

The moment such words were uttered, it would mean death. Furthermore, their entire sect could be annihilated.

“I like the people of Magachon. Especially this tavern keeper here—I like him the most.”

Hyeong Dae said in a trembling voice.

“I apologize. I failed to recognize a noble person and made a mistake.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke to him with a stern gaze. He could have spoken gently, but for Hyeong Dae’s sake, he revealed a cold demonic aura as he spoke.

“This martial world is a place where a single mistake can cost you your life. Today’s mistake isn’t to that degree, but on an unlucky day, you can lose your life over a mistake even more trivial than this.

When your reckless tongue itches to run off someday, be sure to recall this very moment.”

“I will engrave it in my mind.”

Hyeong Soyin politely bowed his head in gratitude.

“Thank you for showing mercy.”

Geom Mugeuk sent a telepathic message to him.

*You should have stopped him earlier and more forcefully.*

—I am sorry. It was my oversight.

—If you raise him like this, you will lose your precious child one day. Let today’s incident be a lesson and educate him properly.

—I will bear it in mind, Young Cult Leader.

Hyeongdae apologized to Jo Chunbae.

“Proprietor, please forgive my rudeness.”

“No, no. I am quite all right.”

After the two of them bowed respectfully to Geom Mugeuk, they left.

Jo Chunbae and Yangin looked at each other. This was why they respected the Young Cult Leader. He was someone considerate enough to settle matters amicably so that no harm would come to them.

As if nothing had happened, Geom Mugeuk looked around the interior of Pungnyu Tavern.

The newly built interior was certainly wider and cleaner.

“You made the inside just as it used to be.”

From the location of the kitchen to the placement of the tables, it had been arranged to revive its former appearance as much as possible.

“I did that because it’s more comfortable for me. Most of the people who come to my place are regulars, too.”

There was something else that had remained the same.

“There is a seat for you on the third floor, Young Cult Leader.”

Originally, the Young Cult Leader’s seat had been on the second floor. The seat offered when the Cult Leader came, or when the Demon Venerables came—now that seat had been moved to the third floor.

Geom Mugeuk went up to the third floor. Jo Chunbae followed after him. Thinking the Young Cult Leader might perhaps have a drink there, Yangin gathered some liquor and simple side dishes and followed them up.

On the third floor, there were only two tables prepared: one small seat and one large seat.

“We do not accept guests here at all.”

“You needn’t go this far. Proprietor, you have a business to run.”

Jo Chunbae smiled faintly and said,

“To tell the truth, I no longer have the strength to run business all the way up to the third floor. Even if I hire more people, I would still have to pay more attention to it. So I made it a place entirely for you, Young Cult Leader.”

Now that he looked, Jo Chunbae had far more white hair than when they had first met.

“Do you not wish to retire now and rest comfortably?”

“There’s something merchants say among themselves. If someone who has worked all his life suddenly rests, that’s when he starts getting sick, and then he dies.

For me, working is resting. Truth be told, I don’t even know how to rest. Ah, but I will rest fully for one day tomorrow. Once the ceremony is over, I’ll go on an outing with my wife as well.”

Geom Mugeuk took out a red envelope from his robes and handed it to him.

“Here, take this. This is what I came to give you.”

When Jo Chunbae checked what was inside, he was startled. It was an official wedding invitation to Geom Mugeuk’s wedding.

“If you show this to the martial artists at the entrance, they will guide you to a seat near the front where you can see the ceremony well. I’ve told them in advance, so our cult’s martial artists will look after you and your wife properly for the day tomorrow.”

“Oh, my! This is something lowly people like us cannot possibly bear.”

Jo Chunbae and Yangin were so flustered they did not know what to do.

“Lowly? Proprietor, do you know how precious you are to me?”

Jo Chunbae looked at Geom Mugeuk with eyes full of emotion. Just what merit had he accumulated in his previous life to receive such fortune?

“Do you know this? Thanks to you, Young Cult Leader, the twilight years of my life are shining the brightest.”

With the liquor Yangin had brought, Geom Mugeuk filled three cups to the brim.

“Now, since I’ll be busy tomorrow and won’t be able to greet you two properly.”

Geom Mugeuk raised his cup and said,

“Let us drink a congratulatory cup in advance.”

Chapter 888: Who Is the Front Row For?
--------------------------------------
A single carriage was racing swiftly along.

The two people inside were Yong Jamyeong, leader of the Eunha Merchant Guild, and Yong Jinhu (龍珍厚), the eldest of his three sons.

“Are we truly going to the wedding of the Demon Cult’s Young Cult Leader?”

“We are.”

No matter how many times he asked, the answer was the same. At first, he had thought his father was teasing him.

But that did not seem to be the case. The decisive proof was that the only guard was Baek Chong, seated on the driver’s bench. Baek Chong was one of the key figures who had built the Eunha Merchant Guild alongside his father, and the warrior his father trusted most.

This was the first time Baek Chong alone had accompanied his father on an outing.

“Don’t tell me our merchant guild has joined hands with the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?”

Yong Jinhu was afraid his father would say yes. Joining hands with the Demon Cult—that was something he did not even want to imagine.

Fortunately, his father shook his head.

“No. This visit is strictly a personal one on my part. I’m bringing you along separately from the guild, as my son. I wanted to introduce you to the Young Cult Leader.”

Yong Jamyeong told his son of a past secret he had never once mentioned before.

“When I was young, I once fell into a trap set by a colleague in the merchant world and nearly lost my life. At that time, a warrior who did not reveal his name saved me. I recently learned that he was the current Demon Cult Leader.”

Yong Jinhu was startled. The Demon Cult Leader was the benefactor who had saved his father’s life?

“Even so, if Father attends the Young Cult Leader’s wedding, word of it will surely spread through the murim.”

“I suppose it will.”

“If this becomes known to the world, many sects and merchant guilds that trade with us will sever ties.”

There was no way his father did not know what the consequences would be.

“Perhaps you intend to attend while wearing a bamboo hat and concealing your identity?”

After all, it was common to attend murim events without revealing one’s identity.

But Yong Jamyeong shook his head.

“I intend to attend without hiding who I am.”

Yong Jinhu found it strange. Leaving aside the fact that the other party was his life-saving benefactor, his father was, no matter what anyone said, a merchant. And that father was willing to shoulder such a great loss? Why on earth?

“Even if you hide your identity, they will understand. What guest could be more honored than you, Father?”

Yong Jinhu took pride in the fact that his father was the greatest merchant guild leader under heaven.

“There will be people more honored than I.”

“How could there be such people?”

Yong Jamyeong thought of Geom Ujin. Then he thought of his son, Geom Mugeuk. Both of them were far too extraordinary.

“To those people, there is no way I would be the greatest guest.”

Having grown up watching his father receive only the treatment reserved for the most honored of guests, Yong Jinhu could not accept those words.

“I hope attending this wedding will become an opportunity to broaden your narrow view.”

All three of his sons lacked aptitude for affairs of the merchant world.

If one had no talent for earning money, then at least one ought to have an eye for people. The reason he had brought along his eldest son, who still showed the most promise of the three, was because he hoped this would be a good experience for him.

Before long, the carriage arrived at its destination.

Yong Jinhu took a deep breath and stepped down from the carriage.

The next moment, he was startled by the scene unfolding before his eyes.

They were near the main gate of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Along the Cult’s massive wall stood countless carriages. Splendid large carriages.

This was the first time Yong Jinhu had ever seen so many carriages in one place.

People alighting from carriages, people just arriving, people conversing with acquaintances—there truly were countless people there.

All around, large-framed warriors stood here and there. Their aura was terrifying, and even he, with his shallow martial skill, could tell they were no ordinary warriors.

Baek Chong said in a low voice.

“Do not stare at them. They are the Divine Cult’s Demon Army.”

Yong Jinhu had also heard of the Demon Army. They were the very Demon Army said to be unstoppable if they charged in with those massive bodies.

Baek Chong, rich in experience, possessed much information and insight regarding the murim, and in preparation for this visit to the Divine Cult in particular, he had studied everything he could about the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Just then, someone recognized Yong Jamyeong and came over to greet him.

“Guild Leader Yong!”

“Manor Lord Geum.”

He was none other than Geum Asu, the Lord of Golden Manor, the wealthiest man in Fujian and a great merchant counted among the top ten in the Central Plains.

In the past, Geom Mugeuk had saved his daughter and grandson, and in return received a bundle of treasures larger than himself. That connection had continued until now, and they too had been invited to this wedding.

The two men knew each other well, and Geum Asu was Yong Jamyeong’s senior in the merchant world.

“This is my daughter and my grandson.”

Geum Asu’s daughter, Geum Sayeon, and his grandson, Yangi, had come with him. Yangi, who had been a little child when Geom Mugeuk saved him, had grown quite a lot before anyone knew it.

“How did you come to be here?”

Geum Asu was surprised by Yong Jamyeong’s attendance. The Eunha Merchant Guild, the greatest merchant guild under heaven, dealt with orthodox, unorthodox, and demonic factions alike. Attending a wedding of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult must surely have been burdensome.

Yong Jamyeong spoke honestly to Geum Asu.

“In my youth, I received the grace of having my life saved by the Demon Cult Leader.”

“What an extraordinary coincidence.”

“What do you mean?”

“We too received the grace of having our lives saved by the Young Cult Leader.”

“Ah, I see.”

Yong Jinhu, listening from the side, could not understand it. They had each received the grace of having their lives saved by the most frightening Demon Cult Leader and Young Cult Leader in the world?

Yong Jinhu looked around. Not only Geum Asu, but many other recognizable figures were present.

Though not quite on his father’s level, they were still major figures of the merchant world, the sort who made one think, That person came here?

Of course, there were more warriors than merchants. They were leaders who contended for regional supremacy.

‘The Demon Cult really is impressive, to make all these people come.’

Just then, a member of the Demon Army walked over.

Yong Jinhu thought he would escort his father first.

But the Demon Army soldier delivered words Yong Jinhu had not expected at all, then turned and left.

“Those who have arrived, please enter at once.”

The people who had been conversing began moving toward the main gate. Naturally, a line formed heading that way.

‘They’re making Father walk together with these people?’

Yong Jinhu found the sight of his father standing in line to enter somewhere far too unfamiliar.

Wherever they went, his father had always been the central figure, but now he was merely one among countless guests.

‘They’re treating Father like this?’

Yong Jinhu said quietly.

“The Demon Cult must be deliberately trying to dampen the Eunha Merchant Guild’s spirit.”

Yong Jamyeong said to his son.

“That is because you have yet to meet the Cult Leader and the Young Cult Leader.”

“What sort of people are they?”

“If they intended to intimidate someone, they would not be people who would use a method as paltry as this.”

Among those standing in line were Jo Chunbae and his wife, Yang In. Dressed in newly bought clothes, the two were terribly excited and happy, but

among people of such tremendous status, they moved along quietly without saying a word.

They had been worrying that if they caught someone’s eye, something like yesterday might happen again.

Then someone strode toward them.

“Welcome, master.”

The person who approached them was none other than the Demon Army Lord, Jang Ho.

Jo Chunbae and Yang In bowed their heads in greeting.

“Demon Army Lord!”

At the words “Demon Army Lord,” the people around them held their breath. The leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s elite Demon Army, whom they had only heard of in rumors, was now standing right before their eyes.

Warriors of the sects that followed the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult respectfully cupped their fists and conveyed the reverence they had long held for him.

After Jang Ho returned their greetings with a cupped-fist salute, he spoke again to Jo Chunbae and Yang In.

“The Young Cult Leader ordered me to escort you two specially. Now, please come this way.”

“We are all right.”

“I, who received the order, am not all right. Let us go.”

“Oh dear.”

Jang Ho led the two out and entered first. In accordance with Geom Mugeuk’s request, seats had been prepared for the two between himself and Seo Daeryong. Among today’s honored guest seats, it was the place where the two would be most comfortable.
