It was Ian, wrapping his neck while her body was bundled in a blanket. Her snow-white shoulders were visible above the covers. Ian asked in a sweet voice,

“When did you wake up?”

“Just a little while ago.”

“I didn’t even notice you getting up. I don’t know why I’m so tired these days.”

“Are you not training too hard?”

Even after their marriage, Ian had not neglected her martial training.

“Could the ambition to unify the martial world be yours, perhaps?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Ian tightened her hold on his neck again and answered like a villain.

“Now that you know my ambition, I can’t let you live.”

“I, too, cannot leave a villain be.”

Geom Mugeuk turned around and tried to embrace her.

But Ian was faster, stepping down to the other side of the bed together with the blanket.

“No. Hurry and wash up. We have to get ready to go out.”

“Where are you going?”

“Father called for me. He said he’d buy me something delicious.”

Geom Mugeuk blinked.

“He didn’t say a word to me.”

Ian smiled faintly and said,

“I suppose he wanted to go only with me.”

“He’s leaving his son aside and taking such care of only his daughter-in-law? How many times have the two of you gone out to eat together now?”

Already, Geom Ujin had taken Ian several times to places famous for their food.

“Today, I’m absolutely going with you!”

***

A single carriage stood in the front courtyard of the Heavenly Demon Hall.

The person sitting in the driver’s seat was Hwi. Seeing Hwi drive the carriage, it seemed they intended to leave quietly, leaving the guards behind.

Geom Ujin walked out of the Heavenly Demon Hall. Ian, who had been waiting beside the carriage, greeted him respectfully.

“Father.”

“Have you been waiting long?”

“No.”

“Let us go.”

At first, Geom Ujin had been truly difficult for her to approach, but after eating and drinking tea together several times, she had become much more comfortable with him than before.

Just as the two of them were about to board the carriage, Geom Ujin paused and said,

“Come out.”

Then, from behind a building in the distance, Geom Mugeuk revealed himself.

“Father, I like delicious things too.”

“Then go and eat them.”

“I mean delicious things that you buy for me, Father. You can’t leave me behind.”

Geom Mugeuk lightly launched himself into the air and blocked the front of the carriage.

“Uncle, if you mean to leave, you’ll have to run me over first!”

Hwi, sitting in the driver’s seat, laughed.

His father’s voice came from inside the carriage.

“Get in.”

Geom Mugeuk swiftly climbed into the carriage.

And so, the carriage carrying the three of them set off.

“No matter how they say a father-in-law dotes on his daughter-in-law, do you like your daughter-in-law that much?”

Geom Ujin merely looked out the window.

Ian teased Geom Mugeuk.

“Why did you follow us? I was going to have fun with Father, just the two of us.”

“What on earth do you talk about when you have fun?”

“That is a secret between Father and me.”

Geom Mugeuk was grateful to Ian.

No matter how fond his father was of his daughter-in-law, given his personality, if Ian showed even the slightest sign of feeling burdened, he would never ask her to go with him.

Because Ian enjoyed it and treated his father well, his father surely kept wanting to be good to her.

It was then, as the carriage was passing through Maga Village.

“Stop the carriage for a moment.”

When the carriage stopped, Geom Ujin called to someone outside the window.

“You there.”

The person his father called was none other than the goat-bearded merchant who sold dolls in Maga Village.

“Cult Leader!”

Startled, he came over and bowed so deeply his back seemed to fold in half.

“Is everything I asked for ready?”

“Yes, I prepared it in advance.”

“Give it to me.”

Goat Beard brought over two wrapped dolls.

When Geom Ujin handed him money, the peddler refused to take it.

“This is a gift from me to you, Cult Leader. Because you permitted me to make the dolls, I have been able to make a living until now. Please accept it.”

“Thank you. Sell many.”

The carriage set off again.

Geom Mugeuk was curious about what his father had received.

“What is that?”

Then Geom Ujin gave one of them to Ian.

Ian unwrapped it and was startled when she saw the doll inside.

The doll was none other than a doll of herself.

“I thought it was time there was one of you as well.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. His father had asked the doll-selling merchant to make a doll of Ian. From now on, Ian’s dolls would be sold in earnest as well.

Ian was so moved that her eyes grew hot.

“Thank you, Father.”

As moving as the fact that he had had a doll made for her was the fact that he had bought two, intending to keep one for himself.

“It’s made so well. It looks exactly like you!”

Ian’s doll was truly beautiful and splendid. It was made so carefully that even the shape of the Ilhwa Sword at her waist had been reproduced.

Geom Mugeuk sighed and said,

“Ah, I’m going to be pushed down one spot in the sales rankings.”

***

The place where the carriage arrived was a noodle shop made of a large tent.

“Ah, this place?”

It was the very place he had once told his father had delicious noodles. The same place where, at the time, his father had said the dumplings used to be delicious.

“Welcome!”

The place was run by a middle-aged couple.

Though it was located in a quiet area, there were quite a few customers.

“Hwi, you eat with us too.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

Today, Geom Ujin told Hwi to join them for the meal as well.

They took their seats and ordered.

“Four bowls of noodles, please.”

“Yes, please wait a moment!”

Just then, an old woman entered the shop and cleared away the bowls from a table that had not yet been cleaned.

“Mother, why did you come out? I told you to rest at home.”

“I’m fine.”

The old woman had come out to help her son and his wife. This had originally been the place where she did business, but her son and daughter-in-law had taken it over.

As she was wiping a table with a cloth, the old woman saw Geom Ujin and flinched in surprise.

“Could it be?”

Geom Ujin said to the old woman,

“Do you remember me?”

“Oh my, of course. Of course I remember.”

The old woman tried to prostrate herself on the spot, but Geom Ujin wrapped her with the power of drawing objects through the air and prevented her from doing so.

“After living so long, a day comes when I get to see such an honored person again.”

“There are times when I think of the dumplings you used to make.”

Whenever he returned from a trip to the Central Plains with the Fist Demon, he would sometimes stop by and buy dumplings.

Back then, he had gone there often, but after ascending to the seat of Cult Leader, he had not come. The old woman, too, had passed the business on to her son as time went by, and so they had lived on, having forgotten.

And yet today, she had met Geom Ujin again.

“No matter how skilled the cooks are, they cannot imitate the dumplings you made.”

The old woman wore an expression of indescribable emotion.

“I will make dumplings right now and serve them to you.”

“It is all right. Today, I came to eat the noodles your son makes.”

The old woman looked at Geom Ujin with moved eyes and said,

“You are exactly as you were in the past.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk playfully interjected.

“Then that means my father had an old face even when he was young.”

“That isn’t what I meant.”

When the old woman became flustered, Geom Ujin scolded Geom Mugeuk.

“Don’t be ridiculous!”

Geom Mugeuk smiled at the old woman and said,

“I’m joking, elder. My father is still handsome, isn’t he?”

At the word father, the old woman’s eyes widened. Soon, she seemed to recall someone.

“Now that I look, you resemble that person exactly.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. His father had once brought his mother here.

“Yes, fortunately, I don’t resemble my father but my mother.”

The old woman recalled the sight of the two of them sitting together in their youth, eating dumplings. It was a memory she had completely forgotten, but upon seeing Geom Mugeuk’s face, it came vividly back to her.

Before long, the noodles came out.

Just as they were eating the noodles together—

Urk.

Ian suddenly retched.

“I’m sorry. Uurk.”

Ian ran outside. She had taken one bite, and nausea had suddenly risen in her.

The old woman and the other woman looked at each other, then nodded.

With a meaningful smile, the old woman carefully said,

“Perhaps there may be good news.”

Guessing what those words meant, both Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin were startled.

Geom Ujin abruptly rose from his seat.

“We will come eat noodles another time.”

Geom Ujin walked outside. Geom Mugeuk and Hwi followed him.

Ian was outside getting some fresh air, taking deep breaths, and lowered her head to Geom Ujin.

“I’m sorry, Father.”

“No. Let us return for today.”

“No, I’m all right.”

But Geom Ujin had already boarded the carriage.

As he climbed into the carriage, Geom Ujin said to Hwi,

“Go straight to the Demon Physician.”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

When the carriage set off, Geom Ujin’s voice came from inside again.

“Drive the carriage slowly.”

***

Geom Mugeuk and Ian were lying on the beach.

It was that place inside the Space-Time Transposition Art that Geom Mugeuk liked most.

There were not two people lying there, but three. Ian’s belly was noticeably swollen.

Looking out at the sea in the distance, Geom Mugeuk suddenly asked,

“Will I be able to become a good father?”

Worried about whether he would become a good father? Ian was more worried that he would become too good of a father.

“Do you want to be a good father?”

“Of course I do.”

“You know that treating a child well no matter what doesn’t make one a good father, right?”

Geom Mugeuk turned his body toward Ian.

“Then what should I do?”

“Hmm.”

Ian seemed to ponder for a moment.

“We should raise the child with a little insufficiency, with some sense of lack.”

After a brief pause, Ian sighed and said,

“That will be impossible, won’t it?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“With the Heavenly Demon as the child’s grandfather, how could we raise them with a sense of lack?”

The two looked at each other and smiled faintly.

Ian thought of something utterly opposite to the blue sea.

“Ah, I want to see the night scenery of Dongting Lake.”

Snap!

Dongting Lake unfolded before her eyes. They were sitting in a small boat drifting upon Dongting Lake.

“No, the real Dongting Lake!”

“It is the same. Even if we go, it will be the same as this.”

“They say it’s good for the baby if the mother moves around a lot. Let’s go, just the two of us!”

Snap.

A green field spread out before the two of them.

“Now, let the two of us walk. Let us go all the way to the end of that plain!”

Ian could only shake her head.

“Yeon, your father is this kind of person.”

Yeon was the baby’s prenatal name. It was the yeon from Geom Yeon. Because the child had come to them as the most precious bond, they had chosen the character yeon, meaning bond, as the prenatal name.

“How dangerous is the martial world, and you want to wander around while pregnant?”

It was the moment Geom Mugeuk’s anxious nature was properly on display.

“What is there to worry about when you’re going with me? In this martial world, you are the most dangerous one!”

“Let us travel to our hearts’ content later. I will take you everywhere you want to go.”

“I clearly remembered that. Yeon, you heard him, right?”

The two walked hand in hand across the green plain.

In truth, Ian was fine whether it was real or fake. If they could hold hands and go together like this, what did it matter where that place was?

“I wish there were fluffy clouds over there.”

Snap!

***

Aaaaaaah!

Ian’s pained cry rang out from inside the room.

At last, the day of childbirth had come. Geom Mugeuk was pacing back and forth in front of the room.

‘Hang in there, Ian.’

He had asked whether he could go inside and stay by her side, but the midwife had told him to wait outside.

“How long has it been since the labor pains started?”

“Three double-hours have passed.”

The person who answered Geom Mugeuk’s question was the Demon Physician. He was standing guard at the door, fully prepared for any emergency that might arise.

“Isn’t it taking too long?”

“The first child usually takes a long time.”

“Shouldn’t you be inside as well, Demon Physician?”

“The ones inside are the finest midwives of our cult. I would only get in the way.”

“She’ll be all right, won’t she?”

“Do not worry. She will have a safe delivery.”

Geom Mugeuk had believed it would only be natural, but as the labor pains continued, he could not help feeling anxious.

Geom Mugeuk shouted in a loud voice so that he could be heard inside.

"Stay strong! I am right outside!"

Just then, another voice spoke.

"You're frantic. Stop that and come here."

The person sitting in the chair with a calm gaze was Gwonma.

"Yes, forgive me."

Geom Mugeuk sat down beside Gwonma. Even if he pretended to be this calm, he too must be burning with anxiety inside.

And Father, waiting for news of the birth at Cheonma Palace, would surely be no different.

"What is there to worry about? This is a child destined to be born as your offspring. Nothing will go wrong."

You little rascal, stop tormenting your mother and hurry out.

Then it happened. A baby's cry sounded from behind the door.

Geom Mugeuk and Gwonma shot up from their seats.

It was unmistakably a baby's cry.

The two stood frozen, holding their breath as if time itself had stopped.

After a moment, the door slowly opened.

Geom Mugeuk prayed desperately.

'Please, let them both be safe! Please!'

The one who opened the door and stepped out was a middle-aged midwife.

"She has safely given birth to a daughter."

An exclamation of "Ah!" escaped Geom Mugeuk's lips, and he conveyed his gratitude to the midwife.

"Thank you. Truly, thank you."

The midwife stepped aside from the door.

"You may enter."

Gwonma entered the room before Geom Mugeuk.

Another midwife was holding the baby, but Gwonma went to Ian before the baby.

"Are you alright?"

"Yes."

Ian was hidden behind Gwonma's broad back.

Then the midwife holding the baby spoke to Geom Mugeuk.

"Would you like to hold her?"

When Geom Mugeuk nodded, the midwife carefully placed the swaddled baby into his arms.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the baby's face.

It was the first moment he was seeing his daughter's face.

The moment he thought of her as his daughter, emotion surged up within him. Was this real? It felt like an illusion within the Spacetime Transference Technique.

'So I've become a father.'

It didn't feel real. A father? Me, a father?

The special nature of having a child was beyond words. He felt he could gaze upon her all day long. He felt he could hold her like this for a hundred days.

Then a voice was heard.

"She's our daughter."

When he raised his head, Ian was looking at him. Completely drained of energy, there was no strength in her voice.

That strong body of hers seemed shattered into pieces, her limbs devoid of strength. Her hair was soaked in sweat and clung to her forehead.

But in Geom Mugeuk's eyes, she had never looked more beautiful.

Holding the baby, Geom Mugeuk approached Ian. Only then did Gwonma gaze at the baby with an incomparably moved expression.

Geom Mugeuk carefully laid the baby down beside her. At the sight of the baby, tears streamed from her eyes.

The birth of a new life had nothing to do with martial arts prowess.

As the labor pains continued, she had been so afraid. It hurt and was exhausting, but more than that, she had worried the baby was suffering more than she was. She had been terrified that something might go wrong with the baby.

She gently stroked the baby with trembling hands.

"You worked hard, baby."

Geom Mugeuk had always used honorifics with her, even after they were married, but not in this moment.

"You worked hard, Ian."

Relief, joy, and happiness flooded over Ian, whose tension finally melted away.

"She looks like you."

Even though she was a newborn, her distinct features made it clear that she resembled Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk smiled brilliantly and spoke to the baby in a whisper.

"Daughter, I congratulate you on being born into this world."

Swoooosh.

Geom Mugeuk turned his head and looked out the window.

Someone was flying toward them from afar at the speed of light.

Geom Ujin was flying toward them, pushing the Cheonma Flying Technique to its limits.

Chapter 894: I Didn’t Know What You’d Like, So I Prepared Everything
--------------------------------------------------------------------
The moment he heard the news that the child had been born, Geom Ujin flew over as swiftly as light.

When he stood before the door, his heart trembled.

It had not trembled even when he fought the Heavenly Slaughter Star.

As soon as Geom Ujin entered, he went straight to Ian.

“Are you feeling all right?”

“Yes, Father.”

When Ian tried to sit up, the gentle energy Geom Ujin released enveloped her and pressed her back down.

“You’ve been through a lot.”

It was only a few words, but Geom Ujin’s feelings could be sensed in their warmth.

Geom Ujin’s gaze turned toward the baby lying beside Ian.

Over the years, Geom Mugeuk had discovered many new expressions on his father’s face. Today, he saw yet another.

“Congratulations on becoming a grandfather.”

For once, Geom Ujin could not hide his emotions. He was moved in a way that words could not express. It was a joy distinctly different from that of having a child himself.

“Hold your granddaughter, Father.”

Geom Ujin looked at the Fist Demon.

“Have you held her yet?”

“I’ll hold her later, when she’s a little bigger.”

The baby was so tiny that he felt awkward holding her with his large body and hands. Even the Fist Demon of the world was completely flustered before his first granddaughter.

Geom Mugeuk picked up the child and handed her to his father.

Geom Ujin carefully took the baby into his arms. It had been an extremely long time since he had held a baby, and even Geom Ujin was tense.

At some point, he had forgotten. That every life in this world began this small.

Yet the baby felt heavy to Geom Ujin. Heavier than any other existence in the world.

The gaze with which Geom Ujin looked down at the baby trembled.

A son who resembled his dead wife, and a granddaughter who looked as though she had been carved from that very son.

The baby’s faint breathing shook Geom Ujin’s heart with a resonance greater than that of the incantations of the Nine Flames Demon Art.

“Have you chosen a name?”

“Please name her, Father.”

When Geom Ujin stared at him in surprise, Geom Mugeuk nodded. He and Ian had already decided on this.

“There was a name your mother wanted to give you if she ever had a daughter.”

Geom Ujin spoke as he gazed at his granddaughter.

“Hayeon (夏緣), Geom Hayeon.”

Ha, meaning summer, and yeon, meaning connection. Whether it was coincidence or fate, no one could know, but the character for connection had found its way into the child’s name as well.

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk and nodded, then said with a radiant smile,

“It’s such a beautiful name.”

Geom Mugeuk took his daughter back into his arms and said,

“Geom Hayeon, from now on, that is your name.”

At that moment, the sound of bells rang out from far beyond the window.

It was not an urgent sound, but a soft, lingering chime.

They were the bells rung whenever a joyful event occurred within the cult.

Every demon stopped in their tracks. They all knew that the Little Demon Queen had safely given birth.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s hand paused as he read his book. No moving passage in its pages could compare with the emotion of this moment.

On the training ground, the One-Flower Sword Venerable raised his sword high toward the heavens.

The Drunken Demon lifted his cup into the air in a toast, while the Poison King released the poisonous insect he had been struggling to hold—for today, at least.

The Demon Buddha quietly recited prayers, wishing for the baby’s health.

The Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable laughed loudly like a madwoman after soothing wandering souls, while the Extreme Evil Little Demon, who had been staring at a white wall, smiled without making a sound.

Seo Daeryong, watching investigators interrogate a criminal, suddenly thrust his hand into the air and shouted hurrah. Jang Ho, who had just been departing on an operation, shouted with the demon warriors inside the large carriage.

“Little Demon Queen! Congratulations on your safe delivery!”

***

“I’ll put her to sleep.”

Geom Mugeuk took Hayeon into his arms. As soon as she entered her father’s embrace, her crying grew louder.

“She drank so much milk, but she still won’t sleep.”

Hayeon woke almost every hour, so Ian had not been able to sleep properly. Seo Daeryong had said it was his first time learning that babies woke so often. She really did wake frequently.

“She isn’t sick, is she?”

Geom Mugeuk placed a hand on Hayeon’s forehead.

“She doesn’t seem to have a fever.”

Being a martial arts expert and raising a child were entirely separate matters. Just as martial arts had no end, neither did child-rearing. A single fist could bring down a cliff, but it could not stop a crying baby.

There was no method to counter it.

“I’ll watch her, so get some sleep.”

“Thank you.”

At that moment, a presence could be sensed outside. From that presence alone, Geom Mugeuk knew who had come visiting at this late hour.

“Yeon, let’s go for a walk in the courtyard with Daddy.”

When Geom Mugeuk carried the baby into the courtyard, his father was already there.

“What brings you here at this hour?”

“I simply couldn’t sleep, so I went for a walk and happened to pass by.”

As if that were true. He had surely come after hearing that Ian was struggling with childcare.

“I’ll watch her for an hour. The two of you get some sleep.”

“You will, Father?”

Geom Ujin nodded.

“How can we ever thank you?”

Without hesitation, Geom Mugeuk handed Hayeon over. The crying Hayeon stopped and looked up at her grandfather.

Perhaps it was because she was in unfamiliar arms. He turned around with his granddaughter, who was staring at him with her eyes opened wide.

“Hayeon, let’s go play with Grandfather.”

Hayeon did not cry until they had left the room.

When Geom Mugeuk returned to the room, Ian had heard everything he and his father had said outside.

“Will Father be all right?”

“He’s not just anyone. He’s my father. He’ll be fine, so don’t worry and let’s sleep.”

Geom Mugeuk lay down on the bed beside Ian.

Ian, who had been sleeping poorly for some time, soon drifted off. Geom Mugeuk then rose silently from the bed and left the room.

‘Of course he won’t be all right!’

The two of them were already at their wits’ end, so how could his father possibly get Hayeon to sleep?

Geom Mugeuk immediately shot into the air.

Once he disappeared, Ian opened her eyes in bed. That worrywart would never be able to sleep peacefully after leaving their daughter with his father.

‘Fine. At least for tonight, let the Geom family take care of our Hayeon.’

By the time Ian had fallen into a quiet sleep, Geom Mugeuk had reached his father’s residence.

He expected to hear Hayeon crying, but the house was silent.

When Geom Mugeuk entered the room, he was startled by the scene before him.

His father and Hayeon were both wearing flower-patterned pajamas of the same design. It seemed he had prepared flower-patterned baby pajamas in advance, tailored to fit Hayeon.

Hayeon was sleeping soundly in her grandfather’s arms.

When Geom Mugeuk opened his eyes wide in astonishment, his father gestured for silence.

Then he carefully laid Hayeon down on the bed.

—How in the world did you do that?

Worried that he might wake her, Geom Mugeuk sent a telepathic message.

—Though I may not look it, I raised two sons.

Come to think of it, his father had once said something similar. He had spoken of carrying his feverish older brother and running to the physician, while treating his own raging fever by wiping himself down with lukewarm water. By the time his second child came along, he had gained that much composure.

—That was incredible, Father.

Geom Mugeuk went over to his daughter. If she could sleep this peacefully, why had she given her mother such a hard time?

—It seems she already realized what kind of person her grandfather is.

Indeed, whose daughter was she? How could she lack such discernment? Yes, there was absolutely no harm in making a good impression on this man.

Geom Ujin smiled as he looked down at Hayeon.

—I’ll watch her from time to time.

***

A new painting had been hung in Geom Mugeuk’s room.

The painting of him dancing with his friends on his wedding day had been pushed into a corner, and paintings of Hayeon now occupied its place.

There was one from when she had been a newborn, another of her taking her first steps, and even one of her wearing a small wooden sword—barely two handspans long—at her waist.

As the child grew, Jang Ho’s skill at painting had improved considerably as well.

“Time really does fly.”

It seemed like only yesterday that she had been born, yet she was already running about and speaking quite well.

Geom Mugeuk turned his head toward the window.

Someone poked their head over the wall, then quickly ducked back down.

As Geom Mugeuk left the room, he said,

“Why are you peeking in so furtively?”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon then entered the courtyard.

“What do you mean, peeking? I was just passing by and happened to glance over.”

“While using your lightness skill?”

Geom Mugeuk had a good idea why the Blood Heaven Saber Demon had come.

“If you came to see Hayeon, you’re a little too late.”

For an instant, disappointment flashed across the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s face.

“What, the Cult Leader isn’t even doing any work?”

“Apparently not.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon gave a hollow laugh.

“Does he like having a granddaughter that much?”

It was not merely because the Cult Leader liked her. Hayeon herself adored her grandfather. Even while playing with her mother and father, she would say that she missed her grandfather and ask them to take her to him.

That was Ian’s time to rest. She could quietly enjoy a cup of tea, and sometimes even practice her martial arts.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon suddenly said,

“So it’s finally this year.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

The five years he had promised his father would end this year. In fact, it would be this very month, only a few days from now.

He had asked his father to give him five years. When he asked again at the end of that period, if his father’s dream was still to unify the martial world, Geom Mugeuk had said that he would stand at the forefront of that war.

“Time really does pass quickly.”

It had seemed like that day would never come, but after having and raising a child, it had arrived before he knew it.

“You rascal, are you really going to talk about the passing of time in front of me?”

Geom Mugeuk laughed. Someone had once said that in one’s twenties, time passed at the speed of two stages of lightness skill, and in one’s thirties, at the speed of three. One day, it would race along at the speed of mastery, would it not?

“I thought you would launch some kind of campaign once this year arrived.”

“What campaign?”

“I thought you would visit the Demon Venerables one by one and ask them to oppose the unification of the martial world.”

“If I asked you, would you listen?”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon shook his head.

“I probably couldn’t.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded as if he had expected that answer.

“If even you won’t do it, which of the other Demon Venerables would? I don’t want to burden you unnecessarily.”

In the past, he had pushed ahead while placing all sorts of burdens on the man.

“Have you become more mature now that you’re a father?”

Geom Mugeuk wore a meaningful expression.

“No. This is also part of my campaign.”

“Your campaign?”

“I plan to settle the matter there, on that very day.”

As if he truly had been thinking along those lines, Geom Mugeuk explained why he had not gone to visit them beforehand.

“If I visit them in advance and ask for their help, they’ll ultimately conclude that they have no choice but to refuse. But that day, I’ll carry out a campaign to win the Demon Venerables’ sympathy.

‘Ah, the Little Cult Leader is going this far. I should step forward and help him.’ I’ll make them feel that way.”

Had it been anyone else, he might have doubted it. But with Geom Mugeuk, it seemed entirely possible.

“Why are you telling me this?”

“This is also part of the campaign. ‘The Little Cult Leader is treating me so honestly. How could I refuse to help him? I should help him, at least!’”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon wore an incredulous expression.

At least he had understood one thing correctly: visiting them in advance would only mean that their conclusions had already been decided.

“Will Father’s dream remain unchanged?”

“No one can know what’s truly in the Cult Leader’s heart. However…”

After a brief pause, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon calmly added,

“The Cult Leader is probably making efforts as well.”

Geom Mugeuk understood what those efforts meant.

At that moment, another visitor arrived. It was the Demon Buddha.

“What brings you here?”

“I bought some clothes for Hayeon while returning from the Central Plains.”

The Demon Buddha handed him the wrapped clothes. He seemed like the sort of man who would never buy a child’s clothing in his entire life, yet here he was.

“Thank you.”

“Where’s Hayeon?”

He had intended to see her face while he was here.

“You’re a little too late.”

The Demon Buddha also realized that Hayeon had gone to the Cult Leader once again.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon joked with the Demon Buddha.

“Take a number and get in line. I’m next.”

Another voice came from behind them.

“What does order matter? Whoever arrives first that day gets to play with Hayeon.”

It was the Drunken Demon.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon gave him a look that seemed to say, Where did this drunkard come from? But the Drunken Demon did not back down.

“Senior, it’s been ten days since I quit drinking. I’m sensitive, so don’t provoke me.”

“What made you quit drinking?”

When the Drunken Demon could not answer, Geom Mugeuk answered for him.

“Hayeon said she didn’t like him because he smelled of alcohol.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon stared at the Drunken Demon in disbelief.

“But why would the Drunken Demon quit drinking over that?”

“That’s why you shouldn’t talk about taking numbers.”

The Demon Buddha seemed unwilling to get involved in the dispute over numbers.

“I only came to deliver the clothes. If they don’t fit, let me know and I’ll exchange them.”

As the Demon Buddha turned to leave, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Hayeon really likes you, Demon Buddha.”

“She likes me?”

The Demon Buddha was startled. The Blood Heaven Saber Demon and the Drunken Demon also pricked up their ears.

“She asked a few days ago when she could see the Golden Buddha Grandfather again.”

“She really said she likes me?”

“It seems she finds the light emanating from your body fascinating and pleasant. Your short stature probably makes you seem more approachable to her as well.”

The Demon Buddha’s expression brightened. The traits he had considered weaknesses had become the very things Hayeon liked about him.

“Still, meeting someone like me won’t do Hayeon any good.”

When he unnecessarily took a step back, the Drunken Demon suddenly said,

“If you put it that way, then what am I? A drunkard?”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon added his own remark.

“Am I any different? My epithet even contains the character for blood.”

“Still, Saber Demon, don’t you read a lot? You’d be a great help to Hayeon.”

To Hayeon, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon was Grandfather Book. She liked going to his home because it was filled with books.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Which of the Demon Venerables does Hayeon like best? You’d know, wouldn’t you?”

All three Demon Venerables’ eyes gleamed at once. Because he was Grandfather Book, because he was the Golden Buddha Grandfather, because he had quit drinking!

Geom Mugeuk’s answer was someone none of them had expected.

“The Poison King.”

At the mention of the Poison King, they were all disappointed, yet curious at the same time.

“Why? Didn’t you say the Poison King put Hayeon through a hard time last time?”

The Poison King had increased Hayeon’s resistance to poison through various methods. As a result, although she had not reached the state of being impervious to all poisons, her constitution had changed so that ordinary poisons could no longer harm her.

It had to be done while she was young for the best effect, but without the absolute trust Geom Mugeuk and Ian placed in the Poison King, they could never have entrusted her to him.

In any case, it must have been difficult for Hayeon, so the Demon Venerable she liked least should have been the Poison King.

“Why the Poison King?!”

At the three Demon Venerables’ jealous cries, Geom Mugeuk answered,

“He’s handsome.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon and the Demon Buddha sighed and clicked their tongues. The Poison King even possessed an exceptionally youthful appearance.

But the Drunken Demon reacted differently from the others.

“I refuse to accept this! Once I get rid of the smell of alcohol, I can win!”

But he could not win.

From far away, the Poison King walked toward them and displayed what he held in his hands, driving the final nail into the ranking of their popularity.

They were twelve small, adorable pouches. Each pouch was embroidered with one of the animals of the Chinese zodiac—a rat, an ox, a rabbit, a tiger, and so on.

“I didn’t know which one Hayeon would like, so I prepared them all.”

Chapter 895: Are You Going to Compete with Your Granddaughter?
--------------------------------------------------------------
Behind twelve dangling pouches stood the Poison King.

Even the cute animal pictures embroidered on the pouches paled before the Poison King’s appearance.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon and the Demon Buddha admitted defeat.

‘We lost. There’s no beating that face.’

Even so, the Drunken Demon made one last struggle that he absolutely should not have attempted.

He stood shoulder to shoulder with the Poison King.

“We’re all members of the Four Handsome Men, aren’t we?”

Geum Mugeuk shook his head and informed him of the cold reality.

“Just because we’re all members of the Four Handsome Men doesn’t mean we’re all the same, big brother. Right now, you look like his uncle standing next to his nephew.”

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon and the Demon Buddha nodded at the same time. How could they leave when things became this entertaining whenever they got tangled up with Geum Mugeuk?

The Drunken Demon let out a sigh, and his hand instinctively moved toward the liquor hanging from his waist. He almost decided to just drink it, but managed to hold himself back in the end.

“Hayoon’s choice is still left!”

The Poison King looked toward the house behind Geum Mugeuk and asked,

“Is Hayoon inside?”

“No, she’s at the Heavenly Demon Hall.”

Disappointment flashed across the Poison King’s face, and his shoulders drooped. If Hayoon wasn’t here, there was no reason for him to remain. The Poison King turned away decisively.

“Won’t you leave that behind?”

“I’ll give it to her myself.”

The Poison King left, but the other three Demon Lords remained.

“Aren’t the three of you leaving?”

At Geum Mugeuk’s words, the Drunken Demon sighed.

“I’m going to wait until Hayoon comes. I even dreamed about playing with Hayoon last night.”

“If you played with her in your dream, you don’t need to play with her in reality.”

“What do you mean by that?”

“I heard Hayoon said she disliked you because you smelled of alcohol.”

“I told you, I quit! It’s been ten days!”

Geum Mugeuk shouted at them.

“Why are you all doing this? She’s my daughter, I tell you! My daughter!”

At that moment, someone shouted from behind them,

“Why is Hayoon your daughter?”

When Geum Mugeuk turned around, he saw the Wind Heaven Cult Leader entering. It seemed they had deliberately chosen today as the day to gather everyone together.

“She’s our daughter.”

All the Demon Lords nodded.

“I said she’s my daughter!”

They only said it to tease him, but Geum Mugeuk was truly grateful that they loved his daughter so much.

The Wind Heaven Cult Leader took a small box from his robes and held it out.

“What is this?”

“It’s a Small Divine Pill. Among elixirs for children, it’s the finest.”

“I know. It couldn’t have been easy to obtain this.”

“There’s nothing I wouldn’t do for our Hayoon.”

“You scolded me so much for giving you a divine object!”

“Are you the same as Hayoon? Make sure you tell her that Grandfather Wind Heaven gave it to her when you administer it. I’m giving it to you because I know you’ll make sure she takes it even better than I would.”

Otherwise, he would have administered it himself while making a great show of the gift.

“Thank you very much, Cult Leader.”

The Wind Heaven Cult Leader puffed himself up in front of the Demon Lords.

“With this much, I suppose I can be the first to play with Hayoon, yes? Now, those of you who came empty-handed, step back over there.”

“Then let’s go to the Heavenly Demon Hall and do exactly that. For your information, my father will probably be empty-handed too.”

“She’s at the Heavenly Demon Hall again?”

Disappointment flashed across the Wind Heaven Cult Leader’s face as well. He could understand the Cult Leader of the Demonic Sect being unable to help it, but—

“Since the Fist Demon Lord is her maternal grandfather, he must see Hayoon every single day, right?”

“He’s with her at the Heavenly Demon Hall even now.”

The Wind Heaven Cult Leader was genuinely disappointed.

“I should have made Ian my adopted daughter!”

“That would have been wonderful. You would have gained a son-in-law like me as well.”

The Wind Heaven Cult Leader flinched. He openly wore an expression that said, Ah, so there’s that problem, before promptly abandoning Geum Mugeuk.

No longer was he this young master—

“So when is our Hayoon coming?”

***

Tension filled the Heavenly Demon Hall.

The Fist Demon stood hidden behind a pillar. Geom Ujin stood at the pillar across from him.

When they were young, the two of them had once stood side by side behind pillars just like this, surrounded by enemies and dodging countless hidden weapons flying toward them like a downpour.

The tension from that time remained unchanged.

A clear voice rang through the hall.

“……Seventeen, eighteen, nineteen, twenty. I’m coming to find you now!”

Geom Hayeon, who had been covering her eyes with her hands, opened them.

Her white skin, cool forehead, sparkling eyes that seemed to hold the night sky, small, pert nose, and red lips revealing pearly white teeth—her distinct features were a perfect likeness of Geum Mugeuk.

Her clear, shining eyes were filled with intelligence, and dimples appeared whenever she smiled. Her jet-black hair gleamed and swayed whenever she moved.

She was so beautiful and adorable that everyone passing by would stop in their tracks and say something about her.

She also spoke better than other children her age and was intelligent. She had already mastered reading and writing, and she calculated numbers faster than anyone. Seo Daeryong evaluated Hayoon this way:

―I’m a prodigy, but Hayoon is a born genius.

Hayoon carefully looked around and began to walk. She was playing hide-and-seek with her two grandfathers. Hide-and-seek in the Heavenly Demon Hall was probably a first in the entire history of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“Grandpa, your concealment technique isn’t working.”

Hayoon’s bright voice echoed through the Heavenly Demon Hall.

She walked between the fearsome pillars of the Heavenly Demon Hall, supported by demons, yet she was not afraid in the slightest. The enormous demon statues of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult were what she had seen since birth.

Hayoon approached Geom Ujin’s side. When she examined the pillar where he had been standing, Geom Ujin had already vanished.

The Fist Demon, hiding behind the pillar across from him, silently laughed.

‘Did he really use his movement technique to escape?’

When the Fist Demon cautiously poked his head out, Hayoon was nowhere to be seen.

The next moment, the Fist Demon raised his hand and turned around. Hayoon was standing behind him.

Hayoon put a finger to her lips, making a shushing gesture, and this time set out to find Geom Ujin. Since he was not deliberately avoiding her like the Cult Leader had, Hayoon found him with ease.

Geom Ujin moved between the pillars, avoiding Hayoon. He could easily have let his granddaughter catch him, but he did not.

‘Is he really trying to have a proper match with his granddaughter?’

The Fist Demon watched with a smile.

When she still could not find Geom Ujin,

Hayoon stood in the center of the Heavenly Demon Hall, closed her eyes, and thought for a moment.

She seemed to be pondering something deeply.

This time, instead of running toward the place where Geom Ujin was hiding, she dashed toward the pillar beside it. Geom Ujin allowed himself to be discovered according to Hayoon’s expectations.

“Found you!”

Having finally found him with such difficulty, Hayoon hopped up and down excitedly.

“Well done, Hayoon.”

Geom Ujin scooped Hayoon up into his arms. His eyes, gazing at his granddaughter, were full of affection.

The Fist Demon walked over to them.

“Yeon, how are you so good at finding people?”

The secret behind Hayoon finding them so easily every time they played hide-and-seek was revealed.

“Grandpa taught me before.”

“What did he teach you?”

“How to find hidden people.”

When Geom Mugeuk had gone hunting, he had taught her the Instant Draw technique that he had once taught Geom Mugeuk.

Although Hayoon used the Instant Draw technique and discovered him, Geom Ujin dodged to the side. Only when Hayoon anticipated that he would move aside and dashed there as well did he finally allow himself to be caught. He wanted to teach her the lesson of moving based on predicting her opponent.

The Fist Demon smiled faintly. Even when playing, Geom Ujin never simply played. It was truly characteristic of the Cult Leader—and of the Cult Leader’s bloodline.

There was a reason Hayoon could use the Instant Draw technique.

Hayoon had inherited the Heavenly Martial Body, the perfect constitution for learning martial arts, just like Geum Mugeuk.

That was not all.

Geom Ujin himself had opened her Conception and Governing Vessels and even cleansed and tempered her sinews and marrow.

Afterward, he taught her a cultivation technique and the Wind God’s Four Steps. To his surprise, Hayoon learned the cultivation technique, accumulated internal energy, and understood the basics of the Wind God’s Four Steps. She had even managed to perform it in practice.

Geom Ujin had become completely absorbed in the joy of teaching martial arts to Hayoon, who possessed an extraordinary martial talent.

“Let’s do it again!”

When Hayoon asked to play again, Geom Ujin and the Fist Demon looked at each other. They had already played hide-and-seek seven times. No more! They shared the same thought.

Geom Ujin said to Hayoon,

“Let’s play doctor.”

“Okay!”

Geom Ujin and the Fist Demon exchanged looks and began a silent battle of wits.

The Fist Demon was about to lie down first when—

Ssssss.

The formless energy Geom Ujin released immobilized the Fist Demon.

Then Geom Ujin collapsed onto the floor as if he had fallen ill.

“Please help me, Doctor.”

The Fist Demon looked at the Cult Leader and spoke with his eyes.

‘This is too much, Cult Leader! No, brother-in-law! How can you do this?’

Geom Ujin smiled and closed his eyes.

Hayoon said to the Fist Demon,

“I need medicinal herbs.”

“Doctor, what medicinal herbs do you need?”

The Fist Demon naturally took on the role of Doctor Geom’s assistant.

“Blood-Severing Herb and Soul-Seizing Herb!”

The poisonous herbs Hayoon named were real. They were herbs she had heard about when she visited the Poison King.

“I’ll go gather them.”

“The Blood-Severing Herb is over there.”

The Fist Demon went to the pillar Hayoon pointed at and pretended to dig up the herb.

“We need to gather a lot of Soul-Seizing Herb.”

All the while, Geom Ujin lay comfortably with his eyes closed. Even the Demonic Cult Leader found playing with a child exhausting.

‘I’ve never seen the Cult Leader look so tired.’

The Fist Demon smiled as he pretended to gather imaginary herbs.

“The herbs are ready.”

Once the herbs were prepared, Hayoon placed her tiny hand on Geom Ujin’s chest and began reciting precisely where the Eight Extraordinary Vessels were located and how to treat every possible ailment.

The Fist Demon asked with a surprised expression,

“Doctor, from whom did you learn medicine?”

“Grandpa Demon Doctor.”

“How many times did he explain it to you?”

“Once.”

Her memory was so exceptional that she remembered everything after hearing it only once.

The Fist Demon wondered what kind of martial artist this naturally gifted child would grow into if the Cult Leader taught her, Geum Mugeuk taught her, and even the Demon Lords taught her.

Perhaps she would become a master who surpassed even her father and grandfathers.

“You’re all healed.”

The Fist Demon was about to quickly become the patient and switch roles—

‘Let me lie down too!’

At that moment, Geom Ujin quietly turned over and lay on his side with his back exposed.

“Doctor, my back hurts too.”

The Fist Demon burst out laughing.

Geom Ujin, who had turned over, laughed as well.

Only after Hayoon treated Geom Ujin’s back, arms, and legs did their game of doctor finally end.

The Fist Demon said to Hayoon,

“Grandpa has to work now, so let’s go outside.”

“Wait!”

Hayoon ran over and kissed Geom Ujin on the cheek.

“See you later, Grandpa. Thank you for playing with me.”

“Yes, yes.”

Geom Ujin’s face was brightly smiling as he gazed at Hayoon.

It was the first time the Fist Demon had seen the Cult Leader smile so brightly since meeting him. It was a smile on an entirely different level from the one he showed his son.

“Maternal Grandpa, carry me.”

The Fist Demon lifted Hayoon up and sat her on his shoulders.

.

As they walked out of the Heavenly Demon Hall, Hayoon said,

“I like your shoulders, Maternal Grandpa. They’re wide.”

“Aren’t you scared being so high up?”

“No.”

Since she was sitting on his shoulders, her face was close to his. For some reason, the Fist Demon became self-conscious.

“Isn’t Grandpa’s face scary?”

“Not scary at all.”

Hayoon hugged the Fist Demon’s face tightly.

“I like you, Maternal Grandpa.”

The Fist Demon did not know that the expression on his face now was not much different from the one the Cult Leader had worn a moment ago.

“Later, this old grandpa will teach you the Fist Technique.”

“Okay!”

At that moment, the Extreme Evil Little Demon was waiting in front of the entrance to the Heavenly Demon Hall. He had seen the Wind Heaven Cult Leader shouting at Geum Mugeuk’s residence from a distance and had come here to wait.

“Uncle Mask!”

“Yeon!”

Hayoon lightly leaped from the Fist Demon’s shoulders. It was a nimble movement technique.

“Here, this is your present.”

What the Little Demon held out was a small mask made to fit Hayoon’s face. Since Hayoon had wanted one, he had ordered the craftsman who made his own mask to create it especially for her. That was why the mask was exceptionally light and did not cause her to sweat even after wearing it for a long time.

Hayoon put on the mask. She looked unbearably cute wearing the little mask.

“Uncle Mask, let’s play together.”

The Fist Demon wore an expression of finally being freed.

The two eyes behind the Extreme Evil Little Demon’s mask smiled brightly. He took Hayoon’s hand and began walking.

“Let’s go see the Faceless Guests!”

***

Several days later.

Geum Mugeuk stood before a mirror, gazing at his reflection. Although it was still early, he had already bathed and dressed himself carefully.

“How do I look?”

In the mirror, Ian could be seen looking at him.

In fact, on the day he had first returned after regressing, he had asked Ian the same question.

Ian remembered her answer from that day exactly.

“You’re the most handsome man in the martial world.”

Originally, when Geum Mugeuk answered that he was bored, Ian had responded, “A person who pays a monthly salary always has a predetermined answer to that question.”

But today, Geum Mugeuk’s answer was different.

“You are the most beautiful woman in the world.”

A blush instantly bloomed across Ian’s face.

She walked over and embraced him from behind.

“Today is finally the day.”

At last, the day he had promised his father had dawned.

She knew very well how important this matter was to Geum Mugeuk. To her husband, it was even more important than his battle against the Heavenly Killing Star.

“Are you nervous?”

Geum Mugeuk honestly nodded.

“Are you afraid?”

Again, Geum Mugeuk nodded.

“Shall I help you win?”

“You mean, by coming with me?”

He had no intention of taking her along, but he jokingly took her hand.

Ian gently pulled her hand free.

“No. I could never oppose Father.”

Geom Ujin had treated her so well.

“If I see Father, I’ll probably say, ‘I’ll stand at the forefront of the war to unite the martial world!’ before anything else.”

Geum Mugeuk laughed at her half-joking, half-serious remark.

“Then how will you help me win?”

“Yeon will have to wake him up.”

At that, Geum Mugeuk burst out laughing. Her attempt to ease his tension had worked perfectly.

Before leaving the house, Geum Mugeuk went to Hayoon’s room.

He covered his daughter with the blanket she had kicked away and gazed at her for a moment before leaving the house.

“I’ll be back.”

“Come back safely, my dear!”

As Geum Mugeuk disappeared into the distance, Ian’s brightly smiling expression hardened.

Worry rose across her face.

‘Stay strong, darling.’

With her eyes closed, she prayed fervently that, at least today, the heavens would be on her husband’s side.

When Geum Mugeuk entered the Heavenly Demon Hall, the Demon Lords had already arrived.

Today, the Eight Demon Lords stood in a line beneath the throne, all looking toward him.

His father sat upon the throne.

At the entrance to the Heavenly Demon Hall, Geum Mugeuk called out loudly as usual,

“Father, I’m here!”

Geum Mugeuk slowly walked forward along the Blood Path.

Chapter 896: Send Me to the Past
--------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk slowly moved forward.

He had walked this path of blood countless times, but had there ever been a day when he felt as tense as today?

The eight Demon Venerables.

Today, they were not the people who had joked around with him. Today, they stood as the subordinates of the Heavenly Demon, as the Eight Demon Venerables of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Even the Fist Demon, in this moment, was not the Cult Leader’s in-law, but a Demon Venerable.

As Geom Mugeuk walked, he met the Demon Venerables’ eyes.

Every one of their gazes seemed to be asking him this.

Are you prepared to persuade the Cult Leader?

He could feel that they, too, were tense. Depending on what happened here today, the fate of murim would change.

The most important thing today was his father’s heart.

“You’ve come?”

“Yes, Father.”

Unlike usual, a taut tension flowed between the two of them.

“Do you have anything to say to me, one last time?”

It was his last chance to persuade his father. The Demon Venerables wondered what secret plan Geom Mugeuk had brought with him.

But the answer that came was unexpected.

“I do not.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Demon Venerables were shocked. The Blood Heaven Saber Demon, in particular, had believed that Geom Mugeuk would somehow persuade them today.

‘You fool, what are you planning to do!’

Geom Mugeuk truly did not try to persuade him at all. For the past five years, he had not tried to persuade his father about this matter. If he had thought he could change his father’s heart through words, he would have gone to him and tried to persuade him throughout those five years.

Instead, Geom Mugeuk had lived diligently for the past five years. He had lived diligently as the Young Cult Leader, and diligently as his father’s son. As one woman’s husband, and as one child’s father. He had also done his utmost in his relationships with the Demon Venerables.

If that life did not get through to him, then there was nothing more he could do.

At last, Geom Mugeuk asked the question.

“What is your dream, Father?”

If the answer was the unification of murim, he had promised that he would become the vanguard of that war and go out to fight.

Geom Mugeuk felt as though only his father and he existed in this space.

He could see no one else. Only his father.

At length, Geom Ujin answered.

“My dream is……”

In that brief silence, the tension rose to its peak.

“To unite murim.”

“!”

In that instant, Geom Mugeuk’s heart dropped.

No, it can’t be. It can’t be. It felt as though his vague hope shattered, and cold reality came crashing down before his eyes.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s face twitched, and a soundless sigh escaped the mouth of the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable lowered his head,

and the Poison King turned his gaze toward the distant window. The Demon Buddha silently rolled the beads of his prayer bracelet, and the Drunken Demon finally opened the stopper of his wine gourd and quietly drank.

The Extreme Evil Smiling Demon and the Fist Demon looked at Geom Mugeuk without any reaction.

Everyone’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk was neither shocked nor agitated. At length, he slowly opened his mouth.

“I will stand at the forefront of that war.”

He did not try to persuade his father to overturn the result. This was a promise between him and his father.

In Geom Mugeuk’s mind, a battlefield flowing with blood appeared. He saw himself standing alone atop a mountain of corpses on that battlefield.

At that moment, the eyes of the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon, whose gaze met his, spoke as they had during the fishing competition.

I will serve you.

Geom Mugeuk nodded. Could he truly bear this war? Could he point his sword at Jin Hagun and Bi Sain?

Just then, Geom Ujin spoke again.

“However……”

As everyone held their breath, waiting for what words would follow,

“The method of that unification of murim will be your method.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes widened. The Demon Venerables, too, exchanged startled looks.

“My method is one that does not break the table. My method is to hold the Majeongsa fishing competition and win it—that is unifying murim.

To hold a hunting competition together, and a cooking competition, and win them—that is unifying murim. That is my method.”

Geom Ujin slowly nodded.

“Then we shall unite it by that method.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. His father had given up on his dream.

“Father.”

Geom Mugeuk closed his eyes. He felt so sorry toward his father that he could not look at him.

After a moment, Geom Mugeuk asked, his eyes filled with apology.

“Why did you change your mind? Was it because of Hayeon?”

He had naturally thought that was the reason.

“I did not change my mind because of Hayeon.”

“Then why?”

An astonishing answer flowed from Geom Ujin.

“Because of the first allowance I ever received.”

Everyone looked at Geom Ujin, their eyes asking what he meant. Only Geom Mugeuk knew. He knew what those words meant. And that was not the only reason.

“Because of the flower-patterned pajamas I was given as a gift. Because of the memory of laughing when I saw the man who had come to ask me to guarantee his debt.”

Only then did the Demon Venerables understand whose memories those were connected to.

“Because of the succession struggle that was settled without seeing blood.”

Geom Ujin’s true feelings, conveyed for the first time, rang throughout the hall. Those low yet resonant words flew one by one into Geom Mugeuk’s heart.

“Because of the go board I received as a gift, and because of the scroll painting of brothers.”

As the memories of the past naturally rose in his mind, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes trembled.

“Because of the hammock I lay in during our trip, and because of that chatter that tells me everything whenever you return after leaving the cult.”

Except when teaching him martial arts, this was the first time Geom Ujin had spoken at such length about his own feelings.

“Because of the whale I saw in the illusion, and because of the nap I took that day, when I slept without any guard up for the first time.”

Geom Ujin’s gaze turned toward the Demon Venerables standing in a row before him.

“Because you made me see anew what kind of people the Demon Venerables standing there truly are.”

All the Demon Venerables had formed new relationships with the Cult Leader. Misunderstandings had been resolved, and fear had been lessened.

“Because of my lovely daughter-in-law, and because of my precious granddaughter.”

Come to think of it, Hayeon had been just one among those many reasons.

“And. Because you made me speak of someone I thought I would never again mention aloud.”

Geom Mugeuk knew that person was his mother.

“The reason I changed my mind is……”

At last, Geom Ujin added one final thing. The very person who had made all of this possible.

“Because of my son.”

Geom Mugeuk was swept up in an overwhelming surge of emotion. He had never imagined that his father would give meaning to all those things.

On the day he regressed, the old man of the heavens had said something to him.

How could a human possibly regress through a great art made by humans? I am allowing you to regress because of your efforts.

His life before regression, and his life after regression—in the end, both had been achieved through effort.

“My son, you have suffered much all this time.”

The instant he heard those words, tears flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s eyes along with a breathless “haa.” It was the moment he shed tears in front of all the Demon Venerables.

When Geom Mugeuk cried, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon turned his head to the side. Seeing the fellow cry had made him feel as though he, too, might shed tears.

All of the Eight Demon Venerables were deeply moved. They had watched Geom Mugeuk’s efforts from beside him. They knew very well how hard he had worked. Those efforts had, in the end, moved the Cult Leader’s heart.

“Thank you, Father.”

Geom Mugeuk performed a deep bow right there.

Drip, drip, drip.

Geom Mugeuk’s tears fell onto the floor.

“Please forgive this unfilial son who broke Father’s dream.”

Geom Ujin rose from his seat. He slowly moved forward and came down from the taishi chair.

He raised up his son, who had remained prostrated until then.

“I will leave the cult for a while, so guard the main cult well.”

Because his father’s gaze as he looked at him was endlessly gentle, the tension naturally left him.

“Where are you going? You’re not going to do all this and then storm the Murim Alliance, are you?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Geom Ujin answered that he was indeed doing just that.

“Yes, I am going to unite murim.”

“What?”

“Did you not tell me before? To travel with the Murim Alliance Leader and the Sado Alliance Leader. So I made an appointment to meet those people. We decided to fish together. You said winning through fishing was your unification of murim, did you not?”

Those words meant he had already decided on today’s result in advance.

Geom Mugeuk jumped in place. He should have realized long ago why Chief Strategist Sama Myeong was not present.

“No, then you had already decided? I thought your son was going to stop breathing back there.”

Geom Ujin’s distinctive sneer appeared at the corners of his mouth.

“If I wanted to make you stop breathing, all of murim would have to move.”

Geom Ujin strode forward, and the Demon Venerables followed behind him as though spreading their wings.

“No, just because Father is going, do all of you really have to follow him? You could stay behind and talk with me, comfort me for all my hardships. I’m the Young Cult Leader.

Later, you’ll have to receive your monthly stipend from me, you know!”

As he passed by, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon poked Geom Mugeuk in the side with the handle of the Myeolcheon Great Saber.

Long ago, he had jabbed him in the side like this and left it bruised, but today, he poked him lightly, playfully.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable tapped the sword hanging at his waist. It meant they should have a sparring match after so long.

The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable passed by with a polite bow, and the Drunken Demon made a gesture of drinking wine. It meant they should drink celebratory wine later.

The Fist Demon raised his fist. Let us have a late-night training gathering after so long.

The Demon Buddha congratulated him while emitting a dazzling golden radiance, and the Poison King sent a voice transmission he had never expected.

—Let us go to the Central Plains next time to get some fresh air.

The Poison King was suggesting that they first do the thing he hated most in the world. This must be the Poison King’s way of congratulating him.

The Extreme Evil Smiling Demon stopped in front of Geom Mugeuk. The two eyes behind the mask were bloodshot red. It seemed that when Geom Mugeuk had cried, he had shed tears with him.

He had not protected this world by himself. He had been able to protect it because they had protected him. Because they had been there, he had learned and realized all the things he could never have known alone.

‘Thank you, everyone.’

Geom Mugeuk drew the Black Demon Sword. The Extreme Evil Smiling Demon immediately understood Geom Mugeuk’s intent.

Clang.

When the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon flicked the sword with his finger, a clear sound rang throughout the Heavenly Demon Hall. It was the sound of the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon’s congratulations, and the sound announcing a new era.

***

Late at night, Gwiryeongja was diligently writing something on paper.

Not only his desk, but the entire room was piled with all kinds of books.

His wife, Im Hyang, came there carrying tea and fruit on a tray.

“Will you be working late again today?”

“I think I must.”

Im Hyang set the tray down on the tea table beside his desk and turned to leave, but then her steps stopped.

“Do you remember?”

Gwiryeongja raised his head and looked at Im Hyang. She continued speaking without turning around.

“My parents opposed our marriage. They said the successors of the Ghost Gate had, for generations, been people who devoted themselves only to research, and that they would make a woman lonely.

Back then, you said that would never happen. You promised that you would never make me lonely.”

Gwiryeongja could not offer any excuse. Because he had indeed promised.

“Once I finish this part I’m researching now, I can make time. I truly am almost done. Once I finish, let us go on a trip.”

Im Hyang did not believe him.

“You said that last time, too. That once you finished that part, it would be done.”

Once one part ended, another part appeared. And like that, the research continued.

“Work, then.”

Im Hyang went outside.

For some reason, today, her back looked even lonelier, and he thought he ought to follow her.

Gwiryeongja rose from his seat, and his gaze turned toward the paper on the desk that he had been writing on for some time.

He had gotten a feeling about his research. If this moment passed, he might ruin the research of the past several days. Yes, he would work today. She would understand.

Gwiryeongja sat down again and diligently wrote something. Just then, the door opened once more, and he sensed someone entering.

“Dear, I am truly sorry today. Tomorrow, let us be sure to drink tea together.”

But Im Hyang gave no answer.

When Gwiryeongja raised his head, someone else was standing there looking at him instead of his wife, Im Hyang.

Startled, Gwiryeongja was about to shout, but soon recognized who the other person was.

“You are?”

The person who had entered was none other than Geom Mugeuk.

In the past, Geom Mugeuk had gone with Ian to stop his marriage. But Gwiryeongja had truly loved Im Hyang, and in the end, that marriage had taken place.

Back then, Gwiryeongja had not known that Geom Mugeuk was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, but now he knew. He knew that the person he had seen then was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“I heard from Seojin who you are. Is my younger sibling doing well?”

“She has become the captain of Gwiyeongdae, the unit directly under my command.”

Gwiryeongja did not know whether to be pleased or worried.

“Is that not a dangerous position?”

Geom Mugeuk walked to the window, drew back the curtain, and opened it to air out the room.

“It is likely less dangerous than spending all your time researching in this stifling place. How many times do you even get up from your seat?”

Immersing himself only in research like this was probably what had eventually driven his relationship with Im Hyang beyond repair. From Im Hyang, who had just left, he could sense that they were nearly at their limit.

“Why have you come to see me?”

Then words Gwiryeongja could never have imagined flowed out.

“Send me to the past.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Gwiryeongja was startled out of his wits.

“How did you know about that?”

Geom Mugeuk gave no answer. Gwiryeongja soon assumed Seojin must have told him.

“The research is not yet complete.”

Geom Mugeuk knew. This man would devote his entire life to completing the Great Regression Art, but he would never be able to gather all the materials.

This time, it would be even harder.

He had already consumed the inner core of the Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp, and even if, by some stroke of luck, another Ten-Thousand-Year Fire Carp were found, the crucial Bimahon, the treasure of the Heavenly Demon, was in his father’s possession.

It would be passed down to him, and no one would be able to take it from him.

This man had staked his life on an impossible dream, and yet his eyes now were filled with resolve.

“I will complete it, no matter what.”

Geom Mugeuk asked him.

“And after you complete it, what then?”

Gwiryeongja could not answer that question. He had never thought about what came after. It was research he had inherited as his family’s legacy.

To him, it was something he naturally had to do, and he had never once considered doing anything else. To him, it was fate.

“Whom will you send back to the past?”

He had never thought about that either.

“At this rate, you will probably be the one who wants to go back to the past.”

Because that was exactly what had happened.

“Me? Why do you think that?”

Geom Mugeuk looked down at the tea and fruit his wife had left behind. Untouched, the fruit had changed color, and the tea had gone cold.

“Ah!”

In that moment, Gwiryeongja understood. This young cult leader was asking whether his relationship with his wife had not already become like that.

He could not deny it.

What Geom Mugeuk meant, in the end, was this: he would want to go back in order to fix his broken married life.

“No matter who you are…”

He could not bring himself to say that Geom Mugeuk should not meddle even in another man’s marriage. The other party was the young cult leader of that terrifying Demon Cult. No, he was the direct superior of his younger sibling.

Geom Mugeuk knew. A few words from him would not change Gwiryeongja, nor would they change his married life.

Even so, Geom Mugeuk did not want him to become unhappy.

“I failed to see others clearly, and because of that, I lost many things. That is why I always keep this in my heart. When it comes to relationships between people, do not imagine things on your own. Look at them properly, and listen to them properly.”

Geom Mugeuk rose from his seat and asked him.

“Are you looking properly at your wife?”

“!”

“Perhaps understanding the heart of the person beside you is harder than the difficult and magnificent great art you are researching. Do not worry about Seojin. I will look after her well.”

With only those words, Geom Mugeuk opened the door and was about to leave when Gwiryeongja shouted from behind him.

“This research is my family’s long-cherished wish!”

Geom Mugeuk turned back toward him.

“Pass down the research exactly as it is to future generations. In some generation, even without pouring in an entire lifetime as you have, a genius will appear who can complete the great art with ease. Keep passing it down until then. That genius will fulfill your family’s wish.”

As Geom Mugeuk turned again and was about to open the door and leave, Gwiryeongja asked from behind him.

“Do you believe this great art will succeed?”

I do. I may be the only person in this world who believes it.

But for the sake of Gwiryeongja’s life, Geom Mugeuk firmly shook his head.

“I do not.”

Geom Mugeuk left him behind and came out.

When he looked back after walking some distance away, he saw Gwiryeongja come out of the study and walk toward the building where his wife was.

At the very least, tonight he would talk with Im Hyang and spend time with her.

‘You are doing well. The past you want to return to is right now.’

Today, you have succeeded in regression.

***

Swaaaash.

Waves rolled in over the sun-shattered surface of the water, and the grains of sand on the beach where crabs crawled glittered like jewels.

Within the Space-Time Transposition Art, Geom Mugeuk sat side by side with Cheonmahon on a chair.

“It is all over now.”

He told Cheonmahon that he had stopped his father’s dream of unifying the murim.

As always, Cheonmahon merely listened in silence.

Having finished speaking, Geom Mugeuk leaned back comfortably in his chair and gazed at the blue sea.

As he rested beneath the warm sunlight, he heard a low, deep voice ring out.

“How will you live from now on?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Cheonmahon in surprise. The one who had just spoken was Cheonmahon.

It was the first time Cheonmahon had ever spoken to him.

Cheonmahon merely stared at the sea, as though he had said nothing at all. He was playing completely innocent.

Geom Mugeuk, too, looked back at the sea as if nothing had happened.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned beyond the sea toward the sky. Even though he tried to hold it back, a smile spread across his face.

Geom Mugeuk’s bright smile, resembling the sea and resembling the sky, rode the wind and spread far away.

The End.

***

To all the readers who have read Absolute Regression until now, thank you from the bottom of my heart.

While writing this work, I received far more love than I deserved.

Throughout the entire time I was writing, I was truly happy as an author.

From my debut work until now, I have written only martial arts fiction, and I think all of you gave a pat on the shoulder to that stubborn devotion.

I bow my head in gratitude to all of you who encouraged me through your comments and sternly scolded me when I strayed down the wrong path.

During the three years and six months I spent with Mugeuk, I, too, did my very best and wrote with all my heart.

When you even created and loved the affectionate nickname “Late-Night Reading Club,” how could I not write diligently?

I truly tried, and tried again. Enough that I can confidently say, “This was the very best I could do.”

And yet, now that I have put down the final period and look back, there are so many parts that feel lacking and regrettable. It must be because of this regret that I go on to write the next work.

An ending is always regrettable. I have already parted thirteen times with the characters in my works, and parted with all of you as well, but I do not think I have ever felt as much regret as I do with this work.

(Since many people are curious about side stories, I will say just one thing.

Because so many of you are so saddened by the ending, I am considering whether I might fill a little of that regret with side stories for the tales I was unable to tell.

The only thing I can promise you is writing, but there is also nothing that refuses to go my way as much as writing does, so I cannot give you a definite answer. Still, while taking time to recharge, I will think deeply about it.)

I would like to take this opportunity to sincerely thank Team Leader Yun Duhan, who handled editing throughout the entire serialization period.

I would also like to thank author Park Jinhwan and author JP, who are currently serializing the webtoon of Absolute Regression. They are truly making it so wonderfully!

And more than anyone else, I thank the readers once again.

Please stay healthy and happy until we meet again!

—Sincerely, Jang Yeonghun.
